# KALSHI MAKER — COMPREHENSIVE RESOLUTION PLAN — 2026-08-07 (proposal; NOTHING BUILT, NOTHING DEMOTED)

## S0. OPERATOR RULINGS — 2026-08-07 (BINDING; recorded verbatim, applied to the sequence below)

**RULING 1 — P2 WINDOW: FREEZE LIVE CHANGES UNTIL THE VERDICT (2026-08-10T14:13Z).**
Until the verdict, ship ONLY offline/telemetry items — atomic-write guards, knob-derivation prints,
shadow columns. NOTHING touching admission, sizing, or the halt meter.
*Consequence applied:* the plan's Waves that change live trading behaviour are all gated behind
2026-08-10T14:13Z. The permitted universe is the 45 items whose files are NOT executed inside the
live quoter process (computed, listed in S0.1) — MINUS any item whose OUTPUT is a hot-reloaded JSON
table, because those are ungated live config pushes (see S1 finding B).

**RULING 2 — D-A (KALSHI_EST_FEED): HOLD ARMING UNTIL THE GUARD LANDS.**
Do not arm EST_FEED until M-9 (activate emitter/gate arithmetic) plus M-6/M-10 (flat-activate
refusal) are fixed and tested.
*Consequence applied:* queue item 2 of the 08-06 EOD handoff (arm D-A after the APRPOTUS
checkpoint) is SUSPENDED pending those three fixes. The 2026-08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS est->credit
checkpoint READ still proceeds — it is a measurement, not an arming. M-9/M-6/M-10 are live-path
fixes, so under Ruling 1 they land after 2026-08-10T14:13Z; D-A arming therefore follows the
verdict, not the checkpoint.

**RULING 3 — R-1 (reference-price model): SHADOW-MEASURE FIRST, THEN ARM.**
Land a pure venue-faithful Target/5 reference as a shadow telemetry column (no behaviour change),
collect ~a week against the estimates feed to measure the real bias, then arm the corrected scorer
on evidence.
*Consequence applied:* n-19 (the pure `_lip_reference` shadow column) becomes the long pole for the
entire R-1 class and should start as early as the freeze permits. **TENSION TO RESOLVE (flagged,
not silently decided):** a shadow column is telemetry and is permitted by Ruling 1, but publishing
it into the live process requires a RESTART, and a restart costs presence-seconds and queue
position inside the very window Ruling 1 protects. The two rulings point opposite ways here.
Options: (a) start the shadow column at the first restart that happens for another reason;
(b) accept one annotated restart now to start the measurement clock ~3 days earlier; (c) hold the
shadow column until after the verdict, accepting R-1 arming slips by ~3 days. DEFAULT if unruled:
(c) — it is the only option that keeps Ruling 1 absolute.

## S1. THE HONEST CONS (operator asked explicitly for true concerning downsides — leading with them)

THE HONEST CONS — 147-item fix plan against a LIVE Kalshi maker, 4 days before a pre-committed verdict

All line numbers verified this session against C:/Users/samwa/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--lockes-picks-polymarket-ai-v2/5dfe0ebf-2821-475d-946c-72012db34c3b/scratchpad/kalshi-wt/kalshi_live/ at HEAD 233ee86. Constants quoted are read from source, not paraphrased. Anything I could not read (the VPS live.env) is labelled UNVERIFIED.

=== THE ONE-PARAGRAPH VERSION ===
The plan is technically good and the individual designs are unusually honest. The problem is not any single fix — it is TIMING and COUPLING. The P2 verdict (CANON, 2026-08-10T14:13Z, rule = window credits > window drag) had WINDOW CREDITS = $0.00 at the last read (08-06T14:14Z, per docs/maker_handoffs/KALSHI_HANDOFF_2026-08-06_EOD.md:54-55), and LIP pays at PROGRAM CONCLUSION — so essentially the entire credit numerator of the verdict lands in the final ~48 hours of the window (APRPOTUS ended 08-07T15:00Z; FIX-H endings 08-09T03:59Z). Every fix in this set that reduces admissions, reduces resting size, or costs a deploy-restart subtracts directly from a number that has NOT YET BEEN EARNED. And because the venue's floor is $1 per market-PROGRAM, the loss is a CLIFF, not a slope: a 30% presence reduction on a program tracking to $1.30 of credit is not -30%, it is -100%.

=== FOUR STRUCTURAL FINDINGS THE ITEM SET ITSELF GETS WRONG ===

(A) "OFFLINE TOOL, NO LIVE PATH" IS FALSE FOR ~18 ITEMS. The live quoter imports and executes six of these "offline" modules: kalshi_presence_calibrate (:1234), kalshi_netev_calibrate (:1392), kalshi_market_scores (:2193/:2646/:5376/:5420/:6032), kalshi_fill_costs (:2683), kalshi_market_sweeper (:2712), kalshi_capital_rank (:453/:791/:3647/:5017). So M-15/m-43/n-28/n-29 (fill costs), M-20/m-40/n-37 (capital rank), n-34/n-35 (market scores), m-45/n-40 (presence calibrate) run INSIDE the live process, not beside it. Their DARK justifications ("offline, no trading path, no flag needed") are wrong as written.

(B) FOUR JSON FILES HOT-RELOAD INTO LIVE MONEY GATES WITH NO DEPLOY PROTOCOL. run_once calls _presence_table_refresh() (:3938) and _netev_table_refresh() (:3942) EVERY CYCLE, mtime-gated; _d3_feedback_cached (:784-797) reloads the credit-feedback table every 60s; _refresh_fill_costs (:3958 -> :2667) rebuilds the fill-cost feed hourly. The md5-vs-HEAD-blob + .bak deploy discipline covers .py files. It does NOT cover kalshi_presence_table.json, kalshi_netev_table.json, the credit-feedback table, or the fill-cost feed — any of which changes live gating within 1-60 seconds of the file appearing on disk. n-40 and m-45-C2 are described as offline regenerations; they are in fact un-gated live config pushes.

(C) kalshi_fill_costs.build() RUNS INSIDE THE LIVE CYCLE. M-15 adds a per-ticker replay over the whole tape plus a population-loss guard; m-43 adds fee-convention detection. Exceptions are swallowed into _SILENT["fillcost_refresh_fail"], but LATENCY is not bounded and neither design names a timeout. An hourly build that gets slower lengthens a live cycle that must complete before the next book move.

(D) SUITE-GREEN PROVES THE WRONG PATH. Baseline 1233/2xf passes with essentially every new behavior behind a default-off flag — i.e. the suite validates the path that already works. Concretely: test_capture_gate.py:236-240 asserts q._qualifying_score == scorecard.qualifying_share. Once M-2 arms KALSHI_LIP_REF=1, that documented invariant is FALSE in the live configuration and the test STILL PASSES because tests run flag-off. Nothing in the plan pins any flag-ON population effect, which is where all the money is.

=== VOLUME ===
147 items; 7 explicit no-fix (n-1, n-53, m-14, n-20, n-16, m-50, n-50); 140 carry change. 52 are self-labelled concerning=true. ~89 touch code executing in the live process (quoter / ws daemon / ws feed / client); adding the ~18 from finding (A)/(B) gives ~107 of 147 on the live path — not the ~60 the "offline" labels imply. The quoter alone is 7,317 lines carrying 149 distinct KALSHI_* names, of which only 26 are hot-reloadable (_refresh_safety_knobs `watch`, :3848-3872) and 9 more are restart-only-warned (:3877-3890) — i.e. ~114 knobs ALREADY give an operator a silent no-op on a mid-flight edit. The plan adds ~35 more (LIP_REF, RAMP_BASIS, PRESENCE_KEEP_S, RETAIN_ON_READFAIL, TWO_SIDED_ENFORCE, STOPFLAT_DEFER, RL_BACKOFF, ALLOC_REF_AGE, ALLOC_RAMP_EST, SCORE_SWING_BOTH, SCORE_PENALTY_SCOPE, LEDGER_LATE_GRACE_S, LEDGER_WAL_RECOVER, FILLCOST_FEE_NORM, WS_SEQ_STRICT, WS_CTX_LEAN, WS_WATCH_MAX, CAP_SPLIT_UNWIND, PIVOT_EARNERS, STRAND_MAX_MKTS, STANDING_ZERO_HONEST, ACTIVATE_ATOMIC, ACTIVATE_CREDIT_MODEL, RAMP_WD_EFFECTIVE, F3_PER_SIDE, MACRO_PROBE_MIN_EDGE/LEVELS, SWEEP_OWN_ORDERS, SCORE_FOLD_ALWAYS, FAMILY_DROP_LOG_MAX, CAPRANK_DEPTH, MAP_BACKFILL, EST_RATE_MIN_SPAN_S, THROTTLE_BAND_KEEP, LIP_REF_FRAC, ...), a ~23% increase. Shipped risk is low because they default off; ARMING risk is combinatorial and completely untested — no test in the plan exercises pairs, and at least three of the new flags (LIP_REF, RAMP_BASIS, F3_PER_SIDE) are independent multipliers on the SAME expected-credit number, two shrinking and one growing.

=== WHERE THE SET OVER-WORRIES ===
m-46 (credit_history pagination) is marked concerning=true on the grounds that recovered credits change the live NETEV table. Lifetime credits were 58 rows / $198.95 at the 08-03T12:40:11Z read against limit=1000 — truncation is not currently binding, so today it is close to inert. Treating it as high-risk risks crowding out M-21/M-22, which change the SAME table's arithmetic materially. n-32 is concerning=true for a fix that changes no number; that is correct to flag, but the concern is epistemic (it retroactively weakens prior residual-based claims), not operational.

=== THE SHAPE OF A SAFE PLAN, IF IT HELPS ===
Nothing that changes admission, sizing, or the halt meter should land before 2026-08-10T14:13Z. Between now and then the only defensible ships are: m-6 (print-only knob-derivation, and a prerequisite for ~6 other items not being silent no-ops), the M-16 atomic-write helper alone (~8 lines, protects the 08-07T15:00Z checkpoint), and pure-telemetry additions (m-12, m-42, n-18, n-19, m-33/n-42, M-26). Everything in Tier 0/1 below wants to land AFTER the verdict, one at a time, with a measured shadow column first.

The ranked concerns follow, each with its do-nothing cost.

- **1. THE VERDICT WINDOW IS THE WRONG PLACE TO STAND (schedule risk, dominates everything)** — P2 is CANON: due 2026-08-10T14:13Z, rule = window credits > window drag. Window credits were $0.00 at the 08-06T14:14Z read (KALSHI_HANDOFF_2026-08-06_EOD.md:54-55). Venue-published: payment at PROGRAM CONCLUSION, and 'final reward below $1 for an individual PROGRAM is not paid' (KALSHI_REWARD_GAME_AUDIT_2026-08-06.md:28-30). So the entire numerator lands in the last ~48h (APRPOTUS 08-07T15:00Z, FIX-H 08-09T03:59Z). CONSEQUENCE: any admission- or size-reducing fix landed now subtracts from an unearned number, and the $1/program floor makes it a CLIFF — a 30% presence cut on a program tracking to $1.30 costs 100% of that program's credit, not 30%. The drag side is already measured at roughly -$12/window (08-06: gas ~-$9.42 of -$11.96 window net), so the verdict is close and a handful of programs decide it. DO-NOTHING COST: exactly zero. The verdict was pre-committed against the CURRENT code; leaving it alone is the only configuration in which a pass or fail means what it was defined to mean. concerning=true.
- **2. DEPLOY CHURN IS ITSELF A REWARD TAX (aggregate, unmeasured)** — The live path is a long-lived daemon (polymarket-maker-kalshi-ws.service). Every quoter deploy is a service restart: WS mirrors dropped and re-seeded, footprint re-selected, standing orders re-diffed (diff_orders cancels standing not in `desired`), queue position forfeited. Reward is share-of-score integrated over 1-second random snapshots — queue position and continuous presence ARE the product. The project norm is one fix per commit; 140 changing items deployed 1:1 is ~140 restarts, batched is fewer restarts but a larger unattributable blast per deploy. Neither is acceptable inside the verdict window. UNVERIFIED: seconds of presence lost per restart — and that absence is itself a finding: not one of the 147 items adds a 'presence lost to restart/redeploy' meter, despite CLASS-NOT-INSTANCE being the standing rule. DO-NOTHING COST: none for the churn itself; the cost of never measuring it is that the lane will keep making deploy-cadence decisions blind. concerning=true.
- **3. M-2 (+M-5/M-7/M-17/M-18) LIP REFERENCE — the single most dangerous fix in the set** — MECHANISM: _qualifying_score (:2536-2582) anchors reference at the best bid and breaks the walk at cum >= target. Venue truth is reference = first level where cumulative depth reaches Target/5 (200ct at the measured target_size=1000 on 3,755 of 3,802 programs) with no break. Both corrections move the same way: rivals above the true reference get 1.0x instead of 0.5^n, and the whole tail counts — so the denominator grows and OUR MODELLED SHARE FALLS (~2x typical, 20x+ thin-top). THE COMPOUNDING NOBODY HAS PRICED: that number feeds THREE live consumers simultaneously. (a) PRESENCE_GATE=1 with MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 (:1213) — an ABSOLUTE floor whose '+20% modelling margin' was fitted on the inflated basis, so arming M-2 is a de facto ~2x tightening of the live entry gate that nothing recomputes. (b) NETEV_GATE=1's unproven-family fallback at :3022-3024: signal = pc/NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT(3.0) - NETEV_FINGERPRINT_USD_DAY(5.0) > 0, i.e. TODAY requires pc > $15/day; after M-2 it requires legacy-basis pc > $30/day. Most of the exploration universe is unproven, so this is the wider blade. (c) CAPTURE_MIN_USD_DAY=5.0 if CAPTURE_GATE is ever armed. THE DOUBLE-DISCOUNT: NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT=3.0 was fitted on the model's measured 2-6x over-prediction; M-2 removes ~2x of exactly that over-prediction, so arming without a joint re-fit discounts the same error twice (~6x effective) — the code's own W12 comment at :2616-2618 documents this identical trap. INVARIANT BREAK: test_capture_gate.py:236-240 pins _qualifying_score == scorecard.qualifying_share; armed, that is false and the test still passes at flag-off. DO-NOTHING COST: real and large — the floor gate and the rank keep systematically over-rating thin-top books, we keep opening markets that pay near-zero while carrying full fill risk, and the '$1.20 = floor + 20%' claim stays a fiction. This fix SHOULD happen; it should not happen before 08-10 and it should not arm without MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT moving in the same deploy. concerning=true.
- **4. m-8 / m-58 / m-38 — THE SERIES-BAN CASCADE AND THE NEW-SERIES DEADLOCK (not named in the item text)** — MECHANISM THE ITEMS DO NAME: the floor-gate mirror at :1324-1329 applies the explore probe clamp ONLY outside SERIES_ALLOW, while the actual sizer at :5311-5317 applies it with NO allowlist condition — so the gate models 25-50ct for rows that actually rest EXPLORE_PROBE_CT. Unifying is correct. MECHANISM THEY DO NOT NAME: markets in an allowlisted series that has a permanently-OUT ($5-rung) member are tagged explore=True at :4095-4103 and :4580-4582. After unification their modelled credit falls by ramp_ct/EXPLORE_PROBE_CT — with D3_RUNGS=[5,10,25,50] (:529-534) that is up to 10x at rung 3 vs a 5ct probe — pushing them under MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 and into presence_skipped. NET EFFECT: ONE $5-rung ban silently gates out the REST of that series. Memory records nine current permanent bans originated at the $5 rung, so this is live, not hypothetical, and it is a ban propagating by side effect — the shape the lane's own canon ('no bans as fixes', feedback_kalshi_no_bans_root_fix_logic) exists to forbid. SECOND ORDER — DEADLOCK: 5ct against Target 1000 cannot model $1.20, so the new-series probe program dies; probes are how a series earns receipts; D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 (:527) holds unproven series at 10ct until receipts exist. Probes gated out => no receipts => clamp never lifts => permanent new-series lockout. This is structurally the SAME deadlock m-20's design explicitly builds a guard against, and m-8 has no equivalent guard. CONDITIONALITY: UNVERIFIED — EXPLORE_PROBE_CT defaults 0 (:402) and ALLOW_PROBE_EXCEPTION defaults 0 (:519); m-8's own text says the live values are 'BELIEVED, not read'. If EXPLORE_PROBE_CT=0 live the explore half is inert and only the D3 half bites. READ live.env BEFORE ANY DECISION HERE. DO-NOTHING COST: the floor gate keeps admitting rows it prices at 25-50ct that rest 5ct — we pay adverse-fill risk for formula-invisible presence, exactly the 'spread thin, earn zero' shape the audit named. concerning=true.
- **5. M-29 NET-EV UNIT FIX — arms a dead gate 0.91 percentage points from the worst family, days before the table is rebuilt under it** — MECHANISM: at :3018 `poor = net_pct < NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT` compares a FRACTION (gas -0.0468) to the live knob -7.0 — structurally unreachable, so 'receipt' grade currently means UNCONDITIONALLY QUOTE. M-29 converts to NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_FRAC = pct/100 and the gate becomes real. ARITHMETIC AT TODAY'S TABLE (:3007-3011): all six receipt families read negative — KXTOPMODEL -3.12%, gas -4.68%, KXTRUMPENDORSEMENTS -5.00%, KXDXYDUD -5.60%, KXTRUMPTIME -5.89%, temp -6.09% — and the recorded ladder is -4% keeps 1 of 6, -5% keeps 2, -6% keeps 5, -7% keeps all 6. So at the live -7.0 the fix is ~a no-op TODAY, with a margin of 0.91pp to the first bench. THE COLLISION: within roughly 72 hours, B8 rebuilds this table on a different (post-fix) basis, M-21/M-22 change how net_pct_notional is computed (market-scope containment / straddle exclusion), and m-46 changes which credits are counted. Any one of those moving temp or gas past -7% benches an ENTIRE FAMILY, silently, and the code's own comment (:3016-3020) warns that benching on this table 'launders defect losses into this family loses money' under RULE SEVEN. Four independent changes to one live gate's input, converging on a 0.91pp margin, is the definition of an unpriced interaction. DO-NOTHING COST: the receipt half of NETEV_GATE stays decorative — the lane believes it has a receipt-calibrated brain and it does not, and every 'the gate protected us' claim is unsupported. concerning=true.
- **6. n-40 / m-45-C2 — a presence-table regeneration is an UN-GATED LIVE CONFIG PUSH, not an offline build** — MECHANISM: _presence_table_refresh() runs every cycle at :3938 and mtime-reloads kalshi_presence_table.json; _presence_factor (:1266-1277) multiplies expected credit by presence_median, falling back to PRESENCE_DEFAULT=1.0 (:1214) and reading the wildcard key `*|bucket` when the family bucket is absent. Arming n-40's wildcard moves every family with no own bucket from 1.0 to the pooled median (item reports ~0.057 measured) — a ~17x cut in expected credit against a $1.20 floor, i.e. near-total mass skip — AND IT TAKES EFFECT WITHIN ONE CYCLE OF THE FILE LANDING. No restart, no md5 check, no .bak, no operator gate. Same channel for m-45-C2 (resting_to_now medians). The precise families it would hit are the exploration ones D3_RAMP/PIVOT_SELECT exist to discover. This is the one place in the plan where a 'read-only offline tool' can take the bot to near-zero quoting with a single file copy. DO-NOTHING COST: the wildcard fallback remains a producer with no contract, so uncalibrated families silently ride PRESENCE_DEFAULT=1.0 — 'assume perfect execution' — which is the OPTIMISTIC direction on a live entry gate. Both halves are true; the fix is right and the delivery channel is wrong. RECOMMENDATION: bring the two JSON gate tables under the same md5/.bak/operator-approval discipline as the .py files before touching either producer. concerning=true.
- **7. n-33 (credit-status filter) changes LIVE RESTING SIZE within 60 seconds, via a hand-invented vocabulary** — MECHANISM: _d3_feedback_cached (:784-797) reloads the credit-feedback table every 60s; _d3_est_ct (:617-636) and _d3_ramp_ct treat a family as `proven` iff credits_n > 0, and unproven clamps the rung to D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 = 10ct instead of up to 50ct. n-33 skips credit rows whose status is in an INVENTED non-final set {pending, reversed, canceled, cancelled, failed, clawed_back, voided} — no such value has ever been observed. If the venue uses any of those words for a FINAL credit, the affected family's credits_n can drop to 0, it becomes unproven, and its live resting size falls 5x — within one minute, with no restart, no flag, and no deploy. It is a no-op today only because 58/58 observed rows are status='applied' (measured 08-03). That is a DATA-DEPENDENT no-op, not a structural one, and the item's darkness justification treats it as the latter. DO-NOTHING COST: genuinely small — we currently accept every status as final, which has been correct on 58/58 rows. This is a fix defending against a hypothesis, priced as if it were free. concerning=true (reclassified upward from the item's 'false').
- **8. m-19 MAKES THE $10 HALT STRICTER — and a halt is the most expensive event this system has** — MECHANISM: :4872 `elif st.get('equity_day') != _day or _consume_day_baseline_marker():` short-circuits, so on a day roll the marker survives; m-19 swaps the operands so the roll consumes it. Its write-up calls this 'strictly stricter, no risk'. On this book it is not low-risk. The halt path (:4927-4936) writes STOP, calls _flatten_all and returns — which ENDS PRESENCE on every market for the duration of the halt. Under the per-program $1 floor, losing the tail of a program can zero that program's whole credit rather than shave it, so a halt is simultaneously a drag event AND a credits-to-$0 event on both sides of the verdict inequality. Empirically this is not remote: the bot auto-halted 08-06 at 15:54:54Z on dd $10.31 from day-peak $300.71 and required a manual operator rearm at 17:36:23Z (that rearm USED the marker mechanism m-19 is changing, consumed 17:37:20Z). Raising halt probability inside the verdict window is a direct threat to the verdict. DO-NOTHING COST: a marker dropped shortly before 00:00Z can grant an unnamed second baseline reset — a real but small, one-shot measurement error on the governor, with no direct money effect. concerning=true (reclassified upward from the item's 'false').
- **9. m-13 (_envb vocabulary) CANNOT BE PROVEN SAFE FROM THE SANDBOX — worst case it flips a live safety flag at restart** — MECHANISM: widening _envb (:170-177) to accept {true,yes,on}/{false,no,off} changes the parsed value of any boolean knob currently spelled with a non-'1' truthy token. TAKER_FLATTEN (:1587, default True) is the sharp one: it gates the ONLY code path in the bot that pays a taker fee, and a silent OFF->ON at the next restart starts real spread-crossing on a live book. Neither I nor the item author can read /opt/pa2-maker-kalshi-live/live.env from this sandbox, so 'provable no-op' is currently UNPROVABLE. The item is correctly gated on a pre-deploy grep, but that grep is a MANDATORY BLOCKER, not a nicety, and it is the kind of step that gets skipped on an S-effort 'parser hardening' commit. DO-NOTHING COST: an operator who writes `KALSHI_X=true` today gets a silent False and a silently disabled feature — the exact silent-no-op class the lane has been fighting since Gov-D9. concerning=true.
- **10. m-29 RL_BACKOFF — the clearest path in the set to a SELF-INFLICTED OUTAGE, and it cannot be tested** — MECHANISM: honoring Retry-After means sleeping on the write worker thread. If that sleep happens while `cold_lock` is held, the cold cycle stalls — and the cold cycle is what places quotes AND what places exits. A malformed or hostile Retry-After (HTTP-date mis-parsed, or a large integer) converts a rate-limit hint into a quoting-and-exit outage of up to RL_MAX_SLEEP_S per occurrence, on a book carrying inventory. UNTESTABLE: no 429 has ever been observed on this account — the counter that would prove the rate exists is being added BY THIS FIX. So the arming decision has literally no evidence to stand on, and the failure mode only appears in the incident the fix exists for. The design's own mitigation (cap the sleep, never sleep under the lock) is correct and must be treated as load-bearing rather than defensive. DO-NOTHING COST: on a real 429 storm the client retries blind and burns the venue's patience; today that has never happened. concerning=true.
- **11. M-9 armed retires most of the activate path; M-6 armed commits up to 50% of the book into a book that is thin by definition** — M-9: D3_RUNGS tops at 50 and D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 holds unproven series at 10ct, so on a Target-1000 program a 'bridge both sides to Target' feasibility test can essentially never pass — arming ACTIVATE_ATOMIC retires the lane by arithmetic rather than by decision. M-6: MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL=150.0 (:238) against _total_cap() = min(10000, mark equity ~$300) (:251-255) is up to HALF THE ENTIRE BOOK committed at the touch into a single thin market — the largest single at-fair-value exposure the bot can take. THE COLLISION: M-6 makes the activate credit gate ask about the book we WOULD create (more permissive), M-9 makes it refuse unless the bridge is fully feasible (far more restrictive). They touch the same lane in opposite directions, so landing both produces a net behavior neither commit predicts, and each is flagged separately as if it were the only change. DO-NOTHING COST: M-9 — the activate path can commit capital into a book it cannot actually bring to Target, i.e. buy depth that pays nobody. M-6 — a second independent kill of the same lane stays invisible. concerning=true (both).
- **12. m-11 (PRESENCE_KEEP_S) LOOSENS a live money gate on a premise the instrument that would test it is switched OFF** — MECHANISM: the fix keeps a market resting under the $1.20 floor for PRESENCE_KEEP_S after we last rested there, on the theory that BANKED accrual makes continuation +EV. That premise is MODELED, not measured. The venue's own accrual number is EST_FEED — which is EST_FEED=0 (dark) and, when armed, is gated at EST_FEED_MIN_FRAC=0.25 (:729) precisely in the LATE window where banked accrual dominates the decision. Its arming is also gated behind the 08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS est->credit checkpoint, which has not yet returned. ## S2. COUPLING AND COLLISION MAP (which fixes must ship together, and in what order)

COLLISION AND DEPENDENCY MAP — 147-item fix set, worktree C:/Users/samwa/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--lockes-picks-polymarket-ai-v2/5dfe0ebf-2821-475d-946c-72012db34c3b/scratchpad/kalshi-wt/kalshi_live/, HEAD 233ee86. All line numbers verified by reading the files this session.

=====================================================================
0. THREE PREMISE CORRECTIONS THAT REORDER THE WHOLE MAP
=====================================================================
Before any grouping is usable, three premises embedded in the item set are wrong or unverified against the code/env I was given, and each of them silently changes which fixes are "safe."

(0-A) WS_HOT IS 1 IN THE LIVE ENV. Four items (M-13, m-1, n-51, m-30) justify shipping unflagged with "gated by KALSHI_WS_HOT=0, provable no-op." The LIVE ENV block in this task states WS_HOT=1 (read 2026-08-06T19:48:01Z), and maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:100 defines WS_HOT with default 0 but :608 routes every book event through `self.hot_event(...) if WS_HOT else "stage_a"`. So Stage B is LIVE, with real money, right now. Consequence: `Daemon.hot_event` (maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:424-459) is a SECOND live call site of `desired_quotes` — and at :447-448 it calls it with NO `own_orders=` and with `stats={}`. Two structural effects that touch a large fraction of the set:
  (i) EVERY fix to an own_orders-dependent gate (M-2 LIP walk, m-2 F5 staleness, m-8/m-58 eff_ct, M-6, n-2, m-10, m-11) behaves DIFFERENTLY on the hot path than the cold path, because hot passes own_orders=None (the legacy double-counting branch of _qualifying_score, maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2570-2578). A fix validated on the cold path is not validated on the live hot path.
  (ii) EVERY standing detector implemented as a qstats counter folded into the plan row is BLIND to hot-path evaluations, because hot discards `stats={}`. Roughly two dozen items in this set propose exactly that kind of detector (presence_continued_under_floor, activate_*, netev_*, d3_*, ramp_floored_markets, onesided_accumulating, …). They will systematically under-count by whatever share of quoting traffic is hot. This is a cross-cutting defect in the DETECTOR design of the batch, not of any one item.

(0-B) ALLOC_KEY's LIVE VALUE IS UNRESOLVED AND GATES SIX ITEMS. The 08-04 EOD handoff records ALLOC_KEY=1; the 08-06T19:48Z flag list in this task omits it; maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3631 defaults it to 0. `_alloc_priority` (:3643-3679) returns `usd_day` verbatim when ALLOC_KEY=0, so with the flag off, kalshi_capital_rank, the fill-cost feed, the sweeper pcap merge and credit_feedback never reach a live decision. m-39/m-61/n-11, M-20/m-40, m-36/n-33, M-15/m-43 all flip between "offline data quality" and "live capital reordering" on that one unread value. NOTHING in the allocation cluster should be scheduled until live.env is read.

(0-C) SCORE_RANK MAKES "TELEMETRY-ONLY" ITEMS NON-TELEMETRY ACROSS CYCLES. n-38 claims it "cannot alter `desired`." True within the cycle — but `_market_telemetry_row`'s output is folded into SCORES at :5422, and SCORES is read at :2196 (kalshi_market_scores.rank → explore/pivot SELECTION), :5026 (select-budget refs) and :3661/3667 (alloc). So n-38's changed capture_usd_day/y_ct feeds NEXT cycle's market selection whenever SCORE_RANK=1. maker_kalshi_quoter.py:388 defaults SCORE_RANK=0 and the live value is not in the given flag list. Same unread-value dependency as 0-B.

=====================================================================
1. THE NAMED COUPLING, AND IT IS A TRIANGLE, NOT A PAIR
=====================================================================
The activate lane (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3084-3131) has FOUR stacked mechanisms, and the operator-named pair is the middle two. Verified reading order inside `desired_quotes`:

  [1] :2900-2907 `unqualifiable` gate — `_addable = MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL / max(best_y,best_n,0.01)` (default 150.0, :238). NOT clamped by INV_HARD_CT (80.0, :1555) and NOT clamped by the D3 ramp. It therefore OVER-states what we can bridge — a defect in the permissive direction.
  [2] :3098-3110 activate PRESENCE_GATE floor — calls `_expected_credit_usd` (:1292), which calls `_prospective_capture` (:2584), which returns **hard 0.0** whenever either side fails `cum >= target` (:2601-2602 `if not (qy and qn): return 0.0`). A void book is void BY DEFINITION of `ext_y < target or ext_n < target` (:2896). So on the activate path the model ALWAYS returns 0, always < MIN_CREDIT_USD (1.20, :1213), and the lane is UNCONDITIONALLY DEAD. This is M-6's "the gate asks about the book as it stands."
  [3] :3115-3119 cost gate — charges `best_y*add_y + best_n*add_n` on the UNCLAMPED add sizes.
  [4] :3125-3131 emitter clamp — rests `min(int(add_y), _acl)` where `_acl = INV_HARD_CT`; then the D3 ramp at :5341-5346 clamps again via `_d3_apply_ramp` (:639-651), whose carve-out list is `("unwind","macro_probe")` — 'activate' is NOT carved out, so activate counts get rung-clamped to D3_RUNGS (5/10/25/50, :529-534) and to D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 (10ct) for unproven series.

The masking is: **[4] is harmless today only because [2] is broken.** Mechanism [4] emits a ladder that provably cannot reach Target on a Target-1000 program (80ct hard ceiling, 5-10ct after the ramp), so the side never qualifies, the snapshot is EXCLUDED for everyone under the venue LIP rule, we earn exactly $0 and carry real fill risk. That never fires because [2] refuses first.

CONSEQUENCES, and this is the part neither item states:
  - M-9 (activate atomicity / feasibility refusal) landed ALONE is a **provable live no-op** — not because it is safe, but because the lane it guards is already dead. It looks like a free commit and is actually a pre-authorization.
  - m-10 (give the activate floor gate the JOIN path's `_resting_here` continuation exemption, mirroring :2933-2942) landed ALONE **un-masks [4]** on any market where we already rest — activate begins emitting infeasible, never-qualifying ladders with live capital.
  - M-6 (add_to_cum: count our intended size toward Target) landed ALONE **un-masks [4] far more broadly** — it is precisely the fix that makes [2] stop returning 0, and it does not touch [4] at all.
  - THERE IS A THIRD UN-MASKER NOBODY NAMED: **arming EST_FEED (D-A) revives the activate lane.** `_expected_credit_usd` :1334-1340 does `expected = max(expected, _est)` from `_est_feed_cached`, and that floor is applied on the ACTIVATE call site (:3105) exactly as on the JOIN one — gated only on `frac >= EST_FEED_MIN_FRAC` (:729), with no `void` scoping. A single positive venue estimate on a void market lifts `_expA` over the $1.20 floor and opens the lane, straight into [4]. Arming EST_FEED is on the queue immediately after the 2026-08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS checkpoint. This is the highest-probability accidental un-masking in the entire batch, and D-A's own item text (m-4, m-15) never mentions the activate path.
  - Gate [1]'s over-statement is symmetric: it is currently harmless because [2] kills everything anyway. If [2] is fixed and [1] is not, the permissive `_addable` (which ignores INV_HARD_CT and the ramp) lets books through that the emitter can never bridge. M-9's feasibility helper is the right home for the correction, which is another reason M-9 must lead.
  - The select-budget walk charges activate markets at `min(est_commit_usd, EXPLORE_PROBE_CT?, _d3_est_ct)` (:5063-5070) — i.e. roughly $5-10 — while `cap_desired` (:3709-3757) charges the real `_mkt_capital(qs)`, which for an activate market can approach MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL. That under-charge is currently invisible because activate never emits. Reviving the lane makes the select budget systematically under-read.

MANDATORY ORDER: **M-9 (dark, KALSHI_ACTIVATE_ATOMIC) must land and be armed BEFORE m-10, before M-6, and before EST_FEED is armed.** All four should be treated as one gated sequence with a single arming decision, not four independent commits.

=====================================================================
2. MORE COUPLINGS OF THAT EXACT SHAPE (defect harmless only because of another defect)
=====================================================================
I hunted specifically for this shape. Seven more, ranked by how likely they are to bite:

(K2) **The net-EV unit bug masks the ENTIRE net-EV table-content cluster.** maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3017-3020: `poor = net_pct < NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT`, where `net_pct` is a FRACTION and the knob is a PERCENT set to -7.0 live. `x < -7.0` is unreachable for any real fraction, so the RECEIPT branch is a structural UNCONDITIONAL PASS, and the UNPROVEN branch (:3021-3024, model minus fingerprint) is the only live gate. Therefore, today, the ONLY way the net-EV table changes live behavior is by moving a family across the `confidence == "receipt"` boundary — the net% VALUES are inert. That means: M-21/M-22 (scope containment can drop families out of receipt grade), m-46 (pagination recovers older credits and can promote/demote grades), n-39 (adds taker-only families as 'unproven'), m-41, m-43 (fee basis) are ALL currently acting through the label only. The moment M-29 lands, the values become live and every one of those items changes meaning. **M-29 must not ship in the same window as any table-content fix or a B8 rebuild**, and the temp family's -6.09% against a -7.0 knob is 0.9 percentage points of headroom — a rebuilt table is the likeliest thing to cross it.

(K3) **The fetch-fail retention is safe only because cap_desired keeps mixed lists unconditionally.** The retention at :5233-5258 deliberately builds a `desired[t]` copy that MATCHES `standing` exactly so the diff emits nothing; the comment says so. `cap_desired` :3730-3736 keeps any list containing an 'unwind' quote whole and unconditionally. m-16 splits that keep by POLARITY. The instant m-16 arms, the accumulating half of every retained list is exposed to the capital cap, can be dropped, and the diff then CANCELS live resting orders on a market we could not read this cycle — the precise stranding the retention exists to prevent. And M-10/M-11/M-30 INCREASE the number of retained mixed lists. **m-16 is only assessable after M-10 is measured; landing both without ordering produces cancels on unreadable books.**

(K4) **n-4 is telemetry-only solely because of the D4 fold guard, and m-37 is the item that moves that guard.** The SCORES fold at :5408 is `if SCORE_RANK and any(_o7.get("reason") != "unwind" for _o7 in q)`. n-4 (score RESTING orders when no accumulating quote exists) is invisible to ranking only because that guard excludes unwind-only cycles. m-37 hoists the fold out of `if MKT_TELEMETRY:` and restructures exactly that block. Land m-37 carelessly and n-4's non-zero capture_usd_day on wind-down cycles starts feeding live rank.

(K5) **The presence table's default multiplier masks the missing wildcard bucket.** `PRESENCE_DEFAULT = 1.0` (:1214) means any family/bucket with no calibrated row is treated as PERFECT execution, which is why those markets clear the $1.20 floor at all. n-40 arming replaces 1.0 with a pooled median; m-45 C2 moves the medians themselves. Both act as a silent multiplier on `_expected_credit_usd` and therefore on the SAME gate M-2, m-11, m-8 and n-2 are all trying to move. Four independent knobs on one gate.

(K6) **m-45 C1's new `fam|unknown` keys are unreachable only because `_life_bucket` never returns 'unknown'.** That is a latent, not an actual, no-op: any future bucket-vocabulary change activates dead rows written by C1. It is fine to ship, but the no-op claim needs to cite the bucket function, not the lookup.

(K7) **n-8's macro-fold exclusion is safe only after M-8's probe-slot exemption.** n-8's own text concedes it: excluding macro_probe rows from the D4 fold leaves the ticker permanently 'unknown' in SCORES, and under SCORE_RANK's unknown-bonus it draws an explore slot every cycle forever. Harmless only once M-8's `_cap_probe_slots` designation exemption exists. **M-8 → n-8, strictly.**

(K8) **n-15's unwind floor is safe only because cancels cannot reach WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE=400 (:1500) today.** `budget = max(0, WRITE_BUDGET - len(cancels))` (:3771). n-15 floors it at the number of reducing creates. The overshoot is bounded by held-ticker count today; m-16 (polarity split) and M-10 (more retained tickers) both increase create counts on exactly the churn cycles where cancels spike. The three together can push total ops meaningfully past the budget in the worst cycle.

(K9) **n-14's lock narrowing is safe only because `shadow_rank` happens not to mutate `scores`.** That is a property of today's callee, not a contract. If it is landed, the invariant needs a test, or the next shadow_rank that caches a decayed value writes into an un-locked snapshot.

(K10) **n-17's dead local is inert only because `flatten_to_zero` was removed from the call path (n-16).** `oids_by_t` at :5129 was the hint-oids feed. Deleting it is right; but n-16 documents flatten_to_zero as dead with a grep test. If any future work revives flatten_to_zero (m-21/n-21 touch its sibling code), the deleted feed is a re-introduction hazard. Land n-16's grep guard with or before n-17.

That is harmless only once M-8's _cap_probe_slots designation exemption exists. Strict order M-8 → n-8. Both are inert while MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS is empty (live), so the whole D-C group (M-4, n-5, n-7, M-8, m-38, n-8, M-31, n-6) is one dark subsystem that must be sequenced internally but does not contend with the live path — provided every one of them short-circuits on the empty set.

Both are silent multipliers on the SAME gate that M-2 (lowers modeled capture), m-11 (adds a keep window), m-8 (lowers eff size) and n-2 (raises modeled per-side size) are all moving. Five independent levers on one $1 floor — do not arm more than one per measurement window, and re-derive the presence table before/after M-2.

Fine to ship, but the commit message must cite _life_bucket, because any future bucket-vocabulary change activates rows written by this commit.
- **C22 — n-14's lock narrowing depends on an unenforced callee property** — n-14 releases SCORES_LOCK before calling shadow_rank on a snapshot, justified by 'shadow_rank never mutates scores'. That is a property of today's callee, not a contract — and _alloc_priority :3667 passes SCORES directly. If it lands, pin the non-mutation with a test, or the next shadow_rank variant that caches a decayed value writes into an un-locked copy.

Both are correct and they are mutually exclusive. Pick one, and be honest that either changes the DEFINITION of a long-lived plan key: cycles that recorded negative values now record 0..n, so any study crossing the fix boundary sees a level shift on exactly the de-risk-heavy days.
- **C25 — Z5 SELECT-BUDGET WALK SWALLOWS FAULTS** — The whole walk (:5014-5100) sits inside one try/except whose handler is `_SILENT['select_budget_fail'] += 1`. M-1, m-8/m-58, m-38, M-31, M-20/m-40 and m-54 all edit inside it. A defect introduced by any of them degrades SILENTLY into an unfiltered footprint (fail-open) rather than crashing — the nastiest failure mode in the batch. Any commit touching this block must add a plan-row assertion that the walk actually ran, not just that it did not raise.
- **C26 — Z7 WRITE BUDGET: n-13 × n-15 × m-16 × M-31 all charge the same 400-op ceiling** — WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE=400 (:1500). n-13 subtracts amends via a new extra_ops kwarg; n-15 floors the budget at the reducing-create count; m-16's polarity split increases create counts; M-31 charges macro probes into the capital budget upstream. n-13's premise depends on AMEND_DECREASE, which defaults to 0 at :351 and is NOT in the given live flag list — verify before assuming n-13 is inert.
- **C27 — m-29 × m-46: pagination multiplies reads on the path the rate-limit gate governs** — m-46 pages get_credit_history (today a single limit=1000 call at maker_kalshi_client.py:419), materially increasing request count on the account read path; m-29 adds the shared rate-limit gate that would honour a 429 by sleeping. m-46 without m-29 raises 429 exposure; m-29 armed can sleep on the write-worker thread while cold_lock is held, stalling the cold cycle and therefore all quoting. Land m-29's DETECTION half (counter + print) first, measure whether a 429 has ever occurred, and only then decide about m-46 and about arming the backoff.
- **C28 — M-19 × M-2: the two ends of the scorecard byte-equivalence pin** — kalshi_market_scorecard.py:76 computes usd_day as (period_reward/10000)/days — the canon violation M-19 fixes (period_reward/10000 IS the daily pool; never divide by window days). Independently, test_capture_gate.py:236-241 pins the quoter's _qualifying_score byte-equivalent to that module's qualifying_share, which M-2 changes. Two different items edit one file that one test binds to the live quoter. Co-schedule, and expect the est-reward column of every scorecard output to move by the window factor — anything quoted from the old basis must be re-derived, not carried forward.
- **C29 — m-36 × n-33 × n-36 (credit feedback) reach live rank only through ALLOC_KEY** — _load_credit_feedback (:482) short-circuits on D2_FEEDBACK (:475, default 0), and its only rank consumers are :856 (caprank telemetry) and :3675 (inside the ALLOC_KEY branch of _alloc_priority). So the D2 cluster's liveness is doubly gated (D2_FEEDBACK and ALLOC_KEY) and the given env states D2=1 without naming which knob that is. All three items edit build_feedback's due/conviction logic; m-36's event-due gate can only reduce convictions, n-33 can only skip rows, n-36 changes timestamp handling — combined they shrink the never-paid penalty, which the W3 sweep identifies as the working lever. Ship as one measured sequence, not three.
- **C30 — OFFLINE STUDY CLUSTERS ARE INTERNALLY SEQUENTIAL BUT LIVE-INERT** — Z20 (M-27 fair-value helper → M-28, n-49, m-56; plus m-55, m-57, n-48, n-50) and Z21 (M-24 → m-50/m-51/n-47; M-26/m-47/m-48/m-54/n-43; M-25/m-49/n-44; m-52/m-53/n-45; M-3/n-3; M-23/n-46) have no live money path. Two crossings into live code do exist and must be tracked: m-47 and m-54 each add a key to the live plan row inside try-protected regions (m-54's lands in the fault-swallowing select-budget block, see C25), and n-44 adds an import-time knob to the quoter that needs m-6's registry. The real risk in these clusters is documentary: M-27, M-28, M-19, M-23 and M-24 all invalidate published numbers, so each must regenerate to NEW dated outputs with a diff table rather than overwriting the frozen 07-23 deliverables.

## S3. PROPOSED DELIVERY SEQUENCE (a proposal — operator rules; no item omitted)

SEQUENCED DELIVERY PLAN — 147 FIXES, LIVE KALSHI MAKER, HEAD 233ee86 (branch claude/maker-kalshi-live)
Coverage verified programmatically: 147 items assigned, 147 unique, 0 missing, 0 duplicated.

====================================================================
PART 0 — FOUR CODE FACTS THAT RESHAPE THE PLAN (read this first)
====================================================================
I read the worktree before sequencing. Four things are true in the code that the item designs either
understate or contradict, and each one moves items between waves. All line numbers are HEAD 233ee86,
read this session.

F1. kalshi_fill_costs.build() RUNS INSIDE THE LIVE PROCESS.
    maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2699 calls `_kfc.build(positions, fills, settlements=settlements)` and
    writes kalshi_fill_costs.json via tmp+os.replace (:2700-2703), on a timer
    KALSHI_FILLCOST_REFRESH_S default 3600.0 (:2664, gated at :2674-2678).
    => M-15, m-43, m-44, n-28, n-29 are NOT offline-builder fixes. They change a feed the live
    process regenerates every hour, which caprank/ALLOC_KEY consume. They belong on the live side of
    the line, post-P2. This is the single biggest reclassification in this plan.

F2. THE TABLE FILES ARE AN UNGUARDED LIVE CONTROL SURFACE.
    _presence_table_refresh (:1245-1264) and _netev_table_refresh (:1434-1451) both compare mtime
    with `!=` (deliberately, so a restored backup with an OLDER mtime still reloads) and are called
    every cycle at :3938 and :3942.
    => Writing kalshi_presence_table.json or kalshi_netev_table.json changes LIVE GATE BEHAVIOR with
    no deploy, no restart, no md5 verification and no .bak. Every "offline builder" item whose output
    is one of those tables (M-21, M-22, m-41, n-39, n-41, m-45, n-40) is code-safe but
    PUBLICATION-DANGEROUS. This plan therefore treats a table write as a deploy event with its own
    gate, and forbids table writes during the P2 freeze.

F3. WS_HOT=1 IN THE LIVE ENV, BUT THE WS ITEMS WERE DESIGNED FOR WS_HOT=0.
    The live env line given to me says WS_HOT=1, WS_BOOK_COLD=1. M-13, m-1, n-51 and m-30 each state
    in their DARK field "gated by the existing KALSHI_WS_HOT flag, which is 0 live." Both cannot be
    right. If WS_HOT is really 1, the entire hot-repricing cluster is LIVE-ACTIVE, not dark:
    M-13/m-1/n-51 would take effect on deploy and, by their own risk text, produce strictly MORE
    cancel/create traffic immediately. m-30's cancel-scale clamp would be live on the anti-refill
    path. This must be resolved in Wave 0 before a single byte of Wave 7 ships. CONCERNING.

F4. "DEPLOYED BUT NOT RESTARTED" IS NOT A SAFE HOLDING STATE.
    A maker deploy is a file copy + md5 verify + .bak; the long-lived
    polymarket-maker-kalshi-ws.service keeps its imported bytecode until restarted. So copying a
    CLASS-R file without restarting is inert for the running process — but the next unplanned restart
    ARMS it unreviewed. And an unplanned restart is not hypothetical here: the $10 daily-loss halt
    self-places a STOP file. Therefore: a CLASS-R file may be deployed-without-restart ONLY if its
    change is flag-defaulted to byte-identical behavior, or if it is genuinely never imported by the
    running process. Everything else waits for a planned restart.

====================================================================

====================================================================
PART 2 — THE SCHEDULE COLLISION, STATED PLAINLY
====================================================================
The P2 pilot verdict is pre-committed CANON, due 2026-08-10T14:13Z, rule = window credits > window
drag. The window is ALREADY OPEN. There is no clean pre-freeze slot: every live change from now
until 14:13Z on 08-10 lands inside the measured window. The plan cannot avoid this; it can only
(a) push all live change to the far side of the verdict, (b) minimize restart count, and
(c) timestamp every perturbation so the verdict can be read with the perturbations annotated.

Three schedule facts collide with that:
  * 2026-08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS ends; its est->credit checkpoint gates D-A arming. The checkpoint is
    CAPTURED on 08-07 but ACTED ON (EST_FEED=1) only after the verdict — arming is a live change.
    Consequence: kalshi_estimates_recorder.py is FROZEN until 2026-08-07T16:00Z. m-3's own text
    offers an "8-line atomic write before the checkpoint" option. I recommend against it. The
    checkpoint is under 19 hours away, the map file has survived to now, and the failure mode of
    editing the only recorder of the checkpoint is losing the checkpoint. Freeze.
  * 2026-08-09T03:59Z FIX-H endings, B8 net-EV rebuild due ~08-09/10. B8's rebuild is offline and
    runs on schedule. Publishing its table is not. RECOMMENDATION: run B8 on time, produce the
    legacy-vs-contained diff document, and HOLD the table file until after 14:13Z on 08-10.
    Publishing a re-scoped netev table into a live gate 24 hours before a pre-committed verdict
    would make the verdict measure a system that changed underneath it.
  * The $10 daily-loss halt can self-place a STOP file at any moment. Per F4 that is an unplanned
    restart. It is the reason no half-armed CLASS-R file may sit on the box.

THE ONE RULING THAT GOVERNS EVERYTHING (R-A): does ANY live-behavior change ship before
2026-08-10T14:13Z? My recommendation is a single, narrow exception — Wave 4 below — and I give both
sides of it there rather than assuming the answer.

HONEST COST — CONCERNING: m-31 changes the semantics of the ledger series MID-MEASUREMENT, four days

WAVE 4 — SAFETY EXCEPTION: HALT AND EXIT PATH. 10 items. TOUCHES LIVE TRADING. ONE RESTART.
Items: M-12, n-16, n-21, m-21, m-22, m-19, n-20, n-15, n-53, n-54
REQUIRES OPERATOR RULING R-A. CONCERNING.
--------------------------------------------------------------------
THIS IS THE ONLY LIVE CHANGE I RECOMMEND BEFORE THE VERDICT, AND ONLY IF THE OPERATOR AGREES.
THE CASE FOR: M-12 is the item where the unfixed path can OPEN A NAKED OPPOSITE POSITION while the
bot is halted — a position taken in the one state where nothing is watching. n-15/n-53 mean a
mass-cancel cycle can consume the whole write budget and DROP the replacement exits, so risk-reducing
orders are the ones suppressed exactly when churn is highest. m-19 is a one-line operand swap that
removes an unnamed amnesty from the $10 daily-loss meter, making it measure true drawdown from the
real day peak. m-22 gives the STOP branch the governor bookkeeping the in-cycle taker paths already
have, so a halt's own flatten is recorded. If any of these fires during the P2 window, it corrupts
the drag number far worse than a timestamped restart does.
THE CASE AGAINST: a restart is itself a perturbation. It resets the D3 ramp first-seen map, forfeits
every queue position, resubscribes the WS watch, and re-imports every table. The verdict rule is
"window credits > window drag" and queue position is what earns credits. A restart on 08-07 is a
visible discontinuity in the credit series 3 days before a pre-committed verdict. An operator who
values the verdict's cleanliness above the halt-path tail risk should decline this wave and move all
ten items into Wave 6.
SCOPE DISCIPLINE IF APPROVED: this is one branch, ten commits, ONE deploy, ONE restart, at a
timestamp recorded in the P2 annotation. M-12 and m-22 both edit _flatten_all and MUST be sequenced
(M-12 changes cancel handling, m-22 changes the return value); n-21 and m-21 both edit the re-rest
region; n-16 is docstring-and-grep-test only and rides along so nobody has to re-enter these
functions later. n-54 ships STEP 1 ONLY (counting) — step 2 stays dark behind
KALSHI_STANDING_ZERO_HONEST=0.
DEPLOYED vs DARK: M-12 behind KALSHI_STOPFLAT_DEFER — and note M-12's own design recommends
DEFAULT 1 (ON), which is a departure from the dark norm, justified because the unfixed path can open
a naked position. That is an operator decision, not mine to assume; if declined, ship at 0 and the
wave becomes telemetry-only and loses most of its value. m-19/n-20, n-15/n-53, m-21, n-21, n-54 step
1, n-16 are all no-op-or-safer and ship unflagged.
HONEST RISK: M-12's deferral means a position keeps a STALE-priced exit until the next flatten (up to
1800s) or until pass 2's taker escalation — and pass 2 requires TAKER_FLATTEN=1 and residual >=
STOP_TAKER_MIN_CT. Confirm TAKER_FLATTEN's live value in Wave 0; if it is 0, the deferral has no
escalation behind it and the trade is stale-exit vs naked-position with no backstop. n-15 can exceed
WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE by the number of held tickers in exactly the cycle already hammering the venue
rate limit.
GATE OUT: suite green; md5 of the deployed quoter blob == HEAD blob; 24h with zero
unwind_creates_dropped, zero negative gated_out, day_baseline_reset emitted at most once per day; a
deliberate dry-run STOP rehearsal (STOP file placed and removed on a quiet cycle) showing pass 1
deferral behaves and state is written; and the restart timestamp recorded in the P2 annotation doc.

WAVE 9 — MONEY-MODEL ROOT FIXES: SIZE AND BASIS. 7 items. TOUCHES LIVE TRADING.
Items: m-2, m-8, m-58, m-20, m-59, M-29, n-2
--------------------------------------------------------------------
RATIONALE: three shared root causes, each of which makes a gate model a size or a unit it does not
actually get. m-8/m-58 unify "how many contracts will ACTUALLY rest" into one `_eff_rest_ct`
authority. m-20/m-59 unify the ramp basis so every gate that models reward at join size uses the same
scalar. M-29 makes the net-EV margin unit explicit. m-2 stops composing a standing snapshot and a
book snapshot as if simultaneous.
STRICT ORDER, ONE FLAG PER 48h: m-2 (no flag; every branch is conservative, larger denominator,
fewer admits) -> m-8/m-58 (no flag; monotone declining-to-open; but it WILL move the admission
baseline on the first cycle and must be announced, not slipped in) -> m-20/m-59 behind
KALSHI_RAMP_BASIS=0, extraction unflagged -> M-29 via KALSHI_NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT=-700.0
(bit-identical to today) then a one-line env edit after the operator re-rules the margin ->
n-2 behind KALSHI_F3_PER_SIDE=0, shadow delta emitted while off.
CONCERNING — THE NON-MONOTONE PAIR: m-8 admits FEWER, n-2 admits MORE. Landing both leaves a net
admission change that cannot be predicted from either commit in isolation. That is why they are at
opposite ends of the wave with a full measurement window between, and why n-2 also invalidates the
current calibration of MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 (whose 20% margin was chosen against the blended number).
Re-derive MIN_CREDIT_USD before arming n-2, not after.
CONCERNING — M-29 ARMS A DEAD GATE: NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT=-7.0 compared against a FRACTION makes
`poor = net_pct < -7.0` structurally unreachable. Today "receipt" means unconditionally quote. After
M-29 the gate actually bites, and the margin between a measured -6.09% and the live -7.0 knob is 0.9
percentage points — any B8 table rebuild can cross it. Do not arm M-29 in the same window as a table
publish.
DEADLOCK GUARD (m-20): if a future session simplifies m-20's window-average ramp basis back to an
instantaneous `_d3_est_ct`, every new market is permanently locked out and the bot silently stops
opening anything. m-20's T4 test is that guard; it must never be relaxed.
GATE OUT: per flag, 48h of admit/refuse counts, the shadow delta distribution, and MIN_CREDIT_USD
re-derived and stated before n-2. Suite green after each.

WAVE 10 — THE LIP REFERENCE PRICE. 5 items. ALONE. THE LARGEST CHANGE IN THE SET.
Items: M-2, M-5, M-7, M-17, M-18
--------------------------------------------------------------------
RATIONALE: the model anchors the reference at the best bid; the venue anchors it at the first level
where CUMULATIVE resting size reaches Target/5, walking down from best bid, and scores every resting
order on a qualifying side with no lowest-qualifying cutoff. M-2's `_lip_walk` replaces the walk in
one place and routes all three call sites — `_qualifying_score`, `_qualifying_breakdown`,
`_prospective_capture` — through it. M-5, M-7, M-17 and M-18 are the same defect seen through the
same helper and add no separate code: M-17 supplies the population-typical ~2x fixture, M-18 supplies
the deep-book whole-tail clause, M-5 names the throttle-step inversion, M-7 pins the third call site.
WHY IT IS ALONE: arming KALSHI_LIP_REF strictly LOWERS modeled capture — by roughly 2x on ordinary
books and far more on thin-top books. With PRESENCE_GATE=1 and MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 live, a large
fraction of the current footprint can drop below the floor the cycle the flag flips. The bot could
stop quoting most of its universe in one restart. Nothing else may be in flight when this is armed.
MANDATORY PRE-CONDITIONS: (1) n-19's shadow reference telemetry from Wave 6 must have at least a week
of live rows across the whole footprint, so the before/after distribution is MEASURED, not modeled.
(2) The arming deploy must move MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT in the SAME commit, with their
new values derived from n-19's distribution. (3) M-19's scorecard qualifying_share and the
test_capture_gate.py:236-240 byte-equivalence pin must be re-derived deliberately in this commit.
DEPLOYED vs DARK: KALSHI_LIP_REF=0 mandatory. The shadow column ships ON (telemetry-only, cannot
alter `desired`). Arming is a separate operator ruling.
CONCERNING, ADDITIONAL: M-18's whole-tail clause makes df_total sensitive to how many levels the book
source returns. WS_BOOK_COLD=1 is live, so the WS mirror and the REST orderbook may not return the
same depth — under LIP mode that difference now changes the score, where today it did not. Pin the
depth contract before arming, or the same market scores differently depending on which read served
it. Also: M-5 leaves THROTTLE_SMART calibrated on the legacy reference, a known and deliberate
residual that will be systematically wrong in an unknown direction.
GATE OUT: a week of shadow rows; a published before/after capture distribution across the footprint;
re-derived MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT; a stated prediction of how many markets fall below
the floor; then arm, and compare footprint size at +1h, +24h, +72h against that prediction. If the
footprint collapses beyond the prediction, revert the flag immediately — that is the whole reason it
is a flag.

CONCERNING, AND I WANT THESE SAID OUT LOUD: m-11 LOOSENS a live money gate on a premise (banked
accrual makes continuation +EV) that is MODELED, not measured — and the venue's own accrual number
(EST_FEED) is dark precisely in the late window where accrual dominates. m-60 restores accumulating
size in the final quotable minutes of short programs, which is the highest informed-flow window and
exactly what the settlement ramp exists to shrink. m-18 armed KEEPS a side resting that currently

brake. m-23 armed DELAYS risk-reducing crosses — the exact loss shape FIX 3 exists to bound. M-9
armed retires most of the activate path. m-17's enforcement can oscillate (cancel -> both re-created
-> one rejected -> cancel) and destroys queue position, which is what LIP pays for.
GATE OUT: per flag, 48h; and for m-11/m-60/m-18 specifically, a fill-quality comparison (adverse
fills as a share of fills) against the pre-arm window, because all three trade presence for exposure
in regimes where fill quality is the thing at risk.

RATIONALE: the ordering key and the charging key are two copies of the same model and only one was
kept current. m-39 (shared root fix #2) gives the sweeper the same own-order truth the quote loop
has; m-61 and n-11 are the same seam and add only the premise correction and the arming precondition.
M-20 collapses the two commit models into one `effective_commit_usd`; m-40 is its ct_cap arm; n-37
makes est_commit_usd's hidden join_size and ref_no assumptions explicit parameters. n-34/n-35 fix the
score cache: it stores only the YES reference, and the swing penalty is applied to a blend of a
measured and a prior term.
BLOCKING PRECONDITION: ALLOC_KEY's live value. The 08-04 EOD handoff records ALLOC_KEY=1; the 08-06
flag list omits it. If it is 1, m-39 and M-20 REORDER LIVE CAPITAL ALLOCATION on deploy and must ship
behind KALSHI_SWEEP_OWN_ORDERS=0 / KALSHI_ALLOC_REF_AGE=0 / KALSHI_ALLOC_RAMP_EST=0 with shadow
fields. If it is 0, they are shadow-log-only. Landing m-39 without reading the flag is the genuinely
bad outcome: an unmeasured allocation reorder on a live money path.
CONCERNING: m-40 is a DIRECTION REVERSAL ON A LIVE RISK CONTROL — today's blindness accidentally
biases ordering toward receipt-proven full-size books and away from young unproven ones, which is
aligned with follow-the-profit and with the W7 clamp's purpose. Arming m-40 removes that accidental
protection. n-35 armed RAISES the score of aged high-swing markets, i.e. removes a discount that
currently keeps us away from books that fill us adversely. n-34's max(yes,no) swing is not always
adverse selection — a rival pulling and re-posting widens then re-tightens, and after arming that
market carries a penalty it may not deserve. m-39's 300s age gate is a guess, not a measurement.
GATE OUT: ALLOC_KEY value on the record; per flag, 48h of shadow_rank vs actual overlap and the
would_enter/would_exit deltas; and a stated check that the reordering did not concentrate capital
into a smaller set of markets than the pre-arm window.

- **FINDING F1 — fill_costs.build() runs INSIDE the live process** — maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2699 calls _kfc.build(...) and rewrites kalshi_fill_costs.json on a KALSHI_FILLCOST_REFRESH_S=3600.0 timer (:2664, gated :2674-2678). Reclassified from 'offline' to runtime, moved to Wave 8 (post-verdict). This is the single largest reclassification in the plan.
- **FINDING F2 — table files are an unguarded live control surface** — _presence_table_refresh (:1245-1264) and _netev_table_refresh (:1434-1451) compare mtime with != and run every cycle (:3938, :3942). Writing kalshi_presence_table.json or kalshi_netev_table.json changes LIVE GATE BEHAVIOR with no deploy, no restart, no md5 verification, no .bak. Consequence: for every CLASS-T item (M-21, M-22, m-41, n-39, n-41, m-45, n-40, m-36, n-33, n-36) the CODE and the PUBLICATION are two separate deploys with two separate gates. - **FINDING F3 — WS_HOT contradiction (CONCERNING, blocks Wave 7)** — The live env line states WS_HOT=1. M-13, m-1, n-51 and m-30 each state in their DARK field that KALSHI_WS_HOT 'is 0 live'. Both cannot be true. If WS_HOT=1, the hot-repricing cluster is live-active on deploy, not dark, and by M-13's own risk text produces strictly more cancel/create traffic with no baseline (the prior failure was silent skipping). Must be resolved in Wave 0 (ruling R-B) before a byte of Wave 7 ships. If 1: ship the reason histogram first plus a hard write-rate cap, measure 48h, then remove the cap.
- **FINDING F4 — 'deployed but not restarted' is not a safe holding state** — A maker deploy is a file copy + md5 + .bak; the long-lived service keeps imported bytecode until restart. Copying a CLASS-R file without restarting is inert for the running process but ARMS it on the next restart — and an unplanned restart is not hypothetical, because the $10 daily-loss halt self-places a STOP file. Rule adopted: a CLASS-R file may be deployed-without-restart ONLY if flag-defaulted byte-identical, or if the running process genuinely never imports/calls the changed code (kalshi_presence_calibrate.measure, maker_kalshi_client.get_credit_history).

If 1, m-39 and M-20 REORDER LIVE CAPITAL on deploy and must ship behind KALSHI_SWEEP_OWN_ORDERS=0 / KALSHI_ALLOC_REF_AGE=0 / KALSHI_ALLOC_RAMP_EST=0 with shadow fields. Landing m-39 without reading the flag is the genuinely bad outcome: an unmeasured allocation reorder on a live money path. CONCERNING: m-40 is a DIRECTION REVERSAL on a live risk control — today's blindness accidentally biases ordering toward receipt-proven full-size books and away from young unproven ones, aligned with follow-the-profit and the W7 clamp's purpose; arming removes that accidental protection. n-35 armed RAISES the score of aged high-swing markets, removing a discount that currently keeps us away from books that fill us adversely. n-34's max(yes,no) swing is not always adverse selection (a rival pulling and re-posting widens then re-tightens). m-39's 300s age gate is a guess, not a measurement. GATE: ALLOC_KEY on the record; per flag 48h of shadow_rank-vs-actual overlap and would_enter/would_exit deltas; plus a check that the reorder did not concentrate capital into a smaller market set than pre-arm.
- **LIVE vs NOT-LIVE roster (the explicit separation)** — DOES NOT TOUCH LIVE TRADING — 57 items, the entire pre-verdict runway: Wave 1 (26 CLASS-O tools+tests), Wave 2 (11 recorders on own timers), Wave 3 (10 builder-code-only). CAVEAT on Wave 3: the code is not live but the PUBLISHED TABLE is (F2) — a table write reaches the gate with no deploy and no md5, so publication is Wave 8. TOUCHES LIVE TRADING — 90 items: Waves 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12 in full. Note that 'telemetry-only' quoter items (most of Wave 6) still need a restart to take effect and therefore still perturb queue position and ramp clocks — telemetry is free in the order path, not in the deploy path. Note the F1 correction: M-15, m-43, m-44, n-28, n-29 read as offline in their own designs but are runtime.
- **Rulings required before anything ships** — R-A does any live change ship before 2026-08-10T14:13Z (governs Wave 4). R-B what is WS_HOT actually set to (reclassifies 4 items and all of Wave 7's risk). R-C ALLOC_KEY and SWEEP_ENABLED (gates Wave 12). R-D M-12's default — 1 (ON, departing from the dark norm because the unfixed path can open a naked position while halted) or 0; and confirm TAKER_FLATTEN since M-12's deferral relies on pass 2. R-E confirm the estimates-recorder freeze until 2026-08-07T16:00Z. R-F confirm B8 table publication is held until after the verdict. R-G after the env dump, are m-13 and m-9 no-ops or live flag changes (if the latter they are config decisions, not bug fixes). R-H M-29's re-ruled margin (current -7.0 against a fraction makes the gate unreachable). R-I M-2 arming with re-derived MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT in the same commit.
- **What I could not verify** — I could not read live.env from this sandbox, so every 'no-op' claim depending on a live flag value is UNVERIFIED: m-13, m-9, m-37, n-13, m-8, M-6, m-39/m-61/n-11, M-20/m-40, TAKER_FLATTEN, and the whole WS_HOT question (F3). Wave 0 exists for exactly this. I did not run the suite — the 1233 passed / 2 xfailed / exit 0 baseline is taken as given, not measured by me. The wave-to-calendar mapping assumes one engineer-session per wave and no incident; a halt inside any wave resets the sequence, because a halt is an unplanned restart and per F4 that arms whatever is sitting on the box. Coverage arithmetic is verified: W1=26, W2=11, W3=10, W4=10, W6=33, W7=19, W8=5, W9=7, W10=5, W11=13, W12=8 — sum 147, unique, complete; Waves 0 and 5 are gates and carry no items by design.
