## S4. ADVERSARIAL CRITIQUE OF THIS PLAN (the skeptic pass — read before ruling)

ADVERSARIAL CRITIQUE OF THE PLAN — verified against the worktree at C:/Users/samwa/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--lockes-picks-polymarket-ai-v2/5dfe0ebf-2821-475d-946c-72012db34c3b/scratchpad/kalshi-wt/kalshi_live/, HEAD 233ee86. Every line number and constant below I read this session. Where I could not verify (the VPS live.env), I say so.

=====================================================================
VERDICT IN ONE PARAGRAPH
=====================================================================
The three planning documents are individually strong and mutually inconsistent in ways that matter. The COLLISIONS doc found the single most dangerous thing in the whole exercise — that arming EST_FEED revives a dead activate lane straight into an infeasible-ladder emitter — and the SEQUENCING doc did not encode it as a gate, because EST_FEED ARMING IS NOT AN ITEM IN THE PLAN AT ALL. It is item 2 in the operator's own post-review queue (docs/maker_handoffs/KALSHI_HANDOFF_2026-08-06_EOD.md, section 3), due right after the 2026-08-07T15:00Z checkpoint, and its guard (M-9) is scheduled in Wave 11, weeks away. That is the plan's structural failure: it sequences 147 items against each other and does not sequence them against the operator's own queue. Second: the plan's safety wave (Wave 4) is internally self-contradicting — it requires a restart that GRANTS a fresh $10 of daily-loss headroom while shipping m-19, whose entire purpose is to REMOVE loss amnesty, four days before a canon verdict whose credit numerator is still $0.00. Third: the plan's verification method is broken for roughly two dozen items, because its standing detectors are qstats counters and the live hot path discards stats.

=====================================================================
1. WHERE THE SEQUENCING IS WRONG OR DANGEROUS
=====================================================================

S1 — THE PLAN DOES NOT GATE THE OPERATOR'S OWN QUEUE. Verified: kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1333-1340, inside `_expected_credit_usd`, applies `expected = max(expected, _est)` when `EST_FEED and frac >= EST_FEED_MIN_FRAC` (default 0.25, :729) with NO void/activate scoping. The activate PRESENCE_GATE at :3105 calls that same function and refuses on `_expA < MIN_CREDIT_USD` (1.20, :1213). Today the lane is dead because `_prospective_capture` returns hard 0.0 when either side fails Target (:2601-2602), so `expected` is always 0. Arm EST_FEED and ONE positive venue estimate lifts `_expA` over the floor and opens the lane into the emitter at :3125-3131, which rests `min(int(add_y), _acl)` with `_acl = INV_HARD_CT = 80.0` (:1555) and is then clamped again by the D3 ramp to 5-10ct for unproven series. On a Target-1000 program that ladder can never qualify; under the venue rule the snapshot is EXCLUDED FOR EVERYONE, we earn exactly $0, and we carry real fill risk on a book that is thin by definition. The plan puts M-9, m-10 and M-6 in Wave 11 (last) and never lists "arm EST_FEED" as a gated action. If the operator follows their own queue on 08-07/08 while following this plan, the guard arrives weeks after the hazard.

S2 — WAVE 4 SHIPS AN AMNESTY AND AN ANTI-AMNESTY ON THE SAME DAY. Verified: kalshi_live/restart_bundle.sh:51 does `touch day_baseline_reset`; the quoter consumes it at :4872 and re-seeds `equity_day_start` and `equity_day_peak` at CURRENT equity. So an operator-named restart hands the daily-loss governor a fresh $10 of headroom for that day. Wave 4 requires a restart, and its headline item m-19 exists to close an amnesty on that exact meter. Net effect on window drag: unpredictable, in a window whose verdict rule is credits > drag. The plan never states which restart path it means, and the two paths (restart_bundle.sh vs a plain systemctl restart) produce materially different drag numbers. That is a decision the plan silently makes by omission.

S3 — WAVE 3 PUTS THE LIVE ORDER CLIENT ON THE BOX AND CALLS IT HARMLESS. The plan states: "m-46's client code should be copied here WITHOUT restart so the offline B8 invocation picks up paged credit_history — it is not called by the quoter, so a crash-arm is harmless." That is flatly false. kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:43 is `from maker_kalshi_client import KalshiOrderClient, API_ROOT, PROD_BASE` — a TOP-LEVEL import, and :3956 instantiates it every cycle. Copying that file without restart leaves an unreviewed change to the live order path sitting on disk, armed by the next restart. The handoff records a daily-loss halt at 2026-08-06T15:54:54Z with a self-placed STOP and an operator rearm at 17:36:23Z, so an unplanned restart inside this window is not hypothetical — it already happened once. This sentence violates the plan's own F4 rule.

S4 — THE PLAN'S "RESTARTS ARE PRECIOUS" PREMISE IS PARTLY WRONG, AND IT USED THE WRONG PART TO DEFER SAFETY WORK. The plan asserts a restart "resets the D3 ramp first-seen map." It does not. `_d3_first_seen_ensure` (:560-578) restores `d3_first_seen` from persisted state, and F14's `d3_last_desired` grace map survives restarts too (:582-600). The genuine costs are queue position and the WS resubscribe. Meanwhile the P2 window already contains three perturbations on 08-06 alone (halt-flatten 15:54:54Z, deploy 17:31:15Z, dark deploy ~18:20Z). Treating Wave 4's single annotated restart as near-sacred while the window already carries three is not consistent, and the inconsistency is what pushed halt-path fixes behind the verdict.

S5 — WAVE 6 IS RISK CONCENTRATION SOLD AS RISK REDUCTION. 33 items, one restart, one .bak. If the post-restart cycle misbehaves there are 33 candidate causes and the plan's own gate ("quote/order counts statistically unchanged") cannot attribute. Worse, four of the 33 (m-13, m-9, m-37, n-13) are explicitly NOT no-ops under unread env values — the plan says so, then batches them with 29 that are.

S6 — n-15 SHIPS THREE WAVES BEFORE THE GOVERNOR IT PUSHES PAST. n-15 floors the create budget at the number of reducing creates, which by its own risk text can exceed WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE (400, :1500) in exactly the churn cycle already at the venue rate limit. m-29's shared rate-limit gate is Wave 7 and ships dark. Backwards.

S7 — THE ONE PIECE OF TELEMETRY THAT UNBLOCKS THE BIGGEST FIX IS SCHEDULED AFTER THE VERDICT. n-19 (the pure `_lip_reference` shadow column) is the long-pole measurement for M-2, and M-2's arming needs a week of it. It costs a plan-row key and a restart. If Wave 4 happens at all, n-19 should ride that restart. The plan does not put it there, which pushes the earliest possible M-2 arming out by roughly a week for no reason.

=====================================================================
2. "SAFE" FIXES THAT ARE NOT SAFE
=====================================================================

m-19 / n-20 — marked concerning=false, "strictly stricter", no flag. This is the clearest case in the set of stricter not equalling safer when the objective function is credits > drag. Window credits were $0.00 at the last read (handoff line 54-55); LIP pays at PROGRAM conclusion; APRPOTUS ends 08-07T15:00Z and FIX-H 08-09T03:59Z, so the entire numerator lands in the last three days. Making the $10 halt fire earlier raises the probability of a flatten in exactly those days. And because the venue floor is $1 per market-PROGRAM, losing part of the remaining presence on a program tracking to $1.20 pays $0, not $0.84 — the loss is a cliff, not a slope. The item text weighs only the loss side.

n-38 / n-4 — "telemetry-only, cannot alter `desired`." True within a cycle. But the telemetry row is folded into SCORES and SCORES is read by `kalshi_market_scores.rank` at :2193/:2646 — i.e. NEXT cycle's selection — whenever SCORE_RANK=1. SCORE_RANK defaults 0 (:388) and its live value is not in the env list I was given. The plan flags this as premise 0-C and then schedules both items anyway rather than gating them on the env read. Same class of error as m-13/m-9.

Wave 1's "26 offline items, zero P2 cost" — two of them are not zero-cost. M-24 makes the ledger identity check read DOES NOT CLOSE on most runs (its own risk text), because accrued-unpaid LIP legitimately sits outside credit_history. M-19 moves every est-reward column in the scorecard by the window factor (x7 up for weeklies, x24 down for hourlies). The operator reads both tools DAILY during this window (handoff section 3, item 7) and must compute the verdict from them. Re-scaling the operator's instruments during the days they read them is an operator-attention cost the plan does not price. The operator is a component of the verdict.

m-42 — genuinely a no-op, but it is the ONLY thing that would make the net-EV signal interpretable in plan rows, and the plan gates it behind a restart that is gated behind the verdict. So for the entire verdict window there is no telemetry to answer "did NETEV_GATE bench something it shouldn't have." If the verdict turns on that, the plan has removed the ability to check.

=====================================================================
3. WHAT THE PLAN ASSUMES THAT THE CODE DOES NOT SUPPORT
=====================================================================

P1 — "Offline tool implies no live path." False for seven modules. The live quoter imports kalshi_market_scores (:408, :2193, :2646, :5265, :5376, :6032), kalshi_capital_rank (:453, :791, :3647, :5017), kalshi_fill_costs (:2683), kalshi_market_sweeper (:2712), kalshi_netev_calibrate (:1392), kalshi_presence_calibrate (:1234), and maker_kalshi_client (:43, top-level). The RISK doc caught most of this; the SEQUENCING doc's Wave 3 carve-out still contradicts it (see S3).

P2 — "Dark flag implies dark." M-13, m-1, n-51 and m-30 each justify shipping unflagged on "KALSHI_WS_HOT is 0 live." maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:100 defaults it to 0; :608 routes every book event through `hot_event` when it is 1; the live env block I was given says WS_HOT=1. One of those is wrong. The deeper consequence the plan states but does not act on: `hot_event` calls `M.desired_quotes(c["m"], ys, ns, M.utcnow(), own=..., stats={}, ...)` at :447-448 — no `own_orders`, and a THROWAWAY stats dict. So on the hot path (a) every own_orders-dependent gate silently takes the legacy double-counting branch of `_qualifying_score` (:2570-2578), and (b) EVERY qstats-based standing detector in this batch is blind to hot evaluations. Roughly two dozen items propose exactly that kind of detector. The plan's per-wave gates ("48h with counter X at zero") are therefore not the proofs they claim to be. This is a defect in the PLAN'S VERIFICATION METHOD, not in any one item.

P3 — "md5-verified deploy implies change control." Four JSON files bypass it. `_presence_table_refresh` (:1244-1264) and `_netev_table_refresh` (:1434-1451) stat every cycle and reload on `!=` mtime (deliberately `!=`, so an older restored file also reloads); `_d3_feedback_cached` reloads the D2 table on a 60s TTL; `_refresh_fill_costs` rebuilds the fill-cost feed IN-PROCESS hourly at :3958. Writing any of these changes a live money gate within 60 seconds with no deploy, no md5, no .bak and no restart. The plan's F2 gets this right and then Wave 8 sequences arming as if publication had a rollback. It does not: there is no backup convention for these files. The `!=` mtime comparison is the rollback MECHANISM, but only if someone kept the old file.

P4 — "Suite green implies verified." Every new behaviour is default-off, so the suite validates the path already running. kalshi_live/test_capture_gate.py pins `_qualifying_score` byte-equivalent to the scorecard; once M-2 arms KALSHI_LIP_REF=1 that documented invariant is FALSE in the live configuration and the test still passes because tests run flag-off. There is no flag-on population test anywhere in the plan, and for M-2 — the item that moves modelled capture by 2x to 20x — the plan never says who computes the predicted footprint-survival number or what threshold triggers the revert it promises.

P5 — THE MODEL IS MISSING A VENUE TERM AND EVERY RE-FIT IN THE PLAN WILL ABSORB IT. The venue rule as given: BOTH sides must meet Target or the snapshot is EXCLUDED for everyone, and payout scales valid/total. `_prospective_capture` (:2584-2603) tests both-sides qualification INSTANTANEOUSLY and returns `snap * usd_day`; there is no valid/total fraction anywhere. `_presence_factor` (:1267-1277) measures OUR resting share, not the book's two-sided-at-Target fraction over the window — different quantity. So the model can forecast positive credit for a market that structurally pays everyone $0, which is the shape W10 already measured. Consequence for the plan: Wave 10 mandates re-fitting MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT in the same commit as M-2, against a model still missing the exclusion term. The haircut will silently re-absorb it, M-2 will be declared root-fixed, and the largest remaining error will be unmeasured and now buried inside a fitted constant. No item covers this.

P6 — AN ENTIRE IN-SCOPE FILE IS ABSENT. kalshi_live/kalshi_market_menu.py (163 lines, "READ-ONLY. Which live LIP series should the clean maker be quoting?") contains BOTH defects the plan's flagship items fix: `pool = (_f(p.get("period_reward")) / 10000) / days` at :79 (the exact canon pool violation M-19 fixes in the scorecard) and `ref = bids[0][0]` at :43 (the exact reference-anchor defect M-2 fixes in the quoter). It appears zero times in the 1410-line review. It feeds operator series-selection. Fixing the scorecard and not the menu is CLASS-NOT-INSTANCE failure inside the plan's own flagship items.

=====================================================================
4. SINGLE MOST LIKELY WAY THIS PLAN CAUSES A LOSS OR A FALSE VERDICT
=====================================================================
PRIMARY (loss, and it contaminates the verdict too): the operator arms EST_FEED on 08-07/08 per their own queue, the plan does not gate it, and the activate lane wakes up unguarded.

Chain, every link verified:
1. The operator's queue (handoff section 3) says: APRPOTUS checkpoint 08-07T15:00Z, then "Arm D-A + D-C on operator naming: env edit + restart."
2. `_expected_credit_usd` :1333-1340 floors `expected` at the venue estimate with no void scoping.
3. The activate gate at :3098-3110 is the only thing keeping that lane shut, and it is shut ONLY because `_prospective_capture` returns 0.0 on a void book.
4. One positive estimate opens it. The emitter at :3125-3131 then rests a ladder clamped to INV_HARD_CT=80 and further clamped by the D3 ramp to 5-10ct for unproven series — a ladder that on a Target-1000 program can never qualify, so the snapshot is excluded for everyone and we earn $0 while holding fill risk.
5. The select-budget walk charges activate markets at roughly probe scale while `cap_desired` charges the real `_mkt_capital` — so the budget under-reads exactly when the lane revives.
6. M-9, the guard, is in Wave 11. m-10 and M-6, which un-mask the same emitter by other routes, are also in Wave 11 — but EST_FEED arming is not in any wave, so nothing stops step 1.

RUNNER-UP (false verdict specifically): Wave 4 executes on 08-07. The restart drops `day_baseline_reset` (restart_bundle.sh:51), which re-seeds the daily governor at current equity (:4872) and grants up to $10 of extra permitted daily loss on a day when drag is already ~$11.96 and credits are $0.00. m-19, in the same wave, then tightens the meter from the new baseline and raises halt probability on 08-08/09. A halt self-places STOP and flattens (:3961-3998); presence goes to zero; and because the floor is $1 per market-PROGRAM, a program ending 08-09 that was tracking marginally above the floor pays $0 rather than a reduced amount. Drag up by as much as $10, credits down discontinuously, in the three days that decide a CANON verdict — from the wave whose stated purpose is safety. A false negative here does not merely mis-measure; it retires the pilot.

=====================================================================
5. WHERE THE PLAN OVER-WORRIES (so the cuts land in the right place)
=====================================================================
m-46 is treated as high-risk for changing the live netev table. Lifetime credits were 58 rows / $198.95 against limit=1000 — truncation is not currently binding, so the pagination fix is close to inert today. M-21/M-22 change the same table's arithmetic materially and deserve the attention m-46 is consuming.
The plan-row byte budget is real but is being used to argue against detectors. The plan row is the only telemetry channel there is; several fixes are unprovable without new keys. Signature-gated emission solves it; refusing keys does not.
Wave 1's drift-study cluster (M-27, M-28, m-55, m-56, m-57, n-48, n-49, n-50), w16, w17, w0b and w11 are genuinely free and could run today in parallel. The plan wraps them in per-item ceremony they do not need.

=====================================================================
6. THREE OPEN QUESTIONS THAT CHANGE WHAT GETS BUILT
=====================================================================
Q1. WHAT IS KALSHI_WS_HOT ON THE BOX, AND IF IT IS 1, DO THE BATCH'S DETECTORS STILL WORK? This is not a scheduling question. `hot_event` passes `stats={}` (:447-448), so if the hot path is live, roughly two dozen of this batch's standing detectors are structurally under-counting by the hot share and the plan's "48h with counter X at zero" gates are invalid as written. If WS_HOT=1, a DETECTOR REDESIGN — threading real qstats out of hot, or a separate hot-side counter set — becomes a prerequisite for Waves 6, 9 and 11, and four items (M-13, m-1, n-51, m-30) move from "dark" to "live-active with a staged arm and a write-rate cap."

Q2. DOES THE SNAPSHOT-EXCLUSION TERM GET BUILT BEFORE M-2's RE-FIT, OR AFTER? The venue excludes a snapshot for everyone when either side is short of Target and scales payout valid/total; the model has no such term (:2584-2603). If the answer is "after," Wave 10 is one commit that re-fits MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT against a V-less model and bakes V permanently into the haircut, making M-2's root-fix claim false. If "before," Wave 10 becomes a two-stage build: measure the two-sided-at-Target window fraction from the WS mirror first, then fit. This changes the SHAPE of the largest fix in the set, not its date.

Q3. DOES ANY LIVE CHANGE SHIP BEFORE 2026-08-10T14:13Z, AND BY WHICH RESTART PROCEDURE? Sub-question (b) is the one nobody has asked: restart_bundle.sh drops `day_baseline_reset` and re-baselines the daily governor (restart_bundle.sh:51 into quoter:4872), while a plain restart inherits the day's carry. Those two paths produce different window-drag numbers in a verdict measured on drag. The answer determines whether Wave 4 exists at all, whether m-19 ships now or on 08-11, and whether n-19's shadow telemetry gets a ride — which in turn sets the earliest possible M-2 arming date by about a week.

=====================================================================
7. HONEST CAVEATS ON THIS CRITIQUE
=====================================================================
I could not read the VPS live.env, so WS_HOT, ALLOC_KEY, SCORE_RANK, EXPLORE_PROBE_CT, TAKER_FLATTEN, SWEEP_ENABLED and the _envb spellings remain UNVERIFIED; I read only the in-code defaults (WS_HOT 0, ALLOC_KEY 0, SCORE_RANK 0, MKT_TELEMETRY 1, EXPLORE_PROBE_CT 0, AMEND_DECREASE 0, TAKER_FLATTEN True, MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL 150.0, INV_HARD_CT 80.0, MIN_CREDIT_USD 1.20, PRESENCE_DEFAULT 1.0, WRITE_BUDGET 400, NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT default 0.0 against a live -7.0). I did not run the suite; the 1233/2/exit-0 baseline is taken as given. The fix set I was handed is COMPACTED and carries no DETECTOR or TEST field for any of the 147 items, and several fix and risk strings are truncated mid-sentence — so I cannot audit detector or test adequacy directly, and my claims about them are inferred from surviving prose. That the operator-facing artifact lost the two fields the engineering norm makes mandatory is itself a completeness defect: nobody downstream can check this plan against the standard it claims to follow.

- **REMOVE: Wave 3's client-copy carve-out (m-46)** — The plan says maker_kalshi_client.py can be copied to the box without restart because 'it is not called by the quoter'. Verified false: maker_kalshi_quoter.py:43 imports KalshiOrderClient at top level and :3956 instantiates it every cycle. Copying it leaves an unreviewed live-order-path change armed by the next restart, and the 08-06T15:54:54Z halt proves unplanned restarts happen. If B8 needs paged credit_history, run B8 from the worktree, not the deployed tree.
- **REMOVE: m-19 and n-20 from Wave 4 (defer to post-verdict)** — Marked concerning=false and 'strictly stricter', but it tightens the $10 daily-loss halt in a window where credits are $0.00 (handoff:54-55), the numerator lands entirely in the last 3 days (LIP pays at program conclusion), and a halt flattens all presence. Because the venue floor is $1 per market-PROGRAM, lost presence on a marginal program pays $0 not a reduced amount. Ship it on 08-11 when a halt costs money instead of a canon verdict.
- **REMOVE: m-13 and m-9 from Wave 6 until the env dump exists** — Both item texts concede they are unverifiable from the sandbox. m-13 widens _envb's vocabulary and, if any live knob uses a non-'1'/'0' truthy token, flips that flag at restart (TAKER_FLATTEN defaults True at :1587 — a flip arms real taker crossing). m-9 strips SERIES_ALLOW and, if live entries are space-padded, returns silently-benched series from 5ct probes to full size. These are config decisions, not bug fixes, and must not be batched with 29 provable no-ops.
- **REMOVE: n-15's unwind create floor from Wave 4 (keep its counter)** — By its own risk text it can exceed WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE (400, quoter:1500) in exactly the churn cycle already at the venue rate limit. m-29's shared rate-limit gate is three waves later and ships dark. Land the counter now, the floor after m-29 exists.
- **REMOVE: M-24 and M-19 from the pre-verdict window** — Both are correct fixes that re-scale instruments the operator reads DAILY (handoff section 3 item 7) to compute the verdict. M-24 makes the ledger identity check read DOES NOT CLOSE on most runs; M-19 moves every scorecard est-reward number by the window factor (x7 up for weeklies, x24 down for hourlies). The rest of Wave 1 is genuinely free.
- **REMOVE: the 33-item single-restart shape of Wave 6** — Risk concentration sold as risk reduction: one restart, one .bak, 33 candidate causes if the post-restart cycle misbehaves, and the plan's own gate ('quote/order counts statistically unchanged') cannot attribute. Split into provable-no-ops in one restart and env-conditional items held out.
- **ADD (gate): make 'arm EST_FEED' an explicit plan item behind M-9** — EST_FEED arming is item 2 in the operator's own queue for 08-07/08 and appears in no wave. quoter:1333-1340 floors expected credit at the venue estimate with no void scoping, and the activate gate at :3105 is the only thing holding the lane shut. Arming it opens the emitter at :3125-3131, which rests an INV_HARD_CT/D3-clamped ladder that can never reach Target on a 1000ct program — $0 earned, real fill risk, snapshot excluded for everyone. M-9 must be armed before EST_FEED, not in Wave 11.
- **ADD: promote n-19 into the first restart that happens** — n-19's pure _lip_reference shadow column is the long-pole measurement for M-2 (a week of rows is M-2's stated arming precondition) and costs a plan-row key. Scheduling it in post-verdict Wave 6 pushes the earliest M-2 arming out by roughly a week for no reason. If Wave 4 runs, n-19 rides it.
- **ADD: a backup/rollback convention for the four hot-reloading table files** — _presence_table_refresh (:1244-1264) and _netev_table_refresh (:1434-1451) stat every cycle and reload on != mtime; _d3_feedback_cached has a 60s TTL; _refresh_fill_costs rebuilds in-process hourly at :3958. None is covered by the md5 + .bak deploy discipline. Wave 8's 'publish then revert if wrong' story is fiction without a kept copy of the prior table.
- **ADD: kalshi_market_menu.py to scope** — 163-line in-scope file absent from the entire 1410-line review. Carries BOTH flagship defects: pool = (period_reward/10000)/days at :79 (canon pool violation, the M-19 defect) and ref = bids[0][0] at :43 (the M-2 reference-anchor defect). Fixing the scorecard and not the menu is CLASS-NOT-INSTANCE failure inside the plan's own flagship items.
- **ADD: the venue snapshot-exclusion term, or an explicit ruling to defer it** — _prospective_capture (:2584-2603) tests both-sides-at-Target instantaneously and has no valid/total fraction; _presence_factor (:1267-1277) measures our resting share, a different quantity. The model can forecast positive credit for a market that pays everyone $0. Wave 10's mandated re-fit of MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT against a term-less model will bake the error into the haircut and make M-2's root-fix claim false.
- **ADD: a hot-path detector audit** — maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:447-448 calls desired_quotes with stats={} and no own_orders. If WS_HOT=1 live, roughly two dozen of this batch's qstats-based standing detectors under-count by the hot share, and every per-wave gate of the form '48h with counter X at zero' is invalid. Either thread real qstats out of hot or add a hot-side counter set before Waves 6, 9 and 11.
- **DISAGREE with the plan: ship m-3's atomic map write before the 08-07 checkpoint** — The plan freezes kalshi_estimates_recorder.py to protect the APRPOTUS checkpoint, but the freeze protects against the FIX, not against the DEFECT — a truncating write to kalshi_program_map.json during the 08-07 batch loses the checkpoint whether or not anyone edited the file. An ~8-line tmp+os.replace with a .bak is the thing that actually protects it. Genuine coin-flip (edit risk vs tear risk); the plan presents it as obvious and it is not.
- **Q1 (open): what is KALSHI_WS_HOT on the box, and do the detectors survive it?** — Not a process question. If WS_HOT=1, four items move from dark to live-active with a staged arm and write-rate cap, AND a detector redesign becomes a prerequisite for Waves 6, 9 and 11 because hot discards stats. Changes what gets built, not just when.
- **Q2 (open): does the snapshot-exclusion term get built before or after M-2's re-fit?** — 'After' makes Wave 10 one commit that permanently absorbs the missing term into NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT and falsifies the root-fix claim. 'Before' makes Wave 10 a two-stage build: measure the two-sided-at-Target window fraction from the WS mirror, then fit. This changes the SHAPE of the largest fix in the set.
- **Q3 (open): does any live change ship before 2026-08-10T14:13Z, and by which restart procedure?** — The unasked half is (b): restart_bundle.sh:51 drops day_baseline_reset, consumed at quoter:4872, re-seeding the daily governor at current equity and granting a fresh $10 of permitted daily loss; a plain restart inherits the day's carry. Those produce different window-drag numbers in a verdict measured on drag. The answer decides whether Wave 4 exists, whether m-19 ships now or on 08-11, and whether n-19 gets a ride (which sets M-2's earliest arming date by about a week).

## S5. COMPLETENESS CRITIC (what the plan misses, per an independent checker)

COMPLETENESS CRITIC — VERDICT

ID coverage is COMPLETE. I reconstructed the finding-id space from the review doc itself (docs/maker_handoffs/KALSHI_ADVERSARIAL_REVIEW_2026-08-06.md at HEAD 233ee86: S2 = M-1..M-31, S3 = m-1..m-61, S4 = n-1..n-55 = 147) and set-differenced it against the 147 fix ids. MISSING = {} and EXTRA = {}. No finding id is unaddressed. The quoter is byte-identical between the review HEAD (e981bf1) and 233ee86 (git diff --stat shows only the two docs), so every line number in the fix set still resolves.

Everything else is a gap, and there are nine classes of them.

THE HEADLINE: an entire in-scope file is missing from the review and therefore from the plan. kalshi_live/kalshi_market_menu.py (163 lines, "READ-ONLY. Which live LIP series should the clean maker be quoting?") appears ZERO times in the 1410-line review. It carries BOTH canon violations the plan is built around: line 79 computes `pool = (period_reward/10000)/days` — the exact ⛔ CANON pool-formula defect M-19 fixes in kalshi_market_scorecard.py — and line 43 sets `ref = bids[0][0]` — the exact reference-anchor defect M-2/M-5/M-7/M-17 fix. It is the same class of operator-decision-feeding tool as the scorecard. Fixing the scorecard and leaving the menu is CLASS-NOT-INSTANCE failure inside the plan's own flagship item.

THE SECOND HEADLINE: the venue formula given to me as ESTABLISHED contains a term the model does not have and no fix adds. "BOTH sides must meet Target or the snapshot is EXCLUDED for everyone (payout scales valid/total)." `_prospective_capture` (quoter:2584-2603) returns `snap * usd_day` where `usd_day` is the full daily pool and the both-sides test is INSTANTANEOUS only; `_presence_factor` (:1267) measures OUR resting share, not the book's two-sided-at-Target fraction over the window. The two are different — our order can rest through a snapshot the market's other side invalidates, and we earn $0 for it. That missing multiplicative term is first-order (W10 measured a market credited $0.00 that the model forecast ~$26). M-2 will change modeled capture 2-20x and demands MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT be re-fitted in the same deploy — that re-fit will be performed on a model still missing V, so the haircut absorbs V again and the "root fix" is not root.

THREE OPERATOR ITEMS ARE ABSENT. No fix in the 147 implements a selection pre-weed, an est release/discount, or per-row est telemetry. I designed all three below, grounded in the actual walk (quoter:5010-5101) and the lazy market fetch (:2110-2128).

NINE MISSING CONFLICT EDGES, each verified against code — including two on the live halt path (M-12 and m-22 both rewrite `_flatten_all`, which spans :6443-6640, and neither names the other) and one where the set directly contradicts itself (n-19 declares `"conflicts_with":[]` while adding a SECOND independent implementation of the LIP reference walk, which M-7's own design ships a source-text test specifically to fail on).

FOUR BAN-SHAPED FIXES: n-7 converts a partial ladder into no ladder; m-23 throttles the de-risk path against the in-code doctrine "de-risk never gated" (:5058); n-40 armed mass-skips the exploration universe via the $1 floor; M-9 armed retires the activate lane while M-6 and m-10 are simultaneously trying to revive it, with no reconciling ruling item.

HONESTY CAVEAT ON DETECTORS AND TESTS: the fix set I was handed is COMPACTED — it carries id/effort/dark/concerning/conflicts_with/no_fix/fix/risk and NOTHING ELSE. The DETECTOR and TEST fields the task requires are absent for all 147, and several `fix` and `risk` strings are truncated mid-sentence (m-12, m-13, M-4, m-8, M-2, m-11...). I therefore CANNOT audit detector or test presence directly; my detector/test findings below are inferred from the surviving fix prose and are flagged as such. That the operator-facing artifact lost those two required fields is itself a completeness defect worth naming, because a plan without them cannot be checked against the norm it claims to follow.

- **COVERAGE VERDICT — all 147 finding ids present, verified by set difference** — METHOD: extracted the id space from the review doc's own section structure (S2 'MAJOR FINDINGS (M-1..M-31)', S3 'MINOR FINDINGS (61)' = m-1..m-61, S4 'NOTES (55)' = n-1..n-55; 31+61+55 = 147) and diffed against the 147 fix ids. `MISSING from fix set: []` / `EXTRA in fix set: []`. All 147 unique, none duplicated.

Secondary check: the review HEAD is e981bf1, the worktree HEAD is 233ee86, and `git diff --stat e981bf1 233ee86` shows only two .md files (the review + the triple-blind verify). kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py is unchanged at 7317 lines, so every line citation in the fix set (`:2558`, `:5010-5101`, `:6572`, `:4872`...) still resolves. I spot-verified nine of them in source and all nine were correct.

What this does NOT certify: that each fix actually resolves its finding. That is the adversarial reviewer's job, not the completeness critic's. It certifies only that nothing was dropped on the floor between the review and the plan.
- **NEW-1 (BLOCKER-class gap) — kalshi_market_menu.py is unreviewed and carries TWO canon violations the plan is built around** — kalshi_live/kalshi_market_menu.py, 163 lines, docstring: 'KALSHI MARKET MENU -- READ-ONLY. Which live LIP series should the clean maker be quoting?' It appears ZERO times in the 1410-line review (verified: `grep -c kalshi_market_menu` = 0) and therefore in none of the 147 fixes.

VIOLATION 1 — ⛔ CANON pool formula. Line 6 (docstring) and line 79 (code): `pool = (_f(p.get("period_reward")) / 10000) / days`, with `days = max((en-st).total_seconds()/86400, 1/24)` at line 77. This is exactly M-19's defect ('Scorecard still divides period_reward/10000 by window days — the R1 form the canon explicitly refuted'), in a second file. The canon entry is unambiguous: `period_reward/10000` IS the daily per-market pool; NEVER divide by window days. On a 7-day program the menu understates the pool 7x; on an hourly program it overstates 24x. The menu's output is `earnable$/day` per SERIES — i.e. it is a series-selection recommender, so the error is a systematic selection bias toward short-window series, the same direction M-19's own risk text describes.

VIOLATION 2 — reference anchor. Line 43: `ref = bids[0][0]` inside `qual_depth()`, which walks bids accumulating raw size to target and weights `DF^N * size` from that ref. That is a verbatim third copy of `_qualifying_score` (quoter:2558) and `qualifying_share` (scorecard:32) — the M-2/M-5/M-7/M-17 class. `share_at()` at line ~55 then composes `Q/(comp_weighted+Q)`, so the decoy-manipulability M-2 documents applies here too.

FIX: extend M-19 to a two-file commit (pool arithmetic) and extend M-2's call-site migration to a fourth site (`qual_depth` -> `_lip_walk`). Both are mechanical once the shared helpers exist.
WHY: the plan currently fixes the arithmetic in the tool the operator reads sometimes and leaves it wrong in the tool that answers 'which series should we quote' — the highest-leverage question in the lane.
STANDING DETECTOR: a repo-wide source scan test asserting that `period_reward` never appears within 3 lines of a division by a days-derived name, and that `bids[0][0]` appears in exactly one place (the shared `_lip_walk`) outside `test_*`/`VER*`/`_refute*` scratch. That scan is the CLASS detector for both violations and it would have caught this file.
TEST (failing-before): the scan test fails at HEAD on kalshi_market_menu.py:43 and :79 today. That IS the failing-before test, and it is the strongest kind — it fails on a file nobody looked at.
EFFORT: S for the two edits; the scan test is S.
RISK: the menu's historical output becomes non-comparable (every pool figure moves by the window factor, every share figure moves by the reference correction) — same comparability break M-19 accepts, now on a second document. Any handoff quoting menu numbers must be re-derived or retracted. No live money path. concerning: false, but the SCAN is what matters: expect it to surface more offenders in the ~95 unreviewed study scripts, and the fix must be to fix or explicitly quarantine them, not to narrow the scan.
DARK: no.
CONFLICTS: M-19 (same arithmetic class, adjacent file), M-2/M-5/M-7/M-17 (`_lip_walk` migration must include this site), n-19 (see NEW-3).
- **NEW-2 (canon-fidelity gap) — the valid/total snapshot fraction is modeled nowhere and no fix adds it** — VENUE FORMULA (ESTABLISHED, given as binding): 'BOTH sides must meet Target or the snapshot is EXCLUDED for everyone (payout scales valid/total).'

WHAT THE CODE DOES: `_prospective_capture` (quoter:2584-2603) computes an INSTANTANEOUS both-sides-qualify check (`if not (qy and qn): return 0.0`) and returns `snap * float(m.get('usd_day', 0.0))` — the FULL daily pool. `_expected_credit_usd` (:1291) then multiplies by `payout_days` and by `_presence_factor(ticker, life_min)`. `_presence_factor`'s own docstring (:1267-1268): 'Measured share of the window we actually stay resting.' That is OUR uptime. It is NOT the fraction of snapshots in which the MARKET's book was two-sided at Target — which is what the venue formula scales the pool by, and which is independent of us (canon: exclusion is MARKET-level, KALSHI_LIP_RULE_CANON.md:117-121).

CONSEQUENCE: expected credit is overstated by 1/V for every market whose book is intermittently one-sided. The `void` gate (:2900) and `gate_one_sided_book` (:2881) catch V=0 THIS CYCLE; nothing carries V forward. This is a plausible mechanical explanation for the W10 zero-payer class (a market that rested 12.84h and was credited $0.00 against a ~$26 unshaped forecast) that is currently absorbed empirically by NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT=3.0.

WHY THIS BREAKS THE PLAN, not just the model: M-2 is the plan's flagship and its arming precondition is an explicit re-fit of MIN_CREDIT_USD (1.20) and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT (3.0) in the same deploy. Re-fitting a haircut on a model missing a first-order multiplicative term re-absorbs V into the haircut — so after M-2 lands, the model is still wrong in the same direction, just with a different constant, and the plan will have spent its most dangerous deploy without closing the class.

FIX (proposed, telemetry-first): add `_snapshot_validity(ticker)` = the EWMA fraction of this ticker's observed cycles in which `void` was False, sourced from data already computed. It is nearly free: `qstats['unqualifiable']` is already incremented at :2909 and already emitted in the plan row at :5987, and the per-ticker `_gates` dict already reaches `_market_telemetry_row`. Store it in kalshi_market_scores alongside `ref`/`ref_move` (the same cache n-34/n-37 are already widening), emit `validity_frac` per row, and — behind KALSHI_LIP_VALIDITY=0 — multiply `pc` by it in `_expected_credit_usd`.
WHY: it makes the model's pool term match the venue's published pool term.
STANDING DETECTOR: per-row `validity_frac` plus a plan-row `validity_p50` / `validity_below_0_5_n`, and the calibration alarm that matters — a scatter of `validity_frac` against realized credit_history dollars per market-program. If the model is right after arming, forecast/realized error should lose its V-correlation; if it does not, V is not the missing term and the finding is refuted honestly.
TEST (failing-before): a market whose observed cycles are 50% void must produce HALF the expected credit of an identical always-two-sided market. Fails before (no V term exists). Plus a no-op pin at KALSHI_LIP_VALIDITY=0.
EFFORT: M (new cache field + EWMA + one multiplication + telemetry).
RISK: concerning: TRUE. (1) Arming it cuts expected credit on exactly the intermittent books, which STACKS with M-2's 2-20x cut and m-8's admission cut — three tightenings in one direction with no measurement between them can collapse the footprint to near-zero and silently stop the pilot. Never arm V and LIP_REF in the same deploy. (2) V is estimated from OUR sampling cadence (30-120s cycles), not the venue's 1s random snapshots, so it is a biased estimator of the venue's valid/total — biased in an unknown direction on fast books. Say that in the commit message; do not present V as measured. (3) It changes a measured baseline inside the P2 window (see the freeze item).
DARK: yes, mandatory — KALSHI_LIP_VALIDITY=0, telemetry ON.
CONFLICTS: M-2 (same expected-credit chain, must not co-arm), m-11 (both change what the $1 floor sees), m-45/n-40 (presence table is the adjacent multiplier and its calibration would double-count V if the presence study's denominator is not corrected at the same time — that interaction is UNEXAMINED in the set), M-29/m-20 (netev model fallback reads the same `pc`).
- **NEW-3 (internal contradiction) — n-19 adds a SECOND LIP reference implementation and declares zero conflicts, which M-7's own detector is designed to fail on** — n-19's fix: 'Add pure helper `_lip_reference(levels, target)` implementing the venue-published rule verbatim... In the quote loop store `ref_lip[t] = ...` in a NEW dict.' Its conflicts_with is `[]`.

M-2's fix: 'NEW pure helpers... `_lip_walk`... routes ALL THREE (score, breakdown, telemetry) through the single helper.' M-7's fix adds: 'The single-implementation assertion is a new source-text test — it will fail on any future legitimate refactor that renames the helper, which is intended (it forces the change to be deliberate).'

So the plan ships a test whose purpose is to fail when a second implementation of the LIP reference exists, and separately ships a second implementation of the LIP reference. Whichever lands second breaks the suite (baseline 1233 passed / 2 xfailed, exit 0 must stay green).

WORSE, the class is wider than either item admits. The best-bid reference walk exists in FIVE places in kalshi_live/ today: maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2558 (`_qualifying_score`), :2731 (`_qualifying_breakdown`), kalshi_market_scorecard.py:32 (`qualifying_share`), kalshi_market_menu.py:43 (`qual_depth` — see NEW-1), and _refute_cf2.py:11 (scratch, ignorable). M-2's concrete design (a) names only quoter helpers. M-5's prose names the scorecard but says 'No separate code change'. Nobody names the menu.

AND: test_capture_gate.py:236-240 (`test_qualifying_score_matches_scorecard`) pins BYTE-EQUIVALENCE between `q._qualifying_score` and `scorecard.qualifying_share` — but it calls with default args, so it stays GREEN at LIP_REF=0 and MASKS the divergence at LIP_REF=1. Armed, the live quoter's model and the operator's scorecard would silently disagree with no test failing.

FIX: (1) fold n-19 into M-2 — `_lip_reference` becomes a thin accessor over `_lip_walk`, not a parallel implementation; n-19's telemetry fields are kept, its helper is not. (2) M-2's migration list becomes FOUR sites: `_qualifying_score`, `_qualifying_breakdown`, `scorecard.qualifying_share`, `menu.qual_depth`. (3) Parameterize `test_qualifying_score_matches_scorecard` over LIP_REF in {0,1} so the equivalence is pinned in BOTH modes.
WHY: closes the mechanism class ('the venue's reference rule has N copies and only some get corrected') instead of adding an N+1th copy.
STANDING DETECTOR: M-7's source-text single-implementation test, WIDENED to scan kalshi_live/*.py excluding test_/VER*/_refute*, and asserting that the tick-exponent expression `df ** round((ref - p) / TICK)` (or equivalent) occurs in exactly one function. It fails at HEAD on four files.
TEST (failing-before): the widened scan fails today. Plus the LIP_REF=1 equivalence parameterization, which fails the moment M-2 lands without the scorecard migration.
EFFORT: M (fold + one extra call site + test parameterization).
RISK: widening M-2's blast radius from one module to three is real — the scorecard and menu are read-only tools, so no money path, but the equivalence test becomes a hard gate on the flagship commit and a mismatch blocks the deploy. That is the correct failure direction. concerning: false.
CONFLICTS: M-2, M-5, M-7, M-17, M-18 (all ride M-2), M-19 (same scorecard file), NEW-1 (same menu file).
- **NEW-4 (duplication the plan creates) — three new helpers answer the same question: 'how much will actually rest?'** — m-8 introduces `_eff_rest_ct(m, now_ts)`: 'The count an ACCUMULATING quote will actually rest at for this row, after the explore probe clamp and the D3 ramp / new-series clamp. Single source of truth.'
m-20 introduces `_ramp_scale(m, best_y, best_n, now_ts)`: '(size that will ACTUALLY rest) / (size the model priced) in [0,1]... Includes [the probe clamp].'
m-38 introduces `_probe_clamp_ct(m)`: the explore probe clamp alone, 'One predicate, three consumers.'

These are the SAME computation at three granularities (absolute count / ratio / one component), landing in the same 7,317-line module, over the same three consumers: `_expected_credit_usd`'s F3 block (:1321-1331), the select-budget est mirror (:5063-5070), and the actual sizing clamp (:5311-5317). I verified all three consumers exist and all three currently contain hand-copied variants of the clamp.

Declared conflicts: m-8 lists m-58, n-2, M-1, m-9. m-20 lists m-59, M-6, M-2, M-29. m-38 lists M-8, n-8. NONE of the three names either of the other two. Whichever lands first leaves the next two rebasing onto a helper that already does most of their job, and the likely outcome is that all three land — recreating, in the fix, the exact multi-copy defect that produced m-8, m-58 and m-38 in the first place.

FIX: ONE helper, `_eff_rest_ct(m, now_ts) -> float`, with `_ramp_scale` and `_probe_clamp_ct` as derived one-liners over it (`_ramp_scale = min(1.0, _eff_rest_ct/join_ct)`, `_probe_clamp_ct` = the probe term exposed for M-8's designation exemption). Land the helper + all three call-site migrations as ONE provably-no-op commit; land the BEHAVIOUR changes (m-8's floor-gate allowlist removal, m-20's two gate multiplications, m-38's designation exemption) as separate flagged commits on top.
WHY: the class is 'the size-that-will-rest predicate is copied per consumer and the copies drift'. Three helpers is two copies with better names.
STANDING DETECTOR: a source-text test asserting `EXPLORE_PROBE_CT` and `_d3_est_ct` are each referenced in exactly one function outside tests. It fails at HEAD (three sites for the probe clamp) and it fails again the day a fourth consumer is added without routing through the helper — which is precisely the recurrence.
TEST (failing-before): a table-driven test over (explore?, designated?, series in SERIES_ALLOW?, rung, join_ct) asserting all three consumers return the SAME effective count. It fails at HEAD because :1324-1329 gates the probe clamp on SERIES_ALLOW membership while :5311 and :5063 do not — that asymmetry IS m-8/m-58, and the unified test pins it as one defect rather than two.
EFFORT: M.
RISK: concerning: TRUE, inherited from m-8. Merging three flag surfaces into one helper means the no-op commit touches all three live consumers at once; a mistake in the helper moves admission, sizing AND the budget walk simultaneously. Mitigation is that the migration commit must be byte-identical-output pinned before any behaviour commit — and the pin must run at the LIVE flag set (D3_RAMP=1, PRESENCE_GATE=1, EXPLORE_PROBE_CT>0), not at defaults. Note also that m-8's own risk text admits EXPLORE_PROBE_CT and ALLOW_PROBE_EXCEPTION are BELIEVED-not-read from live.env; that unread value determines whether the unified helper is a no-op or a live sizing change.
CONFLICTS: m-8, m-58, m-20, m-59, m-38, M-1, M-31, n-2, m-9, plus OP-ADD-1b (which multiplies the same est).
- **NEW-5 (silent migration) — n-18 and n-55 redefine plan['gated_out'], and the in-repo consumer that aggregates it is unreviewed and unlisted** — kalshi_live/kalshi_plans_hourly.py is a real in-repo consumer of the plan rows. It lists `gated_out` in `FIELDS` (:34) and prints it in its hourly aggregate (:101, alongside `empty_books`, `unqualifiable`, `capped_markets`). The file is named ZERO times in the review and is in none of the 147 fixes.

n-18's fix changes `gated_out` from `len(footprint) - len(desired)` to `sum(1 for _m in footprint if _m['ticker'] not in desired)` — a DEFINITION change on a key with long live history (n-18's own risk says 'any study slicing gated_out across the fix boundary sees a level shift'). n-55's commit (1) makes the same change independently via `fp_tickers` intersection. Neither names kalshi_plans_hourly.py; neither names the other in conflicts_with (n-18 lists M-10/M-30; n-55 lists n-17/m-16/n-15/M-31).

So: two items redefine the same key by two different expressions, the in-repo aggregator will silently sum pre-fix and post-fix definitions in the same hourly table, and Rule 3 ('preserve every external interface... these are contracts between components') is violated without anyone noticing, because the consumer was never read.

This generalizes. SEVEN items add or change plan-row keys — n-9 (`drop_no_market_ticker` + `_ALWAYS_EMIT_DROPS`), n-18 (`gated_out`, `desired_offfootprint`), m-42 (`netev_min_receipt_frac`/`netev_min_model_usd`), m-47 (`plan['knobs']`), m-54 (`family_dropped_n`), n-44 (`n_candidates`), n-55 — plus OP-ADD-1c below. NOT ONE of them lists kalshi_plans_hourly.py as a consumer to verify. n-9's risk text mentions it in passing ('kalshi_plans_hourly coerces with .get(f,0) or 0') and then dismisses out-of-repo consumers; that is exactly backwards — the in-repo one is the one that must be updated.

FIX: (1) pick ONE gated_out definition (n-18's is the clearer expression) and make n-55's commit (1) a no-op reference to it, with an explicit conflict edge. (2) Emit the new definition under a NEW key (`gated_out_v2`) for one release and keep the legacy key computed the old way, exactly as m-42 does for `netev_min_signal` — the set already contains the right pattern; apply it consistently. (3) Add kalshi_plans_hourly.py to the consumer list of every plan-key item and update `FIELDS` in the same commit as the producer.
WHY: closes 'plan keys are changed without their consumer' as a class rather than fixing one key.
STANDING DETECTOR: a test that reads the producer's emitted key set (from a dry-run plan row) and asserts every name in kalshi_plans_hourly.FIELDS exists in it, with a WARNING listing producer keys the aggregator ignores. It fails the day a producer key is renamed and it is cheap.
TEST (failing-before): assert `gated_out >= 0` on a synthetic cycle with two strand-unwind tickers and one footprint market — fails at HEAD (goes negative, which is the n-18/n-55 finding), and assert the legacy key retains its old value so the aggregate is not silently redefined.
EFFORT: S.
RISK: keeping both keys grows the row slightly and leaves the wrong number reachable for one release — m-42's own risk already argues that this is the lesser evil versus rewriting historical rows. concerning: false.
CONFLICTS: n-18, n-55, n-9, m-42, m-47, m-54, n-44, OP-ADD-1c.
- **MISSING CONFLICT EDGES — nine, each verified against source** — The conflicts_with lists were delivered intact (unlike detector/test), so these are hard findings, not compaction artifacts.

1. M-1 <-> n-12 — SAME TWO LINES. quoter:4001 `cyc = int(now.timestamp())`, :4002 `st = load_state()`. M-1: 'immediately after `st = load_state()` (:4002), add `if D3_RAMP: _d3_first_seen_ensure(st)`'. n-12: 'Swap the order of :4001-4002 so state is loaded first, then compute cyc = max(...)'. 2. RECOMMENDED ACTION: build the conflict graph mechanically from (file, line-range) tuples rather than by hand. Every edge above is derivable from overlapping line ranges the items already state, which means the hand-built graph is not just incomplete — it is incomplete in a way a five-line script would have caught.
- **BAN-SHAPED FIX #1 — n-7 converts a partial macro ladder into NO ladder** — n-7's fix: 'change the refusal at :707 from `if len(prices) < 2` to `if len(prices) < MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS` — so any TOP that cannot span the ruled number of levels refuses instead of silently shipping a cheaper ladder.'

That is refuse-instead-of-degrade. Under the venue formula the thing that earns is RESTING SIZE on a qualifying side; a 2-level ladder still rests, still counts toward Target, still scores `size x 0.5^ticks`. Refusing rests nothing and earns nothing. The engineering norm is explicit: 'Never disable/blacklist/skip a market, side, series, or feature as a fix' and 'Fix not remove'. n-7 dresses the skip as 'an explicit contract' — the contract framing is right, the terminal refusal is not.

ROOT-FIX SHAPE INSTEAD: build the ladder from `MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS` but EMIT WHAT FITS, counting the shortfall — `prices = [...]` filtered by `>= 0.01` as today, then `stats['macro_probe_levels_short'] += (MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS - len(prices))` and proceed with the levels that exist, refusing only at `len(prices) == 0` (nothing can be priced, which is not a policy choice but an arithmetic fact). If the operator's real intent is 'a 2-level probe is not worth the capital', that is an EDGE question and it belongs in M-4's `MACRO_PROBE_MIN_EDGE`, which already exists in the same commit family — not in a level-count refusal.
WHY: keeps presence (the thing LIP pays for) while making the degradation visible, instead of trading presence for silence.
STANDING DETECTOR: `macro_probe_levels_short` as a live counter plus a WARNING when it is non-zero for N consecutive cycles on the same ticker — that names the real defect (MACRO_PROBE_TOP is set too low for the tick grid) instead of hiding it behind a refusal.
TEST (failing-before): with MACRO_PROBE_TOP=0.02 and MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS=3, assert TWO quotes are emitted and `macro_probe_levels_short == 1`. Fails on n-7's proposed code (which emits zero) and fails at HEAD (which emits two but counts nothing).
EFFORT: S.
RISK: a 2-level ladder on a Target-1000 program is genuinely unlikely to qualify the side alone, so 'emit what fits' can rest capital that earns nothing — which is the honest argument FOR n-7. The answer is that this is an edge/economics decision (M-4's job), and the difference matters: M-4's refusal is priced and knob-controlled, n-7's is structural. Also note n-7's weight generalization: if it changes the 3-level counts by even one contract it is no longer inert inside the dark subsystem. concerning: false (D-C is dark), but it sets a precedent the norm forbids.
CONFLICTS: M-4, n-5, M-31, M-8 (all D-C).
- **BAN-SHAPED FIX #2 — m-23 throttles the DE-RISK path against the code's own doctrine** — m-23 adds `STRAND_MAX_MKTS` — a per-invocation cap on `_strand_cross`, the backstop that taker-crosses stranded residual positions. Default 0 (unlimited) makes it a provable no-op, which is correct shipping discipline. But ARMING it delays risk-reducing crosses, and m-23's own risk text says so: 'A cap of 8 against a 12-ticker backlog means 4 positions wait at least one more STRAND_CROSS_S period.'

This contradicts a doctrine the codebase states repeatedly and implements everywhere else:
- quoter:5058 comment: '_keepset9.add(_t9)  # de-risk never gated; capital already held'
- `cap_desired`'s unconditional keep for unwind lists
- `bound_creates`' unwind-first sort
- n-15's fix, in this very set, adds an UNWIND FLOOR to the create budget precisely so exits are never starved
- the F12 audit note at :2370-2376: 'the house doctrine already exempts reducing orders from every capital gate — so dollars must not clip the exit either'

So the set contains n-15 (never starve exits) and m-23 (cap exits) simultaneously, and neither names the other. The stated justification for m-23 is symmetry with 'its two sibling backstops' — but those siblings bound ENTRY-adjacent work; symmetry with them is the wrong symmetry.

The loss shape m-23 would reintroduce is on the record: 07-27, 42 ct rode from a -$0.59 touch exit to -$15.29 at settlement — the exact event FIX 3 (`_strand_cross`) was built to bound.

RECOMMENDATION: ship m-23's OBSERVABILITY (the oldest-first sort, the per-invocation ticker count, the backlog-age histogram) and do NOT ship the cap — or ship it with a hard-coded exemption so that any ticker whose strand age exceeds a threshold crosses regardless of the cap, which makes the cap a RATE SHAPER rather than a refusal. If the real problem is API burst pressure during a mass strand, the root fix is the shared rate-limit gate m-29 is already building, not a cap on de-risking.
WHY: the mechanism class is 'exits must never be gated by a resource budget'; every other item in the set honours it.
STANDING DETECTOR: `strand_backlog_age_p95` and `strand_deferred_n` as always-emitted plan fields, with a WARNING whenever any residual has been due for more than 2x STRAND_CROSS_S. That detector is worth shipping whether or not the cap ever arms — it is currently absent.
TEST (failing-before): with 12 due strands and the cap at 8, assert the 4 deferred are the 4 NEWEST and that none exceeds the age threshold; and assert an over-age ticker crosses regardless of the cap. Fails on m-23 as designed (no age exemption).
EFFORT: S.
RISK: concerning: TRUE for the armed cap — it is a deliberate, measurable increase in tail loss on the exit path, on a live money system, to buy API headroom that m-29 addresses directly. Leaving it unarmed costs nothing, which is why the default is right and the arming is the problem.
CONFLICTS: n-15, n-53, M-12, m-29, n-21.
- **BAN-SHAPED FIX #3 — n-40 armed is a mass-skip of the exploration universe by calibration, and #4 — M-9 armed retires a lane with no ruling** — n-40 (wildcard presence fallback). Its own risk: 'Turning the fallback on moves every family with no own bucket from 1.0 to the pooled median (measured ~0.057, a ~17x cut in expected credit), which will push most such markets under the $1 LIP floor and mass-skip them. Those are precisely the exploration markets D3_RAMP / PIVOT [depend on].'

A 17x cut to every unmeasured family, feeding a hard $1.20 floor with a `return []` at quoter:2951, is a de-facto exclusion of the discovery universe delivered through a calibration table rather than through a filter. The no-bans norm does not care which mechanism does the excluding. It is also self-sealing: excluded markets never rest, never generate presence samples, never earn their own bucket, and stay excluded — the same death-spiral the code already names at :2941-2945 (`presence_skipped_execution_only`, 'THE DEATH-SPIRAL COUNTER').

FIX SHAPE THAT KEEPS IT NO-BANS: the wildcard median is the right ESTIMATOR and the wrong GATE INPUT. Either (a) exempt explore/probe rows from the $1 floor entirely — they already rest EXPLORE_PROBE_CT dust, so the floor is asking the wrong question of them; or (b) apply the wildcard median to RANKING (kalshi_market_scores) but keep PRESENCE_DEFAULT for the floor gate until the family has its own bucket. Either keeps discovery alive while the estimate becomes honest.
STANDING DETECTOR: `presence_wildcard_applied_n` and `presence_skipped_wildcard_only` — markets skipped SOLELY because the wildcard replaced 1.0. If that counter is large, the calibration is banning, and the operator sees it in one line.
TEST (failing-before): an explore row in an uncalibrated family with wildcard armed must still emit probe-sized quotes. Fails on n-40 as designed.
EFFORT: S on top of n-40.
RISK: keeping unmeasured families at 1.0 preserves the known over-admission n-40 exists to fix — that is a real cost, paid in drive-by sub-$1 markets (W10 §5). The trade is deliberate: over-admit dust rather than stop exploring. concerning: TRUE either way; this needs an operator ruling, not a default.
CONFLICTS: m-45, m-11, NEW-2 (all move the same floor).

M-9 (activate atomicity). Its own risk: 'Armed, this retires most of the activate path: D3_RUNGS tops out at 50 and D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 holds unproven series at 10ct, so on a Target-1000 program activate can only be feasible when BOTH sides are already within ~10-50ct of Target.' - **SCHEDULE COMPLETENESS — the plan has no freeze window and three hard dates land inside it** — The set orders items by conflict, not by clock. Three dated commitments interact with it and none is expressed as a constraint on any item.

1. P2 PILOT VERDICT — pre-committed CANON, due 2026-08-10T14:13Z, rule = window credits > window drag. Any change to admission, sizing or capital deployment inside that window makes the credit and drag series non-homogeneous across the deploy boundary, and the verdict is pre-committed so it cannot be re-specified after the fact. Items that move it: m-8 + m-58 (cut admissions), n-2 armed (admits more), M-2 armed (2-20x capture change), m-11 armed (loosens the floor), M-29 armed (arms a currently-dead bench), m-60 armed (restores settlement-ramp size), n-55 armed (reads further down the pool), m-39 if ALLOC_KEY=1 (reorders allocation), and OP-ADD-1b. NOT ONE of these names the P2 window. RECOMMENDATION: declare a P2 FREEZE — until 08-10T14:13Z, land only provable no-ops, telemetry, and offline-tool corrections; every armed behaviour change waits. That is 60-plus items of the set that can still land, so the freeze costs little.

2. APRPOTUS CHECKPOINT — program ended 2026-08-07T15:00Z, its est->credit reconciliation gates D-A arming. M-16, m-3 and n-31 all edit kalshi_estimates_recorder.py, which runs on a 5-minute timer and is the ONLY recorder of that checkpoint.
