Make it explicit: the atomic-write helper (~8 lines, tmp + os.replace, provably safer) may land immediately; the recovery pull (`_fetch_programs` over the unfiltered feed) and n-31's backfill wait until the checkpoint row is captured and verified. Note also that both M-16 and n-31 propose an unfiltered /incentive_programs sweep whose full capture was ~47MB on 2026-07-23 — on a 5-minute timer, without the page cap, timeout and cooldown, that is a self-inflicted rate-limit incident.

3. B8 NET-EV REBUILD — due ~2026-08-09/10, and FIX-H program endings 2026-08-09T03:59Z. SIX items change what that rebuild reads: M-21, M-22, m-41, n-39, n-41, m-46 (plus M-15 and m-43 through fill costs). B8 rebuilds the table ONCE. If they land unsequenced, the new table reflects an uncontrolled mixture and no family verdict can be attributed. RECOMMENDATION: freeze the netev inputs, land the scope-containment pair (M-21/M-22) with `scope=legacy` default, produce BOTH documents from the same tape, have the operator compare, and only then choose the arming for the single B8 build. m-46's credit recovery must be sequenced BEFORE that comparison or it changes the denominators mid-comparison.

MISSING ARTIFACT: a single ordered ship list with dates. The set gives 147 designs and a conflict graph; it does not give a sequence. Given that six of these items are individually capable of changing live deployment on a $10-daily-halt system inside a pre-committed measurement window, the sequence IS the deliverable the operator needs most, and it is the one thing the plan does not contain.
- **CANON-TRAP CONSTRAINTS MISSING FROM SPECIFIC FIX TEXTS** — No fix in the set OPENLY violates a canon trap — I checked all seven traps against all 147. But four fixes are described at a level of abstraction that permits a violation at implementation time, and the constraint must be written into the design before someone codes it.

1. m-43 / n-29 (fee-convention normalization in kalshi_fill_costs). The design says 'determine the venue's fee convention from data the build already has' and 'normalize only behind KALSHI_FILLCOST_FEE_NORM'. It never states the arithmetic. IF the normalization re-derives a fee rather than using the venue's `fill_fee`, it MUST be `0.07 * C * P * (1-P)` EXACT, no rounding, no ceil (the ceil variant is QUARANTINED canon; the exact form is implemented at kalshi_exit_calc.py:39 and is receipt-verified). Write that into the design and pin it with a test that asserts the normalizer never introduces a rounding step.

2. n-21 (`_venue_fill_ct`). The design walks `('fill_count_fp','fill_count')` and returns 'the first PRESENT value with known=True'. CANON TRAP: on Kalshi the plain-name fields are PRESENT-BUT-None, not absent. A naive `if k in o` presence test therefore returns (None-coerced-to-0, known=True) — the exact silent-zero the fix exists to eliminate, reintroduced by the fix. The predicate must be `o.get(k) is not None`, and the test must include a payload carrying `fill_count: None` alongside a populated `fill_count_fp`, plus one carrying ONLY `fill_count: None` (which must return known=False).

3. m-30 (`cancel_remaining_ct` with `expected_max`). Same trap class: the design reads `o[k]` over a key list. State the `*_fp`-first, `is not None` rule explicitly and pin it, or the clamp silently reads a plain-name None.

4. n-54 (`_standing_from_rows`). The design walks `('remaining_count_fp','remaining_count','initial_count_fp','count')`. Two plain names in that list. The 'first PRESENT' semantics must be None-aware for the same reason, and the STEP 2 arm (KALSHI_STANDING_ZERO_HONEST) must not treat a plain-name None as a genuine 0-remaining — that would cancel-and-recreate live orders on a field-shape artifact, which is precisely the anti-symmetric error n-54's own risk describes.

TRAPS VERIFIED CLEAN ACROSS THE SET: pool formula (M-19 removes `/days` and keeps `/10000` — correct; but see NEW-1 for the second file). Exits-are-SELL / never-flipped-side (M-12, n-15, n-53, m-16, m-17, m-23, n-21 all preserve reducing-order polarity; m-17's enforcement arm is the one to watch — its cancel must be provably restricted to ACCUMULATING sides, and its test must include a ticker holding inventory with a live reducing order to prove the exit is never the side cancelled). Banned reward sources (m-33 and n-42 FENCE rewards_residual / rewards_alloc_by_series rather than consuming them, which is correct and also correctly avoids demoting a recorded series; M-24 replaces residual-derived rewards with credit_history receipts, which is the canon-mandated direction; n-32 explicitly keeps the residual chain byte-identical and adds skew telemetry only). One-bet-per-market and neg-risk are Polymarket-side and not in scope here.
- **OP-ADD-1a — Cheap pre-weed at selection (DESIGN; absent from all 147)** — FIX. The lazy market fetch at quoter:2117, `public_get(f"/trade-api/v2/markets/{_t3}")`, already returns a fully populated market object and the code keeps only `close_time` and `volume_24h_fp`. Capture the prices from the SAME payload — zero extra API calls.
(1) New bounded cache beside `_VOL24_CACHE` (:1737): `_PX_CACHE = {}` holding `{ticker: (yes_bid, yes_ask, mono)}`, `PX_TTL_S = _envf("KALSHI_PX_TTL_S", 900.0)`, with `_px_cache_get` / `_px_cache_put` copied in shape from `_vol24_cache_get` (:1740) and `_close_cache_put` (:1767) — same LRU bound (`CLOSE_CACHE_MAX`), same expire-then-pop-then-None semantics.
(2) At :2118-2124, alongside the existing `_ct3` / `_v3` capture: `_yb3 = _f(_mkt3.get("yes_bid_dollars")); _ya3 = _f(_mkt3.get("yes_ask_dollars"))`. CANON TRAP, LOAD-BEARING: `*_dollars` ONLY. Plain `yes_bid`/`yes_ask` return None (venue-verified 2026-08-06T19:19:40Z on KXTOPMODEL-26AUG31-CLAU5: plain = None, `yes_bid_dollars`="0.1200", `yes_ask_dollars`="0.1500"). Count `_SILENT["px_field_missing"]` when both `*_dollars` are absent — that counter IS the field-rename tripwire.
(3) New pure helper beside `_ok_entry_price` (:2379), reusing that exact predicate so the pre-weed can never drift from the gate:
    def _preweed_verdict(yb, ya):
        """Price-visible quotability of a market-object snapshot, in the SAME predicates
        desired_quotes uses: best_y == yes_bid (quoter:2823); best_n == 1 - yes_ask, because
        the NO side's best bid IS one minus the YES ask. 'ok'|'one_sided'|'entry_band'|'unknown'."""
        if yb is None or ya is None: return "unknown"
        by = yb if yb > 0.0 else None
        bn = (1.0 - ya) if ya < 1.0 else None
        if by is None or bn is None: return "one_sided"          # mirrors :2881
        if not (_ok_entry_price(by) and _ok_entry_price(bn)): return "entry_band"   # mirrors :2883
        return "ok"
(4) THE WEED IS A DISCOUNT, NEVER A DROP. The row gets `r["preweed"] = verdict` and `r["preweed_age_s"]`. NOTHING is removed from `rows`; `select_footprint`'s output is byte-identical. The only consumer is OP-ADD-1b's discount. TTL expiry re-admits the row at full charge — that is the no-bans periodic re-check the review explicitly requires ('gate-refused books DO drift back to quotable').
CRITICAL CONSTRAINT: v1 captures opportunistically ONLY — it must NEVER force a market fetch that the warm close+vol caches made free. The scan budget at :2098 is `min(FOOTPRINT_TOP*4, max(40, READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE//2))` and the D1 comment records that 4x FOOTPRINT starved ctx.build and took the hot path down for a cycle (15:15Z). A TTL-forced refetch policy re-opens that incident and must be priced separately.

WHY. The walk charges the LARGEST est to exactly the rows most likely to gate out (`stale ref -> unknown -> max est`, :5026-5032), and one-sided/empty books are the rows least likely to have a usable ref. Price-visible refusals were ~22 of 39 rows on the 19:19:59Z cycle. The market payload that already gets fetched answers the question for free.

STANDING DETECTOR. Plan row gains `preweed = {ok, one_sided, entry_band, unknown, stale}` AND — the real detector — `preweed_agree` / `preweed_disagree`, computed in the quote loop by comparing the cached verdict against the gate that actually fired for every market that reached `desired_quotes` (`gate_one_sided_book` / `gate_entry_band` / neither, all already in `_gates` at :5384). WARNING when `disagree/(agree+disagree) > 0.25` on a cycle with n>=10. That one meter catches all three recurrence modes: venue renames `*_dollars`, PX_TTL_S set too long, and market-object/orderbook divergence. Plus `px_field_missing`, which goes to 100% the day the plain-name trap moves.

TEST (failing-before). T1 CANON PIN: a market dict carrying ONLY populated plain `yes_bid`/`yes_ask` and no `*_dollars` must return "unknown", NOT "ok" — this fails if anyone ever 'simplifies' to plain names. T2 EQUIVALENCE: over a grid of (yb, ya), `_preweed_verdict` matches the gate that `desired_quotes` fires on a synthetic book with `best_y=yb`, `best_n=1-ya` at flat inventory. Fails before (helper absent). T3 NO-BAN PIN: `select_footprint`'s output ticker list is IDENTICAL with the pre-weed on and off. T4: an expired cache entry returns None and the row is charged full est again.

EFFORT: M (~90 lines across two regions of one file).

RISK: concerning: TRUE. (1) The market object's bid/ask is a venue snapshot that may be staler or differently derived than the `orderbook_fp` the quote loop reads; systematic divergence discounts quotable rows. The agreement meter is the only defence and it must ship BEFORE the discount arms. (2) `PX_TTL_S` is a new live knob on the selection path — too long discounts recovered books, too short spends READ_BUDGET the D1 fix specifically bounded, and that budget starving the WS context is a MEASURED live incident, not a hypothetical. (3) On a warm cache the coverage is thin, so the honest EV is much smaller than the '$138 gap' headline until a refetch policy is priced. The headline should not be repeated without that caveat.

DARK: yes — `KALSHI_PREWEED=0` gates the discount consumer in 1b. Capture, verdict and agreement meter ship ON and unflagged (T3 pins that they cannot alter selection), so the operator sees the disagreement distribution before a dollar moves.

CONFLICTS: n-6 (designation funnel counters, :2021-2165), n-9 (`_ALWAYS_EMIT_DROPS` + drop counters, :1997-2002), M-8 (`_is_designated` migration at :2146), m-9 (SERIES_ALLOW strip feeding `_allowlisted3` at :2146), n-10 (PIVOT coverage cap, same function), m-47 (`plan['knobs']`), OP-ADD-1b, OP-ADD-1c.
- **OP-ADD-1b — Within-cycle est release / carry-forward discount (DESIGN; absent from all 147)** — SHAPE DECISION: implement (b2) carry-forward, REFUSE (b1). (b1) re-walks the budget after the quote loop and needs a second book-read sub-cycle. The quote loop's read budget is the resource that M-11 and M-30 already show truncating the footprint (`except RuntimeError: break` at :5229). Spending more reads to recover reservation is the wrong trade on the exact axis that already breaks, and it would make M-30's exhaustion branch fire more often.

FIX.
(1) PERSIST THE OUTCOME. In the quote loop after `q = desired_quotes(...)`, record `st["gate_hist"][t] = {"ts": now.timestamp(), "rested": <any accumulating quote in q>, "why": <first price-visible gate from _gates>}`. Prune to `GATE_HIST_MAX = 400` newest on save, same bounded-map discipline `_D3_FIRST_SEEN` uses. One dict write per market per cycle, no I/O.
(2) DISCOUNT, NEVER ZERO. In the select-budget walk, immediately after `_est9 = _kcr.est_commit_usd(...)` (:5062) and BEFORE the probe and ramp clamps so those still bind:
    _disc9 = _gate_discount(st, _t9, _now9, preweed=_pw9)   # in [FLOOR, 1.0]
    _est9 *= _disc9
`_gate_discount` returns 1.0 unless the ticker's last persisted outcome was `rested=False` for a PRICE-VISIBLE reason, or 1a's cached verdict is one_sided/entry_band. Discount = `KALSHI_GATE_DISCOUNT` (default 1.0 = no-op; proposed armed value 0.35), DECAYING LINEARLY back to 1.0 over `KALSHI_GATE_DISCOUNT_DECAY_S` (1800), with a hard `GATE_DISCOUNT_FLOOR = 0.20` so a row is NEVER free. Consecutive gated cycles DO NOT COMPOUND — the discount is a function of the last outcome and its age only. Compounding is what turns a discount into a ban.
(3) DE-RISK UNTOUCHED. `_heldset9`, `_incumbent_only` and `_exit_only_mkts` rows already `continue` before the est is computed (:5045-5060) — the discount sits after them and can never re-price a held or exit-only market.
(4) ORDERING UNCHANGED. `_order9`'s key stays `alloc_prio` (:5035). Only the CHARGE moves. This keeps 1b orthogonal to M-20/m-40, which own the ordering key, and means the fix cannot reorder capital allocation.

WHY. The walk is single-pass and never re-credits; the 18:58:24Z row shows `select_budget_used` 198.00 against `committed_usd` 59.77 with `gated_out` 33 and `drop_budget_full` 7 — the budget turned away up to 7 candidates while reserving for 33 rows that produced zero resting orders. A decaying discount lets the same budget fund rows that will actually rest, and the decay guarantees every row re-charges honestly.

STANDING DETECTOR. Three always-emitted plan fields: `select_budget_discounted_n` / `select_budget_discount_usd` (dollars freed), `gate_hist_recovered` (tickers discounted this cycle that DID rest — the FALSE-DISCOUNT meter), `gate_hist_stale_n` (map-leak alarm). CLASS ALARM: WARNING when `gate_hist_recovered / select_budget_discounted_n > 0.5` on a cycle with n>=8 — that says the persisted outcome is not predictive and the discount should be DISARMED, not tuned.

TEST (failing-before). T1 arithmetic: stamped `rested=False` 300s ago -> charged `est*(0.35 + 0.65*300/1800)`; at >= DECAY_S -> exactly `est`. T2 NO-BAN PIN: for every input (any age, any repetition count) `_gate_discount() >= FLOOR > 0`, and a ticker discounted 100 consecutive cycles still enters `_keepset9` when budget allows. This is the tripwire that fails if someone later 'optimizes' the discount to zero. T3 de-risk exemption: held and exit-only tickers are never discounted (asserts the `continue` paths still precede it). T4 MONEY-PATH FAILING-BEFORE: synthetic 10-row footprint where rows 6-10 were gated last cycle and `_limit9` admits ~6 at full est — assert rows 8-10 are in `_saidno9` BEFORE and at least one enters `_keepset9` AFTER. T5 state bound: 500 stamps -> pruned to 400, oldest evicted, save/load round-trips.

EFFORT: M.

RISK: concerning: TRUE. (1) This ADMITS MORE — the opposite direction from m-8/m-58, which cut admissions by unifying `_eff_rest_ct`, and they land in the SAME try-block. Landing both leaves a net admission change neither commit predicts. Sequence and measure between. (2) Freed budget raises actual deployment toward `_limit9` on previously under-deployed cycles. That is a DEPLOYMENT-LEVEL CHANGE INSIDE THE P2 WINDOW (verdict 2026-08-10T14:13Z, rule = window credits > window drag) and it makes the pilot's series non-homogeneous across the deploy. This is the single strongest reason to hold 1b until after 08-10. (3) It is a ONE-CYCLE-LAG predictor on moving books; on fast hourly programs it may be wrong most of the time — `gate_hist_recovered` is the meter and >50% means disarm. (4) `st` gains a map written every cycle; the state file also carries the halt baselines and strand clocks, so a serialization bug there is a live-money failure.

DARK: yes — `KALSHI_GATE_DISCOUNT=1.0` is the no-op default (multiplying by 1.0 is byte-identical), so no new boolean is needed. Ship the gate_hist recording and all three plan fields ON so the counterfactual (dollars that WOULD have been freed) is measured before anything moves.

CONFLICTS: M-1 (same walk, same `st` load), m-8 + m-58 (`_eff_rest_ct` replaces the est mirror at :5063-5070 — the exact lines the discount multiplies), M-31 (`_macro_probe_cap_usd()` charge placed 'BEFORE the explore clamp', the same slot), m-38 (`_probe_clamp_ct` consumer (c) is the same mirror), m-54 (:5099), n-44, n-12 and m-22 (both write `st`), OP-ADD-1a (verdict source), OP-ADD-1c (both add plan fields from this block).
- **OP-ADD-1c — Per-row est telemetry (DESIGN; absent from all 147) — LAND THIS FIRST** — FIX. Split by where the row is observable, so the plan row does not have to carry the whole footprint.
(1) ADMITTED ROWS -> the per-market file. In the walk, stamp the footprint row itself: `_m9["est_usd"] = round(_est9, 2)` (and `_m9["est_disc"] = _disc9` once 1b lands, `_m9["preweed"]` once 1a lands). `_market_telemetry_row` then emits those into `quotes-YYYYMMDD.jsonl`, which is ALREADY one line per market per cycle (:5386-5392) — zero plan-row growth, full per-ticker resolution, and it lands next to `_gates`, the dict that says WHY the row was refused. That adjacency is the whole point: `est_usd` x `gate_reason` per row IS the waste decomposition the review says cannot currently be computed.
(2) REFUSED ROWS -> the plan row, because `_saidno9` tickers never reach the quote loop. `plan["budget_refused"] = [[t, round(est,2), reason] ...][:BUDGET_REFUSED_LOG_MAX]` with `KALSHI_BUDGET_REFUSED_LOG_MAX` default 40 — the same cap and the same justification as `family_dropped_tickers` at :5099 — `reason` in {budget_full, family_full}. Plus ALWAYS-emitted scalars `select_budget_refused_usd`, `select_budget_refused_n` (the TRUE count, never the capped length — that is m-54's lesson applied), `select_budget_admitted_n`, `select_budget_est_p50`, `select_budget_est_p90`.
(3) SURVIVE MKT_TELEMETRY=0. Per m-37 the quotes-row write sits inside `if MKT_TELEMETRY:` (:5384). If that flag is 0 live, part (1) is dark — hence the plan scalars in (2) must stand alone. m-37's hoist and this item should be co-designed.

WHY. `select_budget_used` is the only figure the walk logs; per-ticker `_est9` is recorded nowhere. Without it, every EV claim for 1a and 1b stays HYPOTHESIS, and arming a capital-allocation change with no measurement is exactly what the norm forbids. This is why 1c lands FIRST, not last.

STANDING DETECTOR. `select_budget_est_sum_check` = |sum(per-row est charged) - `select_budget_used`|, WARNING above $0.01. That is the CLASS detector for 'a new charge path was added without telemetry' — which is precisely how the current gap arose (M-31 and m-8/m-38 are all about to add or move charge paths in this block). It would fire on the next such omission automatically.

TEST (failing-before). T1: every ticker in `_keepset9` carries `est_usd`, and the sum over admitted non-held, non-incumbent-only rows EQUALS `plan["select_budget_used"]` exactly. Fails before (`est_usd` absent). T2: `_saidno9` tickers appear in `budget_refused` with the correct reason, capped at 40 while `select_budget_refused_n` reports the true count. T3: held / incumbent-only / exit-only rows are absent from both structures (they are never charged). T4 row-size guard: with a 400-market footprint the plan row's added bytes stay bounded by the cap. T5 SWALLOW GUARD: `_SILENT["select_budget_fail"] == 0` on the happy path — mandatory for any edit inside this try-block.

EFFORT: S-M.

RISK: concerning: FALSE, with one sharp edge. (1) It edits the live quoter INSIDE `try: ... except Exception: _SILENT["select_budget_fail"] += 1` (:5016 / :5100). A mistake is SWALLOWED and silently kills the entire select-budget walk — the footprint goes unfiltered and `cap_desired` becomes the only capital bound. That is the nastiest failure mode in this whole region and it is why T5 is not optional. (2) `quotes-*.jsonl` grows by three fields x ~40 rows/cycle; the quoter's writes fail quietly into `_SILENT` and the VPS disk is shared (n-44 raises the same concern about caprank depth). (3) Mutating `_m9` in place means the footprint dict now carries a walk-derived field — anything that rebuilds footprint rows after the walk (M-30's `footprint = consumed`, M-10's `retain_standing_rows`) must preserve it or `est_usd` silently vanishes for retained rows, which would make the sum check fire spuriously and teach the operator to ignore it.

DARK: no — pure observation; it cannot alter `desired` or `_keepset9`. But the ORDER is binding: 1c before 1a before 1b. Landing 1b without 1c is an unmeasured capital-allocation change, which the norm forbids and which the P2 window makes worse.

CONFLICTS: m-54 (same `:5099` region, same cap-vs-true-count lesson — these two should be ONE commit), n-44, m-47 (`plan['knobs']`), M-1 / m-8 / m-58 / M-31 / m-38 (same walk), n-38 and n-4 (both restructuring `_market_telemetry_row`, the row 1c adds fields to), m-37 (decides whether part (1) is reachable at all), NEW-5 (kalshi_plans_hourly must learn the new keys).

### Items where the designer recommends NO code change (RECOMMENDATION ONLY — operator rules; nothing applied)

- **n-1** (NOTE) F17 sub-hour benefit half-eaten by the SETTLE_UNWIND_MIN floor — 58-min windows  — The code is correct as written; the floor exists to preserve the C8 maker-first/taker-second ordering and removing or scaling it would weaken the guaranteed-flat backstop on short-window markets for at most ~18 minutes of extra entry coverage per 58-min program. The defect is that the F17 headline overstates the benefit, which is fixed by restating the number, not by changing the clock. If the operator later wants that coverage, the honest sequence is: measure from the coverage ledger how much reward those minutes actually carry, then decide about the taker window on its own evidence and its own commit.
- **n-53** (NOTE) bound_creates can drop unwind creates when cancels alone consume the 400-op writ — No separate commit: same root cause, same line, same fix as n-15. Listing it as its own change would violate one-fix-per-commit and produce a second, divergent floor.
- **m-14** (MINOR) PRESENCE_GATE (live) and other armed selection flags are in neither the hot-watc — No separate commit: this is the class m-6's derived-knob-map fix closes wholesale. A hand-added entry per flag would re-create the very list that fell behind.
- **n-20** (NOTE) day_baseline_reset marker survives a UTC day roll and fires a redundant same-day — No separate commit: identical root cause and identical one-token fix as m-19. Two commits on the same expression would collide on rebase and split one audit trail in half.
- **n-16** (NOTE) flatten_to_zero has zero callers — dead taker path with a divergent crossed-coun — Do NOT delete the function and do NOT 'fix' its crossed-counter convention. Rule 5 and RULE NINE make removal an operator decision, and editing a dead function's semantics is unverifiable by construction — no test can fail before, so the change would ship on reasoning alone into code a future session may resurrect from git history. Documenting the trap and pinning the zero-caller invariant delivers the item's actual value (the item itself says 'flagged only') without inventing work on a live money path.
- **n-50** (NOTE) Frozen one-shot artifacts, not standing tools; live path never imports them; tim — The timezone handling ITSELF needs no change — it is verified correct on the current inputs (offset-aware fromisoformat().timestamp() against epoch candle ts), and the guard above is hardening against a future input, not a correction. The 'frozen one-shot artifact, not a standing tool' status is a documentation/canon label, not a defect: do NOT promote these scripts into the live path, do not import them from the quoter, and do not add scheduling, retry or service machinery to make them 'production'. The correct treatment is the provenance banner plus a STATUS header in each of the six files.
- **m-50** (MINOR) 'IDENTITY CHECK closes' is tautological — it can never fail — Not a separate defect: it is the visible symptom of M-24's residual definition. Designing an independent 'make the identity check real' change would either duplicate M-24 or bolt a second check onto a number that cannot fail — do the M-24 fix and this closes with it.

====================================================================================================
## S6. EVERY ITEM, ITS FIX, AND WHY (all 147, original ids and order preserved)

### MAJOR findings (triple-blind verified)

**M-1 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1320 · triple-blind **DOWNGRADE****

- *Defect:* F3 ties the floor gate to _D3_FIRST_SEEN restore order: rung-0 deadlock after restart with SELECT_BUDGET=0
- *Fix:* Restore the ramp first-seen map ONCE per cycle, unconditionally, before anything reads a rung. In run_once, immediately after `st = load_state()` (:4002), add `if D3_RAMP: _d3_first_seen_ensure(st)`. `_d3_first_seen_ensure` (:560-578) is already idempotent (`if _D3_FIRST_SEEN is None`), so the existing call at :5019 inside the SELECT_BUDGET block and the quote-loop belt at :5322-5334 both remain in place and become no-ops — no deletions, no signature change. Do NOT touch `_d3_est_ct`'s unknown->rung-0 rule (:625-627): unknown must stay conservative; the defect is that 'unknown' was reachable for KNOWN tickers, not the rule itself.
- *Why:* `_expected_credit_usd` scales the expectation by `_d3_est_ct/join_ct` (:1321-1331) and `_d3_est_ct` reads a module global whose only restore sites are gated behind SELECT_BUDGET (:5015) or run AFTER `desired_quotes` returns a non-empty book (:5322) — so with SELECT_BUDGET=0 a restart evaluates every established market at the 5 ct rung, the floor gate returns [], the belt never runs, and the under-admission is self-sustaining book-wide.
- *Standing detector:* plan['d3_first_seen_n'] = len(_D3_FIRST_SEEN or {}) emitted every cycle when D3_RAMP (added to _ALWAYS_EMIT_COUNTERS), plus a WARNING whenever `D3_RAMP and not _D3_FIRST_SEEN and st.get('d3_first_seen')` — 'ramp map EMPTY while state holds N entries: every market is being evaluated at rung 0'. That single line catches any future re-introduction of this ordering bug regardless of which flag combination causes it.
- *Test:* test_d3_ramp.py::test_ramp_map_restored_before_first_gate — state file containing `d3_first_seen = {T: now - 3*D3_RUNG_S}` (rung 3 = 50 ct), SELECT_BUDGET=0, D3_RAMP=1, PRESENCE_GATE=1, `q._D3_FIRST_SEEN = None`; monkeypatch `q._d3_est_ct` with a recording wrapper; run one cycle and assert the FIRST recorded call returned 50, not 5. Fails before: the first call returns 5 because the map is still None when the gate runs.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — no-op under the live flag set (SELECT_BUDGET=1), correctness-only otherwise, and it can never make the gate more permissive than the persisted truth.
- *Risk:* With SELECT_BUDGET=1 — today's live setting — `_d3_first_seen_ensure` already runs at :5019 before the quote loop at :5287, so this is a provable live no-op and its correctness value is entirely latent. The money-path change only materializes if SELECT_BUDGET is turned off, and there it ADMITS MORE markets (correctly): more resting capital, more fill risk, and a step change in quoted-market count relative to the broken state — so if the operator ever flips SELECT_BUDGET=0 they must expect that jump and not read it as a regression. Adding a call before select_footprint means a corrupt state file is now touched slightly earlier in the cycle; `_d3_first_seen_ensure`'s own try/except already degrades to {} (identical to today's behaviour).
- *Collides with:* m-8, m-58, n-2

**M-2 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2558 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Share model reference = best bid, not the venue's Target/5 cumulative-walk reference (LIP formula, 08-05)
- *Fix:* SHARED ROOT FIX #1 — replace the model's qualifying walk with the venue LIP walk. Closes M-2, M-5, M-7, M-17, M-18.

(a) NEW pure helpers in maker_kalshi_quoter.py, placed immediately above `_qualifying_score` (HEAD 233ee86, ~:2536):
    LIP_REF = _envi("KALSHI_LIP_REF", 0)          # 0 = legacy best-bid anchor, byte-identical
    LIP_REF_FRAC = _envf("KALSHI_LIP_REF_FRAC", 5.0)   # venue: Target/5 cumulative walk
    def _lip_mult(price, ref, df, lip_ref):
        n = round((ref - price) / TICK)
        return df ** (max(0, n) if lip_ref else n)     # LIP: 1.0x at OR BETTER than reference
    def _lip_walk(bids_desc, target, df, lip_ref):
        '''-> (ref, lowest_scored, df_total, cum_ct, qualifies).
        LIP mode: ref = FIRST level (walking down from best bid) where CUMULATIVE resting size
        >= target/LIP_REF_FRAC; df_total = sum over ALL resting levels of size*_lip_mult(); no
        per-order cutoff at the cumulative-Target boundary. Legacy mode: ref = bids[0][0] and
        the walk BREAKS at cum >= target (byte-identical to today and to
        kalshi_market_scorecard.qualifying_share). Qualification in BOTH modes = total side
        depth >= target (the legacy break is a short-circuit of the same predicate).'''

(b) `_qualifying_score` gains keyword-only `lip_ref=None` (None -> module LIP_REF) and delegates its walk to `_lip_walk`, its distance weight to `_lip_mult`, and its inside-the-scored-set test to `lip_ref or our_price >= lowest_scored - 1e-9` (LIP has no cutoff, so a deep order still scores df^n).

(c) `_qualifying_breakdown` (~:2718) is reduced to a thin wrapper over `_lip_walk` — the two duplicated walks become ONE, which permanently kills the drift class test_market_telemetry.py::T1 exists to police.

(d) `_market_telemetry_row` (~:2764) scores our order with `_lip_mult` instead of the inline `df ** round((ref-our_px)/TICK)`.

(e) `kalshi_market_scorecard.qualifying_share` gets the same keyword `lip_ref=False` and a mirrored walk, so the byte-equivalence pin (test_capture_gate.py:236) is kept and extended to BOTH modes rather than loosened.

CRITICAL SEMANTIC BOUNDARY: row key `y_ref`/`n_ref` MUST keep meaning BEST BID. kalshi_market_scores.update() feeds `ref_yes` into a price-SWING EWMA (kalshi_market_scores.py:128-140) and study/phase3_velocity.py measures ticks/minute from it; the LIP reference hops between price LEVELS when depth changes, not when price moves, so re-pointing y_ref would inject depth noise into the swing penalty and into every historical velocity series. Emit the venue reference as NEW keys `y_ref_lip`/`n_ref_lip` plus `y_ref_gap_ticks`/`n_ref_gap_ticks`.
- *Why:* The venue-published LIP formula sets the per-side Reference Price at the first level where cumulative resting size >= Target/5 and pays 1.0x to everything at or better than it, and it scores every resting order on a qualifying side; the model anchors at best bid and stops accumulating at cumulative Target. Both errors are one-way: every rival level's model weight is <= its venue weight and the model's denominator is truncated, so modeled share >= venue share ALWAYS — the presence floor, the capture gate, the NETEV model fallback and the SCORES ranking all over-admit and over-rank exactly the thin-top / deep-tail books, and the model is decoy-manipulable by a 1ct order at a high price.
- *Standing detector:* Three layers. (1) SHADOW ROW, on from the moment of deploy regardless of flag state: every quotes-*.jsonl row gains `capture_usd_day_lip` computed alongside `capture_usd_day`, plus `y_ref_lip`/`n_ref_lip`/`*_ref_gap_ticks`. This is the pre-arm measurement the operator needs and it is also the permanent recurrence alarm — any future divergence between the two bases shows up as a ratio series, not as a silent bias. (2) PER-CYCLE COUNTERS in qstats/plan: `lip_ref_deeper` (sides whose venue reference is below best bid = the decoy-exposed population), `lip_capture_ratio_min` and `lip_capture_ratio_p50` (model/venue). A p50 ratio drifting back above ~1.2 after the fix means a new anchor bug. (3) CLASS DETECTOR — the one that catches ANY future scoring-model defect, not just this one: extend kalshi_credit_feedback.py to carry a per-program `modeled_period_credit` vs `credit_history` actual column and alarm when the median ratio leaves a band. credit_history is the reward truth (canon 08-01); rewards_residual / rewards_alloc_by_series must not be used as the basis.
- *Test:* New test_lip_reference.py, all failing on current code:
  T1 DECOY BOOK: bids=[(0.55,10.0),(0.50,1200.0)], target=1000, df=0.5, our 100ct at 0.55. Legacy: ref=0.55, total=10+0.5^5*1200=47.5, share=2.105 (a share ABOVE 1.0 — an absurdity the current model can produce). LIP: target/5=200 -> ref=0.50, total=10*1.0+1200*1.0=1210, share=0.0826. assert legacy/lip > 20.
  T2 TRUNCATION (M-18): bids=[(0.50,1000.0),(0.49,5000.0)], target=1000, our 50ct at 0.50. Legacy total=1000 (walk breaks at level 1) -> share 0.05. LIP total=1000+5000*0.5=3500 -> share 0.0143. assert lip_share == pytest.approx(50/3500).
  T3 QUALIFICATION UNCHANGED: a side whose total depth < target returns (0.0, False) in both modes; a side that meets it returns qualifies=True in both.
  T4 FLAG-OFF BYTE-EQUIVALENCE: with LIP_REF=0 the parametrized scorecard pin (test_capture_gate.py:236) still passes verbatim, and _qualifying_breakdown's tuple is unchanged on the T1 test book.
  T5 THIN-TOP DIRECTION: property test over random books — lip_share <= legacy_share for every book (the one-way direction claim is the whole safety argument).
- *Effort:* L · *Dark:* YES, mandatory — KALSHI_LIP_REF=0 default. The shadow column ships ON (telemetry-only, cannot alter `desired`) so the operator gets a measured before/after distribution across the whole footprint BEFORE any gate sees the new basis; arming is a separate operator ruling that must move MIN_CREDIT_USD and NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT in the same deploy. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* THIS IS THE MOST DANGEROUS FIX IN THE SET. (1) Arming it strictly LOWERS modeled capture, by 2x on ordinary books and 20x+ on thin-top books. With PRESENCE_GATE=1 and MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 live, a large fraction of the current footprint can drop below the floor the cycle the flag flips — the bot could go from its present presence to near-zero. That is the 'benching presence' direction canon explicitly forbids, arrived at by a CORRECT model, so the right response is re-fitting the floor, not reverting the model. (2) It invalidates existing calibration: NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT=3.0 was fitted against this exact 2-6x over-prediction, so keeping 3.0 after the fix double-discounts every unproven family; CAPRANK_CALIB and the presence table are fitted on the same biased basis. (3) It changes a measured baseline mid-experiment — every quotes-*.jsonl row before and after the flip is on a different basis, and B8 must be told which side of the flip each row is on (the `capture_usd_day_lip` shadow column is what makes that recoverable). (4) The Target/5 walk's exact tie-break (>= vs >) and whether the venue walks per price LEVEL or per ORDER is my reading of the published text, not a receipt — if the venue walks orders rather than aggregated levels the reference can differ by a tick on books with many small orders at one price. (5) Cross-sectional RANK changes, not just levels: thin-boundary books lose more than thick-boundary books, so SCORE_RANK ordering and the select-budget walk reorder on the flip.
- *Collides with:* m-2, M-6, m-39, n-38, n-4, m-20, m-11

**M-3 — kalshi_live/w0b_payout_timing.py:92 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* 0b tags BEFORE_CLOSE vs the event's LATEST close — multi-market events can mint a false timing canon
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/w0b_payout_timing.py — extract the tagging into a pure function `classify(ts, close_min, close_max, nmk)` -> (tag, delta_h, basis) and call it from main() (main() currently inlines lines 99-103). Rules: (a) nmk<=1 -> basis='single', d = ts-close_max, tags BEFORE_CLOSE / CLOSE_WINDOW(<=48h) / LATE as today; (b) nmk>1 and ts < close_min -> basis='multi-min', tag BEFORE_CLOSE with d = ts-close_min (paid before EVERY market of the event closed — still valid evidence); (c) nmk>1 and close_min <= ts < close_max -> tag AMBIGUOUS_MULTI, basis='straddle', EXCLUDED from the discriminator (this is the false-canon case); (d) nmk>1 and ts >= close_max -> d = ts-close_max, CLOSE_WINDOW/LATE. `closes[0]` (line 85) is already captured and currently discarded at line 99 — bind it. Per-row print gains close_min, close_max, nmk and the basis string. Summary (line 110) prints a DISCRIMINATOR DENOMINATOR line: 'discriminating credits: X of N (single a, multi-min b) | AMBIGUOUS_MULTI excluded: c | UNKNOWN u'. The BEFORE_CLOSE section (line 113-116) lists only discriminating rows and its header loses the bare claim, becoming 'payment not gated on close — discriminating cases only (basis printed per row)'. Optional upgrade inside case (c): if the estimates recorder's kalshi_program_map.json resolves exactly ONE market of that event to a LIP program, re-classify against THAT market's close_time with basis='program-map'; any failure leaves the row AMBIGUOUS. Docstring lines 17-23 rewritten to state the per-EVENT attribution limit explicitly.
- *Why:* Credits are event-keyed (line 40/76-78) but the comparison close is the event's LATEST market close (line 85 stores closes[-1], line 99 binds close_max), so a legitimate close+1 payment for an early-closing sibling strike prints as BEFORE_CLOSE and mints a false 'venue pays before close' canon that FIX-H cash-lock and the D-A est->credit checkpoint interpretation rest on.
- *Standing detector:* The printed DISCRIMINATOR DENOMINATOR line on every run, plus a hard banner when the discriminating set is empty: '!! 0 DISCRIMINATING CREDITS — this run proves NOTHING about payout timing'. An evidence tool whose negative case is indistinguishable from silence is the class; every run now states its own denominator and its per-row basis.
- *Test:* New kalshi_live/test_w0b_timing.py over the pure `classify`: (1) multi-market event, close_min=08-02, close_max=08-20, credit 08-03 -> tag == 'AMBIGUOUS_MULTI' (FAILS BEFORE: current code returns BEFORE_CLOSE at -408h); (2) single-market event, credit 6h before close -> BEFORE_CLOSE; (3) multi-market, credit before close_min -> BEFORE_CLOSE basis='multi-min'; (4) a summary-counter test asserting AMBIGUOUS rows are excluded from the BEFORE_CLOSE tally and counted in the denominator line.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — read-only evidence tool, not on the live trading path; nothing it prints changes bot behavior directly. (It changes what the OPERATOR believes, which is why the denominator banner is mandatory rather than optional.) · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Re-labels evidence that has already been read into the lane's timing reasoning. The realistic outcome is that the discriminating set collapses toward zero — i.e. we lose the 'pays before close' evidence entirely and FIX-H's cash-lock assumption becomes UNPROVEN rather than supported. That is correct but it removes a prop that current live config (FARCLOSE_PAYING_EXCEPTION=1) leans on. Re-running costs ~35-60 throttled venue reads and must happen BEFORE the 08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS checkpoint or the checkpoint gets interpreted against the old (wrong) tags. The program-map upgrade adds a second data dependency (recorder output) that can itself be stale — hence it is optional and self-labels its basis.
- *Collides with:* n-3

**M-4 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:705 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Macro ladder all-or-nothing breaks at placement: post-only reject of one rung rests a partial, unqualifiable ladder
- *Fix:* Three parts, all inside the (currently dark) D-C subsystem. (1) BUILD-TIME EDGE GUARD in `_macro_probe_quotes` (:681-724): for each side, read the OPPOSITE side's best from the levels already passed in (`other_best = max(p for p,_ in nl)` for the yes side, and vice versa). Our side's implied fair ceiling is `1.0 - other_best`. New knob `MACRO_PROBE_MIN_EDGE = _envf('KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_MIN_EDGE', 0.10)`; if `MACRO_PROBE_TOP > (1.0 - other_best) - MACRO_PROBE_MIN_EDGE` (or the opposite side has no bids at all and the market is otherwise decided), refuse the WHOLE market (`return []`) and count `macro_probe_no_edge`. This is deliberately a REFUSAL, not a price clamp: clamping the top downward would make the ladder cheaper, hence MORE likely to pass side_cap and rest, on exactly the decided book where the 'any fill is far below fair' premise fails. It also removes the crossing rung by construction, so post-only can no longer reject one leg of the ladder for price reasons. (2) BEST-EFFORT PLACEMENT ATOMICITY: after the create loop (:5830-5891), a bounded reconciliation block — for each (ticker, side) among `creates` with `reason == 'macro_probe'` (no new dict key needed; the create record already carries ticker/side/reason), compare rungs submitted vs rungs present in `created_ok`; if fewer rested, `client.cancel_order` the group's created ids, count `macro_probe_partial_pulled` (and `macro_probe_partial_pull_failed` when a cancel raises) and PRINT the ticker/side. A partial ladder can never reach Target Size, so under LIP it cannot earn a cent while carrying full fill risk — pulling it is the builder's own 'rest NOTHING' contract enforced where partiality is actually decidable. Wrap the whole block so a fault counts and never raises. (3) Leave the existing build-time all-or-nothing checks untouched.
- *Why:* Affordability and qualification are decided once, all-or-nothing, at quote-BUILD time, but each rung is placed as an independent post_only order the venue can reject alone (create path :5857-5859; the client raises on a GTC order that fails to rest, maker_kalshi_client.py:232-238, caught at :5871) — so a designated market that has ridden into decided territory (designation exempts the late-life and far-close drops, :2041-2052) rests ~60% of Target: depth that mathematically cannot qualify, i.e. pure fill risk with zero reward.
- *Standing detector:* macro_probe_no_edge, macro_probe_partial_pulled and macro_probe_partial_pull_failed emitted in the plan row (via n-5's emission block), plus a WARNING naming ticker+side on every partial pull. macro_probe_partial_pull_failed > 0 is the hard alarm: it means unqualifiable depth is resting right now. The standing invariant is that resting macro depth per side is either 0 or >= need.
- *Test:* test_dc_macro_probe.py — (1) `test_decided_book_rests_nothing`: designated ticker, NO book best 0.97 (yes fair ~0.03 vs TOP 0.03 -> no edge); assert zero macro_probe quotes and `stats['macro_probe_no_edge'] == 1`. Fails before: current code emits three yes rungs at 0.03/0.02/0.01, the top one crossing. (2) `test_partial_ladder_is_pulled`: MockClient whose create raises on the FIRST macro rung for the ticker and succeeds on the rest; run one cycle; assert the two rested siblings were cancelled and `plan['macro_probe_partial_pulled'] == 1`. Fails before: the siblings stay resting with ~60% of Target.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no NEW flag — the subsystem is already dark behind the empty MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS list, and the new refusal lives inside it. KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_MIN_EDGE=0 restores the pre-fix build semantics for an operator who wants them. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* GENUINELY CONCERNING for three reasons. (a) Part 2 touches the SHARED create/cancel path that every order in the system flows through — a mistake there is not scoped to macro probes; it must be strictly keyed on reason=='macro_probe' and fully wrapped. (b) Atomicity is BEST-EFFORT, not guaranteed: the compensating cancels spend from WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE (400) and can themselves fail, leaving the partial ladder resting anyway — hence the pull_failed counter; do not describe this as an atomic ladder. (c) MACRO_PROBE_MIN_EDGE=0.10 is a chosen constant, not a measured one: on a book whose fair is 0.15 it refuses a ladder that might have been fine, so this NARROWS the surface of an experiment the operator has already ruled on and will delay the receipts D-C exists to obtain. D-C is dark today (empty ticker list), so nothing changes live right now — but the semantics the operator arms tomorrow are not the semantics they ruled on, and that must be said out loud before arming.
- *Collides with:* n-5, n-7, n-6

**M-5 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2559 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Reward model anchors the Reference Price at the best bid, not the venue's Target/5 cumulative-depth level
- *Fix:* CLOSED BY M-2 (shared root fix #1). Same defect seen through the same call path: `ref = bids[0][0]` in `_qualifying_score` / `_qualifying_breakdown` / kalshi_market_scorecard.qualifying_share. No separate code change. The one contribution this item makes that M-2's design must honour explicitly, and does: M-5 is the item that names the THROTTLE-STEP inversion — `_throttle_quote` (~:2510) already reasons about 'the depth AT the best price already meets Target Size', which is a Target-boundary argument computed against the WRONG reference. Under the LIP walk a quote one tick behind a thin touch is often still at-or-better than the true reference and keeps full 1.0x credit, so after M-2 arms, `_throttle_quote`'s free-win branch (THROTTLE_SMART) is measuring the wrong thing and its A/B (n=612) is on the legacy basis. Do NOT change _throttle_quote in the M-2 commit; log `throttle_step_still_at_lip_ref` (a boolean per throttled quote) in the telemetry row so the A/B can be re-run on the corrected basis, and re-rule the throttle in a separate commit.
- *Why:* Identical mechanism to M-2 — rivals between the true (deeper) reference and the touch are venue-scored 1.0x but model-scored 0.5^n, so book_total is understated and our share overstated on every consumer: the $1.20 floor gate, SCORE_RANK, the D2 feedback basis, the net-EV model fallback, the sweeper, and every capture_usd_day row.
- *Standing detector:* Covered by M-2's shadow column and counters, plus the M-5-specific `throttle_step_still_at_lip_ref` field so the throttle's free-win claim is re-measurable after the flip.
- *Test:* Covered by M-2's T1/T2/T5. Add one M-5-specific pin: on the decoy book, a quote one tick behind the touch scores 1.0x under LIP (mult == 1.0) and 0.5x under legacy — the exact inversion the throttle economics rest on.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* NO separate flag — rides KALSHI_LIP_REF. The added telemetry field is observation-only.
- *Risk:* The risk is inherited from M-2. The M-5-specific residual: after M-2 arms, THROTTLE_SMART's 'stay at ref, shrink harder' branch and its measured 12%-zeroing result are calibrated on the legacy reference and may be systematically wrong in either direction. Leaving it untouched (my recommendation) means one known-stale sub-behaviour survives the fix; touching it in the same commit violates one-fix-per-commit and re-opens a money-path A/B with no fresh data.
- *Collides with:* M-2

**M-6 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3105 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* PRESENCE_GATE activate credit check structurally refuses EVERY flat activate — seed lane dead, not gated
- *Fix:* Make the activate-path credit gate ask about the book WE WOULD CREATE, not the book as it stands — and expose the second, independent kill of the same lane.

(a) `_qualifying_score` gains keyword-only `add_to_cum=0.0`: contracts we INTEND to add at `our_price` that the venue would count toward Target once resting. Qualification becomes `cum + add_to_cum >= target`; everything else is unchanged. Default 0.0 = today's behaviour exactly.
(b) `_prospective_capture` gains keyword-only `our_ct=None` ({'yes': ct, 'no': ct}) overriding the internal `_capped_join` sizing, and passes each side's ct as BOTH `our_size` and `add_to_cum`.
(c) `_expected_credit_usd` gains keyword-only `activate_ct=None` and threads it to (b).
(d) In `desired_quotes`'s void branch, MOVE the `add_y`/`add_n`/`cap` computation (~:3115-3117) and the `gate_activate_cost` check (~:3118-3121) ABOVE the PRESENCE_GATE block (~:3098-3110), then call `_expected_credit_usd(..., activate_ct={"yes": min(int(add_y), _acl), "no": min(int(add_n), _acl)})`. Counter-attribution changes: a market that is both unaffordable and sub-floor now counts as `gate_activate_cost` instead of `gate_activate_credit` — state that in the commit message, it is a telemetry-meaning change.
(e) THE SECOND KILL, which this fix must surface or it delivers nothing: `_d3_apply_ramp` (~:646) exempts only `unwind` and `macro_probe`, so with D3_RAMP=1 live an activate order is clamped to the rung size (5-10ct) and can never supply Target depth. So the gate must model the size that will actually rest (multiply through m-20's `_ramp_scale`), which under live flags still yields a refusal — but now with a NEW, honest counter `gate_activate_ramp_short` instead of a structurally-impossible credit test. That converts a silent structural kill into a measured, named refusal WITHOUT opening the lane.

What this fix deliberately does NOT do: exempt `activate` from the ramp. The macro-probe exemption is justified by deep-discount pricing (reserved dollars ARE the max loss); activate rests AT the touch, so a full fill is a real at-fair-value position of up to MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL ($150 default) per market. Whether the activate lane should live is an operator ruling with two knobs (ramp exemption + MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL), not an inference I should make inside a defect fix.
- *Why:* The activate floor calls `_expected_credit_usd` on the CURRENT book. `_qualifying_score` returns (0.0, False) whenever a side's raw depth < Target, and `void` on a FLAT market means exactly that by definition (own=0 => ext == raw). So pc is identically 0, expected credit is $0.00 < $1.20, and the branch returns [] on every flat void book: with PRESENCE_GATE=1 live the gate is a constant-refuse, and the lane the code still documents and counts (activate_markets, gate_activate_cost) has been silently unreachable since it armed. The gate was asking whether a book we have not yet supplied depth to can pay — a question whose answer is always no.
- *Standing detector:* (a) NEW plan fields `activate_evaluated`, `gate_activate_credit`, `gate_activate_cost`, `gate_activate_ramp_short` emitted together — a lane whose 'evaluated' count is non-zero while every other counter sums to the same number is a lane that is dead, and that is now visible in every plan row instead of requiring a code read. (b) The class detector, which is the real deliverable: a STRUCTURAL-DEADNESS assertion in the suite — for each named entry lane (join, activate, macro_probe), a test that constructs the most favourable possible input for that lane and asserts it produces orders. A lane that cannot pass its own best case is dead by construction, and this class (a gate that refuses 100% of its population for a structural reason nobody measured) is exactly what went undetected here since 08-02.
- *Test:* Failing-before, new test_activate_credit.py:
  T1 THE FIX PIN: $500/day program, target 1000, rivals resting 900ct on each side, best 0.05 so 100ct costs $5 (well inside MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL), PRESENCE_GATE=1, D3_RAMP=0. Today `desired_quotes` returns [] with `gate_activate_credit == 1`; after the fix it returns two 'activate' quotes. This is the pin the lane never had — test_presence_gate.py contains no activate/void case at all.
  T2 STILL REFUSES A GENUINELY SUB-FLOOR ACTIVATE: same book with usd_day=$1/day -> [] with gate_activate_credit == 1 (the floor still means something).
  T3 RAMP INTERACTION: T1's fixture with D3_RAMP=1 and rung-0 5ct -> refused with `gate_activate_ramp_short == 1`, NOT `gate_activate_credit`. This pins that the fix does not open the lane under live flags.
  T4 NO-OP: `_qualifying_score` / `_prospective_capture` / `_expected_credit_usd` with the new kwargs omitted are byte-identical on every existing fixture (protects the scorecard equivalence pin).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* YES — the behaviour change (counting our intended size toward Target) ships behind KALSHI_ACTIVATE_CREDIT_MODEL=0. With the flag off, only the new counters and the reordered cost check land, so the dead lane becomes measurable before it becomes live. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* CONCERNING. (1) If the operator later arms the lane (ramp exemption), this commits up to MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL per market AT THE TOUCH into books that are thin by definition — the single largest per-market at-fair-value exposure in the bot. MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL's live value is NOT in the env list I was given (code default 150.0); it must be read before any arming decision. (2) The reachable population is narrow — the upstream `_qualifiable` gate (~:2905-2910) already requires ext + MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL/price >= target on BOTH sides — so the fix may reveal that the lane is nearly empty anyway; that is an honest outcome, not a failure, but it means the effort may buy measurement rather than money. (3) Modelling our own not-yet-placed size into the qualification test is a genuine change to what `_qualifying_score` means; a future caller passing `add_to_cum` on a NON-activate path would model qualification we have not supplied — the parameter must stay used at exactly one call site and be named so that is obvious. (4) Counter re-attribution (d) changes the meaning of `gate_activate_cost` in historical comparisons. (5) Interacts hard with M-2: under the LIP walk our activate order at the touch is at-or-better than the reference and takes 1.0x, so the modeled share on a book we mostly supply is near 1.0 — the activate lane is the one place M-2 makes the model MORE generous, and the two must be evaluated together, not sequentially.
- *Collides with:* M-2, m-20, m-2

**M-8 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5311 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* L3 taint can mark a DESIGNATED macro ticker explore; explore clamp collapses the D-C ladder to 5ct dust
- *Fix:* Four sites, one class ('an operator DESIGNATION must survive every downstream clamp'). (1) Add `_is_designated(ticker)` = `bool(MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS) and ticker in MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS`, and migrate the four existing inline checks (:2004, :2041 _is_macro, :2146, :5361) to it — no semantic change, one grep-able definition. (2) THE MONEY FIX: explore probe clamp :5311-5317 changes its per-order predicate from `_o5.get('reason') != 'unwind'` to `_o5.get('reason') not in ('unwind', 'macro_probe')`, byte-mirroring _d3_apply_ramp's own carve-out at :646. After this the clamp cannot resize a Target-meeting ladder no matter HOW the row acquired explore. (3) Both L3 sibling re-clamps (:4094-4104 selection-time and :4578-4583 governor-time) gain `and not _is_designated(_r5.get('ticker',''))` in the taint predicate. (4) _cap_probe_slots :3265-3266 gains `and not _is_designated(r['ticker'])` so a designated row can never be evicted by the slot cap or by probe_close_unknown.
- *Why:* A designated macro row is admitted without `explore` (:2064, probe_only forced False at :2004-2008), so it passes the L3 `not explore` guard and gets tainted by any non-expired banned sibling of its series; the explore clamp then caps every non-unwind quote at EXPLORE_PROBE_CT=5 (15 ct/side), which cannot reach Target, so the ladder qualifies for nothing, rests as pure deep-discount fill risk, and the receipts D-C exists to produce never arrive — while macro_probe_markets still counts a success.
- *Standing detector:* plan['macro_probe_clamped'] — an assert-style counter incremented if ANY quote carrying reason='macro_probe' is ever resized by any clamp; it must stay 0 forever. plan['macro_designation_lost'] — the list of designated tickers present in `progs` but absent from `desired` at end of cycle, with the drop counter that killed them. Pre-fix every count == 5. Plus unit pins: _cap_probe_slots keeps a designated row whose close is unknown; both L3 clamps leave a designated row's 'explore' unset.
