**M-9 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3126 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Activate emitter clamps at INV_HARD_CT vs dollar-based gate — rests depth that cannot reach Target
- *Fix:* Two commits over ONE shared helper. Commit 1 (provable no-op): add `_activate_feasible(best_y, best_n, ext_y, ext_n, target, ticker, now_ts)` returning `(add_y, add_n, feasible, cost)` — the single definition of 'we can bridge this book to Target'. Per side the restable count is `min(dollar budget, _acl)` where `_acl = int(INV_HARD_CT) if INV_HARD_CT > 0 else 10**9`, further reduced by `int(_d3_est_ct(ticker, now_ts))` when D3_RAMP is on (side-effect-free, the same idiom _expected_credit_usd uses at :1321-1331); `feasible` is True only when BOTH sides reach Target at those counts AND the total cost fits MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL. Call it from the unqualifiable gate (:2905-2906, telemetry only in commit 1) and from the emitter (:3111-3131, counts unchanged), and emit `activate_under_target` / `activate_gap_ct` whenever the emitter is about to leave a side short. Commit 2 (dark, KALSHI_ACTIVATE_ATOMIC default 0): with the flag on, `_qualifiable` uses the helper's `feasible` and the emitter returns [] counting `gate_activate_infeasible` instead of resting a stub.
- *Why:* The gate judges bridgeability in DOLLARS ($150/max(best) => up to 3000ct at 5c) while the emitter can only rest INV_HARD_CT=80 — and 5-50ct under the live D3 ramp — so a passed gate rests depth that provably cannot reach Target; per LIP R3 the side never qualifies, the snapshot is excluded for everyone, and the stub earns a guaranteed $0 while carrying full adverse-fill risk. One helper makes the two limbs incapable of disagreeing again.
- *Standing detector:* `activate_under_target` (count) and `activate_gap_ct` (worst per-side shortfall) on EVERY plan row, armed or not — the number that must fall to 0 once the refusal is armed, and the measurement that justifies arming it. Pair with a W17 ledger bucket: activate markets whose side never reached Target and whose program credited $0, versus those a rival later bridged (that split is the whole arming decision).
- *Test:* New test_activate_atomic.py::test_gate_and_emitter_agree_on_bridgeability — void book target=1000, ext_y=100@0.05, ext_n=1000@0.05, flat, PRESENCE_GATE/NETEV/STANDDOWN neutralised, INV_HARD_CT=80, D3_RAMP=0. BEFORE: the market passes _qualifiable (addable=3000) and desired_quotes returns an 80ct activate leaving that side at 180 < 1000. AFTER commit 1: identical quotes, but stats['activate_under_target']==1 and activate_gap_ct==820 (fails before — the keys do not exist). AFTER commit 2 with KALSHI_ACTIVATE_ATOMIC=1: returns [] with gate_activate_infeasible==1. Mandatory companion pin, must pass in every state: a two-sided JOIN book (ext_y=ext_n=1000) still quotes normally with the flag on — proof the gate change did not leak onto the JOIN path.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes for the refusal arm (KALSHI_ACTIVATE_ATOMIC, default 0 => byte-identical); no for the shared-helper + telemetry arm, which must be provably identical in quotes emitted. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Honest and large. Armed, this retires most of the activate path: D3_RUNGS tops out at 50 and D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 holds unproven series at 10ct, so on a Target-1000 program activate can only be feasible when BOTH sides are already within ~10-50ct of Target. Refusal is NOT strictly dominant — an under-Target stub becomes qualifying depth if a RIVAL later fills the gap, and we would forfeit that option value; nobody has measured how often that happens, which is exactly why commit 2 is dark and gated on commit 1's measurement. Second specific hazard: making the unqualifiable gate ramp-aware touches a line the JOIN path also crosses; it must stay a no-op there (ext >= target on both sides => feasible regardless of _acl) or this fix silently benches the entire JOIN universe — that invariant needs its own assertion or the blast radius is the whole book, not the activate path.
- *Collides with:* m-10, m-4, m-60, M-31

**M-10 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5463 · triple-blind **DOWNGRADE****

- *Defect:* Strand-unwind loop cancels a live resting EXIT on any transient book-read failure (no retention, unlike quote loop)
- *Fix:* SHARED ROOT FIX for M-10 + M-11 + M-30 (one commit, one mechanism class: 'a book we could not read must never silently withdraw a ticker from `desired`, because diff_orders reads absence as cancel-everything'). (1) Extract the existing in-loop fetch-fail retention body (:5234-5258) into a pure module-level helper beside apply_drop_grace: `retain_standing_rows(rows, pos, exit_only, accumulating=True, inv_tolerance=INV_TOLERANCE)` -> verbatim [{side, price_dollars, count}] copies, reducing side tagged reason='unwind' when abs(pos)>=inv_tolerance, accumulating copies stripped when exit_only or accumulating=False, [] when nothing survives. Precedent for extract-don't-duplicate is `_cancel_each`'s own docstring ('the body must be identical at all three or the cover guarantee differs by path'). (2) Quote loop :5229 `except RuntimeError:` -> set `_budget_broke = True`, call the helper for THIS in-flight ticker and write desired[t] if non-empty, bump qstats['read_budget_retained'], then break. (3) The generic-Exception branch (:5231) calls the same helper (pinned byte-equivalent by test). (4) Append to `consumed` UNCONDITIONALLY (remove only the `if PIVOT_SELECT:` guard on the append) and change the collapse at :5437 to `if PIVOT_SELECT or _budget_broke: footprint = consumed` — so the never-read pool tail is absent from fp_tickers and drop-grace covers it even at PIVOT_SELECT=0. Byte-identical under the live PIVOT_SELECT=1. (5) Strand-unwind loop :5458-5463: BOTH handlers call the helper for `t` before break/continue (`plan['strand_read_budget']`, `plan['strand_fetch_retained']`). Flag split: the unwind-tagged half ships ON (a reducing order sized <= |pos| that is already resting cannot open a position and can only fill at a price we already accepted -> provably no-worse than cancelling the exit); the ACCUMULATING copies are gated by `KALSHI_RETAIN_ON_READFAIL` (default 0 = today).
- *Why:* public_get raises RuntimeError at READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE (:1855) and _get_book propagates it verbatim (:1890); the RuntimeError branch sits ABOVE the retention branch, so the in-flight market (already appended to `consumed` at :5219, hence grace-ineligible), the unvisited tail under PIVOT_SELECT=0, and every strand ticker whose book read fails all end the cycle absent from `desired` — and diff_orders (:3486-3488) then cancels ALL their standing orders, reason-tagged exits on held positions included.
- *Standing detector:* plan keys `read_budget_exhausted` (0/1 per cycle), `read_budget_retained`, `strand_read_budget`, `strand_fetch_retained`, plus a loud print naming how many pool rows were left unread when the budget breaks the loop. Today exhaustion is only inferable by noticing `reads == READ_BUDGET`. Alarm rule: read_budget_exhausted nonzero on consecutive cycles = the candidate pool no longer fits the read budget (the P2-widened ~3,300-row universe is the predicted trigger).
- *Test:* New kalshi_live/test_readfail_retention.py, on the test_live_hardening MockClient/_run harness: (a) counter-faithful public_get stub with budget=k, footprint of k+3 markets all with standing orders, held +20 on market #k -> assert the emitted cancel list contains NO order id belonging to market #k or to the unread tail, and that market #k's unwind order survives (pre-fix all are cancelled); (b) DROP_GRACE=0, held ticker outside the footprint whose strand _get_book raises -> assert its resting reducing order is not cancelled (pre-fix it is); (c) extraction-equivalence: retain_standing_rows() output equals the old inline block's output on the same inputs.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_RETAIN_ON_READFAIL (default 0) gates the accumulating half; the exit/unwind half ships on because it is provably no-worse than cancelling a live exit. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Retaining ACCUMULATING quotes on a book we could not read is adverse-selection exposure: if the book moved, we are stale and pickable, and today's cancel is genuinely the safer direction for that half — which is exactly why it is flagged and the exit half is not. Retained tickers consume cap_desired headroom and can evict a fresh market from the capital cap (the F4 unwind tag mitigates this only for held tickers). gated_out / quoted_markets / pivot_quoted / pivot_pool all shift on exhausting cycles, a telemetry discontinuity mid-experiment (collides with n-18). Under PIVOT_SELECT=0 plan['footprint'] becomes shorter on exhausting cycles. Python 3.13 whole-function scoping: `_budget_broke` must be initialised before the loop or the new branch raises UnboundLocalError, and no global may be rebound inside it.
- *Collides with:* M-11, M-30, n-18, n-54, m-17

**M-11 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5230 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Read-budget exhaustion mid-quote-loop cancels the in-flight market's whole standing book with no retention
- *Fix:* CLOSED BY THE M-10 DESIGN (same commit) — specifically parts (2) and (4). M-11 is the CONFIRMED, live-today half: under PIVOT_SELECT=1 the in-flight market #k was appended to `consumed` at :5219 BEFORE the fetch, so after `footprint = consumed` it IS in fp_tickers and apply_drop_grace refuses it ('we looked at it'), while the RuntimeError branch left no desired entry and no retention. The fix writes the retained copy for exactly that ticker before breaking, so its two resting quotes and its live unwind survive the exhausting cycle. Nothing additional to implement beyond M-10.
- *Why:* Under the live flag set this is the only ticker whose entire standing book — exits included — is torn down by a budget exhaustion, and it loses the queue position LIP actually pays for.
- *Standing detector:* See M-10: qstats['read_budget_retained'] names how many in-flight tickers were saved; a nonzero value with no corresponding cancel spike is the proof the guard is holding.
- *Test:* test_readfail_retention.py case (a) is the M-11 pin (in-flight market #k). Failing-before: pre-fix the cancel list contains market #k's ids.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* yes — same flag as M-10 for the accumulating half. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Inherits M-10's risks. The M-11-specific one: the in-flight market is the one market we KNOW we could not price this cycle, so retaining its accumulating side is the least-informed retention in the set — this is the strongest argument for keeping KALSHI_RETAIN_ON_READFAIL off initially and shipping only the exit-side retention.
- *Collides with:* M-10, M-30

**M-12 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:6572 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* STOP flatten pass 1 places fresh exit offsets over failed cancels — stacked reducing size can cross THROUGH flat
- *Fix:* Give STOP-flatten pass 1 the main cycle's C7 failed-cancel deferral. (1) New `_cancel_each_tracked(client, ids) -> (n_ok, ok_set)`; `_cancel_each` becomes a two-line wrapper returning only n_ok, so its other two call sites keep byte-identical semantics (its own docstring demands the body be identical at every site). (2) In _flatten_all replace `n += _cancel_each(client, cancels)` (:6572) with the tracked call; build oid -> (ticker, side) from `_std` (the deferred reducing rows — the only rows that can produce a fresh same-side create) and `failed_same_side = {(ticker, side)}` for every id not in ok_set. (3) Retry each failed cancel ONCE immediately before deferring (a 429 usually clears; bounded by the number of held tickers). (4) In the creates loop (:6577-6586) skip any create whose (ticker, side) is in failed_same_side, PRINT it loudly ('flatten: {t} — stale same-side reducing order still resting (cancel failed); fresh offset DEFERRED to avoid 2x|pos| reducing size') and count _SILENT['stopflat_defer'].
- *Why:* _cancel_each swallows failures and returns only a count (:6414-6427), so pass 1 cannot know which cancels failed and rests a fresh reducing offset on top of a stale same-side one — up to 2x|pos| of reducing size, whose full fill crosses THROUGH flat into a naked opposite position while the bot is halted, with the next corrective pass up to STOPFLAT_REPEAT_S=1800 s away. The main cycle has guarded exactly this since C7 (:5797-5843); pass 1 never received it, and pass 2's taker leg is already cancel-confirmed, so this brings pass 1 to the same standard.
- *Standing detector:* _SILENT['stopflat_cancel_fail'] (today _cancel_each swallows with NO counter at all) and _SILENT['stopflat_defer'], both surfaced in the next non-STOP cycle's silent_failures_total, plus the loud print — under STOP the journal is the only channel because run_once returns before append_plan (:3997). Plus a standing invariant check in pass 2's re-read: resting reducing size per ticker must be <= |pos|; violations bump stopflat_overshoot_seen.
- *Test:* Extend test_stop_diff_and_keep.py: MockClient whose cancel_order raises for the stale NO offset's id; STOP present; position +20 with a stale 20 ct NO offset at a now-stale price. Assert NO create is issued for that ticker/side and the deferral is counted/printed. Pre-fix a 20 ct NO create is issued, leaving 40 ct of reducing size against a 20 ct position (failing-before).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_STOPFLAT_DEFER, but I RECOMMEND default 1 (ON) rather than the usual dark-off, because the unfixed path can OPEN a naked opposite position while the bot is halted, which strictly dominates a stale exit. Rollback is `=0` plus restart. Stated as an operator decision, not assumed. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Honest and material: deferring means the position keeps a STALE-priced exit (possibly far from the touch, so possibly never filling) until the next flatten up to 1800 s later or until pass 2's taker escalation — and pass 2 requires TAKER_FLATTEN=1 and residual >= STOP_TAKER_MIN_CT, so with TAKER_FLATTEN=0 a halted bot can sit on a stale exit for 30 minutes where today it at least rests a fresh one (alongside the stale one, which is the defect). The single retry reduces but does not remove that. Touching _cancel_each touches the cancel-cover guarantee for two other call sites, so the wrapper must be provably equivalent. This is a change to the emergency-stop path, the one path that runs when everything else has already gone wrong.
- *Collides with:* n-54, n-21, n-16

**M-13 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:448 · triple-blind **DOWNGRADE****

- *Defect:* Hot path omits F5 own_orders — Stage B recomputes quotes with own-size double-count
- *Fix:* SHARED DESIGN — closes M-13, m-1, n-51 (one commit, one root cause).

In `Daemon.hot_event` (maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:445-459): hoist the standing read ABOVE the quote call and thread it into the model, so hot uses the same F5 basis as cold.

Current order is: build `c`, `ys,ns = mirror.rows()`, `q = M.desired_quotes(...)` (no own_orders), THEN `with self.std_lock: standing_t = list(self.ctx.standing.get(ticker) or [])`.

New order:
```python
c = self.ctx.by_ticker[ticker]
with self.std_lock:
    standing_t = list(self.ctx.standing.get(ticker) or [])
ys, ns = mirror.rows()
try:
    q = M.desired_quotes(c["m"], ys, ns, M.utcnow(), own=c["own"], inv=c["inv"],
                         event_delta=c["ev"], stats={}, cost=c["cost"],
                         own_orders=M._own_order_levels(standing_t))
```
and delete the later duplicate `std_lock` read (ops still built from the same `standing_t`).

`ctx.standing` rows are produced by `M._live_standing` in `HotContext.build` (daemon:181) and hot-maintained in `_exec_hot` with the same `{order_id, side, price_dollars, count}` shape, which is exactly what `M._own_order_levels` (quoter:3548-3559) consumes — no adapter needed, no signature change anywhere.

CLASS PART (not just this instance): the real defect class is "two call sites of the same scoring function drift apart silently." Add a module-level parity self-check in the daemon, run once at `main()` start and logged:
```python
HOT_MODEL_KWARGS = {"own", "inv", "event_delta", "stats", "cost", "own_orders"}
def _model_param_parity():
    sig = set(inspect.signature(M.desired_quotes).parameters) - {"m","yes_levels","no_levels","now"}
    return sorted(sig - HOT_MODEL_KWARGS)
```
logged as `{"ev":"hot_model_params","missing":[...]}`; when non-empty AND WS_HOT=1 also `print("WARNING hot path does not supply desired_quotes kwargs: ...")`. Any future kwarg the cold path starts passing shows up here instead of silently halving hot coverage again.
- *Why:* `own_orders` is consumed only by the admit/skip gates inside `desired_quotes` (presence via `_expected_credit_usd` quoter:2917-2919, netev model fallback :3021-3022, capture :3045-3047, activate credit :3105-3106), and F5's subtraction (`_qualifying_score` :2570-2577) removes our own resting size from the reward denominator. Omitting it makes the hot path compute ~half the share on books we occupy alone, so hot silently returns [] and does nothing on exactly the solo/thin books the thesis targets — the latency edge Stage B exists for is disabled where it matters most.
- *Standing detector:* Two: (1) the `hot_model_params` startup row + WARNING above (catches the CLASS: any future model input the cold path passes and hot does not). (2) A hot-coverage gauge: extend the existing `_log({"ev":"hot_reprice"...})` family with a per-cold-cycle rollup row `{"ev":"hot_outcomes","counts":{reason: n}}` accumulated from `hot_event`'s return values (a plain `collections.Counter` on the Daemon, snapshotted and cleared each cold cycle). A rise in `no_op` with zero `submitted` while books are moving is the exact signature of a gate-basis divergence, and today it is invisible.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, in test_ws_daemon.py next to `test_hot_reprice_executes`: replace `_patch_quotes` with a capturing stub —
```python
def test_hot_path_passes_f5_own_orders_kills_broken_variant(daemon, monkeypatch):
    seen = {}
    def cap(*a, **k):
        seen.update(k)
        return [{"side":"yes","price_dollars":0.37,"count":20}]
    monkeypatch.setattr(D.M, "desired_quotes", cap)
    assert daemon.hot_event("T", _mirror(), time.monotonic()) == "submitted"
    assert seen.get("own_orders") == D.M._own_order_levels(
        [ _std("o1","yes",0.36,20) ])   # {"yes":[(0.36,20.0)],"no":[]}
```
Today `seen["own_orders"]` is absent → KeyError/None → fails. Second test pins the CLASS: `assert D._model_param_parity() == []` (fails if someone adds a cold-path kwarg without threading it).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — the change is unreachable while KALSHI_WS_HOT=0 (the existing flag already is the dark switch; `hot_event` is called from `on_book` only `if WS_HOT` and re-gated in `_hot_precheck`). Adding a second flag would only add a way to arm Stage B with the known-wrong basis. The parity self-check is pure logging and safe live.
- *Risk:* This makes the hot path ADMIT MORE, not less. Today the F5-less denominator makes hot skip solo books; after the fix hot will actually reprice there, so arming WS_HOT=1 will produce strictly more cancel/create traffic than the current (dark) code would have — the write-token burn and queue-position churn that Stage B was budgeted for, but a real step up from zero. Second-order: `own=c["own"]` is still the ctx-build snapshot while `own_orders` now comes from the live `ctx.standing` dict that `_exec_hot` mutates, so the two inputs can disagree for one interval after a hot write (the count-subtraction basis vs the share-denominator basis). That residue is bounded by `hot_reprice_ops` only acting when standing is exactly one order per side matching the desired count, but it is a genuine internal inconsistency; making `own` also derive from `standing_t` would change emitted COUNTS and is deliberately out of scope for this commit. Live impact today: none — WS_HOT=0 and `_hot_precheck` returns "flag_off" before any of this code runs.
- *Collides with:* m-1, n-51, m-24, m-30

**M-14 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ws_feed.py:271 · triple-blind **DOWNGRADE****

- *Defect:* Venue error frame leaves mirrors CLEAN+FROZEN for the whole backoff sleep — Stage C vouches for a dead book
- *Fix:* In kalshi_ws_feed.py, make connection-trust teardown a SINGLE function that every end-of-session path must call, and call it BEFORE the backoff sleep rather than after.

Add to `Feed.__init__`: `self.session_ok = False`, `self.session_ends = {}`, `self.on_disconnect = on_disconnect` (new additive kwarg, default None — see m-25).

New method:
```python
def _end_session(self, reason):
    """The ONLY way a connection's trust ends. Idempotent. Every path that stops
    dispatching frames MUST come through here BEFORE it sleeps or breaks."""
    self.session_ok = False
    self._sub_seq = None
    self.confirmed_channels = set()
    for m in self.mirrors.values():
        m.dirty = True
    self.session_ends[reason] = self.session_ends.get(reason, 0) + 1
    if self.on_disconnect:
        try:
            self.on_disconnect(reason)
        except Exception:
            pass
```
Call sites:
- `_dispatch` error arm (:270-273): `self.error_frames += 1; print(...); self._end_session("error_frame"); return False`
- `_dispatch` seq-gap arm (:279-284): replace the inline `for m in self.mirrors.values(): m.dirty = True` + `self._sub_seq = None` with `self._end_session("seq_gap")` (keep `self.gap_count += 1` first), `return False`
- `run()` except arm (:348-354): replace the inline dirty loop with `self._end_session("exception")` before the backoff sleep
- `run()` finally (:341-344): replace the two clear lines with `self._end_session("close")`
- `run()`: set `self.session_ok = True` immediately after `await self._subscribe(ws)` succeeds.

In `Daemon.mirror_book` (daemon:332-341), add the session predicate as the first trust check after the None check:
```python
if not feed.session_ok:
    self._decl("session_down"); return None
```
and update the docstring's now-false claim at daemon:322-323 to describe `_end_session` as the single teardown path.

Also update `test_ws_book_cold._FakeFeed` (adds `self.session_ok = True`) in the same commit — it is our test double, not an external interface.
- *Why:* The error-frame arm returned False without dirtying anything, and `run()` sleeps the escalating 2-60s backoff BEFORE `break`, so the `finally` that clears `confirmed_channels` runs only after the sleep. For that whole window the Feed advertised a live orderbook_delta ack over mirrors frozen at their pre-error state, and Stage C's decline predicate — which is the entire safety of quoting off the mirror — passed every check and vouched for a dead book. Routing all four teardown paths through one function makes "connection ended ⇒ nothing is trusted" an invariant instead of a per-arm habit.
- *Standing detector:* (1) `feed.session_ends` (counts by reason: error_frame / seq_gap / exception / close) surfaced in the daemon's existing `cold_cycle` log row as `feed_session_ends`, alongside the never-yet-surfaced `feed.error_frames` and `feed.gap_count`. (2) A decline-reason histogram in `mirror_book`: `self._decl` increments a Daemon dict (`no_feed`, `no_ack`, `session_down`, `dirty`, `torn`) emitted in the same row. Today the cold row reports book_ws vs book_rest but never WHY REST was chosen — after this, a Stage C hit-rate collapse names its own cause in one field instead of needing a stopwatch.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, test_ws_daemon.py (extends the existing `test_feed_error_frame_forces_reconnect`):
```python
def test_error_frame_dirties_every_mirror_and_drops_acks_kills_broken_variant():
    feed = F.Feed(["A", "B"], want_fills=True)
    for t in ("A", "B"):
        feed.mirrors[t].apply_snapshot({"yes_dollars_fp": [["0.5000","10"]],
                                        "no_dollars_fp": [["0.4800","10"]]}, seq=1)
    feed.confirmed_channels = {"orderbook_delta", "fill"}
    feed.session_ok = True
    assert feed._dispatch(json.dumps({"type":"error","msg":{"code":6}})) is False
    assert all(m.dirty for m in feed.mirrors.values())
    assert feed.confirmed_channels == set() and not feed.fills_confirmed
    assert not feed.session_ok
```
plus a Stage C level test in test_ws_book_cold.py: seed a clean mirror, dispatch the error frame, `assert _daemon(feed).mirror_book("A") is None`. Both fail today (mirrors clean, ack intact, mirror_book returns a book).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — declining to vouch is strictly safer than vouching, and the fallback path (REST) is the pre-Stage-C behavior that is proven in production. A flag here would mean shipping a switch whose OFF position is the defect. The telemetry is additive and safe live.
- *Risk:* Every error frame now costs the WHOLE footprint its mirrors, so the next cold cycle reads ~40 books over REST (measured 320ms p50 each = ~12.5s of serialized network) instead of from memory, until snapshots re-seed. We do not currently know the live error-frame rate — `error_frames` has never been surfaced anywhere — so the cost is unmeasured going in. If the venue emits error frames routinely, this converts Stage C from mostly-WS to substantially-REST and slows every cold cycle in that window, which on a 5s-debounced event-driven cadence is a real throughput hit. It also compounds with m-28 (any error frame tears down the connection at all). The direction is unambiguously safer — REST-slow is what we did before Stage C, and quoting off a frozen book is the failure this closes — but it is an unmeasured change to a live, measured Stage C baseline. Mitigation and honest sequencing: ship the telemetry half (error_frames / session_ends / decline reasons in the cold row) FIRST, read one day, then ship the dirtying — or ship both together accepting one day of possibly-degraded cycle latency, which is still correct-by-construction.
- *Collides with:* m-24, m-25, m-26, m-27, m-28, n-23, n-25

**M-15 — kalshi_live/kalshi_fill_costs.py:124 · triple-blind **DOWNGRADE****

- *Defect:* Settled-market replay-fault fallback prices all settled markets at revenue-only (cost 0), not ABSENT; fault is silent
- *Fix:* PER-TICKER REPLAY ISOLATION + POPULATION-LOSS GUARD in kalshi_fill_costs.build() (:102-141) and its two callers. (1) Replace the single whole-tape `events,_pos = replay_fills(fills)` (:110) with a per-ticker replay: group fills by `f.get('ticker') or f.get('market_ticker')` (the SAME key expression replay_fills uses at kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:227), then `for tkr,grp in groups.items(): try: events,_=replay_fills(grp) except Exception: BAD_REPLAY_TICKERS.append(tkr); SETTLED_REPLAY_FAILS[0]+=1; continue` and accumulate cash_by/fee_by from that group only. This is bit-identical to the current output when nothing raises: replay_fills nets per ticker (positions dict keyed by ticker, :225-233) and the (created_time, fill_id) sort preserves intra-ticker order, so cash per fill is unchanged. (2) In the settlements loop (:124) add `if t in BAD_REPLAY_TICKERS: continue` — a ticker whose own tape is unreadable stays ABSENT, which is exactly the contract the module already documents at :31-33. (3) Keep the outer try as a catastrophe guard but set `replay_ok=False` and move the WHOLE `for s in settlements:` loop under `if replay_ok:` so no fault path can ever fall through and price 129 settled markets at revenue-only. (4) Add module state `LAST_BUILD = {'n_live':0,'n_settled':0,'replay_bad_tickers':[]}` set at the end of build() (same module-counter idiom as MISSING_FIELD_ROWS, so build() stays pure-return and the signature is untouched). (5) main() (:176-185): print a LOUD warning naming the bad tickers when SETTLED_REPLAY_FAILS[0] or LAST_BUILD['replay_bad_tickers'] is non-empty, and return 2 instead of 0. (6) Write `n_live`/`n_settled` as top-level keys next to `schema`/`markets` in both writers (kalshi_fill_costs.main :173-175 and quoter _refresh_fill_costs :2700-2703) — additive, since kalshi_capital_rank.load_fill_costs (:37-48) reads only `schema` and `markets`. (7) In the quoter's _refresh_fill_costs (2681-2705): after build(), REFUSE the os.replace when `_kfc.LAST_BUILD['replay_bad_tickers']` is non-empty, or when the freshly built `n_settled` is lower than the existing file's `n_settled` while settlements were supplied; count `_SILENT['fillcost_replay_fault']` / `_SILENT['fillcost_settled_row_loss']`, keep the last GOOD file, and stamp a module-level `_FILLCOST_LAST_TRY[0]=time.time()` that the mtime gate also honours (otherwise a held-back write leaves mtime stale and the helper re-pulls two paginated endpoints EVERY cycle instead of hourly).
- *Why:* Closes the class 'one unrecognized row deletes an entire population from a money feed, silently': today a single fill_outcome raise zeroes cash_by for the whole tape and every settled market is then stamped realized=revenue>=0 / cost_usd_day=0.0000, i.e. every historically money-losing market looks free to the capital ranking, kalshi_lumps_ledger and the net-EV calibration. Per-ticker isolation bounds the blast radius to the affected ticker; the population guard makes a total loss impossible to be silent.
- *Standing detector:* Three layers: (a) kalshi_fill_costs.main prints the bad tickers and exits 2 (timer/journal + non-zero exit); (b) the quoter counts _SILENT['fillcost_replay_fault'] and _SILENT['fillcost_settled_row_loss'], which already ride every plan row via _silent_report (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:97-106) — the existing operator surface, no new plumbing; (c) `n_live`/`n_settled` written into kalshi_fill_costs.json so ANY consumer (P2 window arithmetic, lumps ledger, capital rank) can see a population collapse from the file alone, and a future run can diff it.
- *Test:* New test_fill_costs_replay_fault.py, failing-before: tape with two settled tickers — 'GOOD' with a normal 50ct yes fill at $0.40 and a $0 settlement, 'BAD' with a fill dict carrying neither book_side nor outcome_side. Assert (a) out['GOOD']['realized_pnl_usd'] == -20.0 and cost_usd_day == 20.0, (b) 'BAD' not in out, (c) fc.SETTLED_REPLAY_FAILS incremented by 1, (d) fc.LAST_BUILD['n_settled'] == 1. On current code (a) FAILS (whole-tape replay raises -> cash_by={} -> GOOD prices at revenue-only 0.0, cost 0.0) and (b) FAILS (BAD is present at cost 0). Plus a no-op pin: a clean tape produces byte-identical output to the current build (dict equality against the existing test_fill_costs_settled.py expectations).

Flagging it would mean keeping a known-wrong fallback reachable. Merge is still MERGE-ONLY: entries are never deleted, only added/updated.

**M-18 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2566 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Scored set truncated at cumulative Target — rivals below the walk cutoff contribute zero to the share denominator
- *Fix:* CLOSED BY M-2 (shared root fix #1) — specifically clause (a) of `_lip_walk`: in LIP mode there is NO break at cum >= target; df_total accumulates over ALL resting levels and the qualification predicate becomes `total side depth >= target` evaluated over the whole book. The `our_price >= lowest_q` zeroing at :2579 is likewise dropped in LIP mode (the venue scores every resting order on a qualifying side; there is no per-order cutoff).

WHY IT IS NOT A SEPARATE COMMIT: the truncation and the anchor are the same `for price, size in bids:` loop. Splitting them yields an intermediate state that is still wrong in a DIFFERENT direction (a correct anchor with a truncated tail under-weights the deep book relative to both models), and that intermediate would have to be shipped to a live money path to be tested. One commit replacing the walk with the venue formula, one flag, both mechanisms pinned by their own tests.
- *Why:* The LIP spec qualifies at SIDE level and normalizes score = size x distance-mult per side; it contains no per-order cutoff at the cumulative-Target boundary. Deep rivals the venue scores at 0.5^ticks are absent from the model denominator entirely, so modeled share exceeds venue share whenever meaningful size rests just past the Target boundary — and unlike a scalar haircut this bias is book-SHAPE dependent, so it corrupts cross-sectional ranking between thick-boundary and thin-boundary books in a way NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT=3.0 cannot correct.
- *Standing detector:* Covered by M-2's shadow column, plus one M-18-specific telemetry field per side: `*_tail_df` = the df-weighted size BELOW the cumulative-Target boundary (i.e. exactly what the legacy walk discarded). Its distribution over the footprint is the standing measure of how large this bias was and whether any future walk change re-introduces truncation (a `*_tail_df` that collapses to 0 across the board means someone put a break back in).
- *Test:* M-2's T2, written as its own failing-before test: bids=[(0.50,1000.0),(0.49,5000.0)], target=1000, df=0.5, our 50ct at 0.50 -> assert legacy denominator == 1000.0 and LIP denominator == 3500.0, and that `_qualifying_breakdown` returns the SAME denominator as `_qualifying_score` divides by in BOTH modes (extends the existing T1 pin at test_market_telemetry.py:70).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* NO separate flag — rides KALSHI_LIP_REF, same commit as M-2. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Inherited from M-2, and this clause carries most of the magnitude on deep books (the 3.4x in the item's arithmetic). Additional specific risk: including the whole tail makes df_total sensitive to how many levels the book source returns — the WS mirror (WS_BOOK_COLD=1 live) and the REST orderbook may truncate the level list differently, so two sources can now disagree on the denominator where before the walk stopped early and both agreed. Mitigate by recording `y_levels_seen` in the row and comparing mirror vs REST distributions before arming.
- *Collides with:* M-2

POOL OUT OF BAND <ticker> $<usd_day>/day` line per market outside Kalshi's documented $10–$1,000 per-day-per-market band (the exact criterion the 07-25 blind audit used to refute the divided form) and a count in the header. **M-20 — kalshi_live/kalshi_capital_rank.py:142 · triple-blind **DOWNGRADE****

- *Defect:* shadow_rank commit denominator trusts cached ref with NO age cutoff — stale-extreme-ref defect lives in the alloc key
- *Fix:* CLOSES M-20 AND m-40 — collapse the two commit models into one function. (1) kalshi_capital_rank.py gains `effective_commit_usd(ref_yes, market_cap_usd, inv_hard_ct, quantum=5, ref_age_s=None, ref_max_age_s=None, ct_cap=None)`: it drops ref to None when `ref_max_age_s is not None and (ref_age_s is None or ref_age_s > ref_max_age_s)` (the walk's exact 'stale -> unknown -> max est' rule, quoter:5024-5033), calls est_commit_usd, then applies `est = min(est, float(ct_cap))` when ct_cap is not None (the walk's `min(est, probe_ct)` / `min(est, _d3_est_ct)` convention at :5066-5070, ~$1/ct summed across both sides). All-default call == est_commit_usd verbatim. (2) `shadow_rank` gains three additive kwargs defaulting to current behavior: `ref_max_age_s=None`, `ct_cap_fn=None` (callable ticker->contracts), `probe_ct=0`. Per row it computes `ref_age_s = now - float(row['ts'])` for refs taken from SCORES at :142 (a ref substituted from the prospective feed at :156-165 keeps its own upstream age gate in _alloc_priority :3650-3656 and is passed with ref_age_s=None-but-fresh, i.e. not re-gated), builds `ct_cap = min(...)` over probe_ct when `r.get('explore')` and ct_cap_fn(t) when supplied, and calls effective_commit_usd at :189. The component dict gains additive keys `ref_age_s`, `ref_stale`, `ct_cap` (consumers read only ticker/cap_score — quoter:3678). (3) quoter `_alloc_priority` (:3667) and `_caprank_telemetry` (:847) both pass `ref_max_age_s=ALLOC_PCAP_MAX_AGE_S if ALLOC_REF_AGE else None`, `ct_cap_fn=_d3_est_ct if (D3_RAMP and ALLOC_RAMP_EST) else None`, `probe_ct=EXPLORE_PROBE_CT` — identical args at both sites so the shadow log and the live key can never describe different formulas. (4) The select-budget walk (:5066-5070) replaces its inline est_commit_usd plus two min()s with the same `effective_commit_usd(...)` call. Two NEW independent flags: KALSHI_ALLOC_REF_AGE (default 0) and KALSHI_ALLOC_RAMP_EST (default 0).
- *Why:* The ordering key (shadow_rank's commit) and the charging key (the budget walk's est) are two hand-copies of 'what will this market actually commit', and they have drifted: the walk age-gates refs at 6h and applies the probe/ramp caps, the rank trusts a ref up to EVICT_AGE_S=7d old and charges the full join — so the market that wins allocation priority is priced on a denominator the same cycle refuses to charge. Unifying the function makes the two keys agree by construction instead of by vigilance.
- *Standing detector:* (a) `plan['alloc_commit_mismatch']` — per cycle, count footprint tickers where |walk est − rank commit| > $0.01. After unification the invariant is exactly 0; any nonzero value means someone re-forked the model, which is the recurrence signal for this whole class. Ship `plan['alloc_ref_stale_n']` and `plan['alloc_ct_capped_n']` beside it so the two arms' bite is visible before and after arming. (b) Longer-horizon, and the one that catches est/actual drift of ANY origin (ramp, probe, join_size, spread): `commit_est_err` — log per market per cycle the estimated commit against the dollars actually resting after the quote loop (sum of price_dollars x count over accumulating quotes). Systematic bias then becomes a measured series instead of the next audit's finding. n-37 and m-40 both inherit this detector.
- *Test:* New test_alloc_commit_unified.py. (a) test_stale_ref_is_charged_the_max_commitment: SCORES row {'ref':0.95,'capture':10.0,'ts':now-7*3600}; `shadow_rank(..., ref_max_age_s=21600)` must report commit == est_commit_usd(None, cap, hard) (the max/unknown case). FAILS BEFORE — today it prices at 0.95 and returns roughly half that commit, so cap_score reads ~2x high. (b) test_rank_commit_equals_walk_est: build one footprint, run the quoter's select-budget walk (same harness as test_select_budget.py) and shadow_rank with identical kwargs; assert per-ticker equality of walk est and rank commit. FAILS BEFORE on every stale-ref and every ramped ticker. (c) test_defaults_are_byte_identical: shadow_rank without the new kwargs equals today's output on a fixture set (the no-op pin).
- *Effort:* L · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_ALLOC_REF_AGE and KALSHI_ALLOC_RAMP_EST, both default 0, independently armable. The helper extraction and the walk's switch to it are a provable no-op (all-default call reproduces today's arithmetic exactly, pinned by test (c)); only the flags change behavior. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* (1) LIVENESS IS UNVERIFIED. docs/maker_handoffs/KALSHI_HANDOFF_2026-08-04_EOD.md:23-24 records ALLOC_KEY=1 live; the 08-06T19:48Z flag list I was given omits it. If ALLOC_KEY=1 this changes live allocation ordering the cycle a flag arms; if 0 it changes only the caprank shadow log. Read live.env before arming. (2) Arming ref-age pushes stale-measured markets DOWN (bigger denominator). Markets we rest in continuously refresh their ts via the D4 fold, so the ones hit are exactly the explore-probed-then-unselected tail — conservative in direction, but it further starves discovery on top of the existing explore quota. (3) Refactoring the WALK to call the shared helper edits a live sizing path with real money resting, and that block fails OPEN (quoter :5085 except -> unfiltered footprint): a slip there does not stop trading, it removes the budget filter, letting more capital deploy than SELECT_BUDGET_MARGIN=-0.3 intends. This is the single most dangerous line in my whole set. (4) Arming either flag makes the caprank shadow log non-comparable with prior days — the D1/D6 capital-coupling study and the evidence base for arming ALLOC_KEY itself were built on the current formula. (5) The two arms move cap_score in OPPOSITE directions (stale refs down, ramped young markets up); arming both at once makes the net effect unattributable, which is why they are two flags.
- *Collides with:* m-40, n-37

**M-21 — kalshi_live/kalshi_netev_rebuild.py:203 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Markets still OPEN at read time are scored as pure loss — open collateral poisons family net_pct
- *Fix:* ROOT FIX (shared with M-22) — give kalshi_netev_rebuild.build_table an explicit MARKET-SCOPE CONTAINMENT CONTRACT, and stop discarding the data that already proves the defect.

1. New pure helper above build_table:
   `_market_scope(events, positions, window, marks=None, tol=0.005) -> (score_set, detail)`
   Per ticker it walks the replayed events once and records first_fill_ts, any_in_window, prewindow_fills, prewindow_cash, and the read-time position from `positions`, then classifies:
     'in'       every fill inside the window AND |position| <= tol at read time (flat or settled) -> a COMPLETE observation.
     'valued'   in-window, |position| > tol, but `marks` carries a liquidation price -> complete BY VALUATION.
     'open'     in-window, |position| > tol, no usable mark -> terminal leg missing (THE M-21 SHAPE).
     'straddle' in-window but first fill < window lo -> pre-window cash would be imported (THE M-22 SHAPE).
     'out'      no in-window fill.
2. build_table currently throws the open-position dict away at :163 (`events, _pos = replay_fills(fills)`). Keep `_pos` and feed it to the helper — the fix needs no new data source at all.
3. New keyword args, additive, existing signature preserved: `scope=SCOPE_LEGACY` ('legacy'|'contained') and `marks=None`. In legacy mode `_score_set = _traded_in_window` (today's set, byte-identical numbers). In contained mode `_score_set = {'in'} | {'valued'}` tickers.
4. Feed `_score_set` to ALL FOUR attribution legs — the fill loop (:172), the settlement loop (:209), the credit prefix test (:246), and the notional accumulator — so the module's own 'ONE RULE, ONE CLOCK' invariant survives. Scoping cash but not credits would re-create the exact income-without-cost defect the 08-03 credit fix closed.
5. 'valued' markets book a synthetic terminal leg `fam_mark[fam] += mark * position` alongside fam_settle. The mark is the acquired side's BID (liquidation value, deliberately pessimistic), supplied by main() from `/trade-api/v2/markets/{ticker}`; a market with no bid stays 'open' (excluded) rather than being marked at zero — marking an illiquid far-close book at 0 would re-create M-21 in a subtler form.
6. ALWAYS accumulate diagnostics in BOTH modes: per family `open_markets`, `open_cash`, `open_position_ct`, `straddle_markets`, `prewindow_cash`, `excluded_cash_share`; doc-level `scope`, `scope_exclusions {open:{ticker:...}, straddle:{ticker:...}}`, `marks_used`. Money never vanishes from the document — same discipline as the existing `credits_out_of_scope`.
7. In contained mode only, withhold the verdict when the surviving sample is a fragment: `excluded_cash_share > INCOMPLETE_MAX_SHARE (0.10)` -> confidence 'unproven', evidence "incomplete: NN% of this family's cash sits in M market(s) outside scope". In legacy mode the same number is emitted but confidence is untouched, so the flag is genuinely dark.
8. main() gains `--scope {legacy,contained}` (default legacy) and `--mark-open`; build_table stays pure (no I/O), all venue reads stay in main().
- *Why:* A market still OPEN at API read time has no terminal leg: fill_cashflow books its collateral as pure outflow (-price*qty) and the only offset, fam_settle, is built from /portfolio/settlements, which by definition has no row for it. The module root-caused this exact 'half a round trip' shape at the WINDOW edge and made the market the unit of judgment; the READ-time edge (and, per M-22, the LEFT edge) were left unguarded, so a family can be scored net-negative on collateral that has not been lost.
- *Standing detector:* (a) Document-level `scope_exclusions` + per-family `open_markets` / `open_cash` / `prewindow_cash` / `excluded_cash_share` travel inside every emitted table, so a later reader can always see how much of a verdict was built on incomplete markets. (b) main() prints `WARNING SCOPE:` to stderr whenever any family's excluded_cash_share > 0.10 or any single open market carries > $1 of collateral, naming the tickers. (c) `window_days` + the resolved window are echoed so the exclusion set is reproducible. (d) A test asserts the WARNING fields are populated on a contaminated fixture, so the detector cannot be silently deleted.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, in test_netev_rebuild.py:
`test_an_open_market_is_not_scored_as_a_pure_loss`: single 100ct YES @ $0.40 fill in-window on KXAPRPOTUS-26NOV04-X, NO settlement row. Today families['KXAPRPOTUS']['trading_pnl'] == -40.0 with no marker anywhere (that assert pins the before-state). After: with scope='contained' the family is absent from `families` and doc['scope_exclusions']['open']['KXAPRPOTUS-26NOV04-X'] == {'cash': -40.0, 'position': 100.0}; with marks={'KXAPRPOTUS-26NOV04-X': 0.38} the family is present with trading_pnl == -2.0 and doc['marks_used'] == 1. Both new asserts KeyError/fail on current code.
`test_legacy_scope_reproduces_todays_numbers`: every existing fixture re-run with the default arg must give identical rows — pins the dark default.
`test_receipt_is_withheld_when_most_of_the_cash_is_out_of_scope`: 40 complete fills + one open market carrying 80% of the cash -> confidence 'unproven', 'incomplete' in evidence.
- *Effort:* L · *Dark:* YES — `scope` defaults to 'legacy' (today's numbers byte-for-byte) and the produced TABLE FILE is the deploy unit, so arming is `--scope contained` on the B8 rebuild after the operator has compared both documents. Diagnostics are emitted in both modes and are inert to the gate (kalshi_netev_calibrate.load_table returns only data['families'], and the gate reads only `confidence` / `net_pct_notional`). · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* CONCERNING, in the direction opposite to what 'conservative' suggests. (1) The live knob is NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT=-7.0 while net_pct_notional is a FRACTION (gas -0.0468) — `poor = net_pct < -7.0` is unreachable, so today a 'receipt' grade means UNCONDITIONALLY QUOTE and 'unproven' means take the model fallback, which CAN skip. Any sample shrink that drops a family under MIN_RECEIPT_FILLS=40 therefore REDUCES quoting. All six receipt families sit near that line (temp 44 fills, KXTRUMPENDORSEMENTS 55, KXTRUMPTIME 57, KXTOPMODEL 59, KXDXYDUD 61); temp needs to lose 5 fills to flip. (2) Straddle exclusion is brutal on short windows: weekly gas (KXAAAGASW) spans 7 days, so a 4-day B8 window makes nearly every gas market a straddler and gas (282 fills, the largest sample) could vanish from the table entirely. (3) Exclusion is itself biased against exactly what B8 must measure — D-C macro ladders and HELD_MAX_USD holdings are DESIGNED to stay open to program end, so contained-without-marks makes those families structurally unjudgeable and permanently silent. Marks are the only real answer for them, and marks introduce a price-source dependency plus a fresh venue read per open ticker. (4) Every percentage in the shipped caveats block becomes mode-specific; that text is pinned by test_the_caveats_are_not_stale_on_the_canon_disagreement, so labelling must not break those literal asserts. (5) It cannot be validated without a live tape — unit fixtures prove the mechanism, only a real B8 dual-run (legacy vs contained on the same tape) shows the actual family deltas.
- *Collides with:* M-22, m-41, n-39, n-41

**M-22 — kalshi_live/kalshi_netev_rebuild.py:152 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Whole-market attribution pulls PRE-window defect-era cash into a 'clean' window on straddling markets
- *Fix:* CLOSED BY THE M-21 FIX — the 'straddle' branch of `_market_scope`. Concretely: the helper records each ticker's FIRST fill timestamp during the same single pass it uses for the open-position test; a ticker with an in-window fill whose first fill predates the window lo is classified 'straddle' and is dropped from `_score_set` in contained mode, with its `prewindow_cash`, `prewindow_fills` and `prewindow_notional` reported in doc['scope_exclusions']['straddle'] and in the family row's `prewindow_cash` / `excluded_cash_share` fields — emitted in BOTH modes, so even a legacy-mode B8 table SHOWS the contamination the operator did not arm against.

What is deliberately NOT done: per-fill windowing of a straddler's cash. The module already root-caused that (:142-151) — filtering the replay INPUT breaks the position path and scored gas -12.5%..-17.1% against a canon +1.1%. Attributing only the in-window EVENTS of a straddler is the same half-round-trip error one layer up. The complete answer (value the pre-window position at the WINDOW-START price via the candlesticks endpoint and treat it as the opening basis) is a genuine subsystem and is out of scope for the B8 date; exclusion plus measurement is the shippable containment.
- *Why:* A market enters scope on ANY in-window fill and then contributes its ENTIRE lifetime cash, so a window deliberately chosen to exclude the launch-defect era still inherits every defect-era fill of any market touched once inside it — laundering agent defects into a permanent family verdict, which is precisely what the module docstring forbids.
- *Standing detector:* Per-family `prewindow_cash` and `excluded_cash_share` on every row in every mode, plus doc['scope_exclusions']['straddle'] naming the tickers, plus the main() stderr WARNING when the share exceeds 10%. This is exactly the field the item notes does not exist today: contamination is currently invisible in the artifact, and n_fills/notional themselves already include pre-window fills.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, in test_netev_rebuild.py: `test_a_prewindow_fill_does_not_enter_a_clean_window` — tape = 100ct YES @ $0.40 on 2026-07-20 (PRE-window, defect era) + 5ct YES @ $0.40 on 2026-07-25 (in-window), settlement $0, window (07-24, 07-30). Today trading_pnl == -42.0 and nothing reports it. After: with the default scope the row still reads -42.0 BUT families['gas']['prewindow_cash'] == -40.0 and excluded_cash_share ~= 0.952 (both KeyError today); with scope='contained' 'gas' is absent from families and doc['scope_exclusions']['straddle'] names the ticker with prewindow_fills == 1.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* YES — same `scope` flag as M-21; the diagnostics half (prewindow_cash on every row) is unconditional and inert to the gate. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Same commit and same risks as M-21; the straddle branch specifically is the harsher of the two. On any B8 window shorter than the longest market life in a family (weekly gas = 7 days), contained mode can remove that family from the table completely, and 'no row' routes the gate to the model fallback. It also silently changes what 'the window' means: an operator comparing a contained table against the deployed 07-24..08-03 legacy table is comparing two different questions, and nothing but the `scope` field stops that.
- *Collides with:* M-21, m-41, n-39

**M-23 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ledger_reconstruct.py:71 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Reconstruct re-implements the refuted side-derived sign model — wrong on all 'sell no' fills (~49% of tape)
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/kalshi_ledger_reconstruct.py — delete the local (side, action) cash/position model at lines 51-72 and delegate to the venue-verified canon: `import kalshi_attribution_ledger as L`, then `events, positions = L.replay_fills(fills)` and accumulate per ticker from the event rows: `mkt_cash[t] += ev['cash']` (position-aware: -price*opened + (1-price)*offset - fee), `mkt_net_yes[t] += ev['delta']` (L.fill_position_delta, + = more YES). Replace `mkt_gross_buy` with `mkt_gross_acq`: opened = count - min(count, |position_before|) when the fill reduces, else count; `mkt_gross_acq[t] += L.fill_price(f) * opened` — so a NO acquisition (legacy 'sell no', 265 of 542 rows in the committed AUD_fills.json) is counted. Header/column labels change from 'grossBuy$' / 'gross buy cost (both sides)' to 'grossAcq$' / 'gross ACQUISITION cost (both outcomes)'; the round-trip sentence at lines 86-87 is recomputed from the corrected totals. Also switch pagination to `L.get_paginated(L.P + '/portfolio/fills', 'fills')` so the tape is complete — replay_fills is position-path-dependent and a truncated tape (current maxpages=20 x 200) silently starts a ticker mid-position.
- *Why:* Lines 59-72 re-implement the exact legacy sign model that was root-fixed on 2026-07-23 (kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:104-137): it books our resting NO bid's fill as cash IN and +count yes-equivalent when it is us acquiring NO — cash OUT, -count. Delegating to the single verified implementation (3/3 positions, 51/51 settlements, 27/27 balance intervals) removes the second cash model entirely, so it cannot drift again.
- *Standing detector:* Port the ledger's abort gate into this tool: after replay, compare reconstructed net positions against /portfolio/positions `position_fp` on unsettled tickers and print 'position reconcile k/n'; if k/n < 1.0 (unsettled set), print the mismatches and REFUSE to print the per-market table at all. Any future divergence between this tool and the venue stops output instead of publishing numbers.
- *Test:* New kalshi_live/test_ledger_reconstruct.py (pure, no network — factor the accumulation into `accumulate(fills)` returning the four dicts): (1) one fill {action:'sell', side:'no', book_side:'ask', count_fp:10, no_price_dollars:0.30} on a flat ticker -> cash == -3.00 and net_yes == -10 (FAILS BEFORE: +3.00 and +10); (2) the same fill against position_before=+10 -> cash == +(1-0.30)*10 = +7.00, net_yes == 0; (3) gross acquisition includes the NO leg (FAILS BEFORE: mkt_gross_buy is 0 for a sell-no-only ticker). Fixture rows can be lifted verbatim from the committed AUD_fills.json.

Any doc or canon line citing them must be re-derived or retracted — that is a documentation blast radius, not a code one. Second hazard: L.replay_fills REQUIRES the full tape from flat; if pagination is still bounded (or the venue truncates) the position-aware model is wrong from the first fill and would be wrong in a NEW way rather than the old known-wrong way — hence the switch to L.get_paginated plus the position reconcile gate, which must land in the same commit. Re-running needs prod credentials (see n-46).
- *Collides with:* n-46

**M-24 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ledger.py:255 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Fix:* kalshi_live/kalshi_ledger.py — stop deriving REWARDS as a residual; source it from receipts, and turn the residual into a NAMED error term. (3) Line 257 print becomes two lines: 'REWARDS (receipts, credit_history N rows)' and 'UNEXPLAINED residual' with an alarm marker when |unexplained| > LEDGER_UNEXPLAINED_ALARM_USD (new module constant, default 2.00) reading '!! MODEL GAP — accrued-unpaid LIP and/or unpriced lots and/or deposits error'. (4) If the credit read fails or KALSHI_USER_ID is unset: print 'REWARDS UNAVAILABLE — credit_history read failed: <err>' and SKIP the rewards/identity/net lines entirely — never fall back to the residual (RULE ZERO). (5) Snapshot row (lines 282-288) gains "schema": 2, "rewards_receipts", "unexplained", and DROPS "rewards_derived"; show_rate handles both (legacy rows labeled 'residual era, excluded from the reward rate'). (6) Docstring lines 4-8 and 23 rewritten: the 'Kalshi exposes NO credits/ledger endpoint' premise is refuted by maker_kalshi_client.py:419 (canon 2026-08-01); the identity is restated as equity = deposits + realized + rewards_receipts + unrealized + unexplained. This one fix also closes m-50 (the identity check stops being tautological).
- *Why:* rewards = equity - deposits - realized - unreal (line 255) is definitionally the RULE-ZERO-banned residual class under the alias rewards_derived: every model error (unpriced lots, FIFO edges, deposit mis-entry) is printed as LIP income, and the tool's docstring actively invites a future session to cite it as a reward figure. credit_history is the canon reward truth and is already implemented in the repo.
- *Standing detector:* The UNEXPLAINED line with its threshold alarm, persisted per snapshot — a growing |unexplained| is the standing telemetry that the model is drifting (that is exactly the signal the residual used to hide). Plus the printed credit-row count and non-LIP type census, so a feed change (rename/truncation) is visible in the same output.
- *Test:* New kalshi_live/test_kalshi_ledger_rewards.py with fetch()/fetch_credits monkeypatched (no network): (1) equity/deposits/realized/unreal chosen so the residual is $10 while credit_history returns $6 of LIP credits -> printed rewards == 6.00 and unexplained == 4.00 (FAILS BEFORE: rewards == 10.00 and there is no unexplained term); (2) credit fetch raising -> output contains 'REWARDS UNAVAILABLE' and NO rewards number anywhere (FAILS BEFORE: it prints the residual); (3) snapshot row asserts schema==2, has rewards_receipts, and has no rewards_derived key.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — offline operator tool, no trading behavior. The alarm threshold is a module constant (not an env knob) so there is no new live-config surface. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* HONEST DOWNSIDE: rewards are paid at PROGRAM conclusion (canon 08-01), so accrued-but-unpaid LIP sits inside equity while credit_history has not yet booked it — UNEXPLAINED will be structurally positive and the identity check will read DOES NOT CLOSE on most runs. That is truthful, but it converts a line the operator was reading as green into a persistent amber one, and the $2.00 alarm threshold is a guess that will need calibration against the first few runs. Second: the snapshot file becomes schema-split, so any rate window spanning the change is not comparable (handled by labeling, not by magic). Third: the tool now needs KALSHI_USER_ID in the environment where it runs, or it loses its headline entirely by design.
- *Collides with:* m-50, m-51, n-47

**M-25 — kalshi_live/w11_replay.py:96 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Replay baseline 'recorded' is the SHADOW ranking, not live selection — cyc['actual'] never read
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/w11_replay.py — make the live selection a first-class baseline instead of a shadow re-sort. (2) Add `def policy_live_actual(components, ctx)`: returns ctx['actual'] followed by any component tickers not in it, in (-cap_score, ticker) order — so slot stats over top_n<=len(actual) are exactly the live footprint and the tail keeps median-rank computable. (3) POLICIES becomes {'live_actual': policy_live_actual, 'shadow_env': policy_recorded, 'd2_feedback': ...}: rename the 'recorded' KEY (the function stays, it is a correct reproduction of the shadow order — it was only mislabeled) so no report can be read as 'live' again. Every D2 uplift measured against it is shadow-vs-shadow, and with PIVOT_SELECT=1 live the two can diverge structurally.

Update test_recorded_policy_is_cap_score_order to import policy_recorded directly and rename to test_shadow_policy_is_cap_score_order — the old name asserted the defect's framing, not the defect itself.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — offline replay harness; it cannot place an order. The knob it informs (D2) is live, so the report header must carry the changed-baseline note in the same commit. · **CONCERNING**

If live and shadow turn out to diverge a lot, the re-run may show d2_feedback's uplift is smaller (or negative) than believed, which is an argument about a live knob, not a tool bug.
