**M-26 — kalshi_live/w17_coverage_ledger.py:233 · triple-blind **DOWNGRADE****

- *Defect:* B-4 staleness detector: 'never-in-window' sentinel compares lexically NEWER than any date — never alarms
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/w17_coverage_ledger.py lines 232-237 — stop using a lexically-comparable sentinel. Replace with: `last = last_by_series.get(s)`; `stale = (last is None) or (last < stale_cut)`; and split the report so the never-seen case is named: if last is None -> print '  paid-but-NO-CREDIT-IN-WINDOW {s} (B-4, strongest case)' when noprog, else the existing stale line with last[:10]. Additionally emit the three-way census for every proven series regardless of alarm state: fresh / stale / never-in-window counts. Factor the decision into a pure helper `_credit_staleness(last, stale_cut)` -> 'fresh'|'stale'|'never' so it is unit-testable (main() is currently a monolith).
- *Why:* 'never-in-window' starts with 'n' (0x6E) which is lexically greater than any ISO date starting with '2' (0x32), so `last < stale_cut` is always False for exactly the series that produced ZERO parse-matched credits — the semantically most-stale case is the one case the B-4 alarm can never fire on.
- *Standing detector:* The census line ('proven series: F fresh / S stale / N never-in-window') printed unconditionally. This is the class fix: a detector whose all-clear is byte-identical to a detector that cannot fire is not a detector — every alarm in this file should print its denominator (same pattern applied in n-43 and M-3).
- *Test:* New kalshi_live/test_w17_alarms.py: `_credit_staleness(None, '2026-07-23T00:00:00+00:00') == 'never'` (FAILS BEFORE: the sentinel path evaluates stale=False, so the equivalent inline expression yields 'fresh'); plus 'stale' and 'fresh' cases; plus a main-level test with a proven series absent from the credit window and absent from active programs asserting a B-4 line is printed.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — read-only operator diagnostic (w17 header lines 19-20), no state written.
- *Risk:* New alarm lines will appear on the next run for any proven series whose credits are outside the 14-day window — including series that are legitimately dormant. Expect operator noise on the first run and a possible one-time triage pass. Nothing here can change trading behavior.
- *Collides with:* m-47, m-48, m-54, n-43

**M-27 — kalshi_live/kalshi_drift_detect.py:89 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* build_timeline mid has no book-sanity filter; shell-book mids corrupt value/jump_share (audit fix not propagated)
- *Fix:* One canonical fair-value helper, used by both copies; delete the last-trade fallback; make an unusable book produce None rather than a fake mid. (1) NEW module kalshi_live/kalshi_book_fair.py: MAX_SPREAD=0.20; quote(c)->(b,a) floats from yes_bid.close_dollars / yes_ask.close_dollars (None,None when either side absent); classify(b,a)->'book2s'|'boundary'|'wide'|'absent' (book2s: b>0 and a<1 and a-b<=MAX_SPREAD; boundary: (b==0 or a==1) and a-b<=MAX_SPREAD; wide: a-b>MAX_SPREAD); book_mid(c, require_two_sided=True)->float|None. (2) signal_book_drift.fair() (:31-50) becomes a one-line delegate book_mid(c, require_two_sided=True) — semantically identical to today's predicate, proven by an equivalence test over all 14,974 cached candles, so it is a no-op there. (3) kalshi_drift_detect.build_timeline (:75-101): DELETE the last-trade fallback at :84-88; b,a = quote(c); value = book_mid(c, require_two_sided=False) mapped to our side (mid for yes, 1-mid for no) or None; exitable from the RAW quote only (b for yes, 1-a for no; None when that side is absent) — exitable stays honest at 0.00 because 'the bid is zero' is a true fact, not a corrupted mid; spread = a-b or None; NEW field value_src; keep 'quoted' meaning both sides present. (4) All mid consumers abstain on None via a new _v(tl,i) helper: D1 (:155), D6 (:222), D7 (:238), D9 (:255), eval.s_mid_drift (:56), eval.s_value_floor (:95); D8 (:245)/eval.s_spread (:99) skip None spreads and abstain below 3 baseline samples; find_dead_ts (:130) skips rows whose exitable is None (unknown != dead) instead of comparing None. (5) kalshi_drift_jump.decompose computes BOTH jump_share_mid (usable-mid steps only) and jump_share_exit (exitable steps) and the printed headline switches to _exit, because exitable is the series that survives the death minutes and is what we could actually have gotten out at. CRITICAL DESIGN POINT — do NOT copy the sibling's predicate verbatim: I measured the frozen cache and TEMP candles are 79.5% book2s / 14.8% boundary / 5.7% wide (GAS 90.3/6.3/3.4), so requiring b>0 and a<1 would erase 20.5% of TEMP minutes concentrated exactly in the 0.00/0.01 death books. The spread cap is what actually bounds the mid's error (<= MAX_SPREAD/2); two-sidedness is only a proxy for it and over-rejects at the boundaries.
- *Why:* One fair-value definition existed in two copies and the audited correction (signal_fair_value_audit -> signal_book_drift.fair) reached only one of them, so build_timeline still manufactures a mid from a 0.20/0.99 shell book — and a mid from a last-trade print when the book is absent — and that fabricated series is the input to D1/D6/D7/D9, the S1/S7 AUCs and jump_share.
- *Standing detector:* (1) PROVENANCE block printed by the detect/eval/jump mains: per group, window minutes broken down by value_src, with a '⚠ VALUE COVERAGE < 0.90' alarm. (2) test_drift_fair_single_source.py — asserts both call sites resolve to kalshi_book_fair AND agree candle-by-candle across the whole frozen cache; this is the CLASS alarm for 'a fair-value correction was applied to one copy only', which is the actual recurring mechanism. (3) An internal assert in build_timeline: value is not None implies value_src in {book2s, boundary} and spread <= MAX_SPREAD — a fabricated value can no longer be constructed even by a future edit.
- *Test:* kalshi_live/test_drift_book_sanity.py, four cases, all failing today: (a) candle bid 0.2000 / ask 0.9900 / price 0.9800 -> tl[0]['value'] is None (today 0.595) and tl[0]['exitable'] == 0.20 and value_src == 'wide'; (b) candle with no yes_bid/yes_ask but price.close_dollars 0.98 -> value is None and exitable is None (today both 0.98); (c) boundary candle 0.0000/0.0100 -> value == 0.005, guarding against over-rejection of the death minutes; (d) frozen regression: the worst 1-minute mid step of KXTEMPLAXH-26JUL2212-T70.99 is no longer 0.59 and the 1784735640->1784735700 pair contributes no mid step at all.
- *Effort:* L · *Dark:* no — this module family has no live-trading behavior (the live quoter does not import it; grep-verified in n-50). The equivalent of a dark flag here is publication discipline: regenerate outputs under new dated filenames and leave the 07-23 frozen deliverables byte-untouched. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* (a) It changes published magnitudes: jump_share medians, D1/D6/D7/D9 TPR-FPR, S1/S7 AUC and the 'the loss arrives in one minute' headline all move; the 07-23 .txt/.json deliverables become non-comparable, and if they are not regenerated to NEW dated files with a diff table, two versions of the truth will coexist in canon. (b) The mid series still loses the 5.7% wide-book TEMP minutes, and they are NOT missing at random — they cluster near expiry — so post-fix mid detectors are scored on a slightly healthier subsample and every number must be read next to its coverage. (c) A detector may look BETTER after the fix (D2/S2 are untouched, D1 gets a cleaner series); nothing in this change licenses arming a live knob from a 22-vs-27 position sample, and the temptation will exist. (d) find_dead_ts skipping None exitable changes win_end for any position whose terminal minutes lack a bid, which shifts window lengths and every lead-time number. (e) The signal_book_drift delegate is only a no-op if the equivalence test passes; if it does not, that file's published numbers move too, and it is the source of the 'life-frac is the free signal' line.
- *Collides with:* M-28, n-49, m-56, n-48

**M-28 — kalshi_live/kalshi_drift_detect.py:150 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* Windowed detectors treat candle index as minutes; 15.3% of window steps are gaps so W-minute windows span hours
- *Fix:* Stop addressing time by list index; address it by timestamp, and make abstention explicit and counted. (1) _val_at(tl,i,back_min) (:150-152) keeps its signature but resolves by ts: bisect.bisect_left(tl, tl[i]['ts'] - back_min*60, key=lambda r: r['ts']) and returns the value only on an EXACT ts hit, else None (new _row_at does the work; _val_at delegates). tl is already sorted, so no map and no caller change. (2) D2 (j=i-W, :167), D6 (range(i-m+1,i+1), :232), D8 (range(i-W,i), :249) and D9's volume history (:260) resolve each trailing minute by ts and ABSTAIN (return False, increment an abstain counter) when the window is not fully covered; D6 additionally requires each consecutive pair to be exactly 60s. (3) D3/D4 (:176, :191) stop walking timeline rows and iterate the tape by timestamp — for m in range(t1-(W-1)*60, t1+60, 60): a = st['tape'].get(m) — which removes the index dependence AND fixes a second-order bug in the same class: trades landing in a minute that has no candle are currently invisible to the tape detectors. (4) kalshi_drift_eval.py analogues get the identical treatment: s_exit_drift (:61-63), s_tape_share (:66-73), s_tape_net (:76-82), s_spread (:99-103); s_mid_drift inherits the fix through _val_at. (5) matched_window (eval.py:172-185): 'first K decision points' gains a wall-clock cap — the K points must fall inside the first 2K minutes after entry, else the position is dropped exactly as a short position is today. This is the part that actually removes the by-group window inflation. REJECTED ALTERNATIVE, stated deliberately: making the timeline dense (one row per minute) would also make index==minutes true, but the frozen cache goes from 14,974 candle rows to 25,820 dense rows (42% synthetic empties) and every decision-point count, duty cycle and denominator in both .txt deliverables would move for reasons unrelated to the bug. ts-resolution changes only the windows that were actually wrong.
- *Why:* build_timeline drops minutes with no candle, so a 'trailing 6 minutes' window computed as i-6 list entries can span up to 59 minutes (measured: 750/4893 in-window steps are non-60s, max gap 3540s), and the inflation is group-correlated — the positives' death regions are the gappiest — so longer effective windows accumulate more drift and flatter every TPR.
- *Standing detector:* (1) A GRID INTEGRITY block per group: window minutes, minutes present, gap minutes, % non-60s steps, max gap. (2) A per-detector 'cover' column = evaluated / (evaluated + abstained) decision points, with '⚠ COVER < 0.90' printed beside any row whose rate is therefore built on a subsample — this is exactly the alarm whose absence let an hour-wide window masquerade as six minutes. (3) evaluate() and point_samples() return the abstain count, so no table in this family can print a rate without its true denominator.
- *Test:* kalshi_live/test_drift_window_is_minutes.py, four cases, all failing today: (a) 10 one-minute rows, a 59-minute hole, 10 more rows — at the first post-hole index, _val_at(tl,i,6) is None (today it returns a value ~50 minutes old); (b) same series with exitable 0.50 pre-hole and 0.10 post-hole — det_adverse_exitable_drift(st,i,W=6,theta=0.08) is False (today True, asserting a 6-minute 40c drift that actually took an hour); (c) a tape trade inside a gap minute is counted by det_tape_adverse_share (today invisible because no timeline row carries that ts); (d) a position whose first 5 decision points span 90 minutes is dropped by matched_window(...,K=5) (today it is scored as if it were a 10-minute window).
- *Effort:* L · *Dark:* no — offline harness, no live path. Same publication caveat as M-27: regenerate to new dated outputs, never overwrite the frozen 07-23 deliverables. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* (a) Every TPR/FPR/lift/AUC in kalshi_drift_detect.txt and kalshi_drift_eval.txt changes. The direction is toward honesty, but the numbers a future D-E study would tune against become different numbers, and the old ones are already quoted in canon. (b) Detectors abstain more, so N shrinks — the within-TEMP comparison is already 19+17 positions at K=5, and adding a wall-clock cap to matched_window can push it below the point where anything can be said. Saying nothing is the correct answer but it will read as a regression. (c) The matched_window wall-clock cap is a SEMANTIC change to a published frame, not merely a bug fix; it must be labelled a new frame rather than presented as a correction of the old numbers. (d) bisect with key= requires Python >= 3.10 — fine on 3.13 but it is a version floor, and Python 3.13's whole-function scoping trap applies to any rebinding introduced in these loops. (e) If M-27 has not landed first, ts-resolution merely resolves contaminated mids correctly, producing a number that looks authoritative and is not.
- *Collides with:* M-27, n-48, n-49

**M-29 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3018 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* NETEV margin unit mismatch: fraction table values vs percent-scale knob (live -7.0) - receipt bench arm cannot fire
- *Fix:* Make the net-EV margin comparison unit-explicit at the point of comparison, keeping the env var name (an external interface, Rule 3).

(a) Beside the knob (~:1362) add the derived value with the conversion in one place:
    NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT = _envf("KALSHI_NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT", 0.0)   # PERCENT, per its name
    NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_FRAC = NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT / 100.0                # table unit is a FRACTION
(b) The gate (~:3018) becomes `poor = net_pct < NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_FRAC`.
(c) The plan emit (~:6130) emits BOTH: `plan["netev_min_margin_pct"] = NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT` (unchanged key, unchanged value — no consumer breaks) and NEW `plan["netev_min_margin_frac"] = NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_FRAC`, so the number actually compared is on the row.
(d) `_load_netev_table` gains a unit assertion: any row with `abs(net_pct_notional) > 1.0` increments `_SILENT["netev_table_unit_suspect"]` (a fraction cannot exceed -100%; the shipped table's -1.0 for KXB200WS is exactly -100% and must NOT trip a `>` test).

ZERO-BEHAVIOUR-CHANGE DEPLOY PATH, using only existing interfaces: land the code with the operator simultaneously setting KALSHI_NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT=-700.0, which reproduces today's comparison bit-for-bit (-700/100 = -7.0 raw). The operator then dials to -7.0 in a second, one-line env edit whose blast radius is a single number. That is the dark ship — no new flag, no dead code.
- *Why:* Producers write a FRACTION (kalshi_netev_calibrate.py:174 and kalshi_netev_rebuild.py:292 both store net/notional; the shipped table reads gas -0.046816, temp -0.060929) and the gate compares it raw against a knob whose name, whose in-code ladder comment (~:3010-3013: '-4% keeps 1 of 6 ... -7% keeps all 6') and whose live value (-7.0) are all on the PERCENT scale. A family must lose 700% of notional to bench, so the receipt bench arm cannot fire; today's outcome matches operator intent only by accident of scale, and at B8's margin re-rule any percent-looking number the operator picks is a silent no-op.
- *Standing detector:* (a) `netev_min_margin_frac` on every plan row — the number actually compared is now readable without re-deriving it. (b) `_SILENT["netev_table_unit_suspect"]` catches the reverse drift (a future producer writing percents into the table). (c) A permanent PRODUCER/CONSUMER CONTRACT TEST — the class fix: assert over the shipped kalshi_netev_table.json that every `net_pct_notional` satisfies -1.0 <= x <= 1.0, and assert the gate's effective threshold equals knob/100. That test fails the moment either side of the contract changes units again, which is the recurrence class (this is the second unit defect in this lane; a contract test is what stops the third).
- *Test:* Failing-before, in test_netev_gate.py:
  T1 THE ARM FIRES: a receipt family with net_pct_notional=-0.20 and KALSHI_NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT=-7.0 -> flat market is SKIPPED. Today it opens full two-sided (because -0.20 < -7.0 is False).
  T2 THE LADDER REPRODUCES: over the six shipped receipt families, knob -4.0 benches 5 of 6, -7.0 benches 0 of 6 — the exact ladder the in-code comment documents. Today no knob value reproduces it.
  T3 DEFAULT NO-OP: knob 0.0 -> 0.0/100 == 0.0, behaviour identical (guards against the fix changing the default).
  T4 UNIT CONTRACT: the shipped table's rows are all within [-1, 1] and the suspect counter is 0.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* YES, via the existing knob rather than a new flag: deploy with KALSHI_NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT=-700.0 (bit-identical to today), then arm with a one-line env edit after the operator re-rules the margin. Adding a second boolean here would be dead config the moment the ruling lands. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* CONCERNING because it ARMS a gate that is currently a structural no-op, on a live money path, in a way that is invisible unless the operator reads the new plan field. Specifics: (1) temp reads -6.09% and the live knob is -7.0 — a margin of 0.9 percentage points. Any table rebuild (B8 is explicitly planned) that moves temp past -7% now benches an entire family that has been trading, and benching presence on defect-era losses is what RULE SEVEN and the drips canon forbid. (2) Every window in the current table was produced by a bot carrying defects 1-14, so the table cannot separate agent defects from family economics — arming a real comparison against that table launders defect losses into 'this family loses money'. (3) If the operator does NOT set -700 on the deploy, the deploy itself changes live gating in the same instant as the code lands, with no shadow period. (4) The fix does nothing about the deeper question (should the bench arm act on a defect-era table at all) — that is R-2's ruling, not code.
- *Collides with:* m-42, m-20, m-59

**M-30 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5229 · triple-blind **DOWNGRADE****

- *Defect:* Read-budget exhaustion mid quote loop cancels the unread tail's standing book, including held-ticker exits
- *Fix:* CLOSED BY THE M-10 DESIGN (same commit) — specifically parts (4) and (5). The marginal content beyond M-11: (i) always build `consumed` and collapse `footprint = consumed` when the budget broke the loop, so the never-read pool tail is absent from fp_tickers and drop-grace covers it under PIVOT_SELECT=0 too (under the live PIVOT_SELECT=1 the tail is ALREADY covered by DROP_GRACE=3 — the triple-blind DOWNGRADE is correct); (ii) strand retention so a held ticker in the unread tail keeps its exit even after grace exhausts; (iii) the exhaustion telemetry, which is the part that actually matters today.
- *Why:* Sustained exhaustion (not a one-cycle blip) is what tears the tail down: grace is 3 cycles, so a pool that no longer fits the budget starts cancelling and rebuilding the tail every 4th cycle, burning write tokens and queue position on markets nothing was ever wrong with.
- *Standing detector:* See M-10. The M-30-specific detector is the exhaustion-run counter: consecutive cycles with read_budget_exhausted=1, cross-checked against the existing drops['close_unchecked_tail'] (already measured nonzero on the 08-06 warmup) — together they say 'the universe outgrew the budget' before any teardown is visible in P&L.
- *Test:* test_readfail_retention.py case (a) extended to run 5 consecutive exhausting cycles with DROP_GRACE=3 and assert the tail's order ids are never cancelled and never re-created (queue position preserved). Pre-fix the teardown appears on cycle 4.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* yes — same flag as M-10 for the accumulating half; the telemetry half ships on.
- *Risk:* Inherits M-10's. Additionally: forcing `footprint = consumed` on an exhausting cycle at PIVOT_SELECT=0 shortens plan['footprint'] and therefore fp_retained_pct, the stickiness meter — a measured baseline changes shape on exactly the degraded cycles.
- *Collides with:* M-10, M-11, n-18

**M-31 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5070 · triple-blind **CONFIRMED****

- *Defect:* D-C (dark): budget walk charges macro ticker ~$5 while its ladder commits ~$30/side; partial ladder can rest
- *Fix:* One mechanism, three limbs, all inside the dark D-C feature. (1) SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH: add `_macro_probe_cap_usd()` returning `2.0 * min(MACRO_PROBE_USD / 2.0, 0.9 * HELD_MAX_USD)` and use it in _macro_probe_quotes (side_cap = _macro_probe_cap_usd()/2.0, replacing :697) AND in the select-budget walk (:5061-5070): `if _t9 in MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS: _est9 = _macro_probe_cap_usd()` placed BEFORE the explore clamp and the D3_RAMP min, so a designated ticker is charged what it can actually commit rather than a 5-25ct rung it is exempt from at sizing (_d3_apply_ramp exempts macro_probe at :646). (2) ATOMIC PLACEMENT: in the create loop (:5830-5856) pre-compute per-ticker macro_probe group cost once — built only when MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS is non-empty, so a flag-off cycle is byte-identical — and gate the WHOLE group against the same capital limb the per-order gate uses; if the group does not fit, skip every order in it and count macro_probe_partial_refused. (3) CONFIG COHERENCE: at startup, if MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS is set and MACRO_PROBE_USD/2 > 0.9*HELD_MAX_USD, print the effective per-side cap and both knob values — same idiom as the SERIES_MAX_USD footgun warning at :3945-3950.
- *Why:* A partial ladder cannot reach Target Size, so per LIP R3 every snapshot in that market is EXCLUDED — the probe pays nobody while carrying full fill risk, which is precisely the state _macro_probe_quotes refuses to create atomically. The walk's under-charge is what admits the market into a cycle that cannot fund the ladder, so the two limbs must be fixed together or the atomic gate just turns a partial rest into no rest.
- *Standing detector:* `macro_probe_partial_refused` and a per-cycle invariant `macro_probe_under_target` (designated markets where our resting depth on a side is > 0 but leaves ext + ours < target) — both on the plan row, both must be 0. Plus the charged-vs-committed pair `macro_est_usd` against the market's realised _mkt_capital, so any future drift between what the walk estimates and what a market actually commits is visible the cycle it appears — that is the generalisation of this defect, and it applies to non-macro tickers too.
- *Test:* test_dc_macro_probe.py, two failing-before cases. (a) test_walk_charges_macro_ladder_not_rung: footprint of one designated + one ordinary ticker, D3_RAMP=1, _total_cap sized so only one fits. BEFORE the macro ticker is charged _d3_est_ct (5) and both are admitted; AFTER it is charged _macro_probe_cap_usd() and plan['select_budget_used'] reflects the true number. (b) test_macro_ladder_is_all_or_nothing: a 6-order macro ladder with `committed` pre-set so only 2 orders fit under _total_cap(). BEFORE 2 creates are issued and partial depth rests; AFTER 0 creates are issued and macro_probe_partial_refused == 1. Companion pin: with MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS empty, both the walk and the create loop produce byte-identical output to current code.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no new flag — every limb sits inside the D-C feature, which is already dark via an empty MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS. The create-loop pre-pass must short-circuit on the empty set so a flag-off cycle is provably byte-identical. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Once D-C is armed, charging the true ceiling means one or two designated tickers consume $60-120 of a select budget that is _total_cap()*(1+SELECT_BUDGET_MARGIN) — at SELECT_BUDGET_MARGIN=-0.3 that is a visible eviction of ordinary earners, a deliberate allocation change that will move the capture baseline in the same week B8 wants a clean read. The atomic gate can also leave a designated market resting NOTHING for many cycles on a busy book, which an operator reads as 'the probe never fires' rather than 'the probe was refused' — macro_probe_partial_refused is the only thing that distinguishes them, so that telemetry is load-bearing, not decorative. And the whole design hangs on the still-unruled MACRO_PROBE_USD / HELD_MAX_USD pair: raise MACRO_PROBE_USD without raising HELD_MAX_USD and _macro_probe_cap_usd() stays silently HELD_MAX_USD-bound — the walk charge is then right while the operator's intent is not, which is exactly what limb (3) exists to surface.
- *Collides with:* m-16, M-9, n-55


### MINOR findings

**m-1 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:448**

- *Defect:* WS hot path calls desired_quotes without own_orders — F5 double-count survives there (WS_HOT=1 only)
- *Fix:* SAME ROOT CAUSE AND SAME FIX AS M-13 — see the M-13 entry for the concrete diff (hoist the `std_lock` standing read above the quote call in `Daemon.hot_event`, pass `own_orders=M._own_order_levels(standing_t)`, add the desired_quotes kwarg-parity self-check). One commit closes M-13, m-1 and n-51; no separate change here.

m-1's specific contribution to the design is the FAILURE DIRECTION, which is what makes the fix urgent-but-not-dangerous: the divergence collapses to `desired == []` → `hot_reprice_ops` returns no ops → `no_op`. There is no wrong order, only silently missing hot coverage. That is why this ships as a plain correctness fix under the existing WS_HOT flag rather than as a blocker rewrite, and why the `hot_outcomes` reason-histogram detector (M-13) is the load-bearing half: without it, "hot coverage silently shrank" has no observable signature at all.
- *Why:* Same mechanism as M-13: the WS mirror book contains our own resting orders, so a gate evaluated without `own_orders` double-counts us in its own denominator and halves the modelled share on exactly the solo books we target.
- *Standing detector:* See M-13 — `hot_model_params` parity row (class) + per-cycle `hot_outcomes` reason histogram (instance recurrence).
- *Test:* See M-13's `test_hot_path_passes_f5_own_orders_kills_broken_variant`. Add one m-1-specific assertion in the same file: with a solo-book fixture (our 20ct the only depth at the reference) and PRESENCE_GATE on, the real `desired_quotes` returns [] without `own_orders` and non-[] with it — pinning the direction of the divergence rather than just the kwarg's presence.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — gated by the existing KALSHI_WS_HOT flag, which is 0 live.
- *Risk:* See M-13. The m-1-specific residue: because the failure was silent skipping, we have NO measurement of how often hot would have acted. Arming WS_HOT=1 after this fix therefore steps from an unknown-but-small write rate to an unknown-but-larger one in a single move. Arm with `KALSHI_WS_HOT_WRITES_PER_S` lowered for the first session and read the `hot_outcomes` histogram before restoring 3.0.
- *Collides with:* M-13, n-51

**m-2 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2560**

- *Defect:* F5 subtracts cycle-start standing — a fill before the book fetch over-subtracts rivals for one cycle
- *Fix:* ROOT FIX: never compose the standing snapshot and the book snapshot as if they were simultaneous when an intervening state change is observable.

(a) run_once already reads positions (`_held_cost`) AFTER the standing read. Build, in the live branch right after `held_by` is available (~:4218):
    _fill_suspect = {t for t in set(held_by) | set(st.get("prev_held_by") or {})
                     if abs(float(held_by.get(t, 0.0))
                            - float((st.get("prev_held_by") or {}).get(t, 0.0))) > 1e-9}
  and persist `st["prev_held_by"] = {t: float(v) for t, v in held_by.items()}` beside the existing `st["prev_standing_tickers"]` write (~:5454).

(b) NEW helper beside `_own_order_levels` (~:3548):
    def _f5_own_levels(ticker, rows, fill_suspect):
        '''F5 own-order levels for ONE ticker, or None when a position change was observed in
        the window that straddles the standing read. The standing snapshot may then list depth
        the fetched book no longer holds; subtracting it removes RIVAL size and INFLATES our
        modeled share. None = no subtraction = the conservative direction, self-correcting next
        cycle.'''
        if ticker in fill_suspect:
            _SILENT["f5_own_sub_skipped_fill"] += 1
            return None
        return _own_order_levels(rows)

(c) The four call sites (~:5291 desired_quotes, ~:5390 telemetry row, ~:5419 full-size cache row, and the sweeper seam from m-39's fix) switch from `_own_order_levels(_f5_standing.get(t))` to `_f5_own_levels(t, _f5_standing.get(t), _fill_suspect)`.

(d) BELT, inside the subtraction itself (folded into M-2's `_own_rest_df` helper): clamp each own row's count to the depth the fetched book actually shows at that price, consuming it so two own rows at one price cannot double-subtract; count clips in `_SILENT["own_sub_clipped"]`. This catches the solo/thin-book case (where our fill removes the only depth at that level) that the position-change guard would also catch, and additionally catches cancel/WS-skew cases the position feed cannot see. It SUBSUMES the blind-review-#4 `p <= ref` guard, but keep that guard active in legacy (LIP_REF=0) mode for byte-equivalence.
- *Why:* `_live_standing` is read once at cycle start and books are fetched later in the same cycle (later still under the WS mirror). A fill in between leaves the standing snapshot listing depth the book no longer contains, so the F5 subtraction removes phantom size from the rival denominator and inflates our modeled share — anti-conservative, on exactly the markets that just showed adverse-fill pressure.
- *Standing detector:* `_SILENT["f5_own_sub_skipped_fill"]` and `_SILENT["own_sub_clipped"]` surfaced in the plan row. Both are direct recurrence counters: a rising skip rate means the standing read is chronically stale relative to the book (which is also the cleanest live signal of WS-mirror vs REST skew under WS_BOOK_COLD=1), and any non-zero `own_sub_clipped` means we are still claiming depth the book cannot show.
- *Test:* Failing-before, in test_db_reward_fixes.py (the F5 file, where this is F5's residual):
  T1 unit: `_f5_own_levels("T", rows, {"T"})` returns None and increments the counter; with an empty suspect set it returns the same dict `_own_order_levels` returns. (Fails today: the function does not exist.)
  T2 integration through the existing MockClient/_run harness: standing lists 50ct at 0.30 on ticker T; the mocked positions read returns a position for T different from `st["prev_held_by"]`; the book returns [(0.30, 200.0)]. Assert the emitted telemetry row has `y_rest_df == 0.0` (no subtraction) — today it is 50.0.
  T3 no-change pin: a ticker with no position change keeps the subtraction exactly (byte-identical row).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* NO flag needed — every branch is conservative (subtract less => larger denominator => smaller modeled share => fewer admits). This meets the 'provably no-op-or-safer' exemption. State that plainly in the commit message rather than hiding behind a flag nobody will ever flip.
- *Risk:* (1) The guard is one-cycle CONSERVATIVE by construction: a position change that happened BEFORE the standing read (so the snapshot is actually fine) still suppresses the subtraction for one cycle, understating our share and possibly tipping the presence/capture gate CLOSED on a market we legitimately hold — i.e. it can cause a one-cycle skip on a market we just filled in. That is the safe direction but it is not free: repeated fills on an active market could suppress the subtraction most cycles, permanently understating our share there. Watch the skip counter; if it is chronically high the real fix is a post-book standing re-read, not a wider guard. (2) `prev_held_by` is new persisted state — a state-file wipe or a restart makes every ticker look changed for one cycle (mitigate: treat a MISSING prev_held_by map as 'no suspects', not 'all suspects'). (3) The per-level clamp changes legacy-mode numbers in the narrow case where an own order sits at a price with less book depth than we claim; it is strictly conservative but it does break exact byte-equivalence with pre-fix behaviour on that input, so the flag-off no-op claim is 'no-op-or-safer', not 'byte-identical'.
- *Collides with:* M-2, m-39, n-38, n-4

**m-3 — kalshi_live/kalshi_estimates_recorder.py:78**

- *Defect:* Program map written non-atomically — one crash mid-write breaks the merge-only promise
- *Fix:* DUPLICATE OF M-16 — same file, same line region (:53-76 / :73), same root cause; one fix closes both. m-3 is the narrower statement (atomic write only), M-16 adds the recovery half. Implement M-16's helpers (1) `_save_map` tmp+os.replace+.bak, (2) `_load_map` that never silently resets, (3) `_fetch_programs` recovery over the unfiltered feed. If the operator wants the smallest possible change before the 2026-08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS checkpoint, land ONLY `_save_map` (tmp+os.replace, ~8 lines, effort S, provably safer than an in-place truncating write) and defer the recovery half.
- *Why:* Same mechanism as M-16: `json.dump(pmap, open(MAP_PATH,'w'))` truncates in place, so a crash/ENOSPC leaves torn JSON, and the next run's bare `except Exception: pmap = {}` restarts the merge-only map empty while only status=active is re-merged.
- *Standing detector:* Same as M-16 — the `map` health block (size / unmapped / unmapped_ids / recovered / lost) written into the estimates JSONL row and printed, so a map that shrank is visible in the recorded series rather than only in a journal nobody alerts on.
- *Test:* Same as M-16: test_map_write_is_atomic (monkeypatch json.dump to raise; assert existing file byte-unchanged — fails today) and test_torn_map_is_recovered_not_reset (fails today).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — a tmp+os.replace write is strictly safer than a truncating write and changes no value the map produces.
- *Risk:* Same as M-16 with the smaller scope: the atomic-write-only variant has essentially one risk — an os.replace on a filesystem where MAP_PATH and the tmp file are on different mounts would raise; they are in the same directory (HERE), so this is theoretical. Note the .bak adds one more file the deploy's md5 sweep will see.
- *Collides with:* M-16, n-31, n-30

**m-4 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3105**

- *Defect:* EST_FEED floors the activate-path gate with partly-banked dollars; commits capital for near-zero marginal reward
- *Fix:* Make the venue estimate contribute a MARGINAL number instead of a banked one. (1) In `_est_feed_cached` (:737-775) keep TWO points per market ticker from the recorder tail (`estimates-YYYYMM.jsonl`, bounded tail read): the newest snapshot and the newest snapshot at least KALSHI_EST_RATE_MIN_SPAN_S (new knob, default 900.0) older, resolved through the same program map and skipping ended programs exactly as today (:760-768). (2) Add `_est_marginal_usd(ticker, now_ts, seconds_left)` returning `max(0.0, (est_now - est_then) / span_s) * seconds_left`, or None when there are fewer than two usable snapshots / the span is short / either point is unresolvable. (3) In `_expected_credit_usd` (:1334-1340) replace the banked floor `max(ideal, _est)` with `max(ideal, _est_marginal)`; None -> the model alone decides (pre-D-A behaviour), counted. One change covers BOTH call sites (presence gate :2918 and the activate gate :3105). EST_FEED_MIN_FRAC stays as a redundant belt.
- *Why:* The venue estimate is accrued-to-date plus projection, so using it as an ENTRY floor answers 'has this program paid us' when the gate is asking 'will resting MORE here pay'; a first difference over the recorder's own 5-min series times the time left is the only quantity the $1 floor is entitled to compare against.
- *Standing detector:* Plan-row keys `est_marginal_used` (markets where the est floor bound the decision), `est_banked_suppressed` (banked-est >= MIN_CREDIT_USD but marginal-est < it — the exact defect population, reported not hidden), `est_rate_unavailable`, `est_snap_age_s`. Standing reconciliation: after each program end, join the est series against credit_history (w0b_payout_timing.py shape) and alarm when the integrated marginal rate diverges from the credited lump by >30% — that is the check that keeps the marginal reading honest as venue semantics move.
- *Test:* test_da_est_feed.py::test_est_floor_is_marginal_not_banked — write two synthetic snapshot lines 1800s apart for one program ($2.00 -> $2.05) plus a kalshi_program_map.json entry; market with 60 min left, model expectation $0.10, MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20, EST_FEED=1. BEFORE: _expected_credit_usd returns >= 2.05 and the activate gate ADMITS (commits up to MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL). AFTER: marginal = 0.05/1800*3600 = $0.10, gate refuses, est_banked_suppressed == 1. Companion pin: a single-snapshot file -> _est_marginal_usd is None and the result equals the pure-model value (fail-open preserved).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no new flag needed — EST_FEED=0 live, so this only changes unarmed code and the arming decision stays D-A's. The new KALSHI_EST_RATE_MIN_SPAN_S knob must be visible to _refresh_safety_knobs (m-6) or it ships with the silent-no-op defect. · **CONCERNING**

**m-7 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:743**

- *Defect:* _est_feed_cached rescans the entire monthly estimates jsonl every 120s to read one line (armed-only, efficiency)
- *Fix:* Replace the whole-file iteration in `_est_feed_cached` (:741-772) with a stat-gated bounded tail read. (1) Cache `(st_mtime, st_size)` of the newest estimates file alongside the table; if unchanged and a table is cached, return it with zero IO beyond the stat — the common case, since the recorder appends every 5 min while the cache refreshes every 120 s. (2) Otherwise open the file in binary, `seek(max(0, size - TAIL_BYTES))` with TAIL_BYTES=65536, read to EOF, split on b'\n', DISCARD the first fragment unless the seek landed at byte 0, and take the last non-empty line. If no complete line is found, double the tail (bounded at 1 MiB) and retry, then fall back to the existing full scan. (3) Preserve every existing semantic exactly: the EST_FEED_MAX_AGE_S staleness bound, fail-open {}, and the torn-last-line branch that keeps the previous good table while its snapshot is still fresh (:774-779).
- *Why:* The cache re-read walks every line of a monthly jsonl (~8,900 snapshots, each carrying a multi-KB estimate array) to obtain one line, synchronously inside `_expected_credit_usd` on the quote cycle — tens of MB of IO per refresh by month end, on a hot path whose whole value is queue position.
- *Standing detector:* plan['est_feed_read_bytes'] and plan['est_feed_read_ms'] emitted when EST_FEED is on, plus `_SILENT['est_feed_tail_fallback']` counting every widen-or-fall-back. A rising fallback count is the alarm that the recorder's line format or size profile changed and the fast path stopped working — today the cost is invisible entirely.
- *Test:* test_da_est_feed.py — (1) `test_tail_read_matches_full_scan`: build a fixture with 20,000 junk lines plus one valid final snapshot; assert the returned table equals the table produced by the pre-fix full scan. (2) `test_tail_read_does_not_scan_whole_file`: wrap the file object's read() with a byte counter; assert fewer than ~1 MiB read on a multi-MB fixture — fails before (reads the entire file). (3) `test_tail_read_boundary_cases`: file smaller than TAIL_BYTES, last line exactly at the chunk boundary, trailing newline absent.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — EST_FEED is already the flag and is 0 live, so this is a provable no-op on the running system; the change is behaviour-preserving by construction.
- *Risk:* Tail reading is a classic off-by-one source: a mishandled leading fragment yields truncated JSON, which routes to the except branch and KEEPS the previous table — fail-safe, but it would silently freeze the feed until the staleness bound expires, so the test must cover seek-at-0, an exact newline boundary, CRLF, and an empty file. The stat short-circuit could in principle serve a stale table if a writer rewrote the file in place with identical size and mtime (the recorder only appends, so this is theoretical, but it is a real weakening of the freshness guarantee). EST_FEED=0 live, so no live effect today.
- *Collides with:* m-15

**m-8 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1324**

- *Defect:* Floor-gate probe clamp condition diverges from the actual probe sizing clamp for allowlisted explore rows
- *Fix:* SHARED DESIGN — also closes m-58 (identical root cause, same lines). Extract ONE authority for 'how many contracts will actually rest on an accumulating quote for this row' and use it everywhere the size is MODELLED:

    def _eff_rest_ct(m, now_ts):
        """The count an ACCUMULATING quote will actually rest at for this row, after the
        explore probe clamp and the D3 ramp / new-series clamp. Single source of truth for
        every consumer that MODELS the resting size (floor gate, select-budget walk); the
        quote loop still APPLIES the clamps (it needs _d3_ramp_ct's first-seen registration)
        and its result is asserted against this."""
        ct = float(_d3_est_ct(m.get('ticker') or '', now_ts)) if D3_RAMP else float('inf')
        if EXPLORE_PROBE_CT > 0 and m.get('explore'):
            ct = min(ct, float(EXPLORE_PROBE_CT))
        return ct

Use it in `_expected_credit_usd` (replacing the inline eff_ct at :1323 and DELETING the `SERIES_ALLOW and series not in SERIES_ALLOW` condition at :1324-1325) and in the select-budget walk (:5063-5070, replacing both `min()`s). The allowlist carve-out is removed from the MODEL because the A1 exemption belongs where it is actually implemented — `rank(explore_exempt=frozenset(SERIES_ALLOW))` at :2201, which decides whether a row is TAGGED explore — while the L3 series-probe taint (:4094-4104 and the re-clamp :4578-4583) deliberately tags allowlist rows and the sizing clamp at :5311 honours that tag with no allowlist test.
- *Why:* Three sites answered 'what size will actually rest' with three different predicates; the floor gate's extra allowlist condition modelled up to 50 ct on rows that rest 5 ct, so it admitted markets whose real share cannot clear the venue's $1 program floor — the spread-thin-earn-zero class F3 was built to close, re-opened on exactly the rows the probe clamp fires on.
- *Standing detector:* In the quote loop, after the explore and ramp clamps have run, compare the MODELLED `_eff_rest_ct(m, now)` against the minimum accumulating count actually rested; count `qstats['eff_ct_mismatch']` (with the worst modelled/actual pair recorded) and emit `plan['eff_ct_mismatch']` as an always-emit key so 0 means 'checked and agreed'. Any future divergence between model and reality then shows up as a non-zero count on the next cycle instead of as a silent 10x over-admission.
- *Test:* test_presence_gate.py::test_floor_gate_models_probe_size_for_allowlisted_explore_row — SERIES_ALLOW=['KXAAAGAS'], EXPLORE_PROBE_CT=5, D3_RAMP=1 with a rung-3 first-seen for the ticker, the row tagged explore=True (the L3 taint shape), pool sized so the market clears MIN_CREDIT_USD at 50 ct and fails at 5 ct; assert `desired_quotes` returns [] and `stats['presence_skipped'] == 1`. Fails before: the gate models 50 ct, admits, and 5 ct then rests. Companion `test_gate_and_budget_walk_agree_on_eff_ct` asserts both consumers get the same number on the same row.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no flag — the direction is strictly declining-to-open, which is the safer half of the money path. But treat it as a measured change, not a silent one: it will move the admission baseline on the first cycle. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* GENUINELY CONCERNING: this is live-active (D3_RAMP=1 and PRESENCE_GATE=1 are confirmed in the live env; EXPLORE_PROBE_CT>0 with ALLOW_PROBE_EXCEPTION=1 is BELIEVED but I could not read live.env from this sandbox — confirm before deploy). The change is monotone in one direction: eff_ct can only fall, so the gate can only REFUSE MORE. When the L3 taint is broad — the m-58 failure shape, one permanently-OUT gas strike tainting every gas sibling — a receipt-proven payer lane can go from fully quoted to largely refused in a single deploy, cutting live reward income and moving the presence/coverage baseline that the D3 and W17 measurements are built on. It is the CORRECT refusal (5 ct genuinely cannot clear $1), but the honest alternative reading is that the real defect is the L3 taint being too broad; this fix does not fix that, it makes its cost VISIBLE instead of paid silently. Deploy watching plan['presence_skipped_markets'] and plan['series_probe'], and be ready to revert if quoted-market count collapses.
- *Collides with:* m-58, n-2, M-1, m-9

- *Defect:* ACTIVATE-path $1 floor lacks the JOIN path's resting-continuation exemption
- *Fix:* Give the activate-path floor the JOIN path's continuation exemption. **m-11 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2933**

- *Defect:* Floor-gate continuation status is amnestied by a one-cycle absence (rotation/fetch-fail) — stranding sub-$1 accruals
- *Fix:* Give the $1-floor gate the same absence memory F14 gave the D3 ramp clock. Three parts:

(a) NEW config beside PRESENCE_GATE (~:1211): `PRESENCE_KEEP_S = _envf("KALSHI_PRESENCE_KEEP_S", 0.0)` (0 = today's exact behavior) and module map `_PRESENCE_RESTED_AT = {}`.

(b) run_once, live branch where `own = own_resting(standing)` is computed (~:4205): stamp `_PRESENCE_RESTED_AT[t] = now.timestamp()` for every ticker with yes+no resting > 0; persist to `st["presence_rested_at"]` and restore it in the same place `_d3_first_seen_ensure` restores its map (~:560), pruning entries older than PRESENCE_KEEP_S. Deliberately the same shape as `_d3_prune_first_seen` (~:585) — same class, same lifecycle, so a reader who understands F14 understands this without new concepts.

(c) `desired_quotes` (~:2933): 
    _resting_here = (float((own or {}).get("yes") or 0.0) + float((own or {}).get("no") or 0.0)) > 0.0
    if not _resting_here and PRESENCE_KEEP_S > 0:
        _last = _PRESENCE_RESTED_AT.get(m.get("ticker"))
        if _last is not None and (now.timestamp() - float(_last)) <= PRESENCE_KEEP_S:
            _resting_here = True
            if stats is not None:
                stats["presence_continued_by_memory"] = stats.get("presence_continued_by_memory", 0) + 1
Reading a module global from `desired_quotes` matches the existing `_STRAND_STEP` precedent (~:2819), so the function signature — a hard contract with many callers and tests — is untouched.
- *Why:* Continuation status is derived from a SINGLE cycle's standing read. Any one-cycle absence (budget refusal, fetch failure, grace exhaustion) makes the gate treat a market we have been accruing in all window as a fresh flat entry and refuse re-admission on remaining-window credit — stranding the sub-$1 accrual that only continued presence can push over the venue's $1 cliff, after we already carried the fill risk to build it. Note DROP_GRACE=3 live already bridges pure ROTATION (the book is retained, so `own` stays non-zero); the residual class is budget refusal (the ticker IS in the footprint, so grace never applies), grace exhaustion, and post-fetch-fail cycles.
- *Standing detector:* Three counters in the plan row: `presence_continued_by_memory` (how often the memory was load-bearing), the existing `presence_continued_under_floor`, and NEW `presence_refused_after_recent_rest` — emitted even when PRESENCE_KEEP_S=0, i.e. it MEASURES THE DEFECT BEFORE THE FIX ARMS and proves it stays closed after. The class-level detector is the W10 pairing: for every program we rested in and then dropped, join credit_history to ask whether the program paid; a persistent 'rested >0, credited $0' bucket is the recurrence alarm for stranded accrual regardless of which gate stranded it.
- *Test:* Failing-before, new test_presence_keep.py:
  T1 THE FIX PIN: a market whose remaining-window expected credit is below MIN_CREDIT_USD, with `own` empty but `_PRESENCE_RESTED_AT[ticker] = now - 60`. With PRESENCE_KEEP_S=0 -> `desired_quotes` returns [] and `presence_skipped` increments (today's behavior). With PRESENCE_KEEP_S=600 -> quotes are returned and `presence_continued_by_memory` == 1.
  T2 EXPIRY: same fixture with the stamp older than PRESENCE_KEEP_S -> refused again.
  T3 NO-OP: PRESENCE_KEEP_S=0 leaves every existing test_presence_gate.py assertion untouched.
  T4 STATE ROUND-TRIP: the map survives a save_state/load_state cycle (the F14 failure mode was exactly a map that did not).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* YES — KALSHI_PRESENCE_KEEP_S=0 default is the flag (no new boolean needed; 0 is a provable no-op). Do not arm it in the same deploy as KALSHI_LIP_REF. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* GENUINELY CONCERNING — this LOOSENS a live money gate. (1) The premise is that banked accrual makes continuation +EV, and that premise is MODELED, not measured: EST_FEED (the venue's own accrual number) is dark and gated at EST_FEED_MIN_FRAC=0.25 precisely in the late window where accrual dominates, and the close+1 vs program-end payout question is still open pending the 08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS checkpoint. If the modeled accrual was never real (the W10 sub-$1 drive-by class), this fix keeps us paying fill risk in the worst markets for up to KEEP_S after every drop. (2) It compounds with M-2: the LIP fix LOWERS modeled credit, pushing more markets under the floor, and this fix keeps us in more of them — arming both without re-fitting MIN_CREDIT_USD is the bad combination. (3) The memory can re-admit a market the gate refused for a REASON other than the floor if a future edit reuses `_resting_here` (today it gates only the floor branch) — bound it by keeping the memory read inside the PRESENCE_GATE block, never hoisted. (4) New persisted state: a corrupt or hand-edited `presence_rested_at` map silently extends admission; prune on load and treat unparseable entries as absent.
- *Collides with:* M-2

**m-12 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:157**

- *Defect:* Malformed env values silently revert to code defaults and are invisible to env_absent telemetry
- *Fix:* Add a malformed-value REGISTRY beside `_ENV_DECLARED` (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:144) and make it loud. (1) `_ENV_MALFORMED = {}` module-level. (2) In `_envi` (:154) and `_envf` (:162), the `except (TypeError, ValueError)` branch records `_ENV_MALFORMED[name] = os.environ.get(name)` before returning the default; the success path `_ENV_MALFORMED.pop(name, None)`. (3) New `env_malformed()` returning `dict(_ENV_MALFORMED)`, symmetric with `env_absent()` (:180). (4) In run_once beside the existing env_absent block (:4029-4054): `plan['env_malformed_n']`, and the name->raw map emitted with the SAME signature-dedup pattern the env_absent list already uses (row-size discipline), plus a per-cycle `print('WARNING <n> knob(s) SET BUT UNPARSEABLE -> running the CODE DEFAULT: ...')` and a second, louder line when any malformed knob is in `_SAFETY_KNOBS` (:148), mirroring `env_absent_safety`. (5) Symmetrize the hot-reload asymmetry: `_refresh_safety_knobs`'s WATCH branch (:3918-3921) currently `continue`s silently on a malformed cast while the restart-only branch (:3902-3909) prints — give the WATCH branch the same WARNING (naming gname, the raw value, and the running value that is being KEPT) plus `_SILENT['knob_malformed'] += 1`. (6) Add 'env_malformed_n' to `_ALWAYS_EMIT_COUNTERS` (:1071) so 0 means 'checked and clean', never 'not evaluated'.
- *Why:* A knob that is SET but unparseable silently reverts to the code default while `env_absent()` — which only reports keys missing from os.environ — reports it as configured, so the operator's model of the loss envelope can be wrong with zero signal anywhere; the reload path repeats the asymmetry, warning loudly on the restart-only tier and staying silent on the safety tier.
- *Standing detector:* plan['env_malformed_n'] (always-emitted, 0 = evaluated-and-clean), plan['env_malformed'] name->raw on signature change, a per-cycle WARNING naming any malformed _SAFETY_KNOBS entry, and _SILENT['knob_malformed'] for the hot-reload tier. A non-zero count on any row is the alarm; today the same condition produces nothing at all.
- *Test:* test_env_visibility.py — (1) `test_malformed_safety_knob_is_named`: monkeypatch KALSHI_DAILY_LOSS_HALT_USD='10.5 usd', call `q._envf('KALSHI_DAILY_LOSS_HALT_USD', 20.0)`, assert it returns 20.0 AND `'KALSHI_DAILY_LOSS_HALT_USD' in q.env_malformed()` — fails before (no such function, no record). (2) `test_plan_row_names_malformed_knob`: run one cycle through the MockClient harness (`test_live_hardening._run`), assert `plan['env_malformed_n'] == 1` and the knob name is in `plan['env_malformed']` — fails before (keys absent). (3) `test_malformed_watch_knob_warns`: write an env file with `KALSHI_HELD_MAX_USD=abc`, set KALSHI_ENV_FILE, call `_refresh_safety_knobs()`, assert capsys contains 'HELD_MAX_USD' and 'malformed' and the global is unchanged — fails before (silent continue).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — pure telemetry plus prints; the order path is byte-identical, so it is provably a no-op-or-safer.
- *Risk:* No order-path change — telemetry and prints only. Real costs: (a) the plan row grows, and the env_absent DEDUP exists precisely because this field was measured at 44.4% of a live row, so the malformed map must use the same signature-gated emission or it re-creates that bloat; (b) `_ENV_MALFORMED` is populated at IMPORT, so a hot-edited env file is not reflected in it — the plan row states the import-time truth and the hot-reload path needs its own counter (included), and this split must be documented or it becomes a new confusion; (c) if any knob is deliberately set to a non-numeric sentinel today it will start printing a warning every cycle — I found no such use, but I could not read the live env file from this sandbox to confirm.
- *Collides with:* m-13

**m-13 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:177**

- *Defect:* _envb accepts ONLY the literal '1' — 'true'/'yes'/'on' silently disable default-on safety flags
- *Fix:* Give `_envb` (:170-177) an explicit boolean vocabulary and make an unknown token fail toward the PROTECTION, not away from it. Add `_ENVB_TRUE = {'1','true','yes','on'}` / `_ENVB_FALSE = {'0','false','no','off'}` (value `.strip().lower()`). `_envb` then: absent -> `default_on`; token in TRUE -> True; token in FALSE -> False; anything else -> record in m-12's `_ENV_MALFORMED`, print a WARNING at import naming the knob and the raw value, and resolve to `default_on` (a garbage value can never silently DISARM a default-ON backstop). Add `_cast_envb(s)` that returns the bool and RAISES ValueError on an unknown token, and change the hot-reload watch entry `'KALSHI_TAKER_FLATTEN': ('TAKER_FLATTEN', int)` (:3850) to `('TAKER_FLATTEN', _cast_envb)` so 'true' hot-applies instead of raising into a silent `continue`. Also print the RESOLVED value of all four `_envb` knobs (JOIN_ALWAYS :1501, THROTTLE_SMART :1542, TAKER_FLATTEN :1587, PAIR_BOTH_SIDES :1644) once at startup and carry them as `plan['flags']`.
- *Why:* `os.environ.get(name, '1' if default_on else None) == '1'` treats every truthy spelling as OFF, so an operator following the module's own printed instruction to 'set each explicitly' with `KALSHI_TAKER_FLATTEN=true` disarms the guaranteed-flat backstop; the reload path's int() cast then raises and keeps the wrong running value, so the emergency lever cannot repair it either.
- *Standing detector:* Reuses m-12's plan['env_malformed'] for unknown tokens, plus a new plan['flags'] stating the RESOLVED value of all four boolean knobs on every row — that is the exact fact the operator was trying to confirm when they typed 'true', and it removes the need to reason about parser semantics ever again.
- *Test:* test_env_visibility.py — replace `test_envb_non_one_value_is_off` with `test_envb_accepts_documented_true_spellings`: 'true'/'TRUE'/'yes'/'on' with default_on=True -> True and with default_on=False -> True; '0'/'false'/'off'/'no' -> False in both; 'banana' -> equals default_on AND appears in `q.env_malformed()`. Add `test_taker_flatten_hot_reload_accepts_true`: env file with `KALSHI_TAKER_FLATTEN=true`, `_refresh_safety_knobs()`, assert `q.TAKER_FLATTEN` is truthy and the SAFETY-KNOB-LIVE-APPLIED line printed — fails before (int('true') raises -> silent continue).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no new flag, but CONDITIONAL: ship only after the pre-deploy grep proves every live _envb value is exactly '1' or '0' (then it is provably a no-op). If any other spelling exists on the box, do NOT ship it as a silent parser widening — that is a live flag flip disguised as a bug fix. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* GENUINELY CONCERNING and unverifiable from this sandbox: I cannot read the VPS live.env, so I cannot prove no _envb knob is currently spelled with a non-'1' truthy token. If one is, this fix FLIPS that flag at the next restart on a live money path — TAKER_FLATTEN going OFF->ON starts real taker crossings (fees + realized losses booked at the touch) near settlement and on hard inventory breaches; JOIN_ALWAYS going OFF->ON is a drill switch that changes the entire quoting universe; PAIR_BOTH_SIDES changes reducing-side sizing. Second: this fix REPLACES an existing green test (test_env_visibility.py::test_envb_non_one_value_is_off) that deliberately pins the current behaviour as byte-identical to the expressions _envb replaced — that test asserts the defect, and swapping it must be stated in the commit message, not slipped in. MANDATORY pre-deploy gate: `grep -E 'KALSHI_(TAKER_FLATTEN|PAIR_BOTH_SIDES|THROTTLE_SMART|JOIN_ALWAYS)=' <live.env>` and confirm every value is exactly 1 or 0 — if so the change is a provable no-op and can land; if not, land m-12 first, let the operator restate the knob explicitly, then land this.
- *Collides with:* m-12

- *Recommendation:* NO CODE CHANGE — No separate commit: this is the class m-6's derived-knob-map fix closes wholesale. A hand-added entry per flag would re-create the very list that fell behind.

PRESENCE_GATE is the sharpest instance because it is armed live and controls the $1.20 entry floor.

If the 08-07 checkpoint shows the floor gate refusing payers, the operator still needs a restart to change it; the fix removes the silence, not the restart.
