**m-15 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:764**

- *Defect:* D-A est feed: a program-map row with MISSING end_date is treated as live evidence while an unparseable one is excluded
- *Fix:* In `_est_feed_cached`'s program-map loop (:763-768), make a MISSING program end symmetric with an UNPARSEABLE one — both are 'not evidence': `_pend = pr.get('end_date')` -> `if not _pend: skipped_no_end += 1; continue` BEFORE the try, then keep the existing `parse_iso(_pend).timestamp() <= now_ts -> continue` for ended programs and the except branch (counting `skipped_bad_end`) for unparseable ones. Stash both counters on `_EST_FEED_CACHE` so run_once can emit them next to the D-A telemetry.
- *Why:* The D-A floor is only allowed to be raised by credit that is still EARNABLE; a map row with no end_date cannot be shown to be unended, so summing it re-admits banked, already-accrued dollars through the missing-field path and floors an ENTRY gate open on money that cannot grow — the exact accrued-not-earnable class reviews #3/#4 were written against, re-entering through the hole between the two guarded branches.
- *Standing detector:* plan['est_feed_rows_no_end'], plan['est_feed_rows_bad_end'] and plan['est_feed_tickers'] (table size), emitted when EST_FEED is on. A non-zero no_end count is the alarm that the program map is losing the field — and it simultaneously explains why the floor stopped helping, which today would look like an unexplained admission drop.
- *Test:* test_da_est_feed.py::test_row_without_end_date_is_not_evidence — program map row carrying market_ticker but NO end_date, snapshot with a $3 estimate for that program_id; assert `_est_feed_cached(now)` returns {} and the no_end counter is 1. Fails before: returns {mt: 3.0}, which then floors the gate. Companion `test_row_with_future_end_is_evidence` pins that the good path is unchanged.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — EST_FEED is already the flag and is 0 live; within the armed path the change is strictly the conservative direction.
- *Risk:* Strictly reduces the estimate table, so with EST_FEED armed the gate falls back to the model more often — fewer admissions, never more. The honest downside: if kalshi_program_map.json routinely lacks end_date, D-A becomes a near-no-op and the operator would be arming a feature that does nothing; the new counter makes that visible instead of silently wrong, but it means the 2026-08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS est->credit checkpoint could observe an empty feed if EST_FEED is armed before the map is checked. EST_FEED=0 live, so no behaviour change today.
- *Collides with:* m-7

**m-16 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3732**

- *Defect:* cap_desired keeps the WHOLE quote list of any unwind-bearing market — accumulating siblings skip the walk
- *Fix:* In cap_desired (:3731-3736) split the unconditional keep by POLARITY instead of by ticker. Build `kept[t]` from the unwind quotes only, charging total/fam on that notional; collect the ticker's non-unwind quotes in `_deferred_acc[t]`. Then include those tickers in the priority `order` (drop the blanket `if t not in kept` exclusion for them), cost each at _mkt_capital(_deferred_acc[t]), and on admission do `kept[t] = kept[t] + _deferred_acc[t]` charging only the accumulating notional. Returns and the tail_cut semantics are unchanged. Ships behind KALSHI_CAP_SPLIT_UNWIND (default 0 => byte-identical), armed together with PAIR_UNWIND.
- *Why:* `any(q.reason=='unwind')` grants the WHOLE quote list a cap exemption, so accumulating legs that merely share a ticker with an exit skip the priority walk entirely — they consume total-cap headroom ahead of higher-usd_day markets, and the unconditional total can already exceed _total_cap() before the walk starts, after which the walk admits nothing at all.
- *Standing detector:* Plan keys `cap_keep_mixed` (tickers whose unconditional keep contained both polarities) and `cap_keep_usd` (dollars charged by unconditional keeps BEFORE the walk starts), with a WARNING when cap_keep_usd > 0.9 * _total_cap(). That pair is the starvation signature independent of which feature produced the mixed list, so it also catches the next producer.
- *Test:* test_select_budget.py::test_cap_desired_splits_mixed_polarity_ticker — desired = {A: [unwind 10@0.50, join 100@0.50], B: [join 100@0.50]}, usd_day {A: 1, B: 1000}, _total_cap patched to 60. BEFORE: A is kept whole ($55) and B is tail-cut. AFTER (flag on): A keeps only its unwind ($5), B is kept ($50), A's join is dropped, cap_keep_mixed == 1. Companion pin: a pure-unwind ticker is still kept unconditionally with the flag on (exits are never gated).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_CAP_SPLIT_UNWIND, default 0. It changes which markets get funded on a live money path, and the only thing it currently protects against is a dark feature (PAIR_UNWIND). · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Real and specific. Today's only mixed lists come from the fetch-fail retention (:5243-5249) and drop-grace retention of held books — lists deliberately built to MATCH `standing` so the diff emits nothing. Splitting exposes their accumulating half to the cap, so on a tight-capital cycle cap_desired drops that half and diff_orders CANCELS live resting orders that a transient book-fetch error was explicitly not supposed to disturb, surrendering queue position (the thing rewards pay for) on a network blip. Drop-grace's own comment (:5709-5710) says retained books SHOULD be cap-subject, so the grace path is arguably repaired by this while the fetch-fail path is arguably regressed — that asymmetry is why the flag ships off and why arming should be paired with PAIR_UNWIND rather than done alone.
- *Collides with:* M-31, n-15, n-55

**m-17 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5841**

- *Defect:* Per-side create failure leaves a one-sided accumulating book resting up to the ratchet cool-off
- *Fix:* Telemetry unconditional, enforcement dark. (1) ALWAYS compute, after the order-apply block, the post-cycle live ACCUMULATING sides per ticker = (standing survivors not in cancelled_ok) + created_ok, and emit plan['onesided_accumulating'] = count of tickers whose `desired` intended BOTH accumulating sides but which end the cycle with exactly one live. Nothing measures this today. Tickers whose desired is unwind-only (exits, governed strips, retained/grace books) are excluded by construction. (2) Behind `KALSHI_TWO_SIDED_ENFORCE` (default 0): cancel the orphaned accumulating side on those tickers in the same cycle (a cancel is never ratchet-blocked), counted as plan['onesided_unwound'], and count those cancels into n-13's write_ops. Next cycle re-quotes both sides normally — this is invariant enforcement and pacing, never a ban: nothing is blacklisted, nothing is skipped by name. (3) The same pass covers the OTHER producer of the identical state: a mid-loop capital/funding-gate skip that lands one side of a join (:5844-5856). One mechanism, both causes.
- *Why:* The JOIN branch's doctrine is 'BOTH sides ALWAYS rest' (:3148-3150), but execution is per-order and nothing re-checks the invariant afterwards; one rejected create (post-only cross on a stale mirror, tick complaint) plus the J6 ratchet leaves a standing one-sided accumulating lean for up to CREATE_FAIL_RATCHET_MAX_S=3600 s — i.e. we lean directionally exactly while our pricing source is degraded.
- *Standing detector:* plan['onesided_accumulating'], shipped ON regardless of the enforcement flag — a per-cycle count of live one-sided accumulating books. It is the class alarm and it also catches producers nobody has enumerated (family cap, write-budget group drops, breaker interactions, retention edge cases).
- *Test:* Add to test_strand_onesided.py: MockClient rejecting the YES create and accepting the NO on one ticker -> assert plan['onesided_accumulating'] == 1 (pre-fix the key does not exist: failing-before) and, with KALSHI_TWO_SIDED_ENFORCE=1, that the NO order id appears in the same cycle's cancels. Plus a no-op pin that a held ticker resting only its unwind never counts.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_TWO_SIDED_ENFORCE default 0; the detector half is unconditional. Measure onesided_accumulating for several days before arming. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Enforcement pays a real cost: cancelling a good resting side destroys queue position and removes presence, and LIP pays for resting — so on a venue with sporadic create rejections this converts a partial book into no book. It can also oscillate (cancel -> both re-created -> one rejected -> cancel) at up to one round trip per cycle until the ratchet arms, burning write tokens the n-13 budget now has to absorb. The lean it prevents is bounded by the D3 rungs (5-50 ct) and by the holding=>exit-only flip after any fill, so the loss it avoids may well be smaller than the presence it costs — that is unmeasured, which is why enforcement ships off and telemetry ships on.
- *Collides with:* n-13, M-10

**m-18 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2517**

- *Defect:* Throttle step-back can cross the entry band and drop the throttled side instead of shrinking it
- *Fix:* Make `_throttled_quote` (:2498-2517) band-aware — it already knows how to trade price for size in the THROTTLE_SMART branch:

    stepped = round(best - TICK * THROTTLE_STEP_TICKS, 4)
    if not _ok_entry_price(stepped):
        _THROTTLE_BAND_HITS[0] += 1                 # module-level list, counted ALWAYS
        if THROTTLE_BAND_KEEP:
            return best, max(MIN_QUOTE_CT, int(shrunk * (1 - over)))
    return stepped, shrunk

i.e. when the step would leave the entry band, stay AT reference and take the whole risk reduction from SIZE (the double-shrink arithmetic the SMART free-win branch already uses), behind `KALSHI_THROTTLE_BAND_KEEP` (default 0 = today's behaviour byte-for-byte). Use a module-level counter list (the `_SERIES_CAP_SOLO` pattern at :5976) rather than adding a `stats` parameter, so the signature stays intact per Rule 2. `_ok_entry_price` is defined at :2379, above this function, so no reordering is needed.
- *Why:* The throttle's contract is 'smaller and slower to fill', but at best = MIN_PRICE_DOLLARS + 1 tick the stepped price fails the final emit's exclusive lower bound (:2381, :3210-3215) and the side emits NOTHING — un-pairing the join, and a one-sided book is EXCLUDED from every LIP snapshot, so both sides' credit is forfeited at exactly the moment the event is already directional.
- *Standing detector:* plan['throttle_band_dropped'] — an ALWAYS-EMIT counter incremented whenever the stepped price falls outside the entry band, emitted with the flag OFF so the size of the class is measured before anything is armed — plus plan['throttle_band_kept'] once the flag is on. Today this event leaves no trace of any kind.
- *Test:* Unit on `_throttled_quote` with MIN_PRICE_DOLLARS=0.01, best=0.02, THROTTLE_STEP_TICKS=1, over=0.5: with KALSHI_THROTTLE_BAND_KEEP=0 assert the returned price is 0.01 and that `desired_quotes` emits NO yes leg on that book (pins today's defect as the measured baseline) and that `_THROTTLE_BAND_HITS[0] == 1`; with the flag on assert the price is 0.02, the count is <= the flag-off count, and BOTH legs are emitted. Fails before: the flag and the counter do not exist, so the second half is unwritable.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_THROTTLE_BAND_KEEP, default 0. It changes live order placement in a directional-risk regime, so it must be measured (counter live, behaviour dark) before it is armed. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* GENUINELY CONCERNING: with the flag on, this KEEPS a side resting that currently disappears, on a book already drifting against us — the band-drop is today acting as an accidental brake. A bid left at 0.02 on a collapsing longshot can fill and leave inventory in the worst regime (the throttle only fired because |event_delta| > INV_SOFT_CT). The reward argument is solid (one-sided = $0 under the two-sided exclusion), but the risk side is entirely unmeasured, and the class size is unknown — which is why the counter must ship live first and the behaviour must ship dark behind it. Also: the fix cannot be validated without live exposure; a sandbox test can pin the arithmetic but not the fill outcome.

**m-19 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:4872**

- *Defect:* day_baseline_reset marker surviving the day roll fires on cycle 2 of a later day — silent budget amnesty
- *Fix:* Swap the operands at :4872 so the one-shot marker is always consumed on an untorn cycle: `elif _consume_day_baseline_marker() or st.get('equity_day') != _day:`. Python evaluates left to right, so the marker is consumed on the day-roll cycle too and cannot survive to fire on cycle 2. Everything else stays: `plan['day_baseline_reset'] = 1` remains guarded by `st.get('equity_day') == _day`, so a day-roll consumption correctly emits NO amnesty marker (the reset was the ordinary UTC roll) while a genuine same-day operator restart still emits it, and torn cycles still never consume because the branch lives inside the untorn arm. ALSO CLOSES n-20.
- *Why:* `A or B` short-circuits: on the first cycle of a new day the day-roll arm is already True, so the marker is NOT consumed while the baseline reseeds anyway; the stale marker then fires on cycle 2 with equity_day == _day and re-baselines day_start/day_peak mid-day — erasing the day's peak and refreshing the $10 drawdown budget on a day the operator never named.
- *Standing detector:* Always emit plan['day_baseline_marker_consumed'] = 1 on consumption, independent of which arm re-baselined, so a re-baseline can never appear without a matching consumption record. Class detector: a check in kalshi_plans_hourly.py that alarms on any day_baseline_reset=1 row that is NOT the first row of its UTC day — that catches any future path that re-baselines mid-day, not just this one.
- *Test:* Extend test_a7_day_baseline_reset.py::test_marker_does_not_survive_the_day_roll — state equity_day='20260805', marker file present, fixed now on 2026-08-06, drive the meter for two cycles with equity moving slightly. BEFORE: cycle 1 seeds without consuming (the marker file still exists) and cycle 2 emits day_baseline_reset=1 and re-baselines equity_day_peak to current equity. AFTER: cycle 1 consumes (marker gone) and seeds with NO day_baseline_reset key; cycle 2 takes the normal arm and preserves equity_day_peak. Companion pin (existing behaviour must survive): marker dropped with equity_day already == today still produces day_baseline_reset=1 on the next untorn cycle.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — it can only tighten the daily-loss governor; a flag on a stricter halt meter would be a flag on safety.
- *Risk:* Strictly stricter: it removes an unnamed amnesty so the halt measures true drawdown from the real day peak. The one behaviour an operator could miss: a marker dropped shortly BEFORE 00:00Z that they intended to apply to the NEW day is now consumed by the day roll and produces no same-day reset. That is the correct reading of 'the marker names a restart, not a day', but it differs from what a late-night operator might expect, so restart_bundle.sh's runbook line must say so — otherwise the first time it happens it reads as the marker being broken. Tightening a live loss governor is never risk-free in the other direction either: a genuinely-intended reset that is now refused leaves the halt measuring from a pre-restart peak, which can halt earlier than the operator wanted.
- *Collides with:* n-20

**m-20 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3021**

- *Defect:* NETEV model fallback and capture gate score full-join capture while the D3 ramp rests 5-10ct (no F3)
- *Fix:* SHARED ROOT FIX #3 — one ramp basis for every gate that models reward at join size. Closes m-20 and m-59.

(a) EXTRACT the F3 block currently inline in `_expected_credit_usd` (~:1321-1331) into a helper placed just above it:
    def _ramp_scale(m, best_y, best_n, now_ts):
        '''(size that will ACTUALLY rest) / (size the model priced) in [0,1]; 1.0 when D3_RAMP is
        off or the clamp does not bind. Includes the EXPLORE_PROBE_CT clamp exactly as the inline
        block does. Side-effect-free (uses _d3_est_ct, never starts a ramp clock).'''
(b) `_expected_credit_usd` calls it (no behaviour change — pure extraction, pinned by the existing test_db_reward_fixes.py::test_f3_floor_gated_at_ramp_clamped_size).
(c) NEW flag `RAMP_BASIS = _envi("KALSHI_RAMP_BASIS", 0)`. When ON, the NETEV unproven-model fallback (~:3021-3024) and the capture gate (~:3046) multiply their `pc` by `_ramp_scale(...)` before comparing against NETEV_FINGERPRINT_USD_DAY / CAPTURE_MIN_USD_DAY, and both emit `netev_ramp_scale` / `capture_ramp_scale` into stats regardless of flag state (measure before arming).

DEADLOCK GUARD — this is the part that makes the fix safe and it must ship WITH it, not after. `_d3_est_ct` returns rung 0 for a ticker that has never been quoted, and `_d3_ramp_ct` only registers first-seen when we actually intend quotes. So an instantaneous rung-0 scale (5/45 = 0.11) can make the gate refuse a market that would ramp to full size within its window, the market never registers, and it never ramps — a permanent lockout, structurally identical to the 5ct-invisible-to-Target deadlock D-C exists to break. Therefore `_ramp_scale` must be evaluated over the DECISION HORIZON, not the instant: compute the time-average of D3_RUNGS[rung(t)] over min(remaining window, the time to reach the top permitted rung), from D3_RUNG_S and the rung ladder analytically (with the W7 new-series clamp capping the ladder at D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG). Both consumers are RATES ($/day), so the window-average rate is the honest basis for both, and it is what `_expected_credit_usd` (a period integral) should have used for F3 in the first place.
- *Why:* F3 taught only the presence floor to gate at the size that will actually rest. The NETEV unproven-family fallback and the capture gate still price `_prospective_capture` at the full `_capped_join` (45ct at MAX_MARKET_CAPITAL=45) while `_d3_apply_ramp` then rests rung-1 sizes (5-10ct for unproven series under the W7 clamp) — so three gates run three different sizes for the same market in the same cycle, and an unproven family is admitted on a ~9x-too-large reward signal against a size-independent $5/day fingerprint cost.
- *Standing detector:* `netev_ramp_scale` and `capture_ramp_scale` emitted every cycle in the plan row EVEN WITH THE FLAG OFF — the distribution of these scalars is both the pre-arm measurement and the permanent alarm: a gate whose scale is chronically far from 1.0 is a gate deciding at a size it will not rest. Add a hard invariant test asserting every reward-modelling gate multiplies by the SAME helper (source-window assertion over the three call sites, the style already used in test_score_cache_integrity.py:44) — that is the class detector against a fourth gate being added on the unscaled basis.
- *Test:* Failing-before, in test_netev_gate.py and test_capture_gate.py:
  T1 NETEV BASIS: unproven family, deep rival book, D3_RAMP=1 with _D3_FIRST_SEEN empty and D3_NEWSERIES_MAX_RUNG=1 (rung-1 = 10ct vs a 1000ct-priced join), usd_day tuned so pc/HAIRCUT - FINGERPRINT is marginally positive at full size and negative at ramped size. RAMP_BASIS=0 -> 2 quotes; RAMP_BASIS=1 -> skip with netev_skipped == 1. Fails today (the flag and the scaling do not exist).
  T2 CAPTURE BASIS: same shape against CAPTURE_MIN_USD_DAY.
  T3 EXTRACTION NO-OP: `_expected_credit_usd` returns byte-identical values before and after the extraction on the existing F3 fixture.
  T4 DEADLOCK GUARD: a never-seen ticker on a 1-day window with D3_RUNG_S=600 must get a window-average scale > 0.5 (it reaches the top permitted rung in ~30 min of a 1440-min window) — i.e. the fix must NOT refuse a market purely because it has not started ramping. Fails against a naive instantaneous implementation, which is the whole point of the pin.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* YES — KALSHI_RAMP_BASIS=0 default. The extraction (a) and (b) is a provable no-op and ships unflagged in the same commit; the two new gate multiplications are behind the flag with their scalars measured first. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* (1) A naive instantaneous implementation creates a REAL, permanent lockout of every new market (see the deadlock guard) — if a future session simplifies the window-average back to `_d3_est_ct`, the bot silently stops opening anything new. T4 is the guard; it must not be relaxed. (2) Even with the window-average, this fix strictly TIGHTENS both gates, so it reduces the unproven-family admit rate on a live money path — in the same direction as M-2 and compounding with it. (3) The cost side stays flat: NETEV_FINGERPRINT_USD_DAY=$5/day is size-independent while the reward term now scales, so the two sides of the comparison are on different size bases in the opposite direction from today's defect. I am deliberately NOT inventing a size-scaling for the fingerprint — there is no receipt basis for it — but that means the fix makes the comparison MORE conservative than a fully-coherent model would be, which is the safe direction but is not neutral. (4) The window-average depends on `end`/`life_min` parsing; a market with an unusable clock already returns [] earlier, but a market with a wrong `life_min` gets a wrong scale — clamp the helper's output to [0,1] and default to 1.0 on any parse failure (fail toward legacy).
- *Collides with:* m-59, M-6, M-2, M-29

**m-21 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:6269**

- *Defect:* _rest_maker_offset client_order_id lacks a ticker component — same-second exit re-rests collide on venue dedup
- *Fix:* Mirror the WS daemon's already-proven scheme (maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:562 uses `ws-{time.time_ns()}-{coid}-{side}`). Add module-level `_REREST_SEQ = [0]` and `_rerest_client_id(tag, side)` that increments the counter and returns `f"mk-{tag}-{time.time_ns()}-{_REREST_SEQ[0]}-{side}"`; use it at _rest_maker_offset :6268-6269. Deliberately NOT a ticker component: order_id_for's docstring records that a ticker prefix risks venue-side truncation collisions, and a monotone process counter plus nanosecond stamp gives uniqueness without that risk.
- *Why:* `f"mk-{tag}-{int(time.time())}-{side}"` collides whenever two re-rests share a wall-clock SECOND with the same tag and side — reachable because _strand_cross loops all due tickers and an HTTP_POOL cancel-confirm/cross/re-rest sequence is sub-second capable. Kalshi dedups on client_order_id (C5, :6504-6508), so the second EXIT re-rest is REJECTED and that position holds no working exit until the next cycle's unwind pass.
- *Standing detector:* In _rest_maker_offset's except branch, classify the rendered body: a duplicate/existing-client_order_id rejection bumps a new _SILENT['rerest_dup_id'] in addition to rerest_fail_create (RF2 already split the other four failure paths for exactly this reason — an exit failure that cannot name its cause is unfixable from the tape). Nonzero after this fix = the id scheme collided again.
- *Test:* Freeze time.time_ns to a constant, call _rest_maker_offset twice with the same tag and side for two different tickers against a MockClient that records client_order_id and raises on reuse; assert both creates succeed and the ids differ. Pre-fix the second raises (failing-before).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — id generation only, and the failure mode is loud and immediate.
- *Risk:* Any consumer that parses the id positionally (`mk-{tag}-{epoch}-{side}`) now sees an extra field — a grep found no such parser outside the quoter, but re-verify against the ledger/attribution scripts before deploy. time.time_ns() values are longer; if the venue enforces an undocumented client_order_id length limit, a longer id could be rejected — that is the one live-observable risk, so watch rerest_fail_create on the first cycle after deploy.
- *Collides with:* n-21

**m-22 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3996**

- *Defect:* Taker crosses on the STOP-sentinel branch never persisted: governor counts and re-entry cooldowns lost
- *Fix:* Give the STOP branch (:3961-3998) the governor bookkeeping the in-cycle taker paths already have. (1) _flatten_all returns the list of tickers it taker-crossed — it already iterates `residual` with per-ticker (ok, c) at :6632-6640 — and every early `return` becomes `return []`; the signature is unchanged, only the return value, and the only other non-test caller (:4936, the halt path) ignores it today and continues to. (2) In the STOP branch, BEFORE _flatten_all: `_st_stop = load_state()`, seed _TAKER_XN from _st_stop.get('mkt_taker_xn') applying the same UTC-day reset the normal path applies at :4467 (stored day != today => start empty). (3) AFTER _flatten_all: write _st_stop['mkt_taker_xn'] = dict(_TAKER_XN), stamp reentry_cool[t] = now + REENTRY_COOLDOWN_S for every crossed ticker (line-for-line the same shape as :5154-5157 and :5949-5954), then save_state(_st_stop) before _release_lock. The _reads[0]=0 reset and the STOPFLAT pacing branch stay exactly where they are.
- *Why:* The STOP path pays a taker to leave a market and records nothing: _TAKER_XN increments into a dict that was never seeded from state and is never written back, and no re-entry cooldown is stamped — so the taker governor undercounts a market we paid to exit and the bot can re-enter the book it just paid to leave the moment STOP is cleared.
- *Standing detector:* A print on the STOP path — `stopflat: persisted N paid-exit counts, M re-entry cooldowns` — plus a first-cycle-after-STOP reconciliation counter `taker_xn_gap` comparing crosses visible in the journal window against mkt_taker_xn. That is the same live check that caught the original H1 inertness at 16:44Z, made standing instead of ad hoc.
- *Test:* test_stop_diff_and_keep.py::test_stop_flatten_persists_governor_feeds — monkeypatch _flatten_all to bump _TAKER_XN['KXFOO'] and return ['KXFOO'], create the STOP sentinel, clear stopflat.last so pacing does not short-circuit, run run_once() with a stub non-dry_run client, then read the state file. BEFORE: mkt_taker_xn is absent/empty and reentry_cool has no KXFOO entry. AFTER: mkt_taker_xn['KXFOO'] == 1 and reentry_cool['KXFOO'] parses to a future time. Companion pin: a STOP cycle that crosses nothing writes no cooldown and does not zero an existing mkt_taker_xn.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — it persists numbers the code already computes; the only new behaviour is the governor and the cooldown actually seeing them, which is the documented guard working. Gating a guard behind a flag because it might guard is backwards.
- *Risk:* The STOP path now WRITES state, which it never did. save_state is atomic (tmp + os.replace) so the risk is stale content rather than corruption: the branch loads state, waits up to STOP_ESCALATE_S inside _flatten_all's polled escalation, then writes back — any key another writer changed in that window is clobbered. The WS daemon is the only other writer and is serialized by the same run lock, so this is safe today, but it is a NEW coupling between the halt path and the state file that did not exist before. Behaviourally, persisting cooldowns means a cleared STOP no longer re-enters crossed books for REENTRY_COOLDOWN_S — that is the intent, but it is a real reduction in post-halt trading that an operator restarting into a recovery window will feel and should be told about.
- *Collides with:* n-12

**m-23 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:6906**

- *Defect:* _strand_cross has no per-invocation market budget (settle and preclose takers cap at TAKER_MAX_MKTS=8)
- *Fix:* Give _strand_cross the per-invocation market budget its two sibling backstops already have, under its OWN knob so it ships as a provable no-op: `STRAND_MAX_MKTS = _envi('KALSHI_STRAND_MAX_MKTS', 0)` (0 = unlimited = today's exact behaviour). Sort `due` by elapsed clock DESCENDING (oldest strand first) before the cross loop so the cap always spends on the most urgent residual and no ticker can be starved across cycles; count plan['strand_deferred_mkts']; do NOT re-arm the clock for deferred tickers (their clock stays expired, so they are first next cycle). Deferred tickers keep their maker exit resting, untouched — this is pacing, never a ban or a skip-by-name.
- *Why:* The settle-taker caps at TAKER_MAX_MKTS (:5131) and the pre-close pass enforces _pc_taker_mkts (:6817); _strand_cross iterates every due ticker (:6906) and fires an IOC burst on each, so per-ticker pacing bounds repetition in TIME but not in BREADTH — a backlog (post-outage, or clocks that kept running through a TAKER_FLATTEN=0 interval) pays half-spread plus fee across every ticker in ONE cycle, the single-cycle cost spike the other two backstops were explicitly budgeted to prevent.
- *Standing detector:* plan['strand_deferred_mkts'] alongside the existing strand_due / strand_crossed_ct / exit_cross_cost_usd. Sustained nonzero = a backlog the cap is holding back; strand_due >> (crosses + deferred) is the alarm that pacing has become the binding constraint on de-risking.
- *Test:* Add to test_strand_cross.py: 12 due tickers with distinct clock ages, KALSHI_STRAND_MAX_MKTS=8 -> exactly 8 _taker_cross_capped calls, plan['strand_deferred_mkts'] == 4, and the 8 crossed are the 8 OLDEST clocks; with the knob at 0 all 12 cross (no-op pin). Failing-before: the knob does not exist and all 12 always cross.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* yes — new knob KALSHI_STRAND_MAX_MKTS defaulting to 0, byte-identical to today until the operator prices the trade. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Arming the cap DELAYS risk-reducing crosses — the exact loss shape FIX 3 exists to bound (07-27: 42 ct rode from a -$0.59 touch exit to -$15.29 at settlement). A cap of 8 against a 12-ticker backlog means 4 positions wait at least one more STRAND_CROSS_S period, and the oldest-first ordering bounds starvation but not loss. The taker-cost spike it prevents has been reasoned about, never measured; the exit delay it introduces has a measured historical cost. That asymmetry is why it ships at 0.
- *Collides with:* n-21

**m-24 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ws_feed.py:278**

- *Defect:* seq-key disappearance silently disables ALL gap detection while deltas keep applying
- *Fix:* Make "seq was present" a positively-verified property of a connection instead of an unexamined precondition, in kalshi_ws_feed.py `_dispatch` (:277-285).

Add a module gauge next to PARSE_FAILS: `SEQ_MISSING = [0]`, and per-Feed `self.seq_missing_frames = 0` (reset in `_end_session`, i.e. per connection).
```python
if typ in ("orderbook_snapshot", "orderbook_delta"):
    if seq is None:
        SEQ_MISSING[0] += 1
        self.seq_missing_frames += 1        # gap detection is BLIND on this connection
    else:
        if self._sub_seq is not None and seq != self._sub_seq + 1:
            self.gap_count += 1
            self._end_session("seq_gap")     # M-14 helper
            return False
        self._sub_seq = seq
```
Deltas keep applying exactly as today, so Stage A triggering is untouched.

Trust consequence, behind `WS_SEQ_STRICT = M._envi("KALSHI_WS_SEQ_STRICT", 0)`:
- `Daemon.mirror_book`: `if WS_SEQ_STRICT and feed.seq_missing_frames: self._decl("seq_unverified"); return None` → whole footprint falls back to REST, loudly, instead of quoting off a mirror with no drop protection.
- `Daemon._hot_precheck`: add `if WS_SEQ_STRICT and self.feed is not None and self.feed.seq_missing_frames: return "seq_unverified"`.

FOLD IN a small class fix required by that new reason: `on_book` (daemon:609-613) falls back to a full cycle via a hand-maintained tuple of reason strings; any new reason not added there silently loses the Stage A fallback. Invert it — `if out not in ("submitted", "no_op", "debounce"): self.cycle_req.set()` — which is byte-equivalent over today's reason set and immune to future additions.
- *Why:* Gap accounting is conditional on `d.get('seq') is not None`, so a venue dialect that relocates or renames the top-level seq field silently disables ALL drop protection while deltas keep applying: mirrors stay clean, `gap_count` stays 0, PARSE_FAILS stays 0, and Stage C quotes off a misassembled book forever. This is the one dialect-drift axis the 07-30 gauge does not cover, and it is precisely the 'stale book quoted as fresh' class Stage C's safety predicate assumes cannot happen.
- *Standing detector:* `SEQ_MISSING` + per-feed `seq_missing_frames` emitted in the cold_cycle log row beside the existing `feed_parse_fails` — same shape, same place, so the dialect-drift gauge becomes complete (side keys, price/delta keys, AND seq). Alarm condition: `feed_seq_missing > 0` on any cycle. This is also the arming evidence for the strict flag.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, test_ws_daemon.py:
```python
def test_missing_seq_is_counted_not_silently_trusted_kills_broken_variant():
    F.SEQ_MISSING[0] = 0
    feed = F.Feed(["A"])
    feed._dispatch(json.dumps({"type":"orderbook_snapshot","seq":1,
        "msg":{"market_ticker":"A","yes_dollars_fp":[["0.5000","10"]]}}))
    ok = feed._dispatch(json.dumps({"type":"orderbook_delta",   # seq RELOCATED
        "msg":{"market_ticker":"A","side":"yes","price_dollars":"0.5100",
               "delta_fp":"5.00","seq":2}}))
    assert ok is True                       # deltas still apply (no teardown)
    assert F.SEQ_MISSING[0] == 1 and feed.seq_missing_frames == 1
```
Today `SEQ_MISSING` does not exist → import error → fails. Plus a strict-mode test: with `KALSHI_WS_SEQ_STRICT=1`, `mirror_book` returns None after such a frame; and a no-regression test that with the flag OFF the mirror is still served (so the counter ships as a pure no-op).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes for the trust consequence — `KALSHI_WS_SEQ_STRICT`, default 0, armed only after the counter proves zero missing-seq frames over ≥24h live. The counter and the on_book tuple inversion are no-ops and ship unflagged.
- *Risk:* The strict arm is the dangerous half and that is exactly why it is flagged off. If the venue legitimately omits `seq` on some frame TYPE we have not observed (e.g. snapshots only), arming strict mode would make `mirror_book` decline for every ticker on every connection — Stage C silently reverts to 100% REST, cold cycles get ~12s slower, and nothing looks broken except latency. We cannot rule that out from code; only the counter can, which is why the counter ships first and the flag is armed only after it reads 0 for a full day. The counter half itself is a pure increment with no behavioral consequence. Second risk: inverting the `on_book` reason tuple is byte-equivalent today only if I enumerated the non-fallback reasons correctly — `submitted`, `no_op`, `debounce` — a miss there would either spam cold cycles or lose the Stage A fallback; the test must pin all current reasons explicitly.
- *Collides with:* M-14, m-27, m-28, M-13

**m-25 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:625**

- *Defect:* WS reconnect never invalidates HotContext — fills missed during the gap leave hot trusting pre-fill inventory
- *Fix:* Give the Feed a disconnect callback and wire it to HotContext invalidation — it rides the `_end_session` helper introduced in M-14, so this is ~6 lines on top of that commit.

kalshi_ws_feed.py: `Feed.__init__(self, tickers, on_book=None, on_fill=None, want_fills=False, initial_fails=0, on_disconnect=None)` (additive kwarg, every existing caller byte-unchanged) and `self.on_disconnect = on_disconnect`. `_end_session` already invokes it inside a try/except so a raising daemon callback can never kill the feed task.

maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py `_new_feed` (:625-633):
```python
feed = Feed(watch, on_book=self.on_book, on_fill=self.on_fill,
            want_fills=self.client.mode != "dry_run",
            initial_fails=carry, on_disconnect=self.on_feed_disconnect)
```
New method:
```python
def on_feed_disconnect(self, reason):
    """A socket discontinuity invalidates EVERY cached view derived from the
    stream, not just the book mirrors: a fill executed during the gap arrives
    on no channel, so ctx inventory/counts are unverifiable until a cold cycle."""
    self.ctx.invalidate()
    self.cycle_req.set()
    _log({"ev": "feed_disconnect", "reason": reason})
```
Note the callback fires on the FEED's thread/loop — `ctx.invalidate()` is a single attribute store to None (the documented torn-read-safe sentinel, daemon:167-171) and `cycle_req.set()` is thread-safe, so no lock is needed.
- *Why:* The only ctx invalidations today are on_fill, cold cycles, and hot-write errors; `Feed.run` reconnects entirely internally and the daemon cannot observe it. A fill that executes during a reconnect blip arrives on no channel, and after the socket comes back the fill-ack gate re-passes and mirrors re-seed clean while ctx (up to WS_STALE_S=90s old) still reports event_inv=0 and pre-fill counts — hot then reprices the ACCUMULATING side of a ticker that now carries inventory, bypassing the reduce-side protection until the next cold cycle.
- *Standing detector:* `{"ev":"feed_disconnect","reason":...}` rows in ws_daemon_log, plus a per-cycle `feed_disconnects` count in the cold_cycle row (same source as M-14's `session_ends`). Recurrence signature: disconnect rows with no adjacent `cold_cycle` row inside WS_CYCLE_MIN_GAP_S means the invalidation did not translate into a guarded cycle.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, test_ws_daemon.py:
```python
def test_feed_disconnect_invalidates_hot_ctx_kills_broken_variant(daemon):
    seen = []
    feed = F.Feed(["T"], on_disconnect=lambda r: seen.append(r))
    feed.mirrors["T"].apply_snapshot({"yes_dollars_fp":[["0.36","20"]]}, seq=1)
    feed._dispatch(json.dumps({"type":"error","msg":{"code":6}}))
    assert seen == ["error_frame"]
```
plus the daemon half: `daemon.ctx.built_mono = time.monotonic(); daemon.on_feed_disconnect("exception"); assert daemon.ctx.stale() and daemon.cycle_req.is_set()`. Today `Feed.__init__` has no `on_disconnect` kwarg → TypeError → fails.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — invalidating a cache is safe in every configuration, and the only behavioral consequence with WS_HOT=0 is one extra guarded cold cycle per reconnect (the same cycle a book move would have triggered anyway).
- *Risk:* Every reconnect now forces a full cold cycle (`cycle_req`), so a flapping socket turns into a cold-cycle-per-flap, bounded by the existing WS_CYCLE_MIN_GAP_S=5 debounce — i.e. up to 12 full REST cycles a minute during a bad WS period, each ~40 authed reads. That is a genuine rate-limit and cost increase precisely when the venue is already unhappy, and it interacts with m-29 (no 429 backoff): a WS outage could push the REST side into rate limiting. Mitigation: m-29's shared rate-limit gate should land before or with this, and the debounce is the existing throttle. The alternative — leaving ctx trusted across a socket gap — is a money-path error, so the cost is the right trade.
- *Collides with:* M-14, m-26, m-28, n-25

**m-26 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ws_feed.py:288**

- *Defect:* feed_lat_ms grows unbounded in the long-lived daemon — slow memory leak
- *Fix:* Fix the CLASS (unbounded in-memory growth in a process designed to run for weeks), not just the one list.

(a) kalshi_ws_feed.py: `from collections import deque`; `WS_LAT_SAMPLES = int(os.environ.get("KALSHI_WS_LAT_SAMPLES", "2000"))`; `self.feed_lat_ms = deque(maxlen=WS_LAT_SAMPLES)`. The attribute name and every read site survive unchanged — `smoke()` does `sorted(feed.feed_lat_ms)` and `len(fl)` on the resulting list (:376-379), both fine on a deque.

(b) maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py: the same class exists in three per-key dicts that are never pruned — `self.last_hot_mono` (per ticker), `self.last_fill_mono` (per event key), and `self.last_refs` (pruned only on resubscribe). Add one prune, called at the end of `cold_cycle` alongside the existing purge machinery:
```python
def _prune_maps(self, live_tickers):
    keep = set(live_tickers or ())
    now = time.monotonic()
    self.last_hot_mono = {t: v for t, v in self.last_hot_mono.items()
                          if t in keep and now - v < 3600}
    self.last_fill_mono = {k: v for k, v in self.last_fill_mono.items()
                           if now - v < 3600}
    self.last_refs = {t: v for t, v in self.last_refs.items() if t in keep}
```
(1h ≫ WS_FILL_COOLDOWN_S=3 and WS_HOT_DEBOUNCE_MS=250, so no live semantics change.)

(c) Same fix shape applies to `client.intents` — see n-27(b).
- *Why:* `feed_lat_ms.append` runs on every frame carrying ts_ms and is read only by `--smoke`; a daemon Feed persists until a footprint resubscribe, which on a stable footprint is days. At ~10 frames/s that is ~25MB/day of pure dead weight in one list, plus per-ticker dicts that accumulate every ticker the footprint has ever contained. The failure is an unattributable OOM kill whose recovery (daemon restart) drops all quotes for a reconnect window.
- *Standing detector:* A `mem` field in the cold_cycle log row: `{"lat_n": len(feed.feed_lat_ms), "hot_n": len(self.last_hot_mono), "fill_n": len(self.last_fill_mono), "refs_n": len(self.last_refs), "rss_kb": resource.getrusage(resource.RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss}`. Monotonic RSS growth across a day is then readable in the same file as everything else instead of requiring `ps` archaeology — and it catches the NEXT unbounded structure someone adds, which is the actual class. Ship `feed_lat_p50_ms` in the same row while we are there: the ring finally makes production feed latency observable outside `--smoke`.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE:
```python
def test_feed_latency_samples_are_bounded_kills_broken_variant(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(F, "WS_LAT_SAMPLES", 10, raising=False)
    feed = F.Feed(["A"]); feed.feed_lat_ms = F.deque(maxlen=10)
    for i in range(500):
        feed._dispatch(json.dumps({"type":"orderbook_snapshot","seq":i+1,
            "msg":{"market_ticker":"A","ts_ms": 1,
                   "yes_dollars_fp":[["0.5000","10"]]}}))
    assert len(feed.feed_lat_ms) <= 10
```
(today: 500 → fails). Plus `test_prune_maps_drops_departed_tickers_but_keeps_live_ones` pinning that a ticker still in the watch set keeps its debounce timestamp — the regression that would re-enable hot spam.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — bounded memory and pruning stale timestamps cannot change a quoting decision (all pruned entries are older than every window that reads them). Telemetry-only additions otherwise.
- *Risk:* Low but not zero. `--smoke`'s latency percentiles now describe the last N samples rather than the whole run; with N=2000 and a 30s smoke that is every sample, but a longer smoke silently changes meaning — the smoke output should print `n=` (it already does). The prune is the sharper edge: dropping a ticker's `last_hot_mono` entry removes its hot debounce, so if `live_tickers` were ever computed wrong (e.g. passed the truncated watch instead of the full ctx set — see n-24) a still-quoted ticker could be repriced twice inside WS_HOT_DEBOUNCE_MS. The 1h age condition plus passing the FULL ctx ticker set (not the watch) contains it, and WS_HOT=0 makes it unreachable today.
- *Collides with:* M-14, m-25, n-24, n-27

**m-27 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ws_feed.py:144**

- *Defect:* apply_delta lacks _norm_rows' 0<p<1 range guard — out-of-range price becomes a phantom level
- *Fix:* One price-validity contract, enforced in both mirror write paths. kalshi_ws_feed.py:
```python
def _valid_px(p):
    """The mirror's ONE price contract: a Kalshi outcome price is strictly inside
    (0,1) in dollars. Anything else is a dialect we cannot read, not a level."""
    return 0.0 < p < 1.0
```
`_norm_rows` (:75) becomes `if _valid_px(p) and s > 0:` — byte-identical behavior, single source of truth.

`apply_delta` (:136-143): replace `if book is None or price <= 0:` with
```python
if book is None or not _valid_px(price):
    PARSE_FAILS[0] += 1
    PARSE_REASONS["side" if book is None else "px_range"] += 1
    self.dirty = True
    return
```
where `PARSE_REASONS = {"side": 0, "px_range": 0, "snapshot_dialect": 0}` is an additive module gauge (PARSE_FAILS itself is untouched — it is already read by the daemon and by tests).

Also tick `PARSE_REASONS["snapshot_dialect"]` in `apply_snapshot`'s no-recognized-side-key arm (:118) so the three drift axes are separable.
- *Why:* Snapshot rows go through `_norm_rows`, which drops prices outside (0,1); `apply_delta` guards only `price <= 0`. A delta price that normalizes ABOVE 1.0 — e.g. a future cents-as-STRING dialect where '36' → 36.0, which `_norm_price` does not treat as cents because it is not an int — is inserted as a real level at $36.00 and `best()` returns it as the best bid, with the mirror still marked clean. Today that book is caught only by luck of a downstream gate (the crossed-book guard catches the two-sided case; a one-sided book flows into pricing with a garbage reference). The mirror must fail by its own contract, not by a downstream accident.
- *Standing detector:* `PARSE_REASONS` emitted in the cold_cycle row next to the existing `feed_parse_fails`, so the operator can tell 'the venue renamed the side key' from 'the venue changed the price scale' from 'the snapshot dialect moved' — three different migrations that today all collapse into one integer. Alarm on any non-zero.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, test_ws_daemon.py (next to the existing `apply_delta` tests):
```python
def test_out_of_range_delta_price_dirties_instead_of_becoming_a_level():
    before = F.PARSE_FAILS[0]
    m = F.BookMirror("T")
    m.apply_snapshot({"yes_dollars_fp": [["0.3600","100"]]}, seq=1)
    m.apply_delta({"side":"yes","price":"36","delta":"5"}, seq=2)   # cents-as-STRING
    assert m.dirty and 36.0 not in m.yes
    assert m.best()[0] != 36.0
    assert F.PARSE_FAILS[0] == before + 1
```
Today the mirror stays clean, 36.0 is a level, and `best_yes` is 36.0 → fails on all three assertions. No existing test breaks: the ones using `price: 37` pass an INT, which `_norm_price` maps to 0.37 (in range).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — provably safer: the only inputs whose treatment changes are prices that are already outside the venue's own domain, and the new treatment (dirty → REST) is the module's existing fail-safe path.
- *Risk:* If the venue ever legitimately sends a delta at price exactly 0.00 or 1.00 (a level being fully removed at a boundary), that delta is now dropped and the mirror goes dirty → REST refetch for that ticker. Kalshi outcome prices are 1..99 cents so this should be unreachable, and `_norm_rows` has already applied the identical rule to every snapshot row since 07-25 without incident — which is the strongest available evidence that the range holds on this venue. Worst case is an extra REST read, never a wrong order. The `PARSE_REASONS` dict must be reset nowhere (cumulative, like PARSE_FAILS) or the cold-row deltas will read wrong.
- *Collides with:* M-14, m-24

**m-28 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ws_feed.py:273**

- *Defect:* ANY error frame tears down the whole connection — one rejected ticker can pin the feed in reconnect churn
- *Fix:* Root-fix the PIN, not the teardown: today a persistent error frame reconnects with the identical watch list forever, escalating to the 60s cap with no diagnosis and no escape, because the watch only changes when the footprint changes AND the 120s resub hysteresis allows it.

Three parts, all additive:

1) kalshi_ws_feed.py — track the pathology. Per-connection `self.frames_this_session = 0` (incremented in `_dispatch`, reset in `run()` after subscribe) and `self.barren_sessions = 0`, incremented in `_end_session` when the session ends with `frames_this_session <= 1` and reason == "error_frame" (i.e. the connection produced an error and nothing else). New additive kwarg `on_degraded=None`, invoked from `_end_session` when `self.barren_sessions >= WS_BARREN_LIMIT` (default 3), passing `(self.barren_sessions, list(self.tickers))`.

2) maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py — diagnose and escape. `Daemon.on_feed_degraded(n, tickers)` sets a flag consumed by the main loop (never does I/O on the feed's loop):
```python
self.degraded_watch = list(tickers)
self.cycle_req.set()
```
In the main loop, when `self.degraded_watch` is set: probe every watched ticker over the PUBLIC unauthed market endpoint (`M.public_get(f"/trade-api/v2/markets/{t}")`, ~80 reads, ONLY in this pathological path), log
`{"ev":"feed_degraded_probe","n":80,"not_open":[tickers...],"all_open":bool}` and rebuild the watch from the open ones, BYPASSING the WS_RESUB_MIN_S hysteresis for this one rebuild.

3) The excluded ticker is excluded from the FEED ONLY, for one connection generation, and is re-offered on the next paced resubscribe. It is never dropped from trading: the cold cycle keeps quoting it over REST (`mirror_book` declines → REST), so this is a feed-membership decision, not a market ban.

If the probe finds every ticker open, the log row says so — which is itself the diagnosis (the error frame is not a ticker rejection) and points the next session at the frame body, which `_dispatch` already prints.
- *Why:* `_dispatch` returns False on every `type=='error'` frame with no distinction between a fatal connection error and a per-command rejection, and `run()` then reconnects with the same watch list, gets the same error, and pins at the 60s backoff cap indefinitely. All mirrors stay permanently dirty, Stage C degrades to 100% REST and Stage A to the 60s heartbeat — the daemon silently becomes the old 1-2min timer while the log shows only reconnect lines. The mechanism class is 'an unchanging input that the venue rejects, retried forever with no diagnosis'; the fix gives that loop both an observer and an exit.
- *Standing detector:* `barren_sessions` and `feed_degraded_probe` rows in ws_daemon_log, plus a `ws_barren` counter in the cold_cycle row. The decisive alarm is the pair (`book_ws == 0` AND `book_rest == footprint size`) sustained across cycles — that is the 'silently became a timer' signature, and it is one boolean away from being alertable once M-14's decline-reason histogram lands.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, test_ws_daemon.py:
```python
def test_repeated_barren_error_sessions_raise_degraded_kills_broken_variant():
    seen = []
    feed = F.Feed(["A","B"], on_degraded=lambda n, t: seen.append((n, list(t))))
    for _ in range(3):
        feed.frames_this_session = 0
        feed._dispatch(json.dumps({"type":"error","msg":{"code":6}}))
    assert seen and seen[-1][0] >= 3
```
(today: no `on_degraded` kwarg → TypeError). Plus a daemon test that `on_feed_degraded` sets `degraded_watch` and that the probe path rebuilds the watch WITHOUT waiting for WS_RESUB_MIN_S — the hysteresis bypass is the escape and must be pinned.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no for the counters/telemetry (pure observation). The degraded probe + hysteresis bypass are reachable only after 3 consecutive barren error sessions, a state in which the feed is already fully dirty and Stage C already fully REST — there is no working behavior left to protect, so a flag would only gate the recovery.
- *Risk:* Two real ones. (1) The hysteresis bypass exists precisely to prevent resubscribe churn, and this fix punches a hole in it; a bug in the barren-session accounting (e.g. counting a normal close as barren) would turn every reconnect into a full feed rebuild, dropping all mirrors and pushing Stage C to REST — the exact churn J7 was built to stop. The `frames_this_session <= 1 AND reason == error_frame` conjunction is what keeps that narrow, and the test must pin that a healthy session with frames never increments the counter. (2) The 80-read probe runs while the venue is already unhappy; if the WS error is a symptom of an account-wide rate limit, we add REST load to it (mitigated by m-29's shared gate, and by the probe being unauthed and once-per-degradation). (3) Honest scope limit: this diagnoses and escapes the TICKER-rejection shape. If the error frame is command-shaped rather than ticker-shaped, the probe reports 'all open' and we still churn — better observed, not fixed. The complete fix (chunked subscribes with per-sid seq accounting so one rejected chunk cannot kill the connection) is an L-sized rewrite of the seq contract and I am deliberately NOT proposing it: `self._sub_seq` is a single global counter that is live-verified against a single subscription, and multiplexing it on an unverified sid shape would manufacture false gaps and a reconnect storm on a live money path.
- *Collides with:* M-14, m-24, m-25, n-24, n-26

**m-29 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_client.py:194**

- *Defect:* No 429/Retry-After handling anywhere in the client — rate-limit episodes handled only by generic exception paths
- *Fix:* maker_kalshi_client.py — give the client one shared rate-limit gate that ALL request paths respect, without changing the raise contract callers are written against.

Module level (next to PAGINATION_TRUNCATED):
```python
import threading
RATE_LIMIT = {"hits": 0, "until_mono": 0.0, "last_retry_after": None}
RL_MAX_SLEEP_S = float(os.environ.get("KALSHI_RL_MAX_SLEEP_S", "30"))
RL_BACKOFF = os.environ.get("KALSHI_RL_BACKOFF", "0") == "1"
_WRITE_GATE = {"last": 0.0}
_GATE_LOCK = threading.Lock()
```

(a) DETECT (always on, no flag): in `_request`, wrap both transport arms so a 429 (and a 503 carrying Retry-After) records itself before re-raising unchanged:
```python
except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
    if e.code in (429, 503):
        ra = _parse_retry_after(getattr(e, "headers", None))   # int secs, HTTP-date, or None->5.0
        RATE_LIMIT["hits"] += 1
        RATE_LIMIT["last_retry_after"] = ra
        RATE_LIMIT["until_mono"] = max(RATE_LIMIT["until_mono"],
                                       time.monotonic() + min(ra, RL_MAX_SLEEP_S))
        print(f"WARNING {e.code} from {path} — Retry-After={ra}")
    raise
```
The pooled arm already constructs an `HTTPError` with `r.headers`, so one handler covers both.

(b) SHARE THE WRITE FLOOR (always on, strictly safer): `_write` reads/writes `_WRITE_GATE["last"]` under `_GATE_LOCK` instead of the per-instance `self._last_write` (which stays assigned for compatibility). This closes the 'two live client instances — run_once's and the daemon's — do not share the floor, so back-to-back writes across the cold_lock boundary violate the 0.16s spacing' hole.

(c) HONOR (flagged): with `RL_BACKOFF=1`, `_request` sleeps the remainder of `until_mono` (capped at RL_MAX_SLEEP_S) before issuing — so `_get_paginated`'s up-to-50 sequential authed GETs and the write path both back off, not just the write path.

(d) SYSTEMIC: in the daemon's main loop, before starting a cold cycle, `if KC.RATE_LIMIT["until_mono"] > time.monotonic(): _log({"ev":"rate_limited_defer"}); continue` WITHOUT clearing cycle_req — so a 429 during `_exec_hot` no longer immediately schedules ~40 reads plus writes back into the same rate-limit window at the 5s debounce.
- *Why:* Nothing in the client reads Retry-After, `_get_paginated` has no spacing at all, and the two live client instances do not share the write floor. A rate-limit episode therefore has no backoff anywhere: every hot op 429s, each 429 triggers cycle_req, and cold cycles hammer the same limit every 5s while the plan log shows only fetch_failed rows. Creates are never auto-retried so there is no double-order risk — this is pure degradation — but the degradation is self-reinforcing and invisible.
- *Standing detector:* `RATE_LIMIT["hits"]` and the seconds remaining on `until_mono` in the cold_cycle log row (`rl_hits`, `rl_wait_s`) and in the quoter's plan row alongside the existing `PAGINATION_TRUNCATED` gauge. Alarm on `rl_hits > 0` for a day — today a 429 is indistinguishable from any other HTTPError in the logs, which is why we do not know whether this is happening right now.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, new test_rate_limit.py:
```python
def test_429_records_retry_after_and_still_raises(monkeypatch):
    C.RATE_LIMIT.update(hits=0, until_mono=0.0)
    def boom(*a, **k):
        raise urllib.error.HTTPError("u", 429, "Too Many",
                                     {"Retry-After": "7"}, io.BytesIO(b"{}"))
    monkeypatch.setattr(C.urllib.request, "urlopen", boom)
    c = C.KalshiOrderClient(mode="dry_run")
    with pytest.raises(urllib.error.HTTPError):
        c.get_orderbook("T")                 # contract preserved: still raises
    assert C.RATE_LIMIT["hits"] == 1 and C.RATE_LIMIT["last_retry_after"] == 7.0
```
(today RATE_LIMIT does not exist → fails). Plus `test_write_floor_is_shared_across_client_instances`: two `KalshiOrderClient`s, monkeypatched clock, assert the second instance's write waits the 0.16s floor set by the first — fails today because `_last_write` is per-instance.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes for the HONORING behavior (`KALSHI_RL_BACKOFF`, default 0) and for the daemon's cold-cycle deferral. Detection (a) is a counter plus a print on a path that already raised, and the shared write floor (b) is strictly more conservative than two independent floors — both ship unflagged. Arm the backoff only after the counter shows a real 429 or after a demo-mode soak. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* CONCERNING. This is the fix in this set with the most credible path to making live behavior WORSE. (1) A malformed or hostile Retry-After (HTTP-date parsed wrong, or a large value) turns into a sleep on the write worker thread WHILE `cold_lock` is held, stalling the cold cycle and therefore all quoting for up to RL_MAX_SLEEP_S. The 30s cap bounds it, but a 30s quoting freeze on a live money path is a real cost and it is exactly the kind of self-inflicted outage that looks like a venue problem. (2) `until_mono` is process-global, so a 429 on ANY endpoint (including a read for an unrelated study running in-process) gates every subsequent request including exits — and exits are the one thing that must never be delayed. If that matters, the honest carve-out is to have `_write` bypass the gate for DELETE (cancel) so de-risking is never rate-gated by our own backoff, and I would ship it that way. (3) It cannot be tested end-to-end without provoking a real 429, which we should not do deliberately; the unit test proves the parse and the gate, not the venue's actual header shape. That is why (c) and (d) ship dark while (a) and (b) — detection and a shared floor — ship live.
- *Collides with:* m-30, m-46, n-27, m-25

**m-30 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_client.py:311**

- *Defect:* cancel_remaining_ct field shape never live-verified — the B3-1 refill guard rests on an assumed scale
- *Fix:* Turn an assumed field scale into a SELF-DETECTING one, so the B3-1 anti-refill guarantee no longer rests on an unverified guess.

(a) maker_kalshi_client.py — additive optional param (default None keeps every existing caller and the existing parser tests byte-identical):
```python
CANCEL_SCALE_SUSPECT = [0]

@staticmethod
def cancel_remaining_ct(resp, expected_max=None):
    ...
    try:
        v = float(o[k])
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return None
    if expected_max is not None and v > float(expected_max) + 1e-9:
        # The venue cannot have MORE of our order resting than we ever placed.
        # A value above the order's own size means the field is not in contracts
        # (centi-scale or another dialect) -> the scale assumption is REFUTED.
        CANCEL_SCALE_SUSPECT[0] += 1
        print(f"WARNING cancel remaining {v} > order size {expected_max} — "
              f"remaining_count field scale UNVERIFIED; refusing to re-create")
        return None                      # UNKNOWN -> existing never-recreate-blind path
    return v
```
(b) maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py `_exec_hot` (:534): `remaining = self.client.cancel_remaining_ct(resp, expected_max=op["want_count"])`. `hot_reprice_ops` only emits an op when standing count == desired count, so `want_count` IS the order's full original size — a sound upper bound.

(c) ARMING PRECONDITION (operator act, not code): before WS_HOT is ever set to 1, run a one-shot `--probe-cancel-shape`: place a single 1-contract post-only order far from the market, cancel it, and print the raw DELETE response keys and values. This is the only way to learn the shape, it costs at most 1 contract of exposure for seconds, and it converts m-30 from INFERRED to ESTABLISHED. Record the observed keys in the client docstring next to the existing V2 verification notes.
- *Why:* The hot path's entire structural anti-refill guarantee is `place = min(want_count, remaining)` with `remaining = float(resp['remaining_count_fp'])`, and the DELETE response shape has never been exercised live because Stage B has never been armed. The canon's own warning is that plain-vs-fp field guesses invert about half the time; if `remaining_count_fp` were centi-scaled, `min(50, 200) = 50` re-creates a full order when only 2 contracts were unfilled — reintroducing exactly the partial-fill refill B3-1 exists to prevent, on the accumulating side. The clamp makes the wrong-scale case indistinguishable from the already-handled 'venue did not state it' case, which fails safe by design.
- *Standing detector:* `CANCEL_SCALE_SUSPECT` counter surfaced in the cold_cycle row plus the WARNING line, alongside the existing `hot_cancel_no_remaining` log event. Recurrence signature: any non-zero value means the venue's field scale changed under us and hot repricing has degraded to cancel-only — visible in the same row as the hot outcome histogram (M-13).
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, extending `test_cancel_remaining_ct_parser`:
```python
def test_remaining_above_order_size_is_treated_as_unknown_kills_broken_variant():
    P = D.M.KalshiOrderClient.cancel_remaining_ct
    assert P({"order": {"remaining_count_fp": "200"}}) == 200.0        # legacy path
    assert P({"order": {"remaining_count_fp": "200"}}, expected_max=50) is None
```
(today: TypeError, the param does not exist). Plus the money-path test in test_ws_daemon.py: `MockClient(remaining_map={"o1": 200.0})` with a 20ct standing order → `hot_event` returns "submitted" but NO `create_quote` call is made, ctx is invalidated and cycle_req is set. Today that fixture re-creates 20 contracts against 2 actually-unfilled — the refill B3-1 exists to prevent.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — the clamp is a no-op on the assumed-correct shape (remaining ≤ order size always holds there), and the whole path is unreachable while WS_HOT=0. Shipping it dark would defeat its purpose, which is to be already in place when Stage B is armed.
- *Risk:* If the venue's remaining field is legitimately in a unit larger than contracts for some order type we have not seen, the clamp trips on every cancel and hot repricing silently degrades to cancel-with-no-replace — our quote disappears until the next cold cycle (≤60s), forfeiting queue position and reward presence in that window. That is the safe direction (shrink, never grow) and it is loud (WARNING + counter), but it is a real loss of presence on the reward clock, and it would look like 'Stage B does nothing useful' rather than like a venue shape change unless someone reads the counter. Existing tests are unaffected (their fixtures use remaining ≤ want_count: 12.0, 0.0, None, and the 20.0 default against a 20ct order).
- *Collides with:* M-13, m-29

**m-31 — kalshi_live/kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:315**

- *Defect:* Strict-> watermark drops late-visible fills/settlements forever, permanently inflating rewards_residual positive
- *Fix:* GRACE WINDOW + EXPLICIT DEDUPE + COUNT RECONCILIATION in kalshi_attribution_ledger.collect(). (1) Replace the strict watermark filters (:315 `settled_time > sw`, :320 `created_time > fw`) with `ts > (watermark - LATE_GRACE_S)` where LATE_GRACE_S is a module constant (start 900s) — and make admission idempotent by an explicit seen-set carried in ledger_state.json: `seen_fill_ids` (fill_id, falling back to (ticker, created_time, count)) and `seen_settle_keys` ((ticker, settled_time)). A row inside the grace window is admitted ONLY if its key is not in the seen-set. (2) After building the window, prune both seen-sets to keys whose timestamp is newer than (new watermark - LATE_GRACE_S) — bounded size, no unbounded growth. (3) BASELINE PATH FIX (:305-307): when `baseline` is true the seen-sets must be seeded with every row already inside the grace window of the seeded watermarks, otherwise run 2 re-admits the tail of pre-existing history as fresh trading cash — a one-time phantom window. (4) Keep the watermark advance at :391-392 exactly as-is (max of admitted rows).
- *Why:* Closes 'a strictly-monotone cursor over a feed whose rows can become visible late', where a dropped row's dollars still land in `balance` and therefore reappear as an unexplained POSITIVE step that the standing operator rule reads as a REWARD. Grace + dedupe converts 'never counted' into 'counted once, slightly late'.
- *Standing detector:* A cheap exact conservation check that needs no seen-set: store `fills_total_seen` / `settles_total_seen` (the LENGTH of the full tapes pulled at :301-302) in ledger_state.json, and each run assert `len(all_fills) - prev_total == n_admitted_fills` (same for settlements). Any mismatch means a row appeared late, disappeared, or was dropped — print a WARNING and write `late_or_missing_fills` / `late_or_missing_settles` and `late_admitted` into the ledger row, so the anomaly is in the persistent series the P2 verdict reads, not just stdout. This detector is worth landing even if the operator defers the grace window itself.
- *Test:* New test_ledger_late_row.py driving collect() against a stubbed `get`/`get_paginated`: run 1 sees fills [A@14:00:01], run 2's tape adds [B@13:59:58] (older than the advanced watermark) plus A again. Assert run 2's row has new_fills==1, fills_cash equal to B's cash, and A is NOT re-counted; assert `late_admitted`==1. On current code run 2 admits nothing (B is `<= fw`) so new_fills==0 and fills_cash==0.0 — FAILS. Second test pins dedupe: re-running with no new rows must produce new_fills==0 (guards against the grace window double-counting).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — gate the grace window itself behind KALSHI_LEDGER_LATE_GRACE_S (default 0 = today's strict-`>` behaviour, byte-identical) and ship the count-reconciliation detector LIVE and unflagged (pure telemetry). That way the detector measures whether late rows actually occur on this venue BEFORE the semantics change; if the counter never fires, the grace window may never need enabling at all. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* GENUINELY CONCERNING. (1) This changes the semantics of the ledger series MID-MEASUREMENT, four days before the P2 verdict (2026-08-10T14:13Z) reads it; rows before and after the change are not produced by the same rule, so any window that straddles the deploy has a convention boundary — the cash recorder learned this lesson the expensive way and encodes it as in-row convention markers (kalshi_cash_recorder.py:279-301). This fix MUST add the same kind of marker (e.g. `window_basis: 'grace900_dedupe'`) or it recreates that trap. (2) If the seen-set is lost (state file reset, first run after deploy) the grace window re-admits up to LATE_GRACE_S of already-counted rows — a real double-count of trading cash, i.e. exactly the harm m-34 describes, introduced by the fix for m-31. The baseline seeding in step (3) is what prevents it and it must be right. (3) It grows ledger_state.json from 3 scalars to two collections; a corrupt state file now degrades in a new way (falls back to empty seen-sets -> re-admission). (4) The late-row failure is INFERRED, not observed on this venue — this is prophylaxis against an unproven behaviour, paid for with real state complexity on the measurement series.
- *Collides with:* m-32, m-33, m-34, m-35, n-32, n-42, n-30

**m-32 — kalshi_live/kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:80**

- *Defect:* Ledger's private paginator silently truncates at 10k rows; settlements truncation has zero guard coverage
- *Fix:* MAKE THE LEDGER'S PRIVATE PAGINATOR LOUD AND BOUNDED LIKE THE CLIENT'S, in kalshi_attribution_ledger.get_paginated (:80-90). (1) After the loop, `if cursor:` increment a module counter `PAGINATION_TRUNCATED = [0]` and print the same shaped warning the client prints (maker_kalshi_client.py:358-367): which path, how many rows returned, and that every consumer is now reading a partial view. (2) Raise the page size from limit=200 to limit=1000 to match the client's prod-verified default (maker_kalshi_client._get_paginated :336-343), keeping the 50-page bound — the effective cap moves 10k -> 50k rows and the ledger stops being the tightest-capped reader of the same endpoints. (3) Surface it per run: write `pagination_truncated: PAGINATION_TRUNCATED[0]` into the ledger row (:370-385) so a truncated pull is visible in the persistent series, and make collect() print the ticker count it pulled (len(all_fills)/len(all_settles)) so 'the tape stopped growing' is readable from the journal. Do NOT swap in the client's _get_paginated: the ledger is deliberately standalone (openssl-subprocess signer, no maker_kalshi_client import) and that dependency change is a separate, larger contract.
- *Why:* Closes 'a truncation path that is silent while every sibling truncation path in the lane is loud' — and specifically the settlements case, which has NO compensating check because settled tickers are removed from the recon-checkable set at :331, so a truncated settlements pull both undercounts settle_rev and shrinks `settled_tickers`, which can spuriously re-add long-flat tickers to the position recon.

**m-36 — kalshi_live/kalshi_credit_feedback.py:103**

- *Defect:* never_paid_due judges due-ness only over OUR filled tickers — open sibling strikes can't shield their event
- *Fix:* kalshi_credit_feedback.py — judge due-ness on the EVENT's conclusion, not on the subset of strikes we happened to fill. (1) `build_feedback(credits, orders, settlements, now, event_of=None, event_due=None)` — new optional callable `event_due(event_ticker) -> True | False | None`. In the due loop (:104-111), after today's 'all OUR filled tickers of this event settled > DUE_MARGIN_H ago' test passes, additionally require `event_due(ev) is True`; both False (siblings still open) and None (unresolvable) mean 'not evidence either way' and each bumps a counter. Default `event_due=None` behaves as `lambda ev: True`, i.e. byte-identical to today — this pins the existing test_d2_feedback.py verdict tests unchanged. (2) `main()` builds the resolver from venue metadata: for each distinct filled event GET `/trade-api/v2/events/{ev}?with_nested_markets=true`, take max(close_time) over the nested markets, return `(now - max_close) >= DUE_MARGIN_H hours`. Cache POSITIVE results only in kalshi_event_due.json beside the existing kalshi_event_map.json (a concluded event never un-concludes; a False must never be cached). Read failure -> None, counted. (3) Each series row gains `due_blocked_open_event` and `due_unresolved` so the feed itself says WHY a series was not convicted. (4) main() gets `--event-due / --no-event-due` (default OFF for the first deploy) so the operator can build both feeds and diff the verdict table before adopting.
- *Why:* A liquidity credit for an event cannot exist before that event's program concludes, so an event with open sibling strikes is not evidence of non-payment; judging due-ness over our filled subset convicts a series during exactly the window in which it is still accruing the credit that would clear it.
- *Standing detector:* (a) Builder prints `!! DUE-EVENT UNRESOLVED n/m` whenever metadata could not decide, so the fail-open direction (penalty silently disarming) is loud rather than silent. (b) New rule in `feed_integrity_alarms`: a series carrying verdict never_paid_due whose blocking evidence includes an event whose max close_time is in the FUTURE is an impossible state -> alarm. (c) The two new per-series counters make a spike in withheld convictions visible in the daily feed diff, which is the recurrence signal for the quantifier class (evidence judged over the wrong set).
- *Test:* test_d2_feedback.py::test_open_sibling_strike_blocks_conviction — one event, our two filled tickers settled 100h ago (today: due), plus `event_due` returning False for that event; assert verdict == 'insufficient', due_filled_events == 0, due_blocked_open_event == 1. FAILS BEFORE: today the function has no event_due parameter and returns 'never_paid_due'. Companion pin test_event_due_default_is_byte_identical — the full existing verdict fixture with event_due=None reproduces today's verdicts exactly.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no new trading flag (offline builder; the consumer already has KALSHI_D2_FEEDBACK) — but the DEFAULT is the dark state: event_due=None reproduces today exactly, and `--event-due` is the arming step. Ship with the flag off, diff the two verdict tables over at least one build cycle, then flip. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* (1) This is a LIVE rank input (D2_FEEDBACK=1 in the given flag set). Fewer convictions means a weaker never-paid penalty, and the W3 sweep evidence says the penalty — not the bonus — is the working lever. A genuine never-payer on a long-running serial event now escapes conviction until the whole event closes: for a 10-day event that is ~10 days of full-rank exposure to a series that demonstrably has not paid. The fix trades a false-penalty window for a slower true penalty; that is a real behavioral cost, not a free correction. (2) NEW UNVERIFIED VENUE SHAPE: I did not verify `/events/{ev}?with_nested_markets=true` or `close_time` on nested markets against the live API from this worktree — the close_time canon is established for /markets/{ticker} and I am extending it. If the field is absent or renamed, every event resolves None and the D2 penalty silently disarms; the alarm for that prints in an offline builder, not in the trading log. (3) One extra venue read per distinct filled event per build (positive-only caching means open events are re-read every run). (4) Item note correction, stated for honesty: the m-36 failure text says the W7 clamp holds a convicted series at probe rungs — the clamp actually keys on credits_n > 0 (_d3_ramp_ct, quoter:553-557), so it clamps every unproven series regardless of this verdict. The live impact of this fix is the D2 rank multiplier only, not sizing.

    _own = None

If ALLOC_KEY=1 live, this reorders capital allocation on deploy and must ship behind KALSHI_SWEEP_OWN_ORDERS=0 with a shadow field so the reordering is measured before it is armed. Do not deploy until the live value of ALLOC_KEY is read.
- *Risk:* (1) The 300s age gate is a guess, not a measurement — the sweeper's cadence and the quoter's cycle time set the real distribution; too tight and the subtraction never applies (fix is inert), too loose and a cancelled ladder is subtracted from a book that no longer holds it (anti-conservative, the direction m-2 exists to prevent). Measure the age distribution from the new `own_age_s` field before tightening. (2) `_F5_SNAPSHOT` is mutable cross-thread state read by the sweeper thread; it must be replaced atomically (rebind a new dict, never mutate in place) or the sweeper can read a half-built map — a Python-3.13 whole-function-scoping trap is not the issue here, but a torn read is. (3) If m-61's claim that ALLOC_KEY=1 live is correct, this fix CHANGES LIVE CAPITAL ORDERING the moment it lands (pcap feeds `_alloc_priority` ~:3660-3666); if ALLOC_KEY=0 it is data-collection only. I could not verify ALLOC_KEY from the env list given in this task — the operator must run `grep -E 'ALLOC_KEY|SWEEP_ENABLED' /opt/pa2-maker-kalshi-live/live.env` and the answer decides whether this ships dark or plain.
- *Collides with:* M-2, m-2, m-61, n-11

**m-40 — kalshi_live/kalshi_capital_rank.py:189**

- *Defect:* Live allocation key's commit denominator is D3-ramp-blind — young tickers charged full join (~10x actual)
- *Fix:* SHARED DESIGN — see M-20, which closes this item: the ct_cap arm of `effective_commit_usd` plus `shadow_rank(ct_cap_fn=..., probe_ct=...)` plus the quoter passing `_d3_est_ct` under KALSHI_ALLOC_RAMP_EST. Same root cause as M-20: the ordering key and the charging key are two copies of the effective-commit model and only the charging copy was made ramp-aware (operator-ruled 08-06, quoter:5066-5070). STANDALONE VARIANT if the operator wants only the ramp term shipped: add `ct_cap_fn=None, probe_ct=0` to shadow_rank (kalshi_capital_rank.py:106-110), compute `ct_cap = min(probe_ct if r.get('explore') and probe_ct > 0 else inf, ct_cap_fn(t) if ct_cap_fn else inf)` and use `commit = min(est_commit_usd(ref, market_cap_usd, inv_hard_ct), ct_cap)` at :189 with `ct_cap` echoed into the component dict; quoter :3667/:847 pass `_d3_est_ct` when D3_RAMP and the new flag are on, EXPLORE_PROBE_CT always. Note the probe cap is the SAME defect on the same line — the ordering key is blind to `min(est, EXPLORE_PROBE_CT)` too — so any standalone version must cover both caps or it re-forks the model a third time.
- *Why:* With D3_RAMP=1 a non-proven series can only rest D3_RUNGS[clamp]=5-10ct (~$5-10), but cap_score divides its capture by the full ~$50-125 join, so a promising unproven market sorts up to ~25x below its true reward-per-committed-dollar — the ordering key and the charging key disagree by construction on every cycle the ramp is active.
- *Standing detector:* Inherits M-20's `plan['alloc_commit_mismatch']` (must be 0 by construction once one function serves both sites) plus `plan['alloc_ct_capped_n']` (how many rows the ct cap bound this cycle) and the `commit_est_err` estimated-vs-actually-resting-dollars series. Without the mismatch counter this exact defect is undetectable until the next adversarial review — which is how it survived the 08-06 walk fix.
- *Test:* test_alloc_commit_unified.py::test_ramped_ticker_is_charged_the_ramp_not_the_full_join — SCORES ref 0.50, MAX_MARKET_CAPITAL 45, INV_HARD_CT 50, ct_cap_fn returning 5 (rung 0): assert commit == 5.0 and that this ticker now outranks an identical-capture proven ticker whose commit is the full ~$45. FAILS BEFORE: shadow_rank has no ct_cap_fn parameter and returns the full join commit. Companion: test_probe_row_is_charged_probe_ct (r['explore']=True, probe_ct=5).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_ALLOC_RAMP_EST default 0 (kwargs default to today's arithmetic). Must be armed separately from KALSHI_ALLOC_REF_AGE because the two push cap_score in opposite directions. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* DIRECTION REVERSAL ON A LIVE RISK CONTROL. Today's blindness biases the ordering TOWARD receipt-proven full-size books and AGAINST young unproven ones. That bias is accidental, but it is aligned with follow-the-profit and with the W7 clamp's whole purpose — the clamp exists because the first-touch loss (78% of a -$7.97 day, one 30ct fill on a series this account had never traded) came from a young unproven series. Fixing the denominator makes unproven young markets rank HIGHER, i.e. it directs the allocation key toward exactly the population the clamp was built to restrain. The sizing clamp still binds (the fix changes ordering, not size), so the exposure per market stays at 5-10ct — but the footprint composition shifts toward unproven series, and that is a real increase in first-touch exposure count. Second: cap_score inflation is bounded by the ct cap, so with D3_RAMP=1 almost every non-proven row's commit collapses to $5-10 and their cap_scores compress into a narrow band — the ordering among them becomes dominated by the capture term with very little capital discrimination, which is a different distortion, not none. Third: like M-20, arming changes the caprank shadow baseline mid-calibration.
- *Collides with:* M-20, n-37

**m-41 — kalshi_live/kalshi_netev_rebuild.py:200**
