- *Fix:* Replace the ad-hoc notional price/count expression at kalshi_netev_rebuild.py:199-201 with the canonical, receipt-verified accessors from the module the file already imports:

  ...

fill_price derives the NO price as 1 - yes (verified 317/317 on the live tape), normalizes cents payloads, and distinguishes ABSENT from 0.0 (`if raw is None`) instead of the `or` chain that treats a legitimate $0.00 as missing. fill_count already falls back count_fp -> count.

- *Standing detector:* `notional_price_fallback_rows` / `notional_count_fallback_rows` in the document plus the stderr WARNING at rebuild time — the counter the item correctly observes exists in kalshi_fill_costs but not here. A non-zero value means the venue renamed a field and the gate's denominator is being derived rather than read.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, in test_netev_rebuild.py: `test_notional_uses_the_complement_when_a_no_fill_lacks_its_own_price` — take _fill(ticker,'no',0.05,100) (yes 0.95 / no 0.05), then `del f['no_price_dollars']`. Today notional == 95.0 (raw yes price, 19x too big); after, notional == 5.0 and doc['notional_price_fallback_rows'] == 1. Second case: `del f['count_fp']` with `f['count']='100'` -> notional 5.0 not 0.0, counter 1. Both asserts fail on current code.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* NO — provably a no-op on the current tape (measured above) and strictly more correct on the shapes where it differs; the added fields are reporting-only and invisible to kalshi_netev_calibrate.load_table, which returns only data['families'].
- *Risk:* Low, and the no-op is measured rather than asserted: on the frozen tape (kalshi_live/AUD_fills.json, 542 fills) 0 rows are missing outcome_side, yes_price_dollars, no_price_dollars or count_fp, and 0 acquired-side prices are 0.00 — so fill_price(row) equals the old `_px` on every row and the deployed table's numbers do not move. Residual honest risks: (a) fill_outcome RAISES on an unrecognised fill shape where the old code silently booked a wrong number — intentional, and it adds no new crash surface because replay_fills already calls fill_outcome on every fill at :163, so such a fill already crashes the rebuild today; (b) if the venue ever emits cents, fill_price divides by 100 and the denominator legitimately changes under an already-deployed gate — which is exactly why the counters and the WARNING are part of the fix; (c) fill_count's `count` fallback means a row that previously contributed 0 notional now contributes real notional, moving net_pct toward 0 (looser) for that family.
- *Collides with:* M-21, M-22

The live steady state mixes them (6 receipt + ~30 unproven families in the deployed table), so the $/day scale always dominates and the key is uninterpretable — any threshold reasoning built on it at B8 is unit-corrupted at the source, which is precisely the denominator failure RULE SIX exists to prevent.

Pair it with the M-29 unit-contract test so producer and consumer units are pinned on both sides.

Assert `plan["netev_min_receipt_frac"] == -0.046` and `plan["netev_min_model_usd_day"] == -4.8` and `plan["netev_mixed_cycles"] == 1` — today only `netev_min_signal == -4.8` exists and the receipt family's number is unrecoverable from the row.

I judge that better than removing it (removal breaks any existing consumer and rewrites the meaning of historical rows); the `netev_signal_units` marker is what makes it self-labelling. (2) Two new min()-accumulators mean two more keys whose sentinel is 1e18 when the branch never fires — the plan emit must guard on presence, or a cycle with no receipt families writes 1e18 into the row and poisons any aggregation over the file. That is a real footgun in the existing pattern (`netev_min_signal` is only emitted when `netev_skipped` is truthy for exactly this reason) and the fix must copy that guard, not the naive emit.

**m-43 — kalshi_live/kalshi_fill_costs.py:122**

- *Defect:* Fee convention mismatch between live rows and settled rows in the fill-cost feed
- *Fix:* DETERMINE THE VENUE'S FEE CONVENTION FROM DATA THE BUILD ALREADY HAS, STAMP EVERY ROW WITH ITS BASIS, AND NORMALIZE ONLY BEHIND A FLAG — kalshi_fill_costs.build(). (1) Stamp each row with its provenance: live rows (:92-96) get `"basis": "venue_realized"`, settled rows (:136-141) get `"basis": "replay_net_of_fees"` (they already are: fill_cashflow subtracts fill_fee at kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:212). Additive keys; kalshi_capital_rank.shadow_rank reads only cost_usd_day (:192). (2) RESOLVE THE UNVERIFIED CLAIM in the module header (:10-12) using the per-ticker replay M-15 introduces: for every ticker that is UNSETTLED and whose replayed net position is 0 (all pairs closed), the replay's cash sum IS the realized P&L net of fees, so compare it against the venue's realized_pnl_dollars for the same ticker. Matching within a cent on n>=3 tickers => venue is NET; matching (venue_realized - fees) => venue is GROSS. Write the verdict and its evidence count as top-level feed keys: `"fee_convention": "net"|"gross"|"unknown"`, `"fee_convention_n": k`. (3) NORMALIZE behind KALSHI_FILLCOST_FEE_NORM (default 0): when the verdict is 'gross' and the flag is 1, live rows book realized = venue_realized - fees so both populations are net-of-fees and cost_usd_day is comparable. Flag off => byte-identical output plus the new telemetry keys. (4) THIS ALSO CLOSES n-29 (same root cause, same code).
- *Why:* Closes 'two row populations on different accounting bases inside one ranking feed': settled rows are definitively net of fees while live rows carry a venue field whose convention the module itself declares unverified, so cost_usd_day systematically differs by the fee total between the two populations and the ranking prefers not-yet-settled markets for an accounting reason rather than an economic one.
- *Standing detector:* The convention check runs on EVERY build and writes fee_convention/fee_convention_n into the feed — so a venue-side change of convention shows up as the verdict flipping (or n collapsing to 0), which is machine-detectable from the feed file and by the quoter (count _SILENT['fillcost_fee_convention_flip'] when the new verdict differs from the file's previous one). That is the class detector: 'a receipt field silently changed meaning'.
- *Test:* New test_fill_costs_fee_basis.py, failing-before. Fixture: ticker 'FLAT' with a yes fill at $0.40 x50 and an offsetting no fill at $0.55 x50 (replay cash +$2.50 with fee 0, so realized is unambiguous), plus a live positions row for FLAT carrying realized_pnl_dollars = 2.50 + fees and fees_paid_dollars = fees. Assert (a) out['FLAT']['basis'] == 'venue_realized', (b) fc.LAST_BUILD['fee_convention'] == 'gross', (c) with the norm flag on, realized_pnl_usd == 2.50. All three fail today (no keys, no determination, no normalization). Add the no-op pin: flag off => output equals current build output exactly except for the additive keys.
- *Effort:* L · *Dark:* yes — telemetry (basis stamps + fee_convention verdict) ships live and unflagged because it is pure addition; the NORMALIZATION ships behind KALSHI_FILLCOST_FEE_NORM=0 because it changes a measured baseline that other calibrations sit on. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* CONCERNING IF ENABLED. (1) Turning the normalization on CHANGES cost_usd_day for every live market, which changes the caprank shadow ranking mid-experiment and would invalidate any calibration fitted against the current feed (KALSHI_CAPRANK_CALIB, the D2/D3 evidence tables) — and if ALLOC_KEY is ever 1 it changes live capital ordering. That is why it ships dark. (2) The determination can legitimately come back 'unknown' forever: it needs unsettled tickers that are exactly flat, and there may be none on any given build — so the fix may deliver only a stamp and no verdict for a while. Say so rather than pretending it resolves the question on day one. (3) The comparison assumes the venue's realized_pnl_dollars covers the same lifetime as our fill tape for that ticker; if the tape is truncated (m-32/m-35 class) the comparison mis-fires and could produce a WRONG verdict — gate the verdict on the tape passing the position-recon check (m-35's shared helper) before trusting it. (4) Adds two more top-level keys to the feed the P2 verdict reads.
- *Collides with:* M-15, n-29, n-28, m-44

**m-46 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_client.py:433**

- *Defect:* get_credit_history: single un-cursored GET (limit=1000), no truncation tripwire, consumed as full lifetime tape
- *Fix:* maker_kalshi_client.py `get_credit_history` — page it with the same discipline as `_get_paginated`, plus a tripwire for the case where the venue offers no cursor at all. Signature and return key are preserved (all five consumers do `.get("credits") or []`).
```python
def get_credit_history(self, limit=1000):
    if not CREDIT_USER_ID:
        raise RuntimeError("KALSHI_USER_ID unset — credit_history needs the account id")
    rows, seen, cursor, last = [], set(), "", {}
    for _ in range(50):                      # same runaway bound as _get_paginated
        qs = f"limit={int(limit)}" + (f"&cursor={cursor}" if cursor else "")
        last = self._request("GET",
            f"/v1/users/{CREDIT_USER_ID}/credit_history?{qs}", base=CREDIT_BASE)
        page = last.get("credits") or []
        fresh = [r for r in page if r.get("credit_id") not in seen]
        seen.update(r.get("credit_id") for r in fresh)
        rows.extend(fresh)
        cursor = last.get("cursor") or last.get("next_cursor") or ""
        if not cursor or not fresh:          # `not fresh` = the venue ignored our cursor
            break
    out = dict(last); out["credits"] = rows; out["cursor"] = cursor
    if cursor:
        PAGINATION_TRUNCATED[0] += 1
        print("WARNING credit_history hit the 50-page cap with a LIVE cursor — INCOMPLETE")
    elif len(rows) >= int(limit) and not (last.get("cursor") or last.get("next_cursor")):
        PAGINATION_TRUNCATED[0] += 1
        print(f"WARNING credit_history returned {len(rows)} rows == limit and the venue "
              f"offered NO cursor — the lifetime tape may be TRUNCATED and there is no "
              f"way to page it; every consumer is reading a partial view")
    return out
```
The dedupe-by-credit_id + `not fresh` break is load-bearing: if the v1 endpoint ignores an unknown `cursor` param it would otherwise return page 1 fifty times and hand the netev rebuild 50x duplicated credits.
- *Why:* `kalshi_netev_rebuild.main` (:389) and four other tools consume this single un-cursored GET as the COMPLETE lifetime credit tape for whole-market credit attribution and the `credits_out_of_scope` reconciliation. Unlike `_get_paginated` it never inspects a cursor and never compares len(rows) to limit, so once lifetime credits pass 1000 (58+ today and growing daily across ~30 families) the oldest silently vanish: families are under-credited, receipt verdicts bias pessimistic, and the reconciliation mis-reconciles with no warning — the exact 'feed that quietly stops mentioning things' class the module's own pagination comment names.

Wire the same alarm into `kalshi_netev_rebuild` so the table cannot be rebuilt from a truncated tape without saying so.

    assert "cursor=c1" in calls[1]

Today the first fails (one page only, 'b' missing) and the second cannot be expressed.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no flag on the client method (a read that returns MORE of the truth is not a behavior toggle), but the table publish IS the gate: treat `kalshi_netev_table.json` as the dark surface — rebuild, diff, operator-review, then publish. That sequencing is mandatory, not optional. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* CONCERNING, and not for the reason it looks. This is a read-only client method, but its output feeds `kalshi_netev_rebuild` → `kalshi_netev_table.json` → the LIVE NETEV_GATE (NETEV_GATE=1, NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT=-7.0). Recovering previously-invisible older credits changes families' `net_pct_notional`, which changes which families the live gate admits — i.e. a read-path fix silently re-tunes live market selection through a data file, and it invalidates the calibration the current table was built on. It must NOT be shipped as 'just a pagination fix': rebuild the table offline first, DIFF it against the live `kalshi_netev_table.json` family-by-family, and present the verdict flips to the operator before publishing the new table. Second risk: the cursor field name on the v1 users path is a GUESS (the endpoint's pagination shape has never been observed) — the dedupe/`not fresh` break is what keeps a wrong guess from corrupting the tape with duplicates rather than merely failing to page. **m-48 — kalshi_live/w17_coverage_ledger.py:124**

- *Defect:* EARNING bucket measured on INTENDED size, not resting presence — placement failures masked as earning
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/w17_coverage_ledger.py lines 100-135 — bucket on RESTING presence, not intended size, and name the gap. (2) Reclassify: max_rest > PROBE_SCALE_CT -> EARNING (annotated '{max_rest:.0f}ct resting'); 0 < max_rest <= PROBE_SCALE_CT -> LIMITED with the existing limiter naming; max_rest == 0 and max_ct > 0 across ALL recent cycles -> UNKNOWN with reason 'intended {max_ct:.0f}ct, ZERO resting across {n} cycles (placement-failure class)'; max_ct == 0 and gates -> EXCLUDED as today; else UNKNOWN as today. The all-cycles requirement is what prevents a false alarm on a market that legitimately entered this cycle. (3) Factor the decision into a pure `_bucket(agg, gates, probe_scale)` so it is testable.
- *Why:* `ct = max(y_ct, n_ct)` (line 124) is the quoter's INTENDED size ('0 while gated/parked', quoter:2785) while `y_rest_ct`/`n_rest_ct` ('what is ACTUALLY resting', quoter:2786) is ignored — so a market whose orders are rejected, refused by the 1c-wall guard, or torn is reported as EARNING while zero contracts rest at the venue, in the one ledger whose entire purpose is finding non-earning pool dollars.
- *Standing detector:* A dedicated headline counter printed with the UNKNOWN section: 'intended-but-not-resting: N markets, $X/day pool' — the placement-failure class gets its own standing number instead of being absorbed into EARNING. Recurrence is visible as that number moving.
- *Test:* In kalshi_live/test_w17_alarms.py over `_bucket`: rows for 3 consecutive cycles with y_ct=25, y_rest_ct=0, no gates -> bucket == 'UNKNOWN' and reason contains 'ZERO resting' (FAILS BEFORE: ct=25 > 5 -> EARNING). Plus: y_rest_ct=25 -> EARNING; y_rest_ct=3 -> LIMITED; first-cycle-only intent (2 of 3 cycles show resting) -> EARNING, no false alarm.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — read-only diagnostic. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* This moves pool dollars out of EARNING and into the UNKNOWN headline — the alarm bucket — and the size of that move is unknown until it runs. It changes a coverage baseline the operator is actively using to judge whether the bot covers its universe, mid-experiment, and it will look like a regression when it is actually a measurement correction. Also: *_rest_ct reflects own_side at telemetry-build time (state BEFORE this cycle's placements), so a market with a fast cancel/replace cadence could read 0 legitimately in a snapshot-thin window; the all-K-cycles requirement mitigates but does not eliminate this — with --cycles 5 at a 30-120s cadence the window is only a few minutes. Consider requiring K>=5 explicitly in the reason string so the operator can judge.

**m-49 — kalshi_live/w11_replay.py:154**

- *Defect:* never_paid demotion uses frozen full-window fills — lookahead in the D2 replay policy
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/w11_replay.py — replace the frozen whole-window fills map with a time-guarded verdict computed by the LIVE rule. (1) Import the canon builder: `from kalshi_credit_feedback import build_feedback`. (2) New helper `_verdict_at(ts, raw, cache)`: filters credits to created_at <= ts, settlements to settled_time <= ts, and orders to last_update_time <= ts (documented proxy: the latest instant at which that order's fills are certainly complete — order rows carry no per-fill timestamp), then calls build_feedback(credits_t, orders_t, setts_t, now=ts, event_of=lambda t: raw['market_meta'].get(t,{}).get('event_ticker')) and memoizes on the hour bucket of ts (cycles are 30-120s; ~24 rebuilds/day). (3) replay() passes `ctx['feedback'] = _verdict_at(ts, raw, cache)` alongside the existing keys; make_policy_credit_feedback prefers ctx['feedback'] (verdict 'paid' -> bonus, 'never_paid_due' -> never_paid_mult, else untouched) and falls back to the old credited/fills keys when absent so the existing unit tests keep working. (4) replay() gains the `raw` snapshot as an argument (single caller, main()) — signature change is the fix. (5) Print in the honest-limits header: 'fill knowledge is ORDER-granular (last_update_time); replayed demotions can lag the live D2 by up to one order lifetime'.
- *Why:* ctx['fills_by_series'] is computed once from the whole frozen snapshot (line 129) and handed to every cycle, so at an early cycle the policy demotes a series on fills that had not happened yet — and it demotes on a cruder rule (fills>0 and no credit) than the live D2 uses (never_paid_due: zero credits AND a DUE settled filled event, 72h margin). Both errors bias replayed never_paid_mult verdicts pessimistic for late-filling series.
- *Standing detector:* Report field `verdict_transitions_per_series` (how many times each series changed verdict across the replay) plus a printed 'lookahead guard: credits/orders/settlements all filtered at cycle ts' provenance line. A future edit that reintroduces a whole-window input shows up as zero transitions on a series that demonstrably changed state.
- *Test:* In kalshi_live/test_w11_replay.py: series KXC has its only order with last_update_time on day 5 and no credits ever; assert that at a day-1 cycle the d2_feedback ranking does NOT demote KXC, and at a day-6 cycle (after the 72h due margin on a settled event) it does (FAILS BEFORE: demoted from cycle 1 because fills are frozen full-window).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — offline harness. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Cost: build_feedback over ~1000 orders x 24 hourly buckets per replayed day is fine, but a multi-week replay with per-cycle bucketing would not be — the memoization key must stay coarse. Correctness caveat that must be stated in the report: last_update_time is an upper bound on fill time, so the replay now under-demotes slightly where live would have demoted earlier — the bias is reduced and inverted, not eliminated, and no frozen feed can do better without per-fill timestamps. Like M-25 this invalidates the existing W11 numbers; landing it separately from M-25 means two successive baseline moves, so run the report ONCE after both land.
- *Collides with:* M-25, n-44

- *Why:* The chain lets a position that CONTAINS a run-over contract fall through into a negative or control set whenever its net P&L is >= 0 (GAS + worthless lot + profit -> neg_strict; TEMP + worthless lot + flat -> temp_control), which places run-over contracts in the denominator of the only unconfounded comparison the study has.
- *Standing detector:* A CONTROL PURITY assert-and-print in labels.main(): bad = [p for p in pos if p['any_zero_exit'] and p['label'] in ('neg_strict','neg_broad','temp_control')]; assert not bad — printed as 'CONTROL PURITY: 0 worthless-lot positions inside a negative/control set'. Plus a printed cross-tab of (family x any_zero x sign(pnl) x label) so the moment a re-run produces the new cell it is visible, and a 'worthless_flat n=.. pnl=..' line in the group table.
- *Test:* kalshi_live/test_drift_label_control_purity.py — write a small CSV to tmp_path with the real header (type,quantity_fp,market_ticker,side,entry_price_dollars,exit_price_dollars,open_fees_dollars,close_fees_dollars,realized_pnl_without_fees_dollars,realized_pnl_with_fees_dollars,close_timestamp,open_timestamp): a KXAAAGAS position with one lot exiting at 0.00 plus a profitable lot summing to pnl > 0, and a KXTEMP position with a 0.00 lot and net pnl 0.0. load_positions(csv_path) labels them 'neg_strict' and 'temp_control' today; assert both labels lie outside {'neg_strict','neg_broad','temp_control'}. Add a companion assertion that the frozen CSV still reconciles (18 positives, -$46.21), proving the fix is a no-op on the 07-23 numbers.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no
- *Risk:* If a future receipt set contains many worthless_flat positions they now belong to NO group, so the negative sets shrink and FPR denominators fall — the study becomes less powerful rather than wrong, but that must be reported rather than silently absorbed. On the frozen CSV the fix is a verified no-op (0 positions hit the new branch), so it cannot be validated against a real firing case until new receipts exist; the tmp_path CSV is the only proof it works. Anything downstream that enumerates labels as 'not positive' would silently pick up the new label — I checked all four consumers in this family and none do, but a future consumer written that way would reintroduce the contamination.
- *Collides with:* n-50

**m-56 — kalshi_live/signal_book_drift.py:228**

- *Defect:* f_at substitutes future candles (up to +5 min) for at-fill fair value; leakage into prior-drift blocker sweep
- *Fix:* In signal_book_drift.py, lift f_at out of the main() closure to module level and make the search direction explicit and asymmetric: def f_at(idx, tk, t, back=5, fwd=0) -> (value, resolved_ts), searching t, t-60 ... t-back*60 first and only then t+60 ... t+fwd*60, returning the resolved timestamp with the value. Call sites at :238 become f0 = f_at(idx, tk, t, back=5, fwd=0) and fm4 = f_at(idx, tk, t-240, back=5, fwd=0) — strictly no future data on the decision side — and fp10 = f_at(idx, tk, t+600, back=5, fwd=5) with a hard invariant resolved_ts >= t + 300, so the outcome may be sampled a little early or late but never from inside the fill minute. Record f0_lag_s and fm4_lag_s on each rec. Leave sgn = 1 if fl['book_side'] == 'bid' else -1 exactly as written — verified correct against the feed contract (317/317 agreement, kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:110-111) and NOT the side-derived sign trap.
- *Why:* The current search alternates t-k*60 then t+k*60, so when the past minute has no usable book — which is most likely precisely when the book collapsed through us — f0 is silently taken from up to 5 minutes AFTER the fill, and prior = sgn*(f0-fm4) then contains part of the run-over it is supposed to predict.
- *Standing detector:* A LEAKAGE GUARD line in section 4: 'max f0 lag = ..s (must be <= 0), max fm4 lag = ..s (must be <= 0)' backed by an assert, plus 'fills dropped for no prior book: N (run-over M / not-run-over K)'. Printing the missingness split BY CLASS is the real alarm — class-correlated dropout is what turns a leak from noise into a bias, and it is invisible in an aggregate count.
- *Test:* kalshi_live/test_book_drift_no_future_leak.py — build idx = {'TK': {t0: None, t0+60: 0.42}} (past minute unusable, future minute usable) and assert f_at(idx,'TK',t0,back=5,fwd=0) == (None, None); today's closure returns 0.42. Second case: with a usable candle at t0-120, assert the resolved ts is t0-120 and the reported lag is negative. Third: fp10 never resolves to a ts inside [t-60, t+300).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no
- *Risk:* (a) f0 coverage falls, and it falls preferentially on run-over fills, so the run-over n in section 4 may become too small to report — the honest outcome, but it removes a table the 07-23 deliverable published. (b) The prior-drift blocker TPRs drop; the 'prior drift is weak / life-frac is the free signal' conclusion gets stronger rather than weaker, but the supporting numbers change and must be re-issued rather than paraphrased. (c) Extracting the closure makes idx an explicit parameter; the file is a standalone script with no external callers, so the blast radius is contained to main().
- *Collides with:* M-27

**m-57 — kalshi_live/kalshi_drift_fetch.py:155**

- *Defect:* Resume logic freezes a transient market-fetch failure as a permanent empty entry; silent survivorship hole
- *Fix:* Three changes in kalshi_drift_fetch.py plus one reporting change in kalshi_drift_detect.py, all in the class 'a failure must never look like data'. (1) fetch_market(tk) returns (status, market_or_None) instead of collapsing every non-200 into None — the signature IS the defect because it destroys the only evidence of transience; one caller. (2) Extract the per-ticker body of main() into fetch_one(tk) -> dict and, on failure, write {'market': None, 'candles': None, 'trades': None, 'fetch_error': {'status': st, 'at': time.time()}} — with candles/trades None, which the EXISTING resume predicate at :155-157 already handles correctly. The resume check was right; the writer lied by storing []. (3) Tape truncation: when pages == max_pages and a cursor remains and oldest > stop_before_ts, set entry['trades_truncated'] = True and print it; keep max_pages=12 — the cap is a legitimate resource bound, the silence is the bug. (4) End of main(): print 'FETCH FAILURES: [...]' and 'TRUNCATED TAPE: [...]' and sys.exit(1) when either is non-empty, so a partial cache cannot be silently consumed. (5) In detect.py add make_state_reason(p, cache, all_positions) -> (state|None, reason) and reduce make_state to 'return make_state_reason(...)[0]', preserving the signature for all five callers (detect/eval/jump/cadence_refute/selftest), then have detect.main() print a DROPPED POSITIONS table (ticker, side, reason in {no_cache_entry, fetch_error, no_market, timeline_too_short}) in place of the bare 'usable X/Y' line.
- *Why:* A 5xx or timeout freezes as a permanent empty cache entry that resume skips forever, and the affected position then vanishes from every group with no per-ticker record — a survivorship hole that is invisible in the output and that correlates with whatever caused the failure (delisted or freshly-settled markets being the obvious candidates).
- *Standing detector:* The FETCH FAILURES / TRUNCATED TAPE lists plus a non-zero exit code from the fetcher, and the DROPPED POSITIONS table printed by every harness run ('0 dropped' being the healthy line). Together they make 'the study silently lost a position' impossible to miss, which is the recurrence condition for the whole class, not just for this instance.
- *Test:* kalshi_live/test_drift_fetch_resume.py — monkeypatch kalshi_drift_fetch.get to return (503, 'boom'); entry = F.fetch_one('KXTEST-1'); assert entry['candles'] is None and entry['trades'] is None and entry['fetch_error']['status'] == 503. Today the same path yields {'candles': [], 'trades': []}, which the resume predicate treats as complete, so the 'is None' assertions fail today. Second test: a cache containing that entry is NOT skipped by the resume check. Third: make_state_reason returns reason 'no_market' for a fetch_error entry instead of a bare None.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no
- *Risk:* (a) The next real fetch run will retry tickers previously frozen as empty, costing network time, and will keep retrying a genuinely delisted ticker on every run — the fetch_error record carries the status so a permanent 404 can be told apart later, but this fix deliberately does NOT stop retrying 404s, because that would be a ban and a 404 can be transient during settlement migration. (b) A non-zero exit will break any wrapper assuming exit 0; only manual invocation exists today, so this is a latent rather than an active break. (c) make_state keeps its signature but a second entry point now exists, and that duplication is precisely the mechanism that produced M-27 — the wrapper must stay exactly one line, and the review of any future edit must check it.
- *Collides with:* M-27, M-28, n-50

**m-59 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3021**

- *Fix:* CLOSED BY m-20 (shared root fix #3) — same helper, same two call sites (~:3021-3024, ~:3046), same flag. m-59's distinct contribution, which m-20's design carries explicitly: it names the COST side of the inconsistency. After m-20, the reward term scales with the size that will rest while NETEV_FINGERPRINT_USD_DAY=$5/day stays flat, so the two sides of the unproven-family comparison remain on different size bases (now erring conservative instead of anti-conservative). I am NOT inventing a scaling for the fingerprint — there is no receipt basis for it, and inventing one would be exactly the unvalidated-confidence pattern. Instead: emit `netev_fingerprint_usd_day` and `netev_ramp_scale` side by side in the plan row so B8's haircut/fingerprint re-fit has both bases on every row, and state the open question in the commit message as a named B8 input rather than resolving it in code.

- *Test:* Covered by m-20's T1/T4. m-59-specific pin: on a fixture where pc=$18/day at full size and the ramp scale is ~0.11, assert the plan row carries BOTH `netev_ramp_scale` ~0.11 and `netev_fingerprint_usd_day` 5.0 (fails today: neither key exists), so the inconsistency is measurable rather than argued.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* Rides KALSHI_RAMP_BASIS (m-20's flag). The two telemetry fields are unflagged.
- *Risk:* The residual after m-20 is a KNOWN, DELIBERATE inconsistency: a market whose 5ct economics are genuinely fine can still be refused because the flat $5 fingerprint was calibrated against full-size fills. That is a real false-refusal channel on a live money path, left open on purpose because closing it without receipts would be an invented calibration. The honest cost of my recommendation is that the unproven lane stays somewhat over-tight until B8 re-fits.
- *Collides with:* m-20, M-29

**m-60 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3183**

- *Defect:* Settle-ramp anchors on raw WIND_DOWN_MIN (45) while wind-down is effective (30-36) - MIN_QUOTE_CT 9-15min early
- *Fix:* In desired_quotes' settlement ramp (:3180-3185) anchor the taper on the SAME clock the function already stops quoting on at :2839: `_wd = _effective_wind_down_min(m.get('life_min'))`, then `scale = max(0.0, (mins_left - _wd) / max(1.0, ramp_min - _wd))`. - *Standing detector:* Plan key `ramp_floored_markets` = markets whose accumulating counts were driven to MIN_QUOTE_CT by the ramp scale while mins_left is still greater than _effective_wind_down_min (i.e. while the market is still quotable). Nonzero on every short program today; ~0 once armed. It is a standing meter of misalignment between the two clocks, so it catches any future re-divergence — including someone re-introducing an absolute constant.
- *Test:* test_db_reward_fixes.py::test_ramp_anchors_on_effective_wind_down — market life_min=180 (=> _effective_wind_down_min = 36, ramp_min = min(180, 0.5*180) = 90), two-sided qualifying book, flat, now = end - 40 min. BEFORE: scale = (40-45)/45 clamps to 0 and y_cnt == n_cnt == MIN_QUOTE_CT (2). AFTER (flag on): scale = 4/54 and both counts exceed MIN_QUOTE_CT. Companion pin: a life_min=1440 market produces byte-identical counts with the flag on and off (proof the change cannot touch long programs).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_RAMP_WD_EFFECTIVE, default 0. Live money-path change on the flagship short-program class; arm after one day of ramp_floored_markets telemetry. The knob must be registered in _refresh_safety_knobs in the SAME commit (or land m-6 first) or it ships as a silent no-op. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* This restores accumulating size in the final quotable minutes of short programs — the highest informed-flow window, and precisely what the settlement ramp exists to shrink (audit HIGH-2's 'be SMALL at settlement so the settle-taker is a rare backstop'). On a 1-3h program the recovered size sits 36-45 min from settlement, one fill away from SETTLE_UNWIND_MIN=30, so a fill taken in the recovered window has an elevated chance of being crossed out at a paid spread instead of unwound passively at $0 maker fee. It also breaks comparability of the D-G capture baseline for short programs across the arming date — any before/after read of D-G that spans it is invalid.

### NOTE findings

**n-1 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1355**

- *Defect:* F17 sub-hour benefit half-eaten by the SETTLE_UNWIND_MIN floor — 58-min windows still forfeit ~52%
- *Recommendation:* NO CODE CHANGE — The code is correct as written; the floor exists to preserve the C8 maker-first/taker-second ordering and removing or scaling it would weaken the guaranteed-flat backstop on short-window markets for at most ~18 minutes of extra entry coverage per 58-min program. The defect is that the F17 headline overstates the benefit, which is fixed by restating the number, not by changing the clock. If the operator later wants that coverage, the honest sequence is: measure from the coverage ledger how much reward those minutes actually carry, then decide about the taker window on its own evidence and its own commit.
- *Why:* The SETTLE_UNWIND_MIN floor is load-bearing (C8 maker-first ordering: resting accumulating quotes inside the armed taker window re-creates quote->fill->taker churn), so the coverage ceiling is a correct consequence of a correct constraint — what is wrong is the claimed magnitude, which is a measurement/expectation defect, not an order defect.
- *Standing detector:* plan['wind_down_floor_bound'] and plan['wind_down_eff_min_p50'] — they turn 'the F17 headline overstates the gain' into a number on every row, and they are the input any future decision about scaling SETTLE_UNWIND_MIN would need.
- *Test:* Unit pin on `_effective_wind_down_min` (:1482): with SETTLE_UNWIND_MIN=30 assert `_effective_wind_down_min(58) == 30.0` (the floor binds); with SETTLE_UNWIND_MIN=3 assert `== pytest.approx(11.6)` (the proportional term binds). This pins WHICH term binds, so a future edit to either knob cannot silently change the enterable window. The docstring claim is not testable; the number is. Plus one harness assert that plan['wind_down_floor_bound'] is emitted and non-zero on a short-window fixture — fails before (key absent).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — comment plus two counters.
- *Risk:* Telemetry and documentation only; no order-path change. The real risk here is doing MORE than this: scaling SETTLE_UNWIND_MIN for short programs — the 'future work, noted, NOT built' line in the docstring — would let the last-resort taker fire proportionally later on the fastest-moving hourly books, i.e. carry delta closer to resolution exactly where passive unwind is least reliable. Do not bundle that with this item.

**n-2 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:1330**

- *Defect:* F3 scales by the MAX of the two sides' join sizes — asymmetric books get double-discounted
- *Fix:* Stop post-scaling a blended number; model the real per-side size inside the capture estimate. Add a keyword-only `rest_ct=None` to `_prospective_capture` (:2584 — additive, every existing caller byte-identical):

    _cy = _capped_join(best_y, best_n); _cn = _capped_join(best_n, best_y)
    if rest_ct is not None:
        _cy, _cn = min(_cy, rest_ct), min(_cn, rest_ct)
    ry, qy = _qualifying_score(yl, best_y, _cy, target, df, own_orders=_own.get('yes'))
    rn, qn = _qualifying_score(nl, best_n, _cn, target, df, own_orders=_own.get('no'))

Then `_expected_credit_usd` passes `rest_ct=_eff_rest_ct(m, now.timestamp())` (m-8's helper) and DELETES the `join_ct = max(...)` / `pc *= eff_ct/join_ct` ratio at :1322-1331 entirely. This is strictly more faithful than the ratio: `_qualifying_score` already computes each side's share non-linearly, so the clamped size flows through exactly the math the unclamped size does, per side, with no blending artifact.
- *Why:* `join_ct = max(_capped_join(yes), _capped_join(no))` divides the blended pc by the LARGER side's ratio, so on an asymmetric book the smaller side is discounted up to ~5x more than reality — conservative but wrong, refusing borderline payers at the $1.20 floor for a modelling artifact rather than an economic fact.
- *Standing detector:* plan['presence_gate_model'] = 'per_side'|'ratio' so every row states which estimator produced its verdicts, plus plan['presence_exp_delta_p50'] — the median difference between the per-side and legacy-ratio expectation across evaluated markets, computed in SHADOW while the flag is off. The admission-rate change is then a measured number before arming rather than a reward drift discovered afterwards.
- *Test:* test_presence_gate.py::test_asymmetric_book_is_not_double_discounted — yes join 50 ct, no join 10 ct, ramp 5 ct, pool sized so the legacy path lands just under MIN_CREDIT_USD and the per-side path just over; assert refused with KALSHI_F3_PER_SIDE=0 and admitted with 1. Fails before (the flag does not exist and the market is always refused). Companion `test_symmetric_book_is_identical_under_both_models` and `test_sweep_measure_unchanged_by_default` pin the no-op properties.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* YES — KALSHI_F3_PER_SIDE, default 0, with the shadow delta emitted while OFF. It relaxes an entry gate on a live money path; it must be measured before it is armed. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* GENUINELY CONCERNING: this ADMITS MORE markets — the opposite direction from m-8 — so landing both leaves a net admission change that is not monotone and cannot be predicted from either commit in isolation. It also invalidates the current calibration of MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20, whose 20% margin was chosen against the ratio model's known bias, and it moves the presence/coverage baseline mid-experiment (D3 ramp and W17 coverage are both being measured against the current admission rate). `_prospective_capture` is additionally consumed by `_sweep_measure` (:2651) and the capture gate, so the default `rest_ct=None` must be pinned byte-identical for those callers or the sweeper's stored capture numbers shift under the ranking layer.
- *Collides with:* m-8, m-58, M-1

**n-3 — kalshi_live/w0b_payout_timing.py:45**

- *Defect:* 0b throttle retries only HTTP 429 — a transient URLError still kills the run mid-evidence
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/w0b_payout_timing.py — (1) `_get` (lines 44-57): retry on the transient class, not just HTTP 429 — catch urllib.error.HTTPError (retry on 429 and 5xx, re-raise other 4xx immediately, since a 404 is data not weather), urllib.error.URLError, socket.timeout and ConnectionError; keep the existing backoff (0.6 + attempt*2.0) and re-raise the last error after 5 attempts. (2) Wrap the /incentive_programs pagination loop (lines 121-128) in a per-call try that, on failure, records `partial_pages` and breaks instead of aborting the run. (3) Wrap the whole FIX-H section so a failure there cannot lose the per-credit evidence already printed. (4) Every section prints its own completeness marker: 'programs: {n} rows over {p} pages, cursor exhausted: yes|NO (PARTIAL)'.
- *Why:* The retry loop re-raises any non-429 HTTPError immediately and never catches URLError/socket timeout, and the pagination loop has no per-call guard — one transient network fault on the VPS aborts the run mid-evidence, printing the per-credit section but not the FIX-H admitted set, which a hurried reader reads as 'admitted now: 0'.
- *Standing detector:* The per-section completeness markers ('cursor exhausted: yes/NO (PARTIAL)') and a run-level 'EVIDENCE COMPLETE / PARTIAL' final line. This is the same class as M-3's denominator: partial output must be self-labeling, because absence of a section is indistinguishable from a zero result.
- *Test:* In kalshi_live/test_w0b_timing.py with urllib.request.urlopen monkeypatched: raising URLError twice then returning a payload -> `_get` returns the payload (FAILS BEFORE: URLError escapes on the first attempt); a 404 HTTPError -> raises immediately without burning retries; a pagination stub that fails on page 2 -> main() still prints the FIX-H header and the marker contains 'PARTIAL'.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no.
- *Risk:* Broadened retries can mask a genuine venue outage as slowness — the run gets up to 5 x (0.6 + backoff) per call slower and could take minutes on a bad network, and an operator watching for a fast failure now waits. The completeness markers are the compensating control. Retrying 5xx on a read-only GET is safe (no writes anywhere in this tool).
- *Collides with:* M-3

**n-4 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2807**

- *Defect:* Telemetry share reads 0.0 for a resting-but-not-quoting solo book (wind-down keep-reduce edge)
- *Fix:* In `_market_telemetry_row`, when a side has NO accumulating intended quote but we DO have resting orders there, score the RESTING orders instead of reporting zero. Built on top of n-38's list-based scorer (land n-38 first):
    src = "quote"
    if not our_lv and (_own_ord.get(side) or []):
        our_lv = [(p, c) for p, c in _own_ord[side] if p is not None]
        src = "resting"
    ... score computed from our_lv exactly as in n-38 ...
    row[tag + "_src"] = src if our_lv else "none"
The existing denominator line is unchanged (`denom = max(df_total - rest_df, 0.0) + score`): with score computed from the same resting levels that rest_df subtracts, the two cancel and a solo book reads share ~1.0 instead of 0.0 — which is what the venue is actually paying us until the order is cancelled.
- *Why:* During wind-down keep-reduce (and any cycle where the desired list holds only the reducing side), score=0 while rest_df equals the whole book, so denom collapses to 0 and share is logged as 0.0 on cycles where we are in fact the entire qualifying book. That biases the presence table and the B8 calibration LOW for exactly the late-window families where reward accrual is densest.
- *Standing detector:* `y_src`/`n_src` on every row plus a per-cycle `telemetry_resting_only_sides` counter. Standing alarm: any row with `*_rest_ct > 0` and `*_share == 0.0` is now impossible by construction — assert it as an invariant in the nightly quotes-*.jsonl scan, so the whole class ('we measured ourselves as absent while we were resting') is caught, not just the wind-down instance.
- *Test:* Failing-before, in test_market_telemetry.py: solo book [(0.30, 50.0)] both sides, `quotes` containing ONLY a reducing 'unwind' quote on one side (or empty), `own_orders={'yes':[(0.30,50.0)],'no':[(0.30,50.0)]}`, own_side counts 50/50. Assert `row['y_share'] > 0.99` and `row['y_src'] == 'resting'` — today it is 0.0. Second pin: with an accumulating quote present, `y_src == 'quote'` and the row is byte-identical to today's.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* NO. Telemetry-only and it cannot alter `desired`. But it is NOT calibration-neutral — flag the presence-table re-derivation in the same commit message.
- *Risk:* (1) capture_usd_day becomes non-zero on wind-down/unwind-only cycles. The SCORES fold is guarded to cycles with at least one accumulating quote (~:5408), so live RANKING is untouched — but any offline study that averages capture_usd_day over all rows will see a level shift, and the presence-calibration table (kalshi_presence_calibrate) is exactly such a consumer: this fix CHANGES THE CALIBRATION INPUT for late-window buckets, which is the correct direction but does invalidate the current presence table's late-window rows. Re-derive the table after landing, or the presence factor and the row basis disagree. (2) If a resting order is stale (cancelled at the venue but still in our snapshot) this now credits us with a score we are not earning — bounded by m-2's fill-suspect guard and per-level clamp, which is why m-2 should land first.
- *Collides with:* n-38, m-2, M-2

**n-5 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:697**

- *Defect:* Armed-with-defaults D-C rests nothing: side cap $18 (0.9x HELD_MAX_USD=20) < ~$20.85 empty-book ladder cost
- *Fix:* Do NOT change the defaults — their unaffordability is an operator decision on the record. Kill the SILENCE instead, in two parts. (1) Arming-coherence check: new `_macro_probe_arm_check(footprint)` called once per cycle in run_once when `MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS` is non-empty. It computes `side_cap = min(MACRO_PROBE_USD/2, 0.9*HELD_MAX_USD)` (the exact expression at :697) and the default-ladder cost for the largest Target among designated rows in this cycle's footprint (falling back to 1000ct, the venue's ~99% Target, when none is known yet); when `side_cap < cost` it prints ONE line naming BOTH knobs and the exact dollars — e.g. 'macro-probe DESIGNATED 2 ticker(s) but per-side cap $18.00 < $20.85 needed for target 1000ct: raise KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_USD and KALSHI_HELD_MAX_USD TOGETHER or the probe rests nothing' — and sets `plan['macro_probe_arm_ok'] = 0/1`. (2) Surface the counters that exist but never reach telemetry: verified there is NO `plan.update(qstats)` anywhere in the module, so `macro_probe_unaffordable`, `macro_probe_wall_refused` and `macro_probe_markets` (written at :709/:717/:723) are today written to qstats and DROPPED — copy all three into the plan row inside a `if MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS:` block near the presence/netev blocks (:6104-6134), so a flag-off row stays byte-identical.
- *Why:* The two-knob coupling (MACRO_PROBE_USD and HELD_MAX_USD) is invisible at arm time and the refusal counters never reached the plan row at all, so 'designated but structurally resting nothing' is today indistinguishable from 'not designated' in every artifact the operator reads.
- *Standing detector:* plan['macro_probe_arm_ok'] plus the three now-emitted counters (macro_probe_markets / _unaffordable / _wall_refused) and the named-knobs WARNING. The standing invariant: whenever MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS is non-empty, arm_ok must be 1 and macro_probe_markets must be > 0 — any other combination names its own cause.
- *Test:* test_dc_macro_probe.py::test_default_knobs_are_unaffordable_and_say_so — designate one ticker with the CODE defaults (MACRO_PROBE_USD=60, HELD_MAX_USD=20, TOP=0.03, target 1000; note the existing fixture deliberately raises HELD_MAX_USD to 40, so this test must NOT use `_cfg`), run one cycle through the MockClient harness and assert `plan['macro_probe_arm_ok'] == 0`, `plan['macro_probe_unaffordable'] >= 1`, and that capsys contains both 'KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_USD' and 'KALSHI_HELD_MAX_USD'. Fails before: the plan keys do not exist and nothing is printed.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — telemetry and prints only; D-C itself is already dark via the empty ticker list.
- *Risk:* No order-path change. Costs: one WARNING line per cycle for as long as the config stays incoherent (that is the point, and it is bounded to one line); HELD_MAX_USD is hot-reloadable, so the warning can appear/disappear mid-run, which is correct but can read as flapping; and the arm check must not itself raise on a footprint with missing 'target' fields — wrap it and count, per the module's count-don't-swallow doctrine.
- *Collides with:* M-4, n-7, n-6

**n-6 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2004**

- *Defect:* Designation observability gaps: no-program designations vanish uncounted; deny-dropped ones still count as admitted
- *Fix:* Give designation its own funnel accounting in `select_footprint`, keyed off the RAW program list rather than the survivors. Before the loop: `_desig_seen = set()`. Inside, add `t` to it whenever `t in MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS`. At each drop site that can kill a designated ticker — the deny drop (:2021-2028), drop_date_parse (:2032), and close_past_selected (:2163-2165) — increment a named counter (`macro_probe_denied`, `macro_probe_date_drop`, `macro_probe_close_past`) instead of letting the designation vanish inside the generic bucket. After the loop: `drops['macro_probe_no_program'] = sorted(MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS - _desig_seen)[:20]` (bounded, so a fat-fingered 500-ticker designation cannot bloat every row) and `drops['macro_probe_selected'] = <count of designated tickers in the returned picked list>`. Rename nothing: `macro_probe_admitted` (:2009) keeps its meaning — 'reached the designation branch' — and becomes readable against macro_probe_selected (survived selection) and macro_probe_markets (actually rested a ladder, surfaced by n-5).
- *Why:* `select_footprint` only ever sees tickers carrying an active liquidity program, so a designated ticker whose program ended is invisible to every counter; and a designated ticker in a deny family increments macro_probe_admitted and is then dropped, so telemetry can read admitted=N with zero macro markets quoted and no way to tell which.
- *Standing detector:* macro_probe_no_program (named tickets), macro_probe_denied, macro_probe_date_drop, macro_probe_close_past and macro_probe_selected. Together with n-5's macro_probe_markets they give a standing invariant an alarm can assert: when the probe is healthy, admitted == selected == markets; any gap now names its own cause instead of vanishing.
- *Test:* test_dc_macro_probe.py — (1) `test_designated_without_program_is_named`: designate two tickers, feed a program list containing only one; assert `FP_DROPS['macro_probe_no_program'] == [the other]`. (2) `test_designated_in_deny_family_is_counted`: designate a ticker whose series prefix is in SERIES_DENY and not in SERIES_ALLOW; assert `macro_probe_denied == 1` and the ticker is absent from the footprint. Both fail before: neither key exists and the deny drop is indistinguishable from the generic drop_series_deny bucket.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — telemetry only, and it emits nothing when MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS is empty (today's live state), so a flag-off row is byte-identical.
- *Risk:* No order-path change — selection outcomes are identical, only the counting is added. Costs: a per-row string list (capped at 20) and the fact that MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS is hot-reload-warned but restart-only, so the no_program list can look stale relative to an edited env file until the service restarts — that is exactly what the restart-only WARNING already says, but the two signals should be read together.
- *Collides with:* n-5, M-4, n-9

**n-7 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:707**

- *Defect:* Spec drift: D-F ruling says the ladder 'spans 3 real levels'; code accepts a 2-level ladder (refuses only <2)
- *Fix:* Make the level count an explicit contract instead of an accident of arithmetic. Add `MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS = _envi('KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS', 3)`; build `prices = [round(MACRO_PROBE_TOP - i*TICK, 2) for i in range(MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS)]` (:705), keep the `>= 0.01` filter (:706), and change the refusal at :707 from `if len(prices) < 2` to `if len(prices) < MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS` — so any TOP that cannot span the ruled number of REAL levels is refused, counted as macro_probe_wall_refused (key unchanged), and never rested. Generalize `weights` (:711) to a normalized descending list of length MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS whose 3-level case is byte-identical to today's [0.4, 0.3, 0.3], so the default-path counts do not move.
- *Why:* The D-F ruling's contract is a ladder spanning three real levels; the code refuses only a single level, so MACRO_PROBE_TOP=0.02 silently rests a two-level ladder — a configuration the ruling forbids, accepted with no signal.
- *Standing detector:* macro_probe_wall_refused surfaced into the plan row (n-5's emission block) plus n-5's arm-check WARNING naming the offending TOP value — today the counter exists but never reaches any artifact the operator reads.
- *Test:* test_dc_macro_probe.py::test_two_level_top_is_refused — designate a ticker with TOP=0.02, assert no macro_probe quotes and `stats['macro_probe_wall_refused'] == 1`. Fails before: rests a 2-level ladder. Companion `test_three_level_counts_unchanged` pins the exact per-rung counts at TOP=0.03 against today's values so the weight generalization is provably a no-op.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — D-C is already dark via the empty ticker list, and KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_LEVELS is the knob for anyone who disagrees.
- *Risk:* An operator who wanted a deliberately cheap two-level probe now gets a refusal — mitigated by making it an explicit knob rather than an accident, but it is still a behaviour change inside the dark subsystem. The weight generalization is the only real hazard: if it changes the 3-level counts by even one contract the ladder's cost and the affordability verdict move, so the test must pin the existing default-path counts byte-for-byte. Interacts with M-4: after M-4's edge guard, a market can now be refused for two different reasons and the counters must not double-count.
- *Collides with:* M-4, n-5

**n-8 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5342**

- *Defect:* Ramp trigger counts macro quotes (harmless copy); score-cache fold ingests macro-ladder cycles as capture data
- *Fix:* Two predicate corrections, one commit. (1) D3 ramp trigger :5335 and :5342: change both `_o8.get('reason') != 'unwind'` tests to `not in ('unwind','macro_probe')`, mirroring _d3_apply_ramp's own carve-out (:646) — no _q_fullsize copy is made for a macro-only quote set and the D8 recompute is skipped (today it runs on identical quotes: wasted, not wrong). (2) D4 score-cache fold :5408: change `any(_o7.get('reason') != 'unwind' ...)` to `not in ('unwind','macro_probe')` and count the skip as plan['score_fold_skipped_macro'].
- *Why:* Part 2 is the real one: a deep-discount receipts ladder is not a capture attempt, so folding it writes a near-zero measured capture for a ticker whose presence is an OPERATOR DECISION — and with SCORE_RANK on, that measurement deranks the designated ticker in a slot competition where it has no reserved seat. This is D4's own stated rule ('fold ONLY real quoting attempts; a gated or stripped row is OUR decision, not a measurement') applied to a case D4 predates.
- *Standing detector:* plan['score_fold_skipped_macro'] plus M-8's plan['macro_designation_lost'] — together they answer the question the item says is unanswerable today: is the designated ticker's intermittent presence rank-crowding or gating?
- *Test:* In test_dc_macro_probe.py: designate T, SCORE_RANK=1, monkeypatch kalshi_market_scores.update to record calls; run a cycle where T rests its ladder and a normal market rests a join -> assert update was called for the normal market and NOT for T. Pre-fix update is called for T with a ~0 capture. Second pin: with a macro-only quote set, _q_fullsize stays None (no copy).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — inert while KALSHI_MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS is empty.
- *Risk:* T stays 'unknown' in SCORES forever, so under SCORE_RANK's unknown-bonus it keeps drawing an explore slot every cycle — harmless only once M-8's _cap_probe_slots exemption lands, so the two must ship in that order. If the operator later wants the macro ticker's capture measured, it needs a separate instrument; this fix deliberately removes the (wrong) one.
- *Collides with:* M-8, m-38, m-37

**n-9 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2001**

- *Fix:* Two small, additive changes in the selection funnel. (1) At :2000-2002, replace the bare `continue` on a null/absent market_ticker with `drops['drop_no_market_ticker'] = drops.get('drop_no_market_ticker', 0) + 1` before the continue — the last reasonless drop in the funnel (drop_null_fields at :1997 covers only target_size_fp/discount_factor_bps). (2) Extend `_ALWAYS_EMIT_DROPS` (:1097-1101) with 'drop_no_market_ticker' and 'close_past_selected', and — for the identical A3 reason — 'allow_probe_passed', 'farclose_paying_kept' and 'macro_probe_admitted', all of which are accumulation-only writes whose absence is today ambiguous between 'did not fire' and 'the stage never ran'.
- *Why:* The funnel's contract is that every dropped row names a reason and that a seeded 0 distinguishes 'evaluated, did not fire' from 'not evaluated'; a null market_ticker row vanishes with programs_seen unchanged and no code at all — the exact silent-emptying shape the FP_DROPS machinery was built to end.
- *Standing detector:* The drop keys themselves, seeded to 0: a non-zero drop_no_market_ticker is precisely the alarm for the failure the item describes (a venue payload change nulling market_ticker on a program class), and a seeded close_past_selected makes the B-1 stage's silence readable for the first time.

Fails before: the key does not exist. (2) `test_always_emit_drops_covers_close_past_selected`: run a clean cycle and assert `plan['close_past_selected'] == 0` is present. Fails before: the key is absent on a cycle where it never fired.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — counting only, zero effect on which rows are selected.
- *Risk:* Adds ~5 zero-valued keys to every plan row; row size is a real budget on this system (the env_absent dedup was introduced because one field was 44.4% of a live row), so this is a small permanent cost. Behaviour-neutral for in-repo consumers — kalshi_plans_hourly coerces with `.get(f, 0) or 0` — but any external dashboard that treats key PRESENCE as 'this stage fired' would flip to always-true; that inversion is the documented intent of the A3 doctrine, and I found no in-repo consumer relying on absence.
- *Collides with:* n-6, n-10

**n-10 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2287**

- *Defect:* PIVOT coverage floor ignores pool_cap, so the candidate pool can exceed the read-reserve guarantee
- *Fix:* Bound the PIVOT coverage pass by the same cap the density remainder already honours (:2286-2289):

    for s in series_order:                      # 1) COVERAGE floor, bounded by pool_cap
        if len(picked) >= pool_cap:
            drops['pivot_coverage_truncated'] = drops.get('pivot_coverage_truncated', 0) + 1
            continue                            # count EVERY series that got no slot
        for r in by_series[s][:PIVOT_COVERAGE]:
            if len(picked) >= pool_cap:
                break
            picked.append(r); per_series[s] += 1

`series_order` is already rank-ordered under ALLOC_KEY (or usd_day-ordered without it), so the truncation cuts the LOWEST-value series — the correct victim. `pool_cap`'s definition (:2267-2268) is unchanged.
- *Why:* pool_cap = READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE − PIVOT_READ_RESERVE exists to guarantee reads remain for strand/ladder/settle work, but the coverage loop appends one row per active series before the cap is consulted, so an unscoped universe (~150 active liquidity series) starts the pool above the cap and the quote loop can spend into the reserve — and that reserve's job is re-reading a naked residual late in the cycle.
- *Standing detector:* plan['pivot_coverage_truncated'] emitted inside the existing `if PIVOT_SELECT` block, read alongside the pre-existing strand_read_failed. The pair states both that the bound bound and whether the reserve it protects was ever exhausted — which is the actual harm the item describes.
- *Test:* test_pivot_select.py::test_coverage_floor_respects_pool_cap — 150 single-market series, READ_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE=200, PIVOT_READ_RESERVE=30, FOOTPRINT_TOP=60, PIVOT_POOL_MULT=2 (pool_cap = min(120, 150, 170) = 120); assert `len(select_footprint(progs, now)) <= 120` and `FP_DROPS['pivot_coverage_truncated'] == 30`. Fails before: returns 150 rows and the key does not exist.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — PIVOT_SELECT is already the flag, and this only tightens a bound the code already documents as binding.
- *Risk:* Dormant under the current 5-series allowlist scoping, so no live change today; it becomes live the moment SERIES_ALLOW is emptied for a full-universe run, and then it makes the candidate pool SMALLER — a genuine loss of discovery breadth for the lowest-ranked series. That is the intended trade (protect the de-risk reads over the discovery slots) but it should be stated plainly: if the operator would rather buy breadth, the answer is raising KALSHI_READ_BUDGET, which is a knob change, not this code change. Also note the fix makes the truncation VISIBLE for the first time, so a full-universe run will start reporting a number that looks like a regression and is not.
- *Collides with:* n-9

**n-11 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2661**

- *Defect:* _sweep_measure pcap still lacks own_orders threading (F5 residue; consumer gated off)
- *Fix:* CLOSED BY m-39 (shared root fix #2). Encode that precondition in code, not prose: at import, if ALLOC_KEY and not SWEEP_OWN_ORDERS-threading-available, count `_SILENT["alloc_key_unthreaded_pcap"]` every cycle and emit it in the plan row.

- *Standing detector:* The `alloc_key_unthreaded_pcap` per-cycle counter above is the standing detector for the ORDERING class: any future capital-key consumer armed ahead of its feed's correctness shows up as a non-zero counter in the plan row on the first cycle, instead of as a quiet mis-ranking.

- *Collides with:* m-39, m-61

**n-12 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3535**

- *Defect:* order_id_for nonce is whole-second — two cycles in one epoch second reuse client_order_ids
- *Fix:* Make the cycle nonce strictly increasing per process AND across restarts without changing order_id_for's signature or cyc's type. Swap the order of :4001-4002 so state is loaded first, then compute `cyc = max(int(now.timestamp()), int(st.get('last_cyc') or 0) + 1)`, store `st['last_cyc'] = cyc`, and count `_SILENT['cyc_nonce_bumped'] += 1` whenever cyc != int(now.timestamp()). order_id_for (:3531-3535) is unchanged; `sim-{cyc}-{i}` (:5863) and the telemetry cyc field keep their int shape and stay within seconds of epoch time.
- *Why:* `mk-{cyc}-{i}-{side}` is unique only if cyc is, and two run_once invocations inside one epoch second (the WS daemon fires on book moves and fills, not only on a timer) reuse (cyc, i, side) for their early creates; Kalshi dedups on client_order_id, so the second cycle's creates come back as anonymous create_fail and tick the J6 ratchet on tickers that did nothing wrong.
- *Standing detector:* `_SILENT['cyc_nonce_bumped']` surfaced on the plan row: a direct count of same-second cycles, i.e. the first real measurement of how often the collision window is actually entered (currently unknown). The existing first_create_err already carries the venue's duplicate-id text if it ever recurs, so the two together prove the class stays closed.
- *Test:* test_ops_hardening_0730.py::test_cycle_nonce_unique_within_one_second — freeze utcnow() to a single instant, run two dry_run run_once() cycles against one state file with a stub client that records every client_order_id, assert the recorded ids have no duplicates and that st['last_cyc'] advanced by 1. BEFORE: index-0 creates of both cycles collide (mk-<t>-0-yes twice). Plus a pure unit assert that order_id_for is injective over distinct cyc values.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — order ids only become more unique, and no trading decision reads cyc. Provably no-op-or-safer.
- *Risk:* cyc can now exceed the true epoch second by the number of same-second cycles (a handful at worst), so offline analysis that treats cyc as an exact timestamp gains a small skew — quotes-*.jsonl rows carry their own ISO ts, so the skew is cosmetic, but any join keyed purely on cyc should be checked. A corrupt or absent state file resets the guard to the plain epoch value, i.e. exactly today's behaviour, never worse. Reordering load_state above the cyc assignment must not move it above the STOP-file branch (:3961), which returns early and must keep doing so; m-22 changes that same branch, so land n-12 first.
- *Collides with:* m-22

**n-13 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5782**

- *Defect:* Amends are not counted against WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE
- *Fix:* `bound_creates(creates, cancels, usd_day, extra_ops=0)` — optional trailing kwarg (the apply_drop_grace precedent for additive, default-identical parameters), applied in both the early return (`len(cancels)+len(creates)+extra_ops <= WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE`) and the budget computation (`WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE - len(cancels) - extra_ops`). Call site :5773 passes `extra_ops=len(amends)`. Amends keep executing FIRST and are never starved (a decrease only frees exposure). Plan row gains `amends` and `write_ops` (= amends+cancels+creates); write_tokens counts amends at the CREATE rate (10) with an explicit comment that the venue's amend token cost is UNVERIFIED and 10 is the conservative assumption.
- *Why:* The write budget exists to keep a cycle under the venue's rate ceiling, and amends are venue writes accounted nowhere — so a large amend batch plus a full cancel/create budget can exceed the very ceiling the budget models and 429 the creates it was protecting.
- *Standing detector:* plan['write_ops'] plus a hard counter plan['write_budget_exceeded'] (1 when write_ops > WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE — must stay 0), and _SILENT['venue_429'] bumped whenever _err_detail on a create/cancel/amend failure renders HTTP 429. The 429 counter is the real class detector: it is the only way to learn whether WRITE_BUDGET models the actual limit at all.
- *Test:* Pure-function test on bound_creates: 10 cancels, 50 creates, extra_ops=45, WRITE_BUDGET=100 -> kept <= 45 with unwind groups first (pre-fix kept == 50: failing-before). Plus a cycle test with AMEND_DECREASE=1 asserting plan['write_ops'] <= WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — no-op while AMEND_DECREASE=0, and the arithmetic change is only what the budget already claims to do.
- *Risk:* On amend-heavy cycles fewer accumulating creates land -> fewer markets quoted -> less resting presence, which is what LIP actually pays for. Unwind groups are ordered first so exits are not what gets dropped. AMEND_DECREASE defaults to 0 and is not in the published live flag list — VERIFY that before claiming this is a live no-op, because if amends are armed this immediately tightens live throughput. budget_dropped_markets will read higher afterwards; that is the true count, not a regression.
- *Collides with:* m-17

**n-14 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3667**

- *Defect:* _alloc_priority runs shadow_rank while holding SCORES_LOCK
- *Fix:* In _alloc_priority (:3660-3678) narrow the critical section to the READ. Keep the prospective merge inside `with SCORES_LOCK:` exactly as today, take a per-row snapshot inside the lock (`_snap = {t: dict(r) for t, r in SCORES.items()}`), release, then call `_kcr.shadow_rank(footprint_rows, _snap, ...)` outside. Verified safe by reading the callee: shadow_rank never mutates `scores` — it only calls kalshi_market_scores.score (pure read: no assignment into markets) and scores.get(t) — so ranking on a snapshot is output-identical. Add the read-only contract to shadow_rank's docstring so a future writer cannot break it silently.
- *Why:* The full ranking call runs while holding SCORES_LOCK, so the sweeper thread's _sweep_store/_sweep_ages writes block for the ranking's whole duration on every cycle when ALLOC_KEY=1 — aging exactly the pcap feed the allocation key consumes. The lock is being held for a computation that only needs a consistent read.
- *Standing detector:* Plan key `alloc_rank_lock_ms` = milliseconds SCORES_LOCK was held inside this function, with a WARNING above 250 ms. That is the CLASS detector: it catches any future long computation that creeps back inside a lock, not just this one. The existing _SILENT['alloc_key_fail'] continues to cover faults.
- *Test:* test_alloc_unified_key.py::test_ranking_does_not_hold_scores_lock — monkeypatch _kcr.shadow_rank with a stub that asserts SCORES_LOCK.acquire(blocking=False) succeeds (and that its `scores` argument is not the SCORES object itself), then returns a valid comp list. BEFORE: the acquire assert fails because the lock is held across the call. AFTER: passes. Companion equality pin: _alloc_priority output on a fixture footprint is identical to the pre-change implementation.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — ALLOC_KEY=0 live makes it unreachable today, and the output is provably identical when it is reachable.
- *Risk:* One dict-of-dicts copy per cycle over the score cache — bounded today, but a new per-cycle allocation on the hot path if SCORES ever grows to venue scale. The sharper risk is future-facing: if a later shadow_rank variant starts writing back into `scores` (e.g. caching a decayed value), those writes land on a throwaway snapshot and vanish silently — an equality pin on the ranking output will not catch it, which is why the read-only contract goes in the docstring and why the test asserts the callee received a copy, not SCORES itself.

**n-15 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3766**

- *Defect:* bound_creates can starve unwind creates when cancels alone consume the write budget
- *Fix:* In bound_creates (:3762-3784) floor the create budget at the number of REDUCING creates: `_unwind_n = sum(1 for c in creates if c.get('reason') == 'unwind')` then `budget = max(_unwind_n, WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE - len(cancels))`. The existing unwind-first sort (:3775-3777) then guarantees every exit create is emitted and only accumulating groups compete for the remainder. Add `plan['unwind_creates_dropped']` computed after the walk (an invariant that must always be 0) and `plan['write_budget_over'] = max(0, len(cancels) + len(kept) - WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE)` so the cost of honouring the floor is visible instead of silent. ALSO CLOSES n-53 (same line, same mechanism).
- *Why:* `budget = max(0, WRITE_BUDGET - len(cancels))` lets an uncapped cancel wave zero the create budget and drop EVERY create — including the exits for positions we just took fills on. It is the one place in this file where a de-risk order can lose to a rate guard, and it contradicts the polarity doctrine every other gate here follows (capital cap, family cap, incumbent strip all exempt unwind).
- *Standing detector:* `unwind_creates_dropped` on every plan row, treated as an INVARIANT with a WARNING if it is ever nonzero (not a metric to trend — a zero that must stay zero), plus `write_budget_over` so the ops overshoot the floor buys is measured rather than inferred.
- *Test:* test_live_hardening.py::test_write_budget_never_starves_exits — 400 synthetic cancel ids, creates = 2 unwind (tickers X, Y) + 2 join, WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE=400. BEFORE: bound_creates returns ([], 4) — every create including both exits is dropped. AFTER: both unwind creates survive, the joins are dropped, unwind_creates_dropped == 0 and write_budget_over == 2.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — provably a no-op under the live config (cancels cannot reach 400), and strictly polarity-correct when it does engage. A flag here would gate a guard that only fires in the emergency it exists for.
- *Risk:* It can exceed WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE by the number of held tickers carrying exits (bounded by position count, single digits in practice), pushing marginally harder on venue rate limits in exactly the churn cycle that produced the cancel wave — a rejected op there costs a create_fail and a J6 ratchet tick on that ticker. Capping cancels instead was considered and rejected: dropping cancels leaves stale orders resting and stacks same-side size, which is strictly worse. Unreachable at today's config (FOOTPRINT_TOP=60 => ~120 standing orders against a 400 budget), so it is a provable no-op until FOOTPRINT_TOP or ladder depth grows — which is precisely the change that would otherwise make it bite with no warning.
- *Collides with:* m-16, n-53, n-55

**n-19 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5671**

- *Defect:* at_ref/off_ref telemetry measures distance to the best bid, not to the LIP Reference Price
- *Fix:* Add pure helper `_lip_reference(levels, target)` implementing the venue-published rule verbatim: sort bids descending, accumulate resting size, return the FIRST price whose CUMULATIVE size >= target/5; return None when the side never reaches target/5 (no reference -> that side cannot pay anyone). In the quote loop store `ref_lip[t] = (_lip_reference(_byl, target), _lip_reference(_bnl, target))` in a NEW dict, leaving the book_refs 2-tuple untouched (the ladder escape hatch indexes it positionally at :5514). At :5663-5681 ADD new plan keys computed against the LIP reference: lip_at_ref_usd (price at or better than reference = 1.0x), lip_off_ref_usd, lip_at_ref_pct, lip_noref_usd (money on a side with no reference at all), and lip_credit_usd = sum(price*count*0.5**ticks_below_ref). Leave at_ref_usd/off_ref_usd/at_ref_pct computed exactly as today and re-document them in the comment as TOUCH-relative.
- *Why:* LIP's reference can sit BELOW the touch, so money resting between the reference and the touch earns the full 1.0x multiplier while the current split books it as off-reference. The bias is one-directional (understates at_ref), so a D-E fills-vs-distance study or a presence calibration keyed on at_ref_pct concludes placement is off-reference and 'fixes' pricing that was already earning full credit.
- *Standing detector:* lip_noref_usd — dollars resting on sides that cannot reach Target/5 and therefore cannot earn anything — is itself the standing detector for the reward-invisibility class; nothing measures it today. Alarm: lip_noref_usd / (lip_at_ref + lip_off_ref + lip_noref) sustained above a few percent means we are funding zero-credit presence.
- *Test:* Unit tests on _lip_reference against hand-built books from the published rule (target 1000 -> threshold 200 ct): reference == touch when the touch alone carries 200 ct; reference two levels down when the touch is 50 ct; None when total depth < 200 ct. Then a cycle test on a book with a thin touch and our quote one tick below it: assert lip_at_ref_usd > at_ref_usd. Failing-before: the helper and the keys do not exist.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — additive telemetry keys only, legacy keys untouched.
- *Risk:* No money-path risk (telemetry only); the hazard is interpretive and it is bigger than this item. The new keys will DISAGREE with the row-level y_ref/n_ref in quotes-*.jsonl, because the module's R4 replica defines reference = highest bid (_qualifying_score :2555-2558, _qualifying_breakdown :2718) rather than the Target/5 level — i.e. the same touch-vs-LIP mismatch lives inside the capture model that feeds the $1.20 floor gate, the capture gate and SCORE_RANK. Mixing the two definitions in one study is worse than either alone. That model defect is adjacent and outside this item's scope; it must be named to the operator, not silently half-fixed here.

- *Fix:* Distinguish MISSING from ZERO. New helper `_venue_fill_ct(o) -> (fill, known)`: walk ('fill_count_fp','fill_count') and return the first PRESENT value with known=True; if neither key is present return (0.0, False) and bump _SILENT['fill_count_field_missing']. That is a deliberate trade (over-resting can flip the position and open a naked short; under-resting costs one cycle of passive exit), but it is a real downgrade in that narrow scenario.
