**n-22 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:379**

- *Defect:* ctx.build runs every live cold cycle even with WS_HOT=0 — duplicate portfolio reads
- *Fix:* Have the daemon reuse the ticker set run_once ALREADY computed instead of re-deriving it with fresh REST reads, when the full hot context is not needed.

(a) maker_kalshi_quoter.py (additive, no signature change): a module-level publication updated at the end of every completed `run_once`, next to where the plan row is finalized:
```python
LAST_CYCLE = {"tickers": set(), "mono": 0.0}
...
LAST_CYCLE.update(tickers={m["ticker"] for m in footprint} | set(standing),
                  mono=time.monotonic())
```
Set ONLY on the paths that actually computed both (not on the early returns for standing-read failure / reconcile-fail), so a stale set is never republished as fresh.

(b) maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py `cold_cycle` (:378-384) — same guard condition as today, new source:
```python
if not os.path.exists(M.STOP_FILE) and self.client.mode != "dry_run":
    lc = getattr(M, "LAST_CYCLE", None)
    if WS_CTX_LEAN and not WS_HOT and lc and (time.monotonic() - lc["mono"]) < 120:
        tickers = set(lc["tickers"])          # watch list only; hot ctx not needed
        self.ctx.invalidate()                  # explicit: ctx is NOT built, hot stays dead
    else:
        try:
            tickers = self.ctx.build(self.client, self._programs(), self.std_lock)
        except Exception as e: ...
```
with `WS_CTX_LEAN = M._envi("KALSHI_WS_CTX_LEAN", 0)`. The freshness check and the `else` fallback mean a run_once that early-returned (or an older quoter without LAST_CYCLE) transparently gets today's behavior.
- *Why:* `cold_cycle` unconditionally rebuilds the full HotContext in live mode — fresh `_live_standing`, `_held_cost`, `load_state`, `select_footprint` — immediately after `run_once` fetched the same data. With WS_HOT=0 the only consumed output is the ticker set for the WS watch list; the breaker/headroom/event-inventory machinery is dead weight costing 2-4 authed REST round trips and ~1-2s on EVERY book-move-triggered cycle. At the 5s-debounced event-driven cadence that is the single largest avoidable REST consumer left after Stage C eliminated the book reads.
- *Standing detector:* Add `ctx_src` ('build' | 'last_cycle' | 'skipped') and the existing `secs` to the cold_cycle row, plus a `reads` count from the quoter's `_reads` gauge. The alarm is a cycle-time regression: if `ctx_src=last_cycle` cycles are not measurably faster than `ctx_src=build` cycles, the premise is wrong and the flag should go back to 0 — the telemetry makes that a readable fact rather than an argument.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, test_ws_book_cold.py or test_ws_daemon.py:
```python
def test_lean_ctx_skips_the_duplicate_portfolio_reads_kills_broken_variant(monkeypatch, ...):
    built = []
    monkeypatch.setattr(D, "WS_CTX_LEAN", 1); monkeypatch.setattr(D, "WS_HOT", 0)
    monkeypatch.setattr(D.M, "run_once", lambda: D.M.LAST_CYCLE.update(
        tickers={"A","B"}, mono=time.monotonic()))
    monkeypatch.setattr(D.HotContext, "build",
                        lambda *a, **k: built.append(1) or {"A"})
    assert d.cold_cycle() == {"A", "B"}
    assert built == []                    # ctx.build must NOT have run
    assert d.ctx.stale()                  # and hot must stay dead
```
Today `build` runs and `built == [1]` → fails. Plus a staleness test: with `LAST_CYCLE["mono"]` set 200s in the past, `ctx.build` IS called (fallback intact).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — `KALSHI_WS_CTX_LEAN`, default 0. It is a pure optimization with a real chance of changing which markets have mirrors; nothing about it is provably a no-op.
- *Risk:* This creates a NEW cross-module contract: the daemon's WS watch list becomes a function of quoter internals. If a future run_once change moves or renames `footprint`/`standing`, or adds an early return that leaves LAST_CYCLE half-updated, the watch list silently drifts from what the daemon believes it is watching — and a wrong watch list means missing mirrors, which with WS_BOOK_COLD=1 means silently more REST (safe) or, worse, a ticker with no Stage A trigger (slow requoting). The 120s freshness check bounds staleness but does not detect a semantic drift. Also note the sets are not necessarily identical today: `ctx.build` calls `select_footprint` fresh, while run_once's footprint may have been through budget/selection filtering — so the watch membership CAN change on the first lean cycle, which changes Stage C coverage on a live flag. That is precisely why it ships dark and why the first armed session should diff the two sets before trusting it (log both, compare, then switch).
- *Collides with:* n-24, n-52, M-14

**n-23 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:346**

- *Defect:* mirror_book cross-side torn read: yes and no captured non-atomically vs the delta-applying loop thread
- *Fix:* Give BookMirror a mutation generation counter and make `mirror_book`'s read a seqlock, so the cold worker thread can prove the two sides came from the same instant.

kalshi_ws_feed.py `BookMirror`: add `"gen"` to `__slots__`, `self.gen = 0` in `__init__`, and `self.gen += 1` at the end of EVERY mutation path — `apply_snapshot` (both the dirty-return arm and the success arm) and `apply_delta` (the unparseable-dirty arm and the level-update arm).

maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py `mirror_book` (:346-354):
```python
for _ in range(3):
    g0 = m.gen
    try:
        ys, ns = m.rows()
    except RuntimeError:
        continue                      # dict resized mid-iteration
    if m.dirty:
        return None                   # went dirty mid-read — do not trust it
    if m.gen != g0:
        continue                      # a delta landed BETWEEN the two sides — torn, retry
    return {"yes_dollars": ys, "no_dollars": ns}
self._decl("torn")
return None
```
and replace the docstring's safety claim (which today covers only the RuntimeError/dict-resize case) with the generation invariant.
- *Why:* `rows()` reads `self.yes` then `self.no` as two separate sorted() passes while the event loop keeps applying deltas, so a delta landing between them yields a book whose sides are from different instants. The existing dirty re-check catches gaps but not ordinary mid-read mutation. The observable consequence is bounded — the quoter's crossed-book guard turns a torn read into a spurious one-cycle skip of that market — but a spurious skip costs a full cycle of reward presence, whereas the fix's cost (one REST read) costs 320ms. The class is 'a multi-field read across a mutating structure with no consistency proof', and the generation counter is the proof.
- *Standing detector:* A `torn` bucket in the `mirror_book` decline histogram introduced with M-14, emitted per cold cycle. Non-zero is informative rather than alarming (it means the seqlock is doing its job); a sustained HIGH ratio of torn to served means the read is losing races and the mirror should be snapshotted under a lock instead — a decision the counter makes measurable rather than speculative.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, test_ws_book_cold.py, mirroring the file's existing `_DirtiesOnRead` pattern:
```python
def test_p2_torn_cross_side_read_is_declined_kills_broken_variant():
    class _MutatesMidRead(F.BookMirror):
        def rows(self):
            out = super().rows()
            self.gen += 1                 # a delta landed between the two sides
            return out
    m = _MutatesMidRead("T1")
    m.apply_snapshot({"yes_dollars_fp": [["0.5000","10"]],
                      "no_dollars_fp": [["0.4800","10"]]}, seq=1)
    assert _daemon(_FakeFeed({"T1": m})).mirror_book("T1") is None
```
Today this returns a book → fails. Pair it with a no-regression test that a quiet mirror (gen stable) is still served, so the fix cannot silently disable Stage C.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — the change can only ever move a read from 'mirror' to 'REST', which is the pre-Stage-C behavior and the module's documented decline-never-guess contract.
- *Risk:* Real but small: every torn read becomes a REST fallback, so on a burst-active book Stage C's hit rate drops slightly and cold cycles get marginally slower. Order of magnitude: `rows()` on a handful of levels is ~10µs against a measured ~10 frames/s spread across ~40-80 books, so collision probability per read is ~1e-6 and three retries make a decline vanishingly unlikely — but that is an estimate from the 07-27 measurements, not a measurement of the post-fix state, which is what the `torn` counter is for. The `gen += 1` must be added to EVERY mutation path or the seqlock silently under-detects; the dirty-return arms are easy to miss. `gen` is an int read/written under the GIL from two threads — no lock needed, but that reasoning must be in the comment or a future reader will 'fix' it with a lock on the event loop and add latency to the hot path.
- *Collides with:* M-14, m-24, m-27

**n-24 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:692**

- *Defect:* Watch cap [:80] truncates ALPHABETICALLY when footprint+standing exceeds 80 — the F9 'moot' claim has a reachable edge
- *Fix:* SHARED DESIGN — closes n-24 and n-52 (one commit).

Replace `sorted(tickers)[:80]` (maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:692 and :709/:717) with an explicit PRIORITY selection, and make truncation loud.

(a) `HotContext.build` additionally records rank order: `self.footprint_order = [m["ticker"] for m in footprint]` (set inside the existing `std_lock` block), and `cold_cycle` returns the ticker set as today.

(b) New method:
```python
WS_WATCH_MAX = M._envi("KALSHI_WS_WATCH_MAX", 80)

def _select_watch(self, tickers):
    """Feed slots go to money first. (1) tickers we have capital RESTING on,
    (2) footprint in rank order, (3) everything else. Deterministic within each
    tier so a stable pool produces a stable list. Truncation is LOGGED."""
    tickers = set(tickers or ())
    with self.std_lock:
        resting = sorted(t for t in self.ctx.standing if t in tickers)
    ranked = [t for t in getattr(self.ctx, "footprint_order", []) if t in tickers]
    seen, out = set(), []
    for t in resting + ranked + sorted(tickers):
        if t not in seen:
            seen.add(t); out.append(t)
    kept, dropped = out[:WS_WATCH_MAX], out[WS_WATCH_MAX:]
    if dropped:
        _log({"ev": "watch_truncated", "kept": len(kept),
              "dropped": len(dropped), "sample": dropped[:10]})
    return kept
```
(c) Both call sites use it, and the resubscribe test becomes SET-based so a pure ordering change can never trigger a needless rebuild: `if new and set(self._select_watch(new)) != set(watch) and <hysteresis>:`.
- *Why:* `tickers` is footprint (FOOTPRINT_TOP=60, over-selected by PIVOT to as many as PIVOT_POOL_MULT*60 = 120 rows) unioned with standing, and `sorted()[:80]` then keeps the ALPHABETICAL head. Markets we hold resting capital in can fall off the fast feed entirely — no mirror, no Stage A trigger, no hot defence, heartbeat-only latency — while unquoted candidates hold feed slots, and the log still suggests full WS coverage. That is the pre-F9 failure at a higher threshold; F9's 'the truncation is moot' claim holds only while the union stays under 80. Ordering by where our money actually is makes the cap degrade gracefully instead of arbitrarily, and the log row removes the silence.
- *Standing detector:* The `watch_truncated` row (with a sample of the dropped tickers) plus a `watch_dropped` count in the cold_cycle row. Alarm on any non-zero: it means the feed is no longer covering the traded universe and the cap or FOOTPRINT_TOP needs an operator decision. Today this state is completely silent, which is the actual defect — the cap itself is a legitimate resource bound.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, test_ws_daemon.py:

Today `sorted(tickers)[:80]` drops ZZZ-HELD → fails. Plus `test_watch_truncation_is_logged` asserting the `watch_truncated` row exists with the dropped sample, and a no-op test at today's scale: a 33-ticker pool produces the same SET as `sorted(tickers)[:80]`.

A flag would be defensible when the pool actually exceeds 80; at that point the flag would be gating the fix, not the defect, so the better control is `KALSHI_WS_WATCH_MAX` itself, which is now an env knob.

The risk arrives with allowlist expansion, and it is a genuine trade: prioritizing resting/held tickers means footprint CANDIDATES lose feed slots first, so with WS_BOOK_COLD=1 those candidates' books come over REST and cold cycles get slower for new entries — we are deliberately buying protection for deployed capital with latency on prospecting. State that explicitly to the operator rather than letting it be discovered. Second risk: `footprint_order` is a new field on HotContext read via `getattr(..., [])`, so an unbuilt/lean ctx (see n-22) degrades this to alphabetical for the ranked tier — the resting tier still works, but the two fixes must be reviewed together or n-22 quietly guts n-24's ranking.

**n-25 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ws_feed.py:34**

- *Defect:* Feed always connects to PROD WS hosts regardless of client mode; dead-candidate ordering taxes every reconnect
- *Fix:* Two independent, small changes in kalshi_ws_feed.py.

(a) MODE-AWARE HOSTS. Replace the unconditional PROD-derived constant with a function, keeping the module-level name for compatibility:
```python
from maker_kalshi_client import KalshiAuth, PROD_BASE, DEMO_BASE

def ws_candidates(mode=None):
    """WS hosts for the client's CURRENT mode. Demo credentials cannot authenticate
    against prod WS, so a demo daemon offered prod hosts fails its connect loop
    forever and degrades to the heartbeat with no error above reconnect prints."""
    mode = (mode or os.environ.get("KALSHI_TRADING_MODE") or "dry_run").lower()
    base = DEMO_BASE if mode == "demo" else PROD_BASE
    out = [os.environ.get("KALSHI_WS_URL") or "",
           "wss://" + base.split("//", 1)[-1] + WS_PATH,
           ("wss://demo-api.kalshi.co" if mode == "demo"
            else "wss://api.elections.kalshi.com") + WS_PATH]
    return [u for u in out if u]

WS_URL_CANDIDATES = ws_candidates()      # preserved for existing importers/tests
```

(b) STICKY LAST-GOOD ORDERING in `_connect`: module-level `_LAST_GOOD = [""]`; build the attempt list as `[last_good] + others` when last_good is still a candidate; on success set `_LAST_GOOD[0] = url`; count `self.connect_fallthrough += 1` for each candidate that fails before one succeeds, and print the winning url with elapsed seconds.
- *Why:* `WS_URL_CANDIDATES` is built from PROD_BASE unconditionally, so a demo dress-rehearsal of the daemon can never connect and the failure surfaces only as endless reconnect prints. Separately, the candidate list is re-walked in fixed order on EVERY reconnect: if candidate #1 stops serving WS or hangs, each reconnect pays up to CONNECT_TIMEOUT_S=30s of dead time before reaching the working host, and with M-14 landed every second of that is a window where all mirrors are dirty and the whole cold cycle is on REST.
- *Standing detector:* `connect_fallthrough` (candidates tried before success) and the connected url in the cold_cycle row as `ws_url` / `ws_fallthrough`; plus the per-connect print with elapsed time. Sustained non-zero fallthrough is the 'a CDN change made candidate #1 a 30s tarpit' signature, which today is completely invisible.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE:
```python
def test_ws_candidates_follow_trading_mode_kills_broken_variant(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("KALSHI_TRADING_MODE", "demo")
    monkeypatch.delenv("KALSHI_WS_URL", raising=False)
    assert all("demo" in u for u in F.ws_candidates())

def test_last_good_url_is_tried_first(monkeypatch):
    # stub websockets.connect: candidate #1 always raises, #2 succeeds;
    # after the first connect, assert the SECOND connect attempts #2 first
```
Today `ws_candidates` does not exist and the order is fixed → both fail.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — provable no-op for live/dry_run mode (identical candidate list), and the stickiness only reorders attempts whose outcomes are individually unchanged.
- *Risk:* For live mode the candidate list is byte-identical to today's (mode 'live' and 'dry_run' both resolve to PROD_BASE), so (a) cannot change live behavior — it only stops a demo rehearsal from being impossible. (b) is the one with a live edge: if the last-good host later degrades to slow-but-connecting, stickiness keeps us on it rather than trying the alternative, so a soft failure could persist where today's fixed order would have rotated. The loop still falls through on hard failure, and `connect_fallthrough`/`ws_url` make the choice observable, but a 'preferred host is now slower' state would not self-correct. Acceptable given the alternative is paying up to 30s per reconnect on a dead first candidate.
- *Collides with:* M-14, m-28

**n-26 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:656**

- *Defect:* Bootstrap fallback ticker is expired (26JUL31); invalid subscribe may interact with the error-frame teardown
- *Fix:* Two unrelated fixes in maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py; the first removes an expiring constant, the second removes a guessed field name.

(a) BOOTSTRAP. A hardcoded dated ticker is a time bomb by construction — the fix is to delete the class, not to refresh the date. Keep the staticmethod signature and add an optional source of already-cached truth:
```python
@staticmethod
def _bootstrap_ticker(programs=None):
    """Any currently-open market, for the empty-first-cycle case. NO hardcoded
    ticker: a dated constant silently expires and then draws venue error frames
    on subscribe (which tear the connection down — see m-28)."""
    for path in ("/trade-api/v2/markets?limit=1&status=open",
                 "/trade-api/v2/markets?limit=1"):
        try:
            mkts = M.public_get(path).get("markets") or []
            if mkts and mkts[0].get("ticker"):
                return mkts[0]["ticker"]
        except Exception:
            pass
    for p in (programs or []):            # active incentive programs, already cached
        t = p.get("market_ticker") or p.get("ticker")
        if t:
            return t
    return None
```
Call site: `watch = self._select_watch(tickers) or [t for t in [self._bootstrap_ticker(self.programs)] if t]`. When `watch` is empty, do NOT construct a Feed — log `{"ev":"bootstrap_no_ticker"}`, leave `feed = feed_task = None`, and guard the two places the loop touches `feed_task` with `if feed_task is not None`; the next cold cycle builds the feed. An empty subscribe is exactly the invalid command that draws the error frame we are trying to avoid.

(b) FILL TELEMETRY. `on_fill` logs `msg.get("count")`, and under the `*_fp` canon plain names return None. Read the shape instead of guessing it:
```python
def _first(d, *keys):
    for k in keys:
        if d.get(k) is not None:
            return d[k]
    return None
...
cnt = _first(msg, "count", "count_fp", "fill_count", "filled_count")
if cnt is None:
    FILL_COUNT_NULL[0] += 1
_log({"ev": "fill", "ticker": t, "count": cnt,
      "keys": sorted(msg)[:12] if not self._fill_shape_logged else None})
self._fill_shape_logged = True
```
- *Why:* (a) The last-resort bootstrap ticker expired 2026-07-31. It is reached only when the REST probe also fails, but subscribing to an expired ticker plausibly draws a venue error frame — which per m-28 tears the connection down and loops, turning a transient outage into a self-sustaining reconnect churn until the first successful cold cycle. (b) Fill rows almost certainly carry `count: null` today, so the one telemetry we have about our own fills records nothing, and nobody can tell because a null looks like a logging choice.
- *Standing detector:* (a) `{"ev":"bootstrap_no_ticker"}` and `{"ev":"bootstrap_ticker","src":"rest|programs"}` rows — a bootstrap that had to fall back is a fact worth seeing. (b) `FILL_COUNT_NULL` counter in the cold_cycle row plus the one-shot `keys` dump: the first fill of each process records the venue's actual fill-frame shape in the log, which converts every future field-name question from a guess into a lookup. That is the standing detector for the whole `*_fp` canon-trap class on the WS side.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE:
```python

    assert D.Daemon._bootstrap_ticker(programs=[]) is None
    assert D.Daemon._bootstrap_ticker(programs=[{"market_ticker": "KXLIVE-1"}]) == "KXLIVE-1"

def test_fill_count_reads_fp_dialect(daemon, tmp_path):
    daemon.on_fill({"market_ticker": "T", "count_fp": "5.00"})
    assert _last_log_row()["count"] == "5.00"
```
Today the first returns the expired literal and the second logs None → both fail. Add a source-level guard test asserting no `-26[A-Z]{3}\d{2}-` dated literal appears in the daemon source, so the class cannot come back.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — (a) only changes behavior on a path that is currently broken by construction (an expired ticker), and (b) is telemetry.
- *Risk:* (a) The `feed is None` branch is new control flow in the main loop and is the sharp edge: miss one `feed_task` dereference and the daemon raises out of `main()` at startup — a total outage rather than a degraded one. It is reached only when the venue cannot serve a single open market AND we have no cached programs, i.e. a full venue outage, but that is exactly when an unhandled AttributeError would be worst. The guard needs its own test with `feed_task=None` driven through one loop iteration. (b) is cosmetic and cannot affect trading; the only risk is log volume from the one-shot key dump, which is bounded to one row per process.

- *Fix:* Two hygiene fixes in maker_kalshi_client.py; the first must be SEQUENCED or it breaks live tooling.

(a) HARDCODED ACCOUNT UUID (`CREDIT_USER_ID` default, :40-41). The end state is `CREDIT_USER_ID = os.environ.get("KALSHI_USER_ID", "")` — the method already raises a clear RuntimeError when unset. But shipping that alone turns every credit read into an exception in any environment that does not export the var, which today includes an unknown number of paths (the estimates recorder timer, `kalshi_netev_rebuild`, `kalshi_credit_feedback`, `w17_coverage_ledger`, `w0b_payout_timing`). So, in order:
  1. Add `KALSHI_USER_ID` to `/opt/pa2-maker-kalshi-live/live.env` (and to whatever env the recorder/timers use) and extend `test_env_visibility.py`'s expected set to include it — the repo already has that pattern for exactly this class.
  2. Ship an ATTRIBUTION LOG now, unflagged and safe: on every credit read, `print(f"credit_history account={CREDIT_USER_ID[:8]}… src={'env' if os.environ.get('KALSHI_USER_ID') else 'SOURCE DEFAULT'}")`. A wrong-account read stops being silent immediately, which is the actual failure the item names.
  3. Only after (1) is verified by readback, drop the literal in a separate one-line commit.

(b) UNBOUNDED DRY-RUN INTENTS (:150, :188): `self.intents = deque(maxlen=int(os.environ.get("KALSHI_MAX_INTENTS", "5000")))`. Grep-verified: `.intents` has no consumer anywhere in kalshi_live/ outside the client itself, so append/len/iteration are the entire contract and a deque satisfies all three. Same class as m-26's `feed_lat_ms`; land them together or reference one from the other.
- *Why:* (a) The account identifier ships in source as the env fallback, so the module is pinned to one account and any repo reuse against a second account silently reads the FIRST account's credit history — a wrong-account read that produces plausible numbers is the worst possible failure shape for a reward-attribution feed. (b) `self.intents.append` on every dry-run write never truncates, so a long-lived dry-run daemon grows RSS forever — harmless in live mode where the list is never written, but the dry-run soak is exactly the long-running configuration we use for rehearsals.
- *Standing detector:* (a) The per-read account-attribution line above, plus the `KALSHI_USER_ID` entry in `test_env_visibility.py` — that test IS the standing detector for 'a required env var silently missing from the live environment', and it already exists; this just enrolls one more var. (b) Covered by m-26's `mem` row (add `intents_n` to it).

- *Risk:* CONCERNING for (a) if the sequencing is skipped. Removing the default without the env var in place converts five working read-only tools and a systemd timer into RuntimeError at first call, and the recorder failing is not obvious until someone notices missing rows — i.e. the fix's failure mode is the same silent-data-loss class as the defect. There is also a real argument the literal is a SAFETY pin: with it present, a mis-set env cannot point us at another account; without it, a typo in live.env can. - *Fix:* MAKE THE MESSAGE MATCH THE CODE, AND NAME THE FIELD — kalshi_fill_costs. Fails today — the breakdown does not exist and the string says BOTH.

(2) If any operator runbook or grep watches for the literal 'missing BOTH pnl/fee fields', that string changes; grep the handoff docs before landing. **n-30 — kalshi_live/kalshi_cash_recorder.py:333**

- *Defect:* JSONL appends are single unflushed writes; torn final lines possible and only some consumers tolerate them
- *Fix:* ONE SHARED JSONL WRITER/READER FOR THE MONEY SERIES — new module kalshi_jsonl.py (flat layout, deployed alongside the others) with exactly two functions. (1) `append_row(path, obj)`: serialize with separators=(',',':'), `with open(path,'a') as fh: fh.write(line + '\n'); fh.flush(); os.fsync(fh.fileno())` — this does not make the append atomic (no filesystem does for a >PIPE_BUF line), but it bounds the torn-line window to the crash instant instead of leaving whole rows unflushed in a buffer at process exit. (2) `read_rows(path)` -> `(rows, n_torn)`: tolerant line reader that skips unparseable lines AND counts them, so every consumer inherits the same skip policy instead of re-deciding it. Repoint the three appenders — kalshi_cash_recorder.py:333-334, kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:387-388, kalshi_estimates_recorder.py:47-49 — and the one in-repo reader, kalshi_attribution_ledger.report() (:404-409), which then PRINTS n_torn instead of silently continuing. Leave maker_kalshi_quoter._est_feed_cached (:774-779) alone: its fail-open-with-cushion behaviour is deliberate and is the live trading path.
- *Why:* Closes 'each future analysis script re-decides how to handle a torn row in the money series': there is no shared reader today, so a torn cash row is skipped by one consumer, cushioned by another, and will crash or half-parse in the next offline differencing script somebody writes for the P2 window.
- *Standing detector:* n_torn from read_rows — printed by report() and returned to every consumer, so a torn row is COUNTED rather than quietly dropped. Add it to the estimates recorder's map-health block and to any offline differencing so 'rows I could not read' is always reported next to 'dollars I computed'.
- *Test:* New test_jsonl_shared.py: write two good rows plus a truncated third line to a tmp file; assert read_rows returns 2 rows and n_torn == 1; assert append_row on a path whose directory is read-only raises rather than silently dropping the row. Then a per-module test that kalshi_attribution_ledger.report() prints the torn count. Fails today: no module exists, and report() skips torn lines with no count (:407-409).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no — but stage it: the shared module + tests land first with no callers, then each caller flips in its own commit. The 'defensive import' fallback is what makes the first deploy safe without a flag.
- *Risk:* (1) It touches the append path of ALL THREE recorders at once, which is the opposite of one-fix-per-commit unless split into three commits behind one shared module — do that (module + tests first, then one caller per commit). (2) fsync on every row adds a disk sync per 5-minute run (cash), per hour (ledger), per 5 minutes (estimates) — negligible I/O, but on the VPS it makes each run's latency depend on disk flush behaviour; if the disk stalls, the recorder now blocks where it previously buffered. (3) A new shared module is a new deploy artifact that must be md5-verified and present in the FLAT layout at /opt/pa2-maker-kalshi-live/ — if it is missing at import time the recorders fail to start, which is a much worse failure than a torn line. Import it defensively (try/except ImportError -> keep the current inline append) for the first deploy. (4) The quoter is deliberately NOT repointed, so two reader policies still exist by design; document that rather than pretending it is unified.
- *Collides with:* M-16, m-3, m-34, m-35, n-31

**n-31 — kalshi_live/kalshi_estimates_recorder.py:79**

- *Defect:* Programs that ended before the recorder's first run are permanently unmappable, counted but unactionable
- *Fix:* BACKFILL PRE-DEPLOYMENT ENDED PROGRAMS — the same recovery helper M-16 introduces, triggered by a different condition. In kalshi_estimates_recorder, after computing `unmapped` (:79), if unmapped > 0 and the cooldown marker permits, call `_fetch_programs(status=None, need_ids=<the unmapped program_ids>)` over the UNFILTERED /incentive_programs feed and merge whatever it resolves. The unfiltered feed DOES contain ended programs — established by the repo's own probes: VER8.py/VER8b.py/VER10.py paginate `/incentive_programs?limit=10000` with next_cursor and bucket the result into future-start / live-now / ENDED, and VER8.py asserts status=active is a strict subset of unfiltered. Early-exit the pagination once need_ids is empty so the common case is cheap. Report what remains: log `unmapped_ids` after the attempt, because an id that survives a full unfiltered sweep is genuinely absent at the venue and is an operator question, not a bug to retry forever (the cooldown stops the retry loop).
- *Why:* Closes 'the map can only ever learn from the active feed, so anything that ended before deployment is permanently unmappable and its estimate rows sit in an unactionable counter until payout' — which is a hole in exactly the est->credit calibration sample the 0a recorder exists to build.
- *Standing detector:* The map-health block from M-16 (`unmapped`, `unmapped_ids`, `recovered`) written into the estimates JSONL row: unmapped>0 PERSISTING after a backfill attempt is the alarm, and because the ids are recorded, the operator sees WHICH programs are unresolvable rather than a bare count ticking up in a journal.
- *Test:* In test_estimates_map_recovery.py: stub the active pull to return nothing and the unfiltered pull to return one ENDED program p9; write an estimates response containing program_id p9; assert p9 lands in the saved map and the row's `unmapped` is 0 after the run. Fails today — the recorder only ever merges status=active, so p9 stays unmapped forever.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no trading flag needed (read-only recorder); gate it with the same KALSHI_MAP_BACKFILL knob as M-16 so a heavy venue read can be disabled without a redeploy.
- *Risk:* (1) Depends on M-16's helper; standalone it would be M. (2) Same heaviness risk as M-16: an unfiltered sweep over a program universe whose full capture was ~47MB on 2026-07-23 is not a 5-minute-timer-friendly operation without the page cap, timeout, early exit and cooldown — and if the operator's cooldown is too short this becomes a repeated heavy read against the venue. (3) Backfilled rows are merged into a map the LIVE quoter reads when EST_FEED=1 (dark today): a program with an end_date in the past is correctly SKIPPED by the quoter's ended-program guard (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:762-770), so backfilling ended programs cannot loosen the entry floor — verified, but re-verify if that guard ever changes. (4) If the venue's unfiltered feed ever starts excluding very old programs, this silently stops resolving them; the persisting unmapped_ids list is what makes that visible.

**n-32 — kalshi_live/kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:279**

- *Defect:* Non-atomic snapshot: balance read tens of seconds before fills, so in-flight activity lands as +/- residual noise
- *Fix:* MEASURE AND RECORD THE SNAPSHOT SKEW INSTEAD OF PRETENDING THE SNAPSHOT IS ATOMIC — kalshi_attribution_ledger.collect(). There is no atomic venue snapshot endpoint, so the mechanism cannot be removed; it can be bounded and made visible. (1) Keep the existing balance read at :279 and keep using it for `balance`, `rewards_residual` and `last_balance` — the money math and the row-to-row chain stay byte-identical. (2) After the last money-defining read (settlements, :302), take a SECOND balance read and add telemetry-only fields to the row: `balance_post`, `snapshot_skew_usd = round(balance_post - bal, 4)`, `read_span_s = (t_end - t_start)`. (3) In report(), treat a row whose |rewards_residual| <= |snapshot_skew_usd| as NOISE: print it flagged rather than as money, and print a window summary line 'N of M rows had non-zero snapshot skew, total |skew| $X'. (4) Document in the row (and in the residual's own comment at :361-362) that a per-row residual carries an uncertainty of at least the skew — which is the honest statement, since sums are unbiased but individual rows are not.
- *Why:* Closes 'a decomposition presented per-row when its inputs are read tens of seconds apart': a fill executing between the balance read and the fills read is in fills_cash but not in bal, pushing this row's residual negative and the next row's positive by the same amount, and per-row residuals are exactly what the clean-interval and per-day surfaces display.
- *Standing detector:* snapshot_skew_usd and read_span_s on EVERY row — a persistent, machine-readable bound on each row's residual uncertainty, which lets any offline consumer (including the P2 window arithmetic) separate 'money' from 'read skew' instead of assuming the snapshot was atomic. The report()'s skew-summary line is the human surface.
- *Test:* New test in test_attribution.py: stub `get` so the balance endpoint returns 100.00 on the first call and 97.00 on the second (a $3 fill landed mid-snapshot), with a fill tape that does NOT yet contain it. Assert the row has balance == 100.00 (unchanged behaviour), snapshot_skew_usd == -3.0, and that report() flags that row as skew-contaminated rather than printing it as rewards. Fails today: neither field exists and the row prints as money.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — the second balance read and the new fields are pure telemetry and provably do not touch the residual chain. Only if the operator ever wants the residual COMPUTED against balance_post would a flag be needed; that is a different, later change. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* CONCERNING FOR A REASON THAT IS NOT A BUG: this fix does not change any number — it PROVES that per-row residuals in the EXISTING history carry an unbounded, unmeasured error, which weakens any prior claim built on individual rows or on a day split at midnight. That is the correct outcome and the operator should hear it plainly before the P2 verdict, not after. Operationally: (1) one extra authenticated read per run (~0.6s of spacing) — trivial; (2) if the second balance read FAILS, the row must still be written with snapshot_skew_usd = null rather than aborting the run (wrap it); (3) adding fields changes the row shape mid-series again (additive); (4) the report()'s noise flagging changes what the operator sees for historical rows that lack the field — those must render as 'skew unknown', not as 'skew 0'.
- *Collides with:* m-31, m-32, m-33, m-34, m-35

**n-33 — kalshi_live/kalshi_credit_feedback.py:67**

- *Defect:* Credit rows' `status` field ignored — a pending/reversed credit would count as 'paid' forever
- *Fix:* kalshi_credit_feedback.py — stop ignoring the credit row's `status`. Module constants: `CREDIT_STATUS_FINAL = {'applied'}` (the only value ever observed: 58/58 status=applied, recorded in cash_identity_check.py:18) and `CREDIT_STATUS_NONFINAL = {'pending','reversed','canceled','cancelled','failed','clawed_back','voided'}`. In the credit loop (:66-76) normalize `st = str(r.get('status') or '').lower()`, SKIP the row (no usd, no n) iff `st in CREDIT_STATUS_NONFINAL`, count it otherwise, and always tally `status_counts[st or '<absent>']`. Return the tally so the doc written by main() carries `credits_status_counts`. Add two rules to `feed_integrity_alarms`: any status outside FINAL∪NONFINAL -> `!! UNKNOWN CREDIT STATUS '<v>' n=… — vocabulary unverified, counted as FINAL`; any NONFINAL row present at all -> `!! NON-FINAL CREDIT ROWS PRESENT`. DIRECTION IS DELIBERATE: unknown statuses keep counting (today's behavior preserved, no silent regression), only explicitly-known non-final values are excluded. The allowlist alternative (count only 'applied') was rejected because a venue rename would instantly convict every payer.
- *Why:* Both 'paid' (kalshi_credit_feedback.py:121-122) and D3 'proven' size-unclamping (quoter _d3_ramp_ct :553-557) key on credits_n > 0, so a pending or reversed row would buy a series a rank bonus and full-size rungs on money the venue never settled; classifying the status makes the receipt test actually test a receipt.
- *Standing detector:* `credits_status_counts` written into the feed file on every build — a vocabulary census, so a new venue status value shows up as data on the next build instead of as an assumption. Plus the two alarms above (unknown-value and non-final-present). This is the detector for the class 'we parse a venue field whose vocabulary we never established'.
- *Test:* test_d2_feedback.py::test_reversed_credit_does_not_prove_payment — credits = [{'reason':'Liquidity Incentive for event KXA-26AUG01','amount_cents':500,'status':'applied'}, {'reason':'…KXB-26AUG01','amount_cents':500,'status':'reversed'}] with orders/settlements making both series filled and due; assert KXA verdict 'paid', KXB verdict 'never_paid_due', credits_n 1 and 0. FAILS BEFORE: today KXB reads 'paid' because status is never inspected. Companion test_unknown_status_counts_and_alarms pins the deliberate direction.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — at today's data (58/58 'applied') the change is a provable no-op; behavior only differs if a status value appears that has never been observed, and that case is alarmed.
- *Risk:* The NONFINAL vocabulary is invented — no non-'applied' row has ever been observed, so this is defensive code against a hypothesis. If the venue actually uses one of those words for FINAL credits (e.g. labels settled credits 'cleared' and uses 'pending' loosely), a real payer flips to never_paid_due and takes the 0.5 D2 multiplier plus the D3 clamp at 10ct. The existing VERDICT REGRESSION alarm in feed_integrity_alarms fires on exactly that transition, but it PRINTS and the feed is written and consumed anyway — the alarm is not a gate. Separately: credits are not deduped by credit_id today, so if the venue ever emits a pending row and later an applied row for the same credit, credited_usd double-counts (verdicts are unaffected since they key on n > 0). I did not add dedup — one fix per commit — but the status census is what would reveal it.
- *Collides with:* m-36, n-36

**n-34 — kalshi_live/kalshi_market_scores.py:128**

- *Defect:* Swing penalty tracks the YES reference only — NO-side reference volatility invisible to ref_move
- *Fix:* kalshi_market_scores.py — the cache must remember BOTH references (shared root with n-37: only the YES side is stored today). (1) `update(markets, ticker, capture_usd_day, ref_yes, now=None, ref_no=None, swing_both=False)` — additive params, every existing caller and test unchanged. Always store `row['nref'] = float(ref_no)` when ref_no is not None, and always compute a SECOND EWMA `row['ref_move_no']` from prev nref vs new nref under the same `gap_ok` (SWING_MAX_GAP_S) rule as the yes side. `row['ref_move']` — the value score() consumes at :216 — becomes `max(move_yes, move_no)` ONLY when swing_both is True; otherwise it stays exactly today's yes-only EWMA. Two separate accumulators, never one merged value, so the flag can be armed and disarmed without corrupting the series. (2) quoter :5423 passes `ref_no=_cache_row.get('n_ref')` (the telemetry row already carries it, built at :2784 from the NO-side _qualifying_breakdown) and `swing_both=SCORE_SWING_BOTH`, a new env flag KALSHI_SCORE_SWING_BOTH defaulting to 0. RECORDING SHIPS DARK-ON, CONSUMPTION SHIPS FLAGGED.
- *Why:* ref_move is the adverse-selection proxy and we quote BOTH sides; a book whose NO reference whips while the YES bid is pinned (spread collapse/expansion, one-sided depth withdrawal) runs through our resting no-bid and carries zero swing discount today.
- *Standing detector:* `ref_move_no` is recorded on every fold regardless of the flag — a standing measurement of the thing we currently cannot see. Add `plan['score_swing_no_gt_yes_n']`: count of folds this cycle where move_no > 1.5 x move_yes. A persistent zero says the flag is not worth arming (n_ref really is the mechanical mirror of y_ref); a persistent nonzero is the defect firing and is the evidence needed to arm. This is the 'measure before proposing' shape applied to a scoring knob.
- *Test:* test_score_rank.py::test_no_side_swing_is_penalized_when_armed — fold two observations with y_ref pinned at 0.50 and n_ref going 0.45 -> 0.30; with swing_both=True assert the resulting score() is strictly below the swing_both=False score, and assert the flag-off row's `ref_move` is byte-identical to today's yes-only value. FAILS BEFORE: `update` has no ref_no parameter, so the input cannot even be expressed. Companion test_nref_bootstrap_is_not_a_spike: a row with no prior 'nref' folds move_no = 0 (no phantom penalty on the first observation after deploy).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_SCORE_SWING_BOTH default 0. The recording half (nref + ref_move_no) is dark-safe and should ship in the same commit but unarmed, so the arming decision is made from `plan['score_swing_no_gt_yes_n']` rather than from theory. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* On a tight book n_ref ~= 1 - y_ref, so max() usually reproduces today's number; the cases where it differs are precisely spread expansion/contraction — which is NOT always adverse selection. A rival pulling and re-posting makes the book widen then re-tighten, and after arming that market carries a swing discount even though nothing ever crossed our orders: a real risk of penalizing good markets, and the penalty feeds selection (SCORE_RANK=1 per the 08-04 EOD handoff, UNVERIFIED at 08-06). max() is also strictly stronger than today, so arming shrinks scores globally and fewer markets beat their pool prior — an interaction with the blend, not just a per-market change. Finally, existing cached rows have no 'nref', so the no-side EWMA warms up over one fold per market: the flag's effect is near-invisible for one cycle and then arrives everywhere at once, which looks like a step change in the caprank log.
- *Collides with:* n-35, n-37

**n-35 — kalshi_live/kalshi_market_scores.py:216**

- *Defect:* At intermediate decay the swing penalty still partially discounts the pool-prior component of the blend
- *Fix:* kalshi_market_scores.py — expose the blend split and scope the penalty to the component it describes. (1) New `score_parts(markets, ticker, pool_usd_day, now=None, swing_penalty=1.0, unknown_bonus=1.0)` returning `(measured, prior, kind)` where `measured = cap * decay * penalty` and `prior = pool_usd_day * unknown_bonus * (1 - decay)`; never-measured -> `(0.0, pool*unknown_bonus, 'unknown')`. (2) `score()` keeps its exact signature and gains `penalty_scope='blend'`: 'blend' returns today's `blended * penalty_eff` byte-for-byte (:228-229 untouched on that path), 'measured' returns `measured + prior`. (3) `ks.rank` and `kcr.shadow_rank` each gain the same pass-through kwarg with the same default; the quoter plumbs `penalty_scope=SCORE_PENALTY_SCOPE` from a new env KALSHI_SCORE_PENALTY_SCOPE defaulting to 'blend'.
- *Why:* ref_move describes the measurement, not the pool prior, so multiplying the blended value applies a fraction of the swing discount to the prior component at intermediate ages; the 08-04 fix corrected the endpoints by decaying the penalty, and scoping it is the exact form that fix approximated.
- *Standing detector:* The caprank component rows gain `penalty_scope`, so every logged ordering row states which formula produced it — no silent formula swaps inside the evidence base (the failure mode that made the D1 change look byte-unchanged when it was not). Plus a property test asserting endpoint identity between the two scopes at decay ~1 and decay ~0 over randomized inputs, which is what catches a future refactor breaking the continuity the 08-04 comment claims.
- *Test:* test_score_rank.py::test_penalty_scope_measured_does_not_discount_the_prior — row capture=0, ref_move=0.12, pool=100, age = one HALF_LIFE_S: assert score(..., penalty_scope='measured') == approx(50.0) (the prior half undiscounted) while the default 'blend' path keeps today's ~42.3, and assert both scopes agree at age 0 and at age 10*HALF_LIFE_S. FAILS BEFORE: there is no 'measured' branch, and score() rejects the kwarg.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes — KALSHI_SCORE_PENALTY_SCOPE default 'blend' is byte-identical to today on every input; 'measured' is the armed state.
- *Risk:* The correction RAISES the score of aged high-swing markets — it removes a discount that currently, accidentally, keeps us further away from books that fill us adversely. The principle is right; the live direction is less conservative than today. It also introduces a second formula into a module consumed by both selection (ks.rank) and the allocation key (shadow_rank), so the two scopes must never be mixed inside one evidence window: a mid-day flip makes caprank rows incomparable and would be indistinguishable from a market-regime change in the logs. Magnitude is small (mid-curve only, bounded by the prior's weight) and ordering-only, which is why this is the last item to arm, not the first. score_parts is also the split the shadow_rank docstring (:126-128) says is needed to haircut only the PRIOR component — that second consumer is deliberately NOT wired in this commit.
- *Collides with:* n-34

**n-36 — kalshi_live/kalshi_credit_feedback.py:108**

- *Fix:* kalshi_credit_feedback.py — two independent root fixes of 'the offline builder trusts a venue literal'. (a) `_iso()` (:42-43) must honor its implicit contract of returning a tz-AWARE datetime: parse as today, then `if d.tzinfo is None: d = d.replace(tzinfo=dt.timezone.utc)`, and count naive-parsed rows in a module counter. (b) Single source of truth for the credit limit: module constant `CREDIT_LIMIT = 1000`; main() calls `c.get_credit_history(limit=CREDIT_LIMIT)` (:164) and `feed_integrity_alarms(creds, matched_n, CREDIT_LIMIT, prev_series, fb)` (:206), so the requested bound and the alarm's bound are the same literal by construction instead of two hand-synchronized constants.
- *Why:* (a) A tz-naive settled_time kills the whole builder at `now - st`, and the D2 feed then goes stale silently because every live consumer fails open to a 1.0 multiplier — a loud offline crash becomes a silent live degradation. (b) The B-7 truncation alarm compares row count against a literal duplicated from the client's default; change either and the alarm fires never or always.

(b) test asserting `CREDIT_LIMIT == inspect.signature(KalshiOrderClient.get_credit_history).parameters['limit'].default` — the suite fails if either side moves, which is the drift detector for duplicated-constant class. (c) The real failure mode here is 'D2 feed goes stale and nobody notices', so the matching live detector is a `credit_feedback_age_h` counter in the quoter plan, computed from the feed doc's `generated` field in _load_credit_feedback (:482) / _d3_feedback_cached (:784) — today a dead builder is completely invisible in the trading log. Flagged as arguably its own small fix (see cross_item_notes).

FAILS BEFORE with TypeError: can't subtract offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes. Plus test_credit_limit_matches_client_default (fails if the constant is reintroduced by hand on either side).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — offline builder only, no trading path; provably a no-op on the current venue shape (settled_time is Z-suffixed 127/127 per the client's measured note) and the constant change is a pure de-duplication.
- *Risk:* Attaching UTC to a naive stamp is an ASSUMPTION: if the venue ever emitted local-time stamps, a naive row is mis-dated by the offset — bounded at a few hours against a 72h margin, so it can only flip a conviction for an event sitting within hours of the due boundary. The alarm makes the assumption visible rather than silent, which is the point. Note also that the crash today is arguably the SAFER behavior (fail-loud on an offline builder) and this fix converts it into a continue-with-assumption; that is why the naive counter and the alarm are not optional. The limit constant is inert today (both values are already 1000).
- *Collides with:* m-36, n-33

**n-37 — kalshi_live/kalshi_capital_rank.py:80**

- *Defect:* est_commit_usd hard-assumes JOIN_SIZE=0 and complement pricing (no side = 1 - yes ref)
- *Fix:* kalshi_capital_rank.est_commit_usd (:80-103) — make both hidden modelling assumptions explicit parameters. Signature becomes `est_commit_usd(ref_yes, market_cap_usd, inv_hard_ct, quantum=5, join_size=0, ref_no=None)` (additive; defaults reproduce today byte-for-byte). (a) `join_size > 0` mirrors _capped_join's other branch exactly (quoter:2336-2337): `n = min(join_size, dollar_ct)` with NO quantize and NO INV_HARD_CT clamp, then the shared `max(1, n)`. (b) `ref_no` given: price the NO side at `min(0.99, max(0.01, ref_no))` instead of `1 - p_yes`; absent -> today's complement, unchanged. (c) Update the docstring — it currently claims the estimate and the live sizing 'can never drift in shape', which is true only in the JOIN_SIZE=0 branch; after this it is true in both. (d) Callers: `_alloc_priority` (:3667), `_caprank_telemetry` (:847) and the select-budget walk (:5066) pass `join_size=JOIN_SIZE`; `ref_no` is passed only once the score cache carries `nref` (n-34's recording half) and rides n-34's arming decision.
- *Why:* The estimator is a hand-copy of one branch of _capped_join, so setting KALSHI_JOIN_SIZE > 0 silently forks the ordering and charging denominator from the live sizing; and pricing the NO side at 1 - p_yes ignores the spread, overstating the cheap side's price (0.70 modeled vs 0.60 actual on a 0.30/0.60 book) wherever INV_HARD_CT rather than the dollar cap binds.
- *Standing detector:* Extend the existing T1 equivalence pin (test_capital_rank.py:33, test_commit_estimate_matches_capped_join_exactly) to parametrize over JOIN_SIZE in {0, 20, 100} x the existing ref grid — the suite then fails the moment the two implementations diverge in ANY configuration, which is the actual class (a copy that is only checked in the one branch the config happens to use). Runtime: M-20's `commit_est_err` series (estimated commit vs dollars actually resting) measures the spread-induced overstatement directly, so the residual model error becomes a number instead of an argument.
- *Test:* test_capital_rank.py::test_commit_estimate_matches_capped_join_at_nonzero_join_size — monkeypatch q.JOIN_SIZE = 20, compute expected = _capped_join(p_yes,p_no)*p_yes + _capped_join(p_no,p_yes)*p_no, assert est_commit_usd(ref, 60.0, 60.0, join_size=20) == expected across the ref grid. FAILS BEFORE: est keeps the INV_HARD/quantize shape and mismatches at most refs. Companion test_no_side_priced_from_its_own_ref on a 0.30/0.60 book asserts the estimate uses 0.60, not 0.70.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* partly. (a) join_size is a provable no-op at JOIN_SIZE=0 and needs no flag (it removes a divergence rather than creating behavior). (b) ref_no changes live ordering wherever a no-side ref exists, so it must ride n-34's KALSHI_SCORE_SWING_BOTH-era arming or its own flag — never default-on.
- *Risk:* The join_size half is inert at today's config (JOIN_SIZE=0, operator 07-29) — but it means that if JOIN_SIZE is ever raised, sizing AND ordering move together in one config change instead of sizing alone; that is correct, and it must be called out in the JOIN_SIZE knob comment or the next tuner will be surprised by an ordering shift they did not ask for. The ref_no half is NOT inert: pricing NO at its true (lower) ref shrinks the modeled commit on wide books, so wide-spread books rank UP in cap_score by roughly the spread ratio (~15% on a 10-tick book). That is a genuine re-rating of a market class in the allocation key, on a NOTE-severity correction — arm it with n-34's flag, not on its own, and expect the caprank ordering to shift for exactly the books where our spread capture is widest. It also creates a hard dependency: without n-34's `nref` recording there is no ref_no to pass, so shipping (b) alone accomplishes nothing.

**n-38 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:2759**

- *Defect:* _market_telemetry_row scores one quote per side — D-C macro-probe ladders would measure top level only
- *Fix:* Generalize `_market_telemetry_row` from one quote per side to the FULL intended ladder on that side. In the per-side loop (~:2757-2789) replace the `next((x for x in quotes if x.get('side') == side), None)` single pick with:
    oqs = [x for x in quotes if x.get("side") == side and x.get("price_dollars") is not None]
    our_lv = [(float(x["price_dollars"]), float(x["count"])) for x in oqs]
    our_px = max((p for p, _ in our_lv), default=None)      # TOP level = the row's headline price
    our_ct = sum(c for _, c in our_lv)                      # TOTAL intended size on this side
    score = 0.0
    if qual and ref is not None:
        score = sum(_lip_mult(p, ref, df, LIP_REF) * c for p, c in our_lv
                    if LIP_REF or (low_q is not None and p >= low_q - 1e-9))
and add two additive keys: `y_levels`/`n_levels` (len(our_lv)) and `y_px_low`/`n_px_low` (the deepest level's price). Everything else in the row is untouched.

SEMANTIC NOTE that must be in the commit message, not discovered later: `y_ct` becomes the SUM across levels and `y_px` the TOP level. For every single-quote side — i.e. every market on the current live book, since the ladder path is only reachable via the dark macro probe — both are byte-identical to today. Consumers grepped at HEAD 233ee86: smoke_dryrun.py:58,69-71 (dev tool, sum is the right number there), and the study/* scripts read `*_rest_ct`, `*_ref`, `*_share`, `capture_usd_day` — none read `y_ct`.

The mechanism class is broader than the probe: any multi-level quoting the bot ever does is mis-measured by a single-quote reader.
- *Standing detector:* `y_levels`/`n_levels` in every row. The alarm is trivial and permanent: any row with `*_levels > 1` whose `*_score` equals the top level's own contribution means a single-quote reader crept back in. Pair it with a per-cycle `telemetry_multilevel_sides` counter so the population is visible the first cycle D-C arms.
- *Test:* New test in test_dc_macro_probe.py, failing before: build the 3-level ladder `_macro_probe_quotes` produces (0.03/0.02/0.01 x counts) against a book that qualifies, and assert `row['y_score']` equals the sum of `_lip_mult(p)*c` over all three levels — today it equals only the first. Second pin: `row['y_levels'] == 3` and `row['y_ct'] == sum of counts`. Third: a single-quote side produces a row byte-identical to today's (dict equality against a frozen expected row).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* NO. Telemetry-only — `_market_telemetry_row` is a pure function that cannot alter `desired` (the call site at ~:5384 is the last thing in the loop body and is wrapped). It is a no-op on every single-quote side, which is the entire live population today.
- *Risk:* (1) `y_ct` semantics change is a silent-migration risk for any consumer I did not grep — I grepped the repo at HEAD and found only smoke_dryrun.py, but historical analysis notebooks and the operator's own ad-hoc jq over quotes-*.jsonl are outside the repo and would see a changed meaning for multi-level rows only. (2) capture_usd_day rises for macro-probe rows (correctly), which changes the SCORES fold for those tickers — but only when D-C is armed, and the fold is already guarded to accumulating quotes. (3) Summing counts across levels for `y_ct` mixes sizes resting at different prices into one number that no longer has a single distance attached; `y_levels` and `y_px_low` are what make it interpretable, so dropping them to save lines would leave the row ambiguous.
- *Collides with:* M-2, n-4, m-2

- *Standing detector:* The row itself IS the detector: `excluded_taker_fills` / `excluded_taker_cash` now appear for the family they belong to, so a window dominated by an emergency flatten is visible in the artifact instead of being a silent absence. Add a main() line printing the total taker-excluded cash when exclude_taker is on, so the size of what was withheld is stated rather than inferred.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, in test_netev_rebuild.py: `test_a_fully_taker_excluded_family_still_emits_its_audit_row` — tape of 3 fills on KXAAAGASD-... all with is_taker=True, build_table(..., exclude_taker=True). Today doc['families'] == {} (the assert KeyErrors). After: families['gas']['excluded_taker_fills'] == 3, ['excluded_taker_cash'] != 0, ['n_fills'] == 0, ['notional'] == 0.0, ['confidence'] == 'unproven', and 'excluded as taker' in ['evidence'].
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* NO — additive to an experiment-only code path (exclude_taker defaults False) and provably inert to the armed gate as argued above.
- *Risk:* Provably no-op for the live gate, and the argument is worth stating because it is what makes this shippable without a flag: such a row necessarily has n_fills == 0 and notional == 0, so it can never reach 'receipt'; the gate's non-receipt branch (maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3011-3016) is byte-identical to what it already does when `ent is None`, which is what it gets today. exclude_taker also defaults OFF, so the deployed table is untouched entirely. Residual risk: a human reading the new row could mistake an unproven taker-only row for evidence about the family — mitigated by the explicit evidence string. Second-order: if a future change ever lets a taker-excluded family accumulate notional, this row could become receipt-eligible on excluded data — pin that with the notional == 0 assert in the test.
- *Collides with:* M-21, M-22

**n-40 — kalshi_live/kalshi_presence_calibrate.py:193**

- *Defect:* presence_for's '*|bucket' wildcard fallback key is never produced by measure() — dead lookup path
- *Fix:* Make the wildcard fallback a real producer contract instead of deleting it (removal-as-fix is forbidden by the project norm, and the fallback is the right idea — presence is dominated by market LIFE, not family, which is the module's own measured premise at :40-42).

In kalshi_presence_calibrate.measure(orders, market_lives, wildcard=False, ...): after the per-(family,bucket) pass, run a second pass that pools EVERY market's presence value by life bucket alone and builds `*|{bucket}` rows with the same field shape (presence_median/mean, n_markets, resting_hours, fees_usd, fees_per_resting_hour, orders, short_lived, filled) plus `family: '*'`.
  * ALWAYS emit them under a new top-level `wildcard_buckets` block — reporting only, invisible to the live gate because load_table returns data['families'] alone.
  * Emit them INTO `families` only when wildcard=True (CLI `--wildcard`). That is the arming step, and it changes live behavior with zero quoter edits because maker_kalshi_quoter._presence_factor already consults `*|{bucket}` at :1272-1274.
Also emit doc-level `families_without_own_bucket` (count of family|bucket combinations present in the order tape with no measured row) so the size of the gap the wildcard is meant to fill is stated rather than assumed.
- *Why:* presence_for and the quoter's _presence_factor both consult a `*|bucket` key that no producer in the repo has ever written, so the family-agnostic fallback silently never fires and every unmatched family is quoted at presence_factor 1.0 — assume-perfect execution on exactly the new families where the measured median is ~5.7%.
- *Standing detector:* `wildcard_emitted` (bool) and `families_without_own_bucket` (int) at doc level, printed by main(); the second is the standing measure of how much of the live book is being quoted on assume-perfect presence. A pin asserts producer and BOTH consumers agree on the key format — kalshi_presence_calibrate.presence_for AND maker_kalshi_quoter._presence_factor, since the quoter deliberately keeps its own copy of the lookup and only a test keeps the two byte-equal.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, in test_presence_gate.py: `test_the_wildcard_bucket_is_produced_and_actually_resolves` — measure() over two DIFFERENT families in the same 6-24h life bucket. Today res['families'] has no '*|6-24h' key and cal.presence_for(res['families'], 'KXBRANDNEW-26AUG09-X', 12.0) == 1.0 (the dead path). After: res['wildcard_buckets']['*|6-24h'] exists unconditionally; with wildcard=True the key is in families and presence_for returns the pooled median (not 1.0); and q._presence_factor with PRESENCE_TABLE set to that families dict returns the same value — pinning that the quoter's independent copy of the lookup resolves it identically.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* YES — `wildcard=False` default plus the reporting-only `wildcard_buckets` block means the numbers are visible before they are believed; arming is an explicit `--wildcard` regeneration of the table file. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* CONCERNING when armed. Turning the fallback on moves every family with no own bucket from 1.0 to the pooled median (measured ~0.057, a ~17x cut in expected credit), which will push most such markets under the $1 LIP floor and mass-skip them. Those are precisely the exploration markets D3_RAMP / PIVOT_SELECT / SELECT_BUDGET are ramping into, so arming this can collapse the exploration surface in one table regeneration — with no code change and therefore no md5 diff to notice. It also assumes presence is family-independent within a life bucket; the module says life DOMINATES, which is not the same as family being irrelevant, and pooling a 300-order family against a 3-order one lets the big family define the small one's factor. Second-order: pooling uses per-MARKET presence values, so a family with many short-lived markets skews the bucket median. Deploy hazard: as with m-45, do not bump kalshi_presence_calibrate.SCHEMA — load_table fails closed on mismatch and the quoter would fall back to presence 1.0 everywhere.
HONEST ALTERNATIVE the operator should be offered: keep the wildcard REPORT-ONLY indefinitely and use `wildcard_buckets` as the input to a deliberate per-family PRESENCE_DEFAULT decision, rather than letting one table regeneration silently re-price every uncalibrated family.
- *Collides with:* m-45

**n-41 — kalshi_live/kalshi_netev_rebuild.py:377**

- *Defect:* Date-only --until bounds at 00:00Z, excluding effectively the whole final day; ET windows need manual math
- *Fix:* Add an explicit bound helper to kalshi_netev_rebuild.py and use it in main(); leave _iso (the parser) alone so the existing UTC-default regression pins keep passing.

  def _iso_bound(s, end=False, tz_offset_h=0.0):
      """Window bound from a CLI string. A DATE-ONLY value names a whole DAY: as --since it is
      that day's first instant, as --until its LAST. _in_window's comparison is inclusive (<=),
      so a date-only --until previously admitted only the midnight instant and silently dropped
      the entire final day. tz_offset_h places a date-only bound on another clock — the canon
      window is ET DATES, i.e. -4.0 during EDT."""
      if s is None: return None
      d = _iso(s)
      if 'T' not in str(s):                      # date-only -> a whole day on the chosen clock
          d = d - dt.timedelta(hours=tz_offset_h)
          if end: d = d + dt.timedelta(days=1, microseconds=-1)
      return d

main(): `lo = _iso_bound(a.since, tz_offset_h=a.tz)`, `hi = _iso_bound(a.until, end=True, tz_offset_h=a.tz)`, new `--tz` (float hours, default 0.0, help: 'clock for DATE-ONLY bounds; -4 = ET during EDT'), updated --until help ('window end, ISO; a DATE-ONLY value means THROUGH THE END of that day'), and a stderr line printing the RESOLVED window and its inclusive day count BEFORE building. Add `window_days` to the emitted document so the resolved span travels with the artifact.
- *Why:* _iso('2026-08-09') is 00:00:00Z and _in_window is inclusive, so --until 2026-08-09 admits one instant and drops the whole final day — the same off-by-one-day class as the 08-02 'FREE TEST Aug 3/4/5' error, on the CLI that produces the table the armed gate consumes.
- *Standing detector:* main() prints the RESOLVED window and its inclusive day count before every build, and `window_days` is written into the document beside the existing `window` pair — so a table built over the wrong span is visible in the artifact itself instead of only in a shell history nobody kept.
- *Test:* FAILING-BEFORE, in test_netev_rebuild.py: `test_a_date_only_until_covers_the_whole_final_day` — assert nr._iso_bound('2026-08-09', end=True) == nr._iso('2026-08-09T23:59:59.999999Z') and nr._in_window('2026-08-09T20:00:00Z', (nr._iso_bound('2026-08-06'), nr._iso_bound('2026-08-09', end=True))) is True, while the current construction (nr._iso('2026-08-09')) makes the same assertion False. Plus `test_a_full_timestamp_until_is_untouched` (no silent widening) and `test_a_tz_offset_shifts_a_date_only_bound` (_iso_bound('2026-07-21', tz_offset_h=-4.0) == _iso('2026-07-21T04:00:00Z'), the like-for-like ET bound the caveats block already documents).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* NO — an operator-facing bound whose old behavior is simply wrong, it changes no default (a full-timestamp --until behaves identically), and the resolved window is now printed and stored so the change is loud rather than silent.
- *Risk:* It changes what an already-typed command means: any operator invocation that passed a date-only --until now covers one extra day of trading, so a new table is NOT comparable to one built with the same command string before the fix. The deployed table was built with a full-timestamp --until (window hi = 2026-08-03T17:06:00.554496Z), so nothing currently on disk shifts, but a B8 run must state its RESOLVED window rather than its command line. --tz applies a FIXED offset with no DST handling — documented in the help text rather than pretended away (kalshi_netev_calibrate._date carries the same fixed -04:00 assumption, so the two agree during EDT and would both be wrong in EST). Making the end bound inclusive also means a fill at exactly 23:59:59.999999Z is in scope while a microsecond later it is not; that is the intended semantics, and the printed resolved window is what tells the reader.
- *Collides with:* M-21

**n-42 — kalshi_live/kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:383**

- *Defect:* Banned fields rewards_residual / rewards_alloc_by_series still produced and persisted by their origin module
- *Fix:* FENCE THE PRODUCER RATHER THAN KILLING IT — pairs with m-33 (same fields, same module). (1) Do NOT remove rewards_residual / rewards_alloc_by_series from collect()'s row (:383-384): removal is a demotion of a recorded series and the residual is still the ledger's only balance-anchored arithmetic check. (2) Add the m-33 provenance field and the module-docstring ban banner so the producer carries its own warning. (3) Add the CLASS DETECTOR, which is the real deliverable here: a new test_banned_reward_basis.py that walks every *.py in kalshi_live/, greps for the identifiers `rewards_residual` and `rewards_alloc_by_series`, and asserts the set of files containing them equals an explicit allowlist {kalshi_attribution_ledger.py, test_attribution.py, test_banned_reward_basis.py}. Any new module that reads either field fails the suite with a message naming RULE ZERO and pointing at credit_history as the reward truth.
- *Why:* Closes 'the banned basis is one attribute-access away from a new consumer': nothing in the money path reads these fields today, but they are computed, persisted and conveniently per-series, so the next tool that imports kalshi_attribution_ledger for its HTTP helpers can adopt them without anyone noticing — the ban lives only in a memory file, not in the build.
- *Standing detector:* The repo-scan test IS the standing detector, and it is the right shape for a ban: it catches recurrence at commit time, in the suite the deploy gate already runs, rather than depending on a reviewer remembering the rule. It also documents the allowlist explicitly, so adding a legitimate consumer becomes a deliberate, reviewed act.
- *Test:* test_banned_reward_basis.py itself is the failing-before artifact in the weak sense (it passes on today's tree, which is the point — it is a regression fence, not a bug pin). To make it a genuine failing-before, land it together with a fixture check: add a temporary sentinel file under a tmp dir that the scanner is pointed at, assert the scanner FAILS on it, then assert it PASSES on kalshi_live/. That pins the detector's own detection power, which is the usual weakness of grep-based guards.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — a test and a label; no runtime effect whatsoever.
- *Risk:* (1) A source-grep guard has false positives (a comment or docstring mentioning the field name trips it) — the m-33 docstring banner will contain the words, so the allowlist and the scan must tolerate the producer file and any doc mention, which slightly weakens it. Use whole-word matching and allow the strings inside comment lines only in allowlisted files. (2) It adds a test that fails on a legitimate future consumer, which could be read as the suite blocking work; the failure message must say 'add to the allowlist deliberately' rather than 'never do this'. (3) It does not stop anyone reading the fields straight out of the ledger-YYYYMM.jsonl files with an ad-hoc script — no in-repo guard can. Say so; the JSONL-side protection is m-33's in-row rewards_basis marker.
- *Collides with:* m-33, m-31, m-32, m-34, m-35, n-32

**n-43 — kalshi_live/w17_coverage_ledger.py:221**

- *Defect:* A-1 parse-rate alarm counts non-LIP credit types (referral etc.) against the 90% threshold
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/w17_coverage_ledger.py lines 218-221 — make the A-1 denominator the LIP-eligible rows, not every credit type. Credit rows carry a `type` field (venue-verified in the W10 snapshot: 57 rows type='incentive' with the LIP reason, 1 row type='referral' 'Referee Credit'). New: `lip = [x for x in creds if (x.get('type') or '').lower() == 'incentive']`; `matched = [x for x in lip if reason.startswith(CREDIT_PREFIX)]`; alarm when `lip and len(matched) < 0.9 * len(lip)`. Print a type census on every run: 'credit types: incentive=57 referral=1' plus '!! UNKNOWN CREDIT TYPE(S): {…}' for any type outside the known set {incentive, referral}. If no row carries a `type` at all, fall back to the old denominator and print 'A-1 denominator = ALL rows (no type field present)'. Factor as pure `_parse_rate(creds)` -> (n_lip, n_matched, types).
- *Why:* matched filters on the LIP reason prefix while len(creds) counts every credit type, so the ratio conflates 'the venue renamed the reason' (the A-1 target) with 'the account received other credit types' — a promo/referral batch of 7+ rows on a 58-row history trips a false alarm, and a real prefix change on a minority series is diluted by the healthy majority.
- *Standing detector:* The type census line, including the unknown-type alarm — a new credit type appearing (the venue adding a rebate/promo class) becomes a named event rather than silent denominator drift. Combined with M-26's census, every FEED INTEGRITY alarm now publishes its denominator.
- *Test:* In kalshi_live/test_w17_alarms.py over `_parse_rate`: 51 incentive rows all matching + 7 referral rows -> no alarm and n_lip == 51 (FAILS BEFORE: 51/58 = 0.879 < 0.9 -> 'PARSE RATE DEGRADED'); 51 incentive rows of which 40 match -> alarm; rows with no type -> fallback path and the caveat string.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no.
- *Risk:* If the venue ever renames the TYPE as well as the reason, the new denominator goes to zero and the alarm silently cannot fire — which is why the unknown-type alarm must land in the same commit (a zero-length LIP set with unknown types present must itself alarm). Otherwise this is a read-only diagnostic with no live path.
- *Collides with:* M-26, m-47, m-48, m-54

**n-44 — kalshi_live/w11_replay.py:146**

- *Defect:* Replay candidate pool is the shadow's top-FOOTPRINT_TOP (60) — deeper candidates invisible to every policy
- *Fix:* Two edits. (1) maker_kalshi_quoter.py _caprank_telemetry (lines 858-868): add `CAPRANK_DEPTH = _envi('KALSHI_CAPRANK_DEPTH', FOOTPRINT_TOP)` and record `'components': shadow[:CAPRANK_DEPTH]` while keeping `'shadow'/'overlap'/'would_enter'/'would_exit'` computed on shadow[:FOOTPRINT_TOP] (so the existing fields are byte-identical), plus `row['n_candidates'] = len(rows)` so the truncation is measurable even when the depth is not raised. (2) w11_replay.py: read `n_candidates` and `len(components)` per cycle and print/persist an explicit limit line — 'candidate pool: {k} of {n} ranked candidates recorded; policies cannot rescue anything below rank {k}' — and compute `payers_outside_pool`: series in credited_ever that never appear in any cycle's components. Both go into w11_replay_report.json.
- *Why:* components stores only shadow[:FOOTPRINT_TOP] (60), so every replayed policy can only reorder within the top 60 — a proven payer the shadow ranked 61st can never be selected by any policy, making D2's core claim (surfacing under-ranked proven payers) structurally untestable from this telemetry.
- *Standing detector:* `payers_outside_pool` in every replay report: a non-zero value states, in the report itself, that the harness cannot see the regime the knob targets. That number is the alarm; it also tells the operator exactly how much depth to buy by raising KALSHI_CAPRANK_DEPTH.
- *Test:* In test_w11_replay.py: a synthetic caprank cycle with components for KXA/KXB only, n_candidates=80, and a frozen credit for KXZ -> report contains candidate_pool_depth == 2, n_candidates == 80, payers_outside_pool == ['KXZ'] (FAILS BEFORE: none of these fields exist and the report silently implies full coverage).
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* Yes by knob default — KALSHI_CAPRANK_DEPTH ships equal to FOOTPRINT_TOP, so the recorded payload is unchanged until an operator raises it. Note the knob is import-time; it belongs in the restart_only warning table (quoter:3878) alongside the other telemetry knobs, or an operator edit is a silent no-op.
- *Risk:* Raising the depth is where the real risk sits: caprank rows already carry full component dicts per variant, so depth 200 with several variants multiplies the row several-fold; on the live VPS a full disk degrades the quoter (its writes are try-wrapped and counted into _SILENT, i.e. it fails quietly). Measure one row's byte size before raising, and prefer raising depth for a bounded window rather than permanently. At the default (= FOOTPRINT_TOP) the telemetry is byte-identical apart from n_candidates.
- *Collides with:* M-25, m-49, m-54

**n-45 — kalshi_live/w16_successor_finder.py:113**

- *Defect:* Single convicted TICKER escalates to whole-SERIES conviction in the evasion watch (grain escalation)
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/w16_successor_finder.py line 113 — keep the series-grain scan (successor matching only works at series grain) but stop asserting a series-grain ruling. Build `banned_tickers` (the raw mkt_out ∪ mkt_out_backup entries) alongside the series set, and for each convicted series compute its grain: `n_banned = len(tickers of s)` vs `n_active = sum(1 for t in active programs if t.split('-')[0] == s)`. Label the scan_sets entry accordingly — 'SERIES-BANNED ({n} of {n} strikes)' when every active strike is banned, otherwise 'STRIKE-BANNED ({k} of {n} strikes: KXA-1, KXA-7)' — and print the banned ticker list under the direction header. Also carry the credit-feedback verdict ('never_paid_due') as a separate, clearly-labeled conviction source, since that one IS series-grain.
- *Why:* `convicted |= {t.split('-')[0] for t in mkt_out}` escalates a per-TICKER operator ban to a whole-franchise conviction, so one toxic strike in an otherwise-healthy series makes its legitimate successor program read as 'conviction evasion' in the operator report — the label overstates the ruling's grain.
- *Standing detector:* The grain annotation printed for every convicted entry, plus a summary count 'conviction entries: {series-grain} series-wide / {strike-grain} single-strike'. A report that cannot state its own grain is the class; this makes over-reach visible on every run.
- *Test:* In kalshi_live/test_w16_lineage.py: mkt_out ['KXA-1'] with 5 active KXA strikes -> the conviction label for KXA contains 'STRIKE-BANNED (1 of 5' and the banned ticker is listed; mkt_out covering all 5 -> 'SERIES-BANNED' (FAILS BEFORE: no grain information exists — the series is flatly labeled CONVICTED/BANNED).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no.
- *Risk:* Purely a labeling change to a read-only report, so the risk is interpretive: an operator may now under-react to a strike-grain conviction that is in fact a franchise-wide problem (nine of the current permanent bans came from the $5 rung, which is a per-market signal that can still indicate a series-level pathology). The summary counts keep both readings available. No suppression of any entry — the scan set is unchanged.
- *Collides with:* m-52, m-53

- *Defect:* API key id hardcoded as an in-source default
- *Fix:* Fix the CLASS (inline credential defaults in offline tools), not the one line. (1) New tiny helper used by both offline ledger tools — `def _require_env(name, hint)` returning os.environ[name] or raising RuntimeError(f'{name} unset — export it (see {hint}) before running this READ-ONLY tool'). (2) kalshi_ledger_reconstruct.py:10 — `KID = _require_env('KALSHI_API_KEY_ID', 'live.env')`, removing the literal access-key id. Add one behavioral test: with KALSHI_API_KEY_ID unset, importing/calling the tool's `sign` raises RuntimeError naming the env var (FAILS BEFORE: it silently signs with the production key id).

- *Risk:* Operator runbooks or cron entries that relied on the default now fail loudly instead of working — that is the intent, but it will break the next unattended run of anything invoking kalshi_attribution_ledger without the env exported, and that module is imported by kalshi_ledger.py (M-24's tool) at module scope. Verify the systemd/timer units and the restart_bundle.sh path export KALSHI_API_KEY_ID before landing, or a scheduled reconciliation goes dark. **n-47 — kalshi_live/kalshi_ledger.py:310**

- *Defect:* --rate spans first-ever to last snapshot, including deposit changes and both residual eras
- *Fix:* kalshi_live/kalshi_ledger.py show_rate (lines 295-322). (1) Report deposit-neutral series: `de = (b['equity'] - (b['deposits'] or 0)) - (a['equity'] - (a['deposits'] or 0))` so a mid-window deposit cannot masquerade as equity drift.
