**n-48 — kalshi_live/kalshi_drift_detect.py:353**

- *Defect:* evaluate()/point_samples swallow all detector exceptions as 'did not fire'; bugs read as negative results
- *Fix:* Delete the blanket except Exception handlers that convert a crash into a negative result — kalshi_drift_detect.evaluate (:352-355) and kalshi_drift_eval.point_samples (:163-165), matched_window (:183-184) and the two mx closures (:353-355, :393-395) — and let the exception propagate through a thin wrapper that attaches context: def _call(fn, st, i, kw): try: return fn(st, i, **kw) except Exception as e: raise RuntimeError(f"detector {fn.__name__} raised at {st['p']['ticker']}/{st['p']['side']} i={i} ts={st['tl'][i]['ts']}: {type(e).__name__}: {e}") from e. The detectors already express 'not enough data' explicitly (if i < W: return False; if j < 0: return False) and gain the None-guards from M-27/M-28, so after those land a raise means a bug, and a bug must stop the run. This is forbidden-pattern #2 (a try/except that hides the real error) applied to a measurement harness rather than to a trading path.
- *Why:* A detector that crashes on every call currently prints TPR 0.00 and is read as 'this signal carries no information' — the harness cannot distinguish a null result from a broken detector, which is the single distinction the whole file exists to make.
- *Standing detector:* (1) Exceptions now abort with ticker, side, index and timestamp in the message, and kalshi_drift_selftest.py already exits non-zero on control failure, so schema drift on a re-fetch fails the run instead of publishing a clean row of zeros. (2) The abstain/'cover' column introduced by M-28 is the complementary alarm: an all-zero row is only readable next to its coverage, and a row with cover 0.00 is a broken detector by inspection rather than by luck.
- *Test:* kalshi_live/test_drift_detector_errors_are_loud.py — def boom(st, i): raise KeyError('yes_bid_fp'); using a minimal synthetic state (reuse kalshi_drift_selftest.synth('flat')), assert pytest.raises(RuntimeError) from D.evaluate(st, boom, {}), from E.point_samples(st, boom, {}) and from E.matched_window(st, boom, {}, 5). All three return clean zeros today, so all three assertions fail today.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no
- *Risk:* (a) The study now aborts on the first raise instead of finishing with a partly-degenerate table; if the operator wants a full run despite one broken detector, that option is gone. That is the intent, but it turns a soft failure into a hard one on a script someone may run under time pressure. (b) matched_window's except currently swallows a failure mid-loop and continues to the next decision point, so removing it changes control flow, not only reporting. (c) SEQUENCING HAZARD: if M-27/M-28 have not landed, some detectors legitimately raise on short windows and None values today, so removing the net first would abort the harness immediately. Either land n-48 after M-27/M-28, or land it first and accept that surfacing those crashes IS the point — the operator should choose deliberately rather than discover it.
- *Collides with:* M-28, M-27

**n-49 — kalshi_live/kalshi_drift_jump.py:56**

- *Defect:* decompose worst-step ignores 60s spacing (sibling checks it); latent jump_share inflation on gappy data
- *Fix:* In kalshi_drift_jump.decompose (:54-58) build the step list only from index pairs that are BOTH exactly one minute apart AND both usable, counting what is skipped: for i in idx[1:]: if tl[i]['ts'] - tl[i-1]['ts'] != 60: skipped_gap += 1; continue; if tl[i]['value'] is None or tl[i-1]['value'] is None: skipped_nobook += 1; continue; step = tl[i-1]['value'] - tl[i]['value']. Carry n_steps_used / n_steps_skipped_gap / n_steps_skipped_nobook into the emitted row, the printed table and kalshi_drift_jump.json. The same guard applies to the pre2/pre4/pre6 lookbacks at :60-63, which must resolve worst_i-1-k by timestamp rather than by index. Same commit, same defect class: build_tape_minutes (detect.py:118) maps a trade at an exact minute boundary to the NEXT candle — minute = int(tt // 60) * 60 + 60 becomes minute = int(math.ceil(tt / 60.0)) * 60 (math is already imported at detect.py:40), so a trade at exactly ts=T lands in the candle whose end_period_ts == T. Writing the guard against BOTH conditions (spacing and usability) makes the fix correct whether or not M-27/M-28 have landed, removing the ordering dependency.
- *Why:* Both sites assume an alignment they never check: consecutive list entries are assumed one minute apart (15.3% of in-window steps are not), and a trade timestamp is assumed to fall strictly inside a candle period. The first inflates jump_share — the exact number the 'the loss is a gap, so detection is misconceived' verdict rests on.
- *Standing detector:* jump_share is never printed without its coverage: the table gains used/gap/nobook columns, the JSON carries them per position, and any position whose skipped steps exceed 10% of its window is flagged with a '⚠'. A jump_share computed over a series that is 40% holes then declares itself as such instead of being quoted as a clean fraction.
- *Test:* kalshi_live/test_jump_step_spacing.py — a synthetic state whose largest apparent step spans a 59-minute hole (0.50 -> 0.10 across the hole, with real one-minute steps of at most 0.03): assert decompose(st)['worst_min_drop'] <= 0.03 and ['n_steps_skipped_gap'] == 1; today worst_min_drop is 0.40. Second test in the same commit: a trade whose created_time is exactly on a candle boundary is aggregated into the candle with that end_period_ts (today it lands one minute late).
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no
- *Risk:* (a) On the frozen data this is a verified no-op for the six RUNOVER positions (all their worst steps sit on exact 60s spacing), so it cannot be validated against a real firing case with the current cache — only the synthetic test proves it works. (b) If M-27 has landed, the value-is-None skips remove terminal minutes from the mid decomposition for the dying contracts, and jump_share_mid may become uncomputable for exactly the positions this file is about; that is why M-27 adds the exitable-based decomposition, and without M-27 the None branch is dead code. (c) The tape-boundary change shifts a small number of trades one minute earlier, perturbing D3/D4/S3/S4 slightly — real but sub-noise, and it must be re-run rather than asserted identical.
- *Collides with:* M-27, M-28

**n-50 — kalshi_live/kalshi_drift_detect.py:1**

- *Defect:* Frozen one-shot artifacts, not standing tools; live path never imports them; timezone handling verified clean
- *Recommendation:* NO CODE CHANGE — The timezone handling ITSELF needs no change — it is verified correct on the current inputs (offset-aware fromisoformat().timestamp() against epoch candle ts), and the guard above is hardening against a future input, not a correction. The 'frozen one-shot artifact, not a standing tool' status is a documentation/canon label, not a defect: do NOT promote these scripts into the live path, do not import them from the quoter, and do not add scheduling, retry or service machinery to make them 'production'. The correct treatment is the provenance banner plus a STATUS header in each of the six files.
- *Why:* labels.ts()/fetch.ts() would silently reinterpret a naive timestamp as machine-local time on any re-export, misaligning every window against epoch candle timestamps while every printed shape still looks plausible; and the six files are frozen one-shot deliverables whose silent re-use for the queued D-E study is the note's stated risk surface.
- *Standing detector:* The INPUT FINGERPRINT banner — any re-run on new receipts announces that its numbers are not the published ones — plus kalshi_live/test_live_never_imports_drift_study.py: a static AST walk of the live module graph rooted at maker_kalshi_quoter.py, maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py and maker_kalshi_client.py asserting no transitive import of kalshi_drift_* or signal_book_drift. Static rather than runtime, so it cannot be defeated by import side effects, and it is the alarm for 'someone wired the study into the bot'.
- *Test:* kalshi_live/test_drift_naive_timestamp_refused.py — pytest.raises(ValueError, labels.ts, '2026-07-22T12:00:00') and the same for fetch.ts; today both silently return a machine-local epoch, and on a UTC box they even return the right number, which is exactly why this is a latent environment-dependent defect. Companion no-op proof: the frozen CSV still yields 18 positives / -$46.21 through the guarded ts().
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* no
- *Risk:* (a) The tz guard makes the harness refuse a re-export that drops offsets — intended, but it converts 'runs and quietly lies' into 'does not run', and someone under time pressure may patch the guard out rather than fix the export. (b) The fingerprint digests are module constants; the queued D-E study will legitimately extend the cache, so the banner will shout on every run until the digests are re-recorded, and shouting-by-default trains people to ignore it. The wording must therefore be the TRUE statement ('not comparable to the published .txt'), never an error. (c) The AST import test will fail if the live quoter ever legitimately grows a shared helper that also lives in the study family — which is exactly what M-27's kalshi_book_fair.py could become; the test must whitelist that module by name or M-27 will break it.
- *Collides with:* m-55, m-57, M-27

**n-51 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:448**

- *Defect:* Hot path (dark) calls desired_quotes without own_orders - F5-less gate basis if WS_HOT is armed
- *Fix:* SAME ROOT CAUSE AND SAME FIX AS M-13 — no separate change. One commit closes M-13, m-1 and n-51: hoist the `std_lock` standing read above the `desired_quotes` call in `Daemon.hot_event` and pass `own_orders=M._own_order_levels(standing_t)`, plus the `desired_quotes` kwarg-parity self-check as the class detector. See M-13 for the diff, the detector and the test.

n-51's contribution to the design is the SAFETY ARGUMENT that keeps this out of the blocker tier and justifies shipping it as an ordinary fix under the existing WS_HOT flag: `hot_reprice_ops` only emits an op on a same-side, same-count, different-price match, so a gate divergence can only ever collapse desired to [] and yield `no_op`. No wrong order is reachable from this defect — only silently missing hot coverage, and only when WS_HOT is armed.
- *Why:* Identical mechanism to M-13: `own_orders` is the F5 correction that removes our own resting size from the reward denominator, and the hot path does not supply it, so presence/netev/capture would evaluate on the double-counted basis the cold path no longer uses. The two paths would compute different desired books from identical state.
- *Standing detector:* See M-13: the `hot_model_params` parity row is the class detector (any future cold-path model input missing from hot), and the per-cycle `hot_outcomes` reason histogram is the recurrence detector (a rise in `no_op` with zero `submitted` while books move).
- *Test:* See M-13's `test_hot_path_passes_f5_own_orders_kills_broken_variant` plus the parity assertion `assert D._model_param_parity() == []`.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — gated by the existing KALSHI_WS_HOT flag (0 live).
- *Risk:* See M-13. n-51-specific: the divergence is invisible in today's telemetry, so there is no baseline for how much hot coverage the fix restores — which is why the reason histogram must ship in the same commit rather than after arming.
- *Collides with:* M-13, m-1

**n-52 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_ws_daemon.py:692**

- *Defect:* Watch list truncates alphabetically at 80 over the PIVOT pool - resting tickers can fall off the fast feed
- *Fix:* SAME ROOT CAUSE AND SAME FIX AS n-24 — no separate change. One commit closes n-24 and n-52: replace `sorted(tickers)[:80]` with `_select_watch()` (resting/held tickers first, then footprint rank order, then the rest, capped at `KALSHI_WS_WATCH_MAX`), make the resubscribe comparison set-based, and log a `watch_truncated` row whenever the cap actually drops tickers. See n-24 for the code, the detector and the tests.

n-52's contribution is the SIZING of the exposure: `ctx.build`'s ticker set is `select_footprint`'s pool, which under PIVOT_SELECT (live: PIVOT_SELECT=1) can be up to PIVOT_POOL_MULT × FOOTPRINT_TOP rows before the union with standing — so the 80-cap is not a distant theoretical bound, it is one allowlist widening away. That is what makes this worth fixing before the pool grows rather than after someone notices KX-late-alphabet markets requoting on the 60s heartbeat.
- *Why:* Same as n-24: the union of the PIVOT pool and standing orders is truncated ALPHABETICALLY, so a resting/held market late in the alphabet loses its mirror, its Stage A book-move trigger and its Stage C service while an unquoted candidate keeps a feed slot — and nothing anywhere says so.
- *Standing detector:* See n-24: the `watch_truncated` log row (kept/dropped/sample) plus a `watch_dropped` count in the cold_cycle row. Alarm on any non-zero.
- *Test:* See n-24's `test_watch_selection_keeps_resting_tickers_over_alphabet_kills_broken_variant` and `test_watch_truncation_is_logged`. Add the n-52-specific no-op pin: at the current ~33-row pool the selected SET equals `sorted(tickers)[:80]`, proving the change is inert until the pool actually grows.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — same set as today at the current pool size; see n-24.
- *Risk:* See n-24. n-52-specific: because it is a no-op at today's pool size, the fix ships with zero live evidence that the new ordering behaves as intended under truncation — the tests are the only proof, and the first real exercise will be the day the allowlist widens. Make sure the `watch_truncated` row is being read at that point rather than discovered afterwards.
- *Collides with:* n-24, n-22

**n-53 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:3771**

- *Defect:* bound_creates can drop unwind creates when cancels alone consume the 400-op write budget
- *Recommendation:* NO CODE CHANGE — No separate commit: same root cause, same line, same fix as n-15. Listing it as its own change would violate one-fix-per-commit and produce a second, divergent floor.
- *Why:* Same mechanism, same statement: cancels are uncapped and consume the create budget first, so a churn cycle can defer every de-risk create by a full cycle — the description here is n-15's failure viewed from the teardown side.
- *Standing detector:* n-15's `unwind_creates_dropped` invariant and `write_budget_over` cover this instance; no separate telemetry.
- *Test:* Covered by n-15's test_write_budget_never_starves_exits. Optional second case in the same test: 420 cancels (budget arithmetic already negative) with 1 unwind create — asserts the unwind still survives, which is the n-53 scenario verbatim.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* no — see n-15.
- *Risk:* Inherits n-15's risk exactly: honouring the floor can push total ops past WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE by the number of held tickers, in the cycle already hammering the venue. Nothing additional. The only n-53-specific note: the teardown cycle it names (a mass rotation) is also the cycle most likely to hit venue rate limits, so if the overshoot ever causes rejections it will show up here first — watch write_budget_over on rotation cycles specifically.
- *Collides with:* n-15, m-16

**n-54 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:7271**

- *Defect:* _standing_from_rows falls through remaining_count_fp==0 to initial count, overstating a filled resting row
- *Fix:* Two steps, telemetry first. STEP 1 (ships on, no behaviour change): in _standing_from_rows replace the `or` fall-through with an explicit None-aware walk over ('remaining_count_fp','remaining_count','initial_count_fp','count'); when the first PRESENT remaining field is 0, bump _SILENT['standing_zero_remaining'] and KEEP today's fall-through to the initial count. STEP 2 (behind KALSHI_STANDING_ZERO_HONEST, default 0, armed only if step 1 shows the class is real): honour the zero by keeping the row in `standing` with count == 0. Deliberately NOT dropping the row: with count 0 the diff emits a cancel (harmless on an already-filled order) AND a fresh create for that side, and the existing failed-cancel deferral (:5837-5843) prevents a duplicate create if the cancel fails. Dropping the row instead would create a duplicate order with no such protection.
- *Why:* `remaining_count_fp or remaining_count or initial_count_fp or count` treats a legitimate 0 as falsy and reports a fully-filled order as still resting at its ORIGINAL size — `standing`, committed capital and the diff all see a phantom, the diff 'survives' it and does not re-create, and the side goes unquoted for a cycle.
- *Standing detector:* _SILENT['standing_zero_remaining'] — how often the venue actually lists a 0-remaining resting row. It is both the class detector and the arming evidence for step 2, and it sits beside the existing standing_row_skip counter whose own comment names duplicate-create as the risk of getting this parse wrong.
- *Test:* _standing_from_rows with {'remaining_count_fp':'0','initial_count_fp':'50',...}: step 1 asserts the counter fires and the parsed count is still 50 (failing-before: the counter does not exist); step 2 with the flag on asserts count == 0 and that diff_orders emits BOTH a cancel and a create for that side.
- *Effort:* S · *Dark:* yes for step 2 (KALSHI_STANDING_ZERO_HONEST, default 0); step 1 is counting only and ships on.
- *Risk:* Arming step 2 makes us cancel-and-recreate a row the venue reported as 0-remaining. If that 0 is a listing artifact for a genuinely resting order, we churn a LIVE order — losing queue position and a write pair — which is the anti-symmetric error to today's. Today's direction costs one unquoted cycle with no money at risk; the fix's direction touches a live order. That asymmetry is the whole reason step 2 is dark and gated on the counter proving the class is real. Also `committed` under-counts a 0-count row by its full notional for one cycle (bounded by the same failed-cancel deferral).
- *Collides with:* M-10, M-12

**n-55 — kalshi_live/maker_kalshi_quoter.py:5216**

- *Defect:* PIVOT early-stop counts unwind-only/retained tickers as earners; gated_out can go negative with strands
- *Fix:* Two commits. (1) TELEMETRY CORRECTNESS (no flag, no behaviour change): compute gated_out against the tried set — `'gated_out': len(footprint) - sum(1 for t in desired if t in fp_tickers)` — so strand-unwind tickers that enter `desired` from outside the footprint can no longer drive it negative; fp_tickers is already built at :5443, so this is a reordering plus an intersection. Add a WARNING if gated_out is ever < 0. (2) EARNER-COUNTING EARLY STOP (dark, KALSHI_PIVOT_EARNERS default 0): maintain `_earners`, incremented only where `desired[t] = q` is set AND `any(o.get('reason') != 'unwind' for o in q)`, and make the early stop read `if PIVOT_SELECT and (_earners if PIVOT_EARNERS else len(desired)) >= FOOTPRINT_TOP: break`. The fetch-fail retained path (:5243-5258) and the reduce-only strips (:5350-5367) then correctly stop occupying earner slots.
- *Why:* PIVOT_SELECT's own contract is 'stop once FOOTPRINT_TOP markets are actually QUOTED (earning)', but len(desired) counts exit-only books and fetch-fail retentions, so an inventory-heavy cycle stops reading the pool with several slots held by markets that cannot earn — while the plan row reads full.
- *Standing detector:* Plan keys `pivot_earners` and `pivot_nonearner_slots` (= len(desired) - earners) emitted whenever PIVOT_SELECT is on. A persistent gap between pivot_quoted and pivot_earners IS the slot-efficiency leak, and it stays visible after the fix (it should fall toward 0 once armed). Plus the gated_out >= 0 assertion, which is the standing proof that the telemetry arithmetic stays sound as new paths write into `desired`.
- *Test:* test_pivot_select.py, two failing-before cases. (a) test_early_stop_counts_earners_only: FOOTPRINT_TOP=2, pool of 4 where markets 1-2 return unwind-only quotes (held, exit-only) and 3-4 return joins. BEFORE the loop breaks after market 2 and no join ever rests; AFTER (flag on) markets 3-4 are read and quoted and pivot_earners == 2. (b) test_gated_out_never_negative: a strand-unwind ticker outside the footprint lands in `desired`; BEFORE gated_out can go negative, AFTER it equals the tried-but-unquoted count and the WARNING never fires. Companion pin: with KALSHI_PIVOT_EARNERS=0 the quoted set is identical to current code.
- *Effort:* M · *Dark:* yes for the early-stop change (KALSHI_PIVOT_EARNERS, default 0 => byte-identical); no for the gated_out correction, which cannot alter any decision and should land on its own. · **CONCERNING**
- *Risk:* Arming the earner count makes the loop read FURTHER down the pool on every inventory-heavy cycle: more _get_book calls against a bounded read budget, and `except RuntimeError: break` at :5229-5230 truncates the footprint outright when that budget is exhausted — so the fix can, on a heavy cycle, trade a short earner list for a truncated footprint. It also deploys strictly MORE capital and more standing orders per cycle (up to FOOTPRINT_TOP genuine accumulating markets rather than FOOTPRINT_TOP slots), which pushes on the write budget (see n-15), the family cap and the total cap simultaneously. And because footprint is reassigned to `consumed` at :5437-5438, changing what gets consumed also changes fp_retained_pct, prev_footprint and the strand-unwind eligibility set — the stickiness meter's history is not comparable across the arming date.
- *Collides with:* n-17, m-16, n-15, M-31


====================================================================================================
## S7. DESIGNER SET SUMMARIES

### quoter1
17 items across the quoter's config, selection, floor-gate, macro-probe and throttle layers. They collapse into 6 root causes: (1) env parsing that hides malformed values and only accepts '1' (m-12, m-13); (2) three sites computing "what size will actually rest" with three different predicates (m-8, m-58, and n-2's max-of-both-sides ratio); (3) the D3 ramp map restored AFTER the gate that reads it (M-1); (4) the macro-probe ladder deciding all-or-nothing at build time while the venue enforces per-order, plus its spec drift and its silent two-knob arming coupling (M-4, n-5, n-7); (5) est-feed hygiene — a missing end_date treated as live evidence and a monthly file rescanned to read one line (m-15, m-7); (6) funnel/telemetry silence (n-6, n-9, n-10). m-9 (unstripped SERIES_ALLOW), m-18 (throttle step-back crossing the entry band) and n-1 (F17 doc drift, no code change recommended) stand alone. FIVE fixes are flagged concerning: m-13 and m-9 can flip live behaviour at the next restart depending on live.env text I could not read from this sandbox and both carry a mandatory pre-deploy grep; m-8/m-58 is live-active today and only ever REFUSES MORE, so it can visibly shrink the quoted book on a receipt-proven lane in one deploy; n-2 moves in the OPPOSITE direction (admits more) and invalidates the MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20 calibration, so it ships dark; m-18 keeps a side resting that today disappears in a directional regime and cannot be validated without live exposure, so counter-live/behaviour-dark. Only two fixes need new flags (n-2: KALSHI_F3_PER_SIDE; m-18: KALSHI_THROTTLE_BAND_KEEP) — the macro-probe and est-feed work is already dark behind empty MACRO_PROBE_TICKERS and EST_FEED=0. One systemic finding surfaced while reading: there is NO `plan.update(qstats)` anywhere in the module, so macro_probe_markets/_unaffordable/_wall_refused are written and then discarded — the D-C subsystem currently has NO telemetry in any artifact the operator reads.

*Cross-item notes:* CONFLICT CLUSTERS AND LANDING ORDER.\n\nCluster A — env helpers (:154-182, :3848-3925): m-12 then m-13. m-12 creates the `_ENV_MALFORMED` registry that m-13's unknown-token path writes into, and m-12's WATCH-branch warning is what makes m-13's `_cast_envb` failure visible. m-13 also REPLACES an existing green test (test_env_visibility.py::test_envb_non_one_value_is_off) that deliberately pins the current behaviour — that test asserts the defect and the swap must be stated in the commit message.\n\nCluster B — floor gate / D3 (`_expected_credit_usd` :1316-1341, walk :5061-5070, clamp :5311-5346): land M-1 first (provably a live no-op today, makes the map trustworthy), then m-8 (+m-58 closes with it — one commit, two ids), then n-2 LAST and dark. All three edit the same ~10 lines; m-8 deletes the SERIES_ALLOW condition that n-2's rewrite would otherwise re-touch, and n-2 removes the ratio m-8 leaves in place. Landing n-2 before m-8 would produce a non-monotone admission change with no way to attribute it. m-9 also lands in this cluster's blast radius: it changes what SERIES_ALLOW CONTAINS while m-8 removes one of its consumers, so do not land them in the same deploy — the resulting admission change would be unattributable.\n\nCluster C — macro probe (`_macro_probe_quotes` :681-724 plus its telemetry): land n-5 first (pure telemetry — it is the only way to SEE the other two land), then n-7 (level contract), then M-4 (edge guard + placement reconciliation). M-4 and n-7 both rewrite the price/refusal block and n-7's refusal must not double-count with M-4's; n-6 adds selection-side designation counters that read against n-5's quote-side counters, so n-6 should land with or after n-5.\n\nCluster D — `_ALWAYS_EMIT_DROPS` / FP_DROPS (:1097-1101, :2000-2028, :2286): n-9, n-6 and n-10 all add keys to the same tuple and the same drops dict. Trivially mergeable but they will textually collide; land n-9 first (it defines the doctrine extension), then n-6 and n-10.\n\nCluster E — `_est_feed_cached` (:737-781): m-15 (semantic, small) then m-7 (IO rewrite). Landing m-7 first would force m-15 to be written against unfamiliar tail-read code for no benefit.\n\nSTANDALONE: m-18 touches only `_throttled_quote`; n-1 is documentation plus two counters.\n\nCROSS-CUTTING OBSERVATION worth an operator decision, NOT proposed as a fix here: m-8 and m-58 both trace their real cost to the L3 series-probe taint (:4094-4104, re-clamp :4578-4583), which sets explore=True on EVERY sibling of a series with one permanently-OUT open member, including allowlisted receipt-proven payers. Aligning the floor gate with the sizing clamp makes that taint's cost visible (many refusals on a proven lane) rather than paid silently (sub-$1 accrual plus fill risk). If the refusal count after m-8 looks unacceptable, the correct next question is whether the taint's blast radius is right — not whether to re-introduce the gate's allowlist carve-out.\n\nPRE-DEPLOY FACTS I COULD NOT ESTABLISH FROM THIS SANDBOX (all three gate a concerning fix): the live values of KALSHI_EXPLORE_PROBE_CT and KALSHI_ALLOW_PROBE_EXCEPTION (gates m-8's live impact), the exact text of KALSHI_SERIES_ALLOW (gates m-9), and the spelling of the four _envb knobs (gates m-13). Each needs one grep on the box before its commit ships.

### quoter2
17 items collapse into 8 distinct fixes plus 9 references. Shared fix #1 (M-2, closing M-5/M-7/M-17/M-18) replaces the model's best-bid-anchored, Target-truncated qualifying walk with the venue's published LIP walk (Target/5 cumulative reference, 1.0x at-or-better, no per-order cutoff) behind KALSHI_LIP_REF=0, routing all three duplicated walks through one helper pair and shipping a shadow capture column on day one. Shared fix #2 (m-39, closing m-61/n-11) threads a freshness-gated standing snapshot into the sweeper's prospective capture. Shared fix #3 (m-20, closing m-59) extracts the F3 ramp scale into one helper used by the presence floor, the netev model fallback and the capture gate — evaluated over the decision horizon, not the instant, because the naive instantaneous version creates a permanent new-market lockout. The four singletons: m-2 (never compose a stale standing snapshot with a fresher book — conservative fallback plus a per-level clamp), M-29 (net-EV margin unit, shipped bit-identical via knob -700 then armed with a one-line env edit), m-42 (split the mixed-unit telemetry key additively), M-6 (the activate credit gate must model the book we would create — and the same fix surfaces a SECOND, independent kill of that lane: D3_RAMP clamps activate orders to 5-10ct, so the lane stays honestly closed under live flags but is finally measured). Four fixes are flagged concerning: M-2 and M-18 (arming lowers modeled capture 2-25x and invalidates NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT=3.0, MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20, CAPRANK_CALIB and the presence table simultaneously), m-11 (loosens a live entry gate on a modelled, not measured, accrual premise), M-29 (arms a bench gate against a defect-era table where temp sits 0.9pp from the live threshold), m-20 (tightens two live gates and, done naively, deadlocks new markets), M-6 (opens the largest at-fair-value per-market exposure in the bot if later armed). No item warranted a no-fix answer; every reference item carries a distinct contribution (a fixture, a premise correction, an arming precondition, or a telemetry field) rather than an invented change.

*Cross-item notes:* SEQUENCING (they collide in the same four functions — _qualifying_score, _qualifying_breakdown, _prospective_capture, _market_telemetry_row): m-2 -> M-2 -> m-39 -> n-38 -> n-4 -> M-6, then independently m-20 -> m-59, and M-29 -> m-42 in the net-EV block. Landing m-2 first is deliberate: its `_own_rest_df` per-level clamp is the helper M-2's walk rewrite folds in, and its fill-suspect guard is what makes m-39's off-cycle subtraction safe. M-6 must land LAST of the walk family because its `add_to_cum` parameter is defined against the post-M-2 walk.

DO NOT ARM MORE THAN ONE FLAG PER DEPLOY. The five behaviour-changing flags (KALSHI_LIP_REF, KALSHI_PRESENCE_KEEP_S, KALSHI_RAMP_BASIS, KALSHI_ACTIVATE_CREDIT_MODEL, and the KALSHI_NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT -700 -> -7 dial) push live admission in incompatible directions: LIP_REF and RAMP_BASIS both tighten (fewer, smaller admits), PRESENCE_KEEP_S and ACTIVATE_CREDIT_MODEL both loosen. Arming LIP_REF and PRESENCE_KEEP_S together is the specifically bad pair — the first pushes markets under the $1.20 floor and the second then keeps us resting in them.

CALIBRATION INVALIDATION, stated plainly: arming KALSHI_LIP_REF makes NETEV_MODEL_HAIRCUT=3.0 wrong by construction (it was fitted against the very over-prediction being removed), and makes MIN_CREDIT_USD=1.20, the presence table and CAPRANK_CALIB stale. The `capture_usd_day_lip` shadow column exists so B8 can re-fit from rows collected BEFORE the flip; without a shadow period there is no basis to re-fit from and the flip is a blind config change on live money. n-4 separately invalidates the presence table's late-window buckets (it changes what a wind-down row's share means), so the table should be re-derived once, after both n-4 and the LIP shadow period, not twice.

THINGS I COULD NOT VERIFY AND THAT GATE DECISIONS: (1) KALSHI_ALLOC_KEY and KALSHI_SWEEP_ENABLED are absent from the live env list supplied with this task — m-61 claims both are 1. If ALLOC_KEY=1, m-39 reorders live capital on deploy and must ship dark; read the live file before landing it. (2) KALSHI_MAX_ACTIVATE_CAPITAL is likewise absent (code default $150) and bounds M-6's worst case. (3) The exact tie-break in the venue's Target/5 walk (per aggregated price LEVEL vs per ORDER, >= vs >) is my reading of the published formula, not a receipt — on books with many small orders at one price the reference can differ by a tick.

STANDING-DETECTOR THEME (CLASS NOT INSTANCE): four of these defects are instances of one class — a model quantity computed on a basis nobody measures against ground truth. The single detector that catches the whole class going forward is the credit_history residual: per program, modeled period credit vs the credit_history payout, bucketed and alarmed on median-ratio drift. rewards_residual and rewards_alloc_by_series remain banned as reward sources. Every per-fix counter listed above is a local alarm; that residual is the one that would have caught M-2, M-17, M-18 and m-20 without any of them being noticed by a reviewer.

### quoter3
17 items in the quoter's activate/sizing/allocation subsystem (maker_kalshi_quoter.py ~3105-5216), designed against the real code at worktree HEAD 233ee86. Three shared root causes collapse 6 items into 3 fixes: n-15+n-53 (write-budget starvation of exits), m-6+m-14 (hand-maintained knob maps ⇒ silent no-op class), m-19+n-20 (day-baseline marker survives the UTC roll). Two independent items (M-9, M-31) are the same DOCTRINE — "depth that cannot reach Target Size earns $0 for everyone under LIP R3, so rest all of it or none of it" — and their fixes are deliberately shaped identically. 6 items are flagged concerning: M-9 (its honest fix retires most of the activate path under the live D3 ramp, and forfeits the option value of a rival bridging the gap), m-4 (the marginal-rate fix can silently turn D-A into a no-op, and its direction is only correct if the 08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS checkpoint rules the estimate is accrual-bearing), m-60 (restores accumulating size in the last quotable minutes of short programs — the informed-flow window the ramp exists to shrink — and breaks the D-G baseline mid-experiment), m-16 (splitting cap_desired's unconditional keep exposes fetch-fail-retained books to the cap, so a network blip can cancel live queue position), M-31 (arming the true macro charge evicts ordinary earners from the select budget the same week B8 wants a clean read), n-55 (earner-counting deploys strictly more capital and reads per cycle). 4 items need one new dark flag each; m-6's derived knob map must land FIRST or every new flag ships with the exact silent-no-op defect m-14 names. Nothing here was edited — planning only.

*Cross-item notes:* SEQUENCING (matters — several fixes touch the same functions):
1. LAND m-6 FIRST. Five of these fixes add a knob (m-60 KALSHI_RAMP_WD_EFFECTIVE, M-9 KALSHI_ACTIVATE_ATOMIC, m-16 KALSHI_CAP_SPLIT_UNWIND, m-4 KALSHI_EST_RATE_MIN_SPAN_S, n-55 KALSHI_PIVOT_EARNERS). Without m-6's derived restart-only rule each one must be hand-added to _refresh_safety_knobs or it ships as a silent no-op — i.e. this batch would otherwise MANUFACTURE five new instances of the defect m-14 reports. m-6 is warning-only and cannot move an order, so it is the safest thing here to land first.
2. desired_quotes collision group: m-4, m-10, M-9, m-60 all edit desired_quotes, and m-4/m-10/M-9 all edit the SAME activate block (:3098-3131). They must be sequenced, not parallelised: M-9 (shared _activate_feasible helper + telemetry, no-op) → m-10 (continuation exemption) → m-4 (marginal est, dark feature) → m-60 (ramp anchor, separate branch). Every rebase after M-9 must re-read the block; line numbers in this batch go stale immediately.
3. run_once collision group: n-12 (cyc/load_state ordering) and m-22 (STOP branch) touch lines within ~40 of each other; n-17 (delete :5129) and n-55 (loop at :5209) share the quote-loop block. Land n-12 before m-22 (m-22 adds a load_state call to the STOP branch and both fixes care about where state is first read).
4. Capital-pipeline group: M-31 (select-budget walk charge) → m-16 (cap_desired keep split) → n-15 (bound_creates unwind floor) → n-55 (more earners upstream). They are consecutive stages of ONE pipeline; arming them out of order can make the walk and cap_desired disagree about the same ticker in the same cycle. Arm one per day with a plan-row read between.
SHARED DOCTRINE: M-9 and M-31 are the same rule from LIP R3 ("both sides must meet Target or the snapshot is EXCLUDED for everyone"). Write ONE atomicity idiom and use it twice, or the next reader will treat them as two unrelated special cases and a third path will re-open the class. _macro_probe_quotes already implements it correctly (:691-692, "Unaffordable Target -> rest NOTHING") — copy that shape, do not invent a second one.
BASELINE HYGIENE: m-60, n-55, M-31 and M-9-armed each move a measured baseline. Do not arm two of them in the same UTC day or the credit/capture attribution for that day is unreadable, and B8's netev refit inherits the confusion. Every arming needs a dated line in the handoff naming which baseline it broke.
NOT IN THIS SET but adjacent, flagged not fixed: the STOP path's own client-order-id nonce (mk-stopflat-{int(time.time())}, :6506/:6582) has n-12's exact collision class; it is unreachable today only because STOPFLAT_REPEAT_S paces repeat flattens. If that pacing is ever shortened, fix it with n-12's helper.
DEPLOY: every fix here is in the deployed quoter blob (md5-verified against HEAD). Full suite baseline 1233 passed / 2 xfailed exit 0 must stay green; the four dark flags must each be proven byte-identical at default-0 by a flag-off plan-row diff, which is this file's established discipline (see the funding/pivot/standdown/capture observability blocks at :5996-6134).

### quoter4
17 items, 13 distinct fixes: M-10/M-11/M-30 collapse into ONE root fix (a book-read failure must never silently withdraw a ticker from `desired`, because diff_orders reads absence as cancel-everything) applied at three call sites — the quote loop's RuntimeError branch, the footprint collapse, and the strand-unwind loop's two escape paths. Four items ship behind new dark flags (KALSHI_RETAIN_ON_READFAIL, KALSHI_TWO_SIDED_ENFORCE, KALSHI_STRAND_MAX_MKTS, KALSHI_STANDING_ZERO_HONEST); M-12 ships behind KALSHI_STOPFLAT_DEFER with a recommendation to default it ON because the unfixed STOP path can open a naked opposite position while the bot is halted. M-8/m-38/n-8 are all the same clamp block and must land in that order. Five items are concerning: M-10/M-11 (holds live quotes on unread books), M-8 (an operator designation now defeats the L3 risk limiter), m-17 (trades earning presence for an unmeasured inventory-lean risk), M-12 (a deferred offset means a stale exit for up to 1800 s on the halt path), m-23 (any armed cap delays risk-reducing exits). One item (n-16) gets no code change: removal of dead code needs operator signoff, so it ships as a docstring warning plus a zero-caller grep test. Every fix carries a standing detector; three of them (plan['onesided_accumulating'], lip_noref_usd, _SILENT['fill_count_field_missing']) measure classes that nothing measures today.

*Cross-item notes:* LANDING ORDER (collisions are real, not hypothetical): 1) M-10 (+M-11,M-30) → 2) n-18 (it counts M-10's new off-footprint entries correctly only after M-10 lands) → 3) M-8 → 4) m-38 → 5) n-8 → 6) m-37 (3-6 all touch the :5311-5423 loop tail and will textually collide) → 7) n-13 → 8) m-17 (its enforcement cancels must be counted in n-13's write_ops) → 9) m-21 → 10) n-21 (9-10 share the re-rest path) → 11) M-12 → 12) n-54 (M-12 parses the same rows _standing_from_rows produces) → 13) m-23 → 14) n-19 → 15) n-16.

ADJACENT DEFECT SURFACED WHILE DESIGNING n-19, OUT OF THIS ITEM SET, NEEDS ITS OWN OPERATOR RULING: the module's R4 replica defines the per-side reference as the HIGHEST BID (_qualifying_score :2555-2558, _qualifying_breakdown :2718, _prospective_capture :2584), while the venue-published LIP rule is the first level whose CUMULATIVE resting size reaches Target/5 — which can sit BELOW the touch. Every consumer of that model inherits the mismatch: the $1.20 floor gate (_expected_credit_usd), the capture gate, W12 price shape, and SCORE_RANK's capture_usd_day. n-19 deliberately adds new lip_* keys rather than redefining at_ref_*, precisely so the two definitions are never mixed in one study; do not let a future session 'harmonise' them by changing the telemetry to match the model.

VERIFY BEFORE ANY DEPLOY (the published live flag list is known incomplete): the actual live values of KALSHI_MKT_TELEMETRY, KALSHI_SCORE_RANK, KALSHI_AMEND_DECREASE and KALSHI_READ_BUDGET. m-37 is a no-op or a live ranking change depending on MKT_TELEMETRY; n-13 is a no-op or an immediate throughput tightening depending on AMEND_DECREASE; M-10/M-30's reachability depends on READ_BUDGET vs the post-P2 candidate pool.

TEST BASELINE: the suite is 1233 passed / 2 xfailed, exit 0. Every fix above adds tests, so each commit must record its own new pass count; a commit that leaves the count unchanged has not added the failing-before test the money-path rule requires. M-10, m-17, M-12, m-23, n-21 and n-54 are all money-path changes and none of them may ship without their failing-before pin.

WHAT CANNOT BE TESTED WITHOUT LIVE EXPOSURE: (a) whether WRITE_BUDGET_PER_CYCLE models the venue's real rate limit at all — n-13's _SILENT['venue_429'] counter is the only way to learn it, and it needs live cycles; (b) whether a longer client_order_id is accepted (m-21) — undocumented venue limit, first-cycle watch item; (c) the live cost of the one-sided accumulating lean (m-17) versus the presence its enforcement would destroy — the detector must run for days before the flag is armed; (d) M-12's stale-exit-versus-through-flat trade on the halt path, which by definition only exercises during a halt.

### ws_client
19 items across the WS/client subsystem collapse into 15 distinct fixes, because two triples/pairs share one root cause each: M-13 + m-1 + n-51 are one missing `own_orders` kwarg on the hot path's `desired_quotes` call (design on M-13), and n-24 + n-52 are one alphabetical `[:80]` watch truncation (design on n-24). The dominant structural theme is that kalshi_ws_feed.py has FOUR ways a connection's trust can end and only two of them invalidate anything — M-14 (error frame leaves mirrors CLEAN+FROZEN for the whole backoff sleep, while Stage C is LIVE with WS_BOOK_COLD=1), m-25 (no reconnect callback, so HotContext survives a socket gap that hid a fill), m-24 (gap detection silently disabled if `seq` moves) and m-27 (an out-of-range delta price becomes a phantom level) are all instances of "the mirror vouches for something it cannot prove". The proposed spine is one `Feed._end_session(reason)` helper that every teardown path must call BEFORE it sleeps, plus a `mirror_book` decline-reason histogram and a completed dialect-drift gauge (side keys, price scale, seq presence) in the existing cold_cycle log row — which today reports book_ws vs book_rest but never WHY. Only two items are truly latent behind WS_HOT=0 (M-13/m-1/n-51 and m-30); everything else touches code that runs on live money right now. Three fixes are flagged concerning: m-29 (a Retry-After sleep can stall quoting on the write worker while cold_lock is held, and cannot be tested without provoking a real 429 — ships with detection live and honoring dark), m-46 (a read-only pagination fix that reaches the LIVE netev gate through kalshi_netev_table.json and invalidates its calibration — the table publish, not the code, is the gate), and n-27a (removing the hardcoded account UUID breaks five credit-reading tools unless KALSHI_USER_ID lands in live.env first). Nothing in the set requires disabling a market, side, series or feature; every fix either declines to vouch (falling back to the proven REST path) or supplies information that was already computed elsewhere.

*Cross-item notes:* RECOMMENDED LANDING ORDER (one fix per commit; the dependency is real, not stylistic):
1. M-14 — introduces `Feed._end_session(reason)`. m-24, m-25, m-27, m-28 and n-25 all edit the same `_dispatch`/`run` region and assume that helper exists. Landing any of them first guarantees a rebase conflict and, worse, a half-migrated teardown where one arm dirties and another does not.
2. m-25 (rides `_end_session`'s `on_disconnect` hook, ~6 lines), then m-27 (price contract), then m-24 (seq gauge + strict flag + the `on_book` reason-tuple inversion), then m-26 (deque + prune), then n-25 (mode-aware/sticky hosts), then m-28 (barren-session escape — needs `_end_session`, `on_degraded`, and the watch rebuild from n-24).
3. Daemon side: n-23 (seqlock), n-24/n-52 (watch selection), n-26 (bootstrap + fill shape).
4. Hot path: M-13/m-1/n-51 (own_orders + parity check), then m-30 (cancel-scale clamp). Both are dark under WS_HOT=0; land them before Stage B is ever armed, not after.
5. Client: m-29 (rate-limit gate), m-30 already landed, m-46 (credit pagination — table diff BEFORE publish), n-27 (attribution log now, UUID literal removal only after live.env carries KALSHI_USER_ID).
6. n-22 last — it introduces a new cross-module contract (quoter publishes LAST_CYCLE, daemon consumes it) and its ticker set interacts with n-24's ranking; landing it before n-24 would quietly gut the footprint-rank tier via the `getattr(..., [])` fallback.

TELEMETRY HUB CONFLICT: eight fixes add fields to the single `_log({"ev": "cold_cycle", ...})` row in `Daemon.cold_cycle` (M-14 session_ends + decline histogram, m-24 seq_missing, m-26 mem/rss/lat_p50, m-27 parse_reasons, m-28 barren, m-29 rl_hits, m-30 cancel_scale_suspect, n-24 watch_dropped, n-25 ws_url/fallthrough). Sequential commits each touching that dict literal will conflict textually every time. Suggested mitigation, decided once up front: refactor the row's construction into a `Daemon._cycle_telemetry()` method returning a dict, in the M-14 commit, so each later fix appends one key to a method body instead of re-editing a call-site literal.

TEST-DOUBLE UPDATES REQUIRED (ours, not external interfaces, but they must move in the same commits): `test_ws_book_cold._FakeFeed` gains `session_ok` (M-14) and `seq_missing_frames` (m-24); `test_ws_daemon.MockClient.cancel_remaining_ct` inherits the new optional param automatically (m-30, verified — its fixtures use remaining ≤ want_count so no existing assertion flips); `_patch_quotes` already uses `lambda *a, **k` so M-13's extra kwarg passes through unchanged. I checked for tests that assert the DEFECT rather than the behavior and found none: `test_feed_error_frame_forces_reconnect` asserts only the False return and the counter, and the `price: 37` delta tests pass an INT (→ 0.37, in range) so m-27's range guard leaves them green.

SHARED-CAUSE MAP: M-13 ≡ m-1 ≡ n-51 (hot `own_orders`); n-24 ≡ n-52 (watch truncation); m-26 ≡ n-27(b) (unbounded growth in a long-lived process — same fix shape, deque with maxlen, land together or cross-reference); M-14 ≡ m-25 (connection-discontinuity invalidation — the mirrors half and the ctx half of one invariant).

DEPLOY NOTE: the deployed quoter blob is md5-equal to HEAD, and n-22 is the only fix in this set that touches maker_kalshi_quoter.py (an additive LAST_CYCLE publication). Every other fix is confined to the three subsystem files, so the md5 verification surface stays small — keep n-22 last so a quoter blob change never rides along with a WS-only deploy.

### scoring
Eight items across the scoring/rank/feedback/scorecard subsystem. Two root causes dominate and collapse the set: (1) DUPLICATED MODELS — the same quantity is implemented twice and only one copy got fixed (R1 pool: quoter fixed, scorecard not [M-19]; "effective commit": select-budget walk age-gates refs and applies probe/ramp caps, shadow_rank does neither [M-20 + m-40]; est_commit_usd is a hand-copy of _capped_join's JOIN_SIZE=0 branch only [n-37]). (2) HALF-A-BOOK BLINDNESS — the score cache only remembers the YES reference, so swing penalty [n-34] and the commit denominator's NO price [n-37] are both computed off one side of a two-sided quote. The credit-feedback builder items (m-36, n-33, n-36) are all "the trust plane parses one venue string with no vocabulary/quantifier check". Recommended shipping order: n-36 (pure safety) -> n-33 -> m-36 (all kalshi_credit_feedback.py, sequenced because m-36 rewrites the due loop n-33 touches); n-37 join_size half (provable no-op) -> M-20 unified effective_commit_usd (closes m-40) -> n-34 recording half dark -> n-34/n-37 ref_no arming -> n-35 last. M-19 is independent and can ship any time. Three items are genuinely concerning (M-19, M-20/m-40, m-36, n-34) — see each RISK. One liveness fact I could NOT verify from the worktree: KALSHI_ALLOC_KEY. The 08-04 EOD handoff (docs/maker_handoffs/KALSHI_HANDOFF_2026-08-04_EOD.md:23-24) records ALLOC_KEY=1 and SCORE_RANK=1 live; the 08-06T19:48Z flag list in my brief omits both. Everything in M-20/m-40/n-37 is money-path if ALLOC_KEY=1 and telemetry-only if 0 — the operator must read live.env before arming any of it.

*Cross-item notes:* SEQUENCING / COLLISION MAP. (a) kalshi_capital_rank.py commit path: M-20, m-40, n-37 all edit est_commit_usd or its call site at :189 — land n-37 (signature widening, inert at JOIN_SIZE=0) first, then M-20's effective_commit_usd wrapper (which subsumes m-40), then n-37's ref_no arming. Landing them in any other order forces a rebase of the same 20 lines three times. OUT-OF-SET OBSERVATION, FLAGGING NOT DESIGNING (may already be an item in another family's set): the venue LIP formula in my brief defines the per-side Reference Price as the first level where CUMULATIVE resting size >= Target/5 walking from the best bid. Both implementations in this codebase use ref = best bid: kalshi_market_scorecard.qualifying_share:32 and the quoter's replica maker_kalshi_quoter.py:_qualifying_score (:2536) / _qualifying_breakdown (:2716-2741), and the two are pinned byte-equivalent by test_capture_gate.py:236-240. If that divergence is real, every R4 share number in the scorecard, the capture gate, the net-EV gate, the sweeper's pcap and the SCORES capture measurement is computed against the wrong reference level, and any correction must move all of them plus the pin in one commit. I did not design that fix (out of my item set) but M-19 touches the exact file, so whoever fixes M-19 should check whether a reference-rule change is queued before splitting kalshi_market_scorecard.py into helpers.

CLASS FINDING FROM M-19's DETECTOR (worth an operator decision on its own): the refuted /window_days R1 form is still live in SEVEN more modules besides the scorecard — allowlist_census_probe.py:83, allowlist_density_census.py:146, kalshi_coverage_gap.py:150, kalshi_footprint_dilution.py:98, kalshi_gate_attribution.py:113, kalshi_horizon_census.py:276+288, kalshi_market_menu.py:79. Several of those produced numbers cited in handoffs (coverage gap, footprint dilution, horizon census). The proposed detector's allowlist forces each one to be triaged as either "era-frozen, numbers stand on the old basis" or "re-run needed", which is the decision the operator should make, not me.

WHAT NO FIX IN THIS SET ADDRESSES: the credit-feedback feed has no freshness signal at the live consumer. _load_credit_feedback (quoter:482) and _d3_feedback_cached (:784) both fail open with no age check, so a dead offline builder is invisible in the trading log while D2 multipliers and the W7 clamp quietly read a week-old table. I folded a `credit_feedback_age_h` plan counter into n-36's standing detector because that is where the crash class lands, but it is really its own small fix and should be named as such if the operator wants it split.

### recorders
Sixteen items across the money MEASUREMENT layer collapse into ten distinct fixes: three real defect classes (a whole population silently repriced to zero by one bad row; a state file that is its own only copy; measurement surfaces that present unbounded or banned quantities as money) plus a set of observability gaps that are all the same shape — a degradation that is real, recoverable, and completely silent. Two duplicate pairs share one design each (M-16 covers m-3 and, via the same recovery helper, n-31; m-43 covers n-29). The highest-value work is M-15 (per-ticker replay isolation so one unrecognized fill can no longer make all 129 settled markets read cost $0.0000 in the feed the P2 verdict, kalshi_lumps_ledger and the net-EV calibration all read) and M-16's atomic map write. Nothing here needs a trading flag except m-43's fee normalization, m-31's grace window and m-34's WAL adoption, all of which change measured baselines and ship dark by default; everything else is either provably safer or pure telemetry.

*Cross-item notes:* SEQUENCING AND COLLISIONS — six of the sixteen items edit kalshi_attribution_ledger.py and four edit kalshi_fill_costs.py, so parallel landing will conflict. Proposed order, one fix per commit: (A) kalshi_fill_costs — M-15 (per-ticker replay + population guard, includes the quoter's _refresh_fill_costs edit), then n-28 (message/breakdown), then m-44 (stale defect-14 comment), then m-43/n-29 (fee basis + dark normalization) last because it builds on M-15's per-ticker replay. (B) kalshi_estimates_recorder — m-3's atomic write FIRST and alone (small, provably safer), then M-16's recovery half and n-31's backfill AFTER the 2026-08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS checkpoint. (C) kalshi_attribution_ledger — m-32 (paginator loudness, self-contained), then n-32 (skew telemetry), then m-34 (WAL field + detector; adoption dark), then m-31 (count-reconciliation detector live, grace window dark), then m-35 (extract the shared recon helper; also touches kalshi_cash_recorder), then m-33 + n-42 (labels + repo-scan guard). (D) n-30's shared JSONL module lands as its own module+tests commit and then one caller per commit; it must precede m-34's tail-recovery reader or m-34 needs its own inline torn-line walk.\n\nTIMING AGAINST THE TWO DEADLINES — the 08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS est->credit checkpoint is TOMORROW and the estimates recorder is the only thing capturing it; editing that file today risks losing the rows the fix protects, so the atomic-write half is the only thing I would land before it. The P2 verdict (2026-08-10T14:13Z) reads kalshi_fill_costs.json, cash-YYYYMM.jsonl and ledger-YYYYMM.jsonl; every ledger fix marked concerning changes those series mid-window. Where a fix changes what a recorded column MEANS, it must carry an in-row convention marker — kalshi_cash_recorder.py:279-301 already documents why (a convention change without a marker produced a +$330.0381 step indistinguishable from income).\n\nDEVIATION FROM THE 'LOWEST-NUMBERED ID' CONVENTION — for the two duplicate pairs I put the shared design on the higher-severity id rather than the numerically lower one (M-16 rather than m-3; m-43 rather than n-29), because the operator reads by severity and the MAJOR carries the triple-blind verdict. Both partner entries state the closure explicitly and add only their own delta.\n\nWHAT I DID NOT PROPOSE — no item here warranted 'no fix': m-44 is a comment edit rather than a code change but it still needs the edit, and n-42's answer is a guard test rather than removing the fields (removing a recorded series would be a demotion). I also deliberately did NOT propose weakening kalshi_attribution_ledger.fill_outcome's raise-on-unknown-shape (:140-152) — that hard fault is correct and is what leaves the ledger's watermark unadvanced; M-15 isolates the blast radius in the CONSUMER instead. And I did not repoint the ledger onto maker_kalshi_client._get_paginated (m-32): the ledger's standalone openssl signer is a deliberate independence property, not an oversight.

### netev
Seven findings across the net-EV producer chain, all read and verified against the real code in the worktree. The two MAJORs (M-21, M-22) share one root cause and get ONE fix: build_table attributes WHOLE markets with no containment or completeness check, so a market still open at read time books naked collateral as loss (right edge) and a market first traded before the window imports its pre-window, possibly defect-era cash (left edge). The fix is a `_market_scope` helper that classifies every ticker in/valued/open/straddle/out using the open-position dict build_table already computes and throws away at :163, applies ONE scope set to all four attribution legs (cash, settlement, credits, notional), optionally values open positions at a supplied liquidation mark, and reports every excluded dollar — all behind a `scope='legacy'` default so the deployed numbers do not move until the operator arms it. m-41 swaps a hand-rolled notional price/count expression for the receipt-verified kalshi_attribution_ledger accessors plus a venue-rename counter (measured no-op on the frozen 542-fill tape). n-39 puts the taker-only family back into the key union with honest evidence text (provably inert to the armed gate). n-41 gives a date-only --until whole-day semantics plus an optional ET offset. m-45 and n-40 both live in kalshi_presence_calibrate.measure and collide: unknown-life buckets get emitted with null presence (inert), still-resting orders get credited to read time (dark), and the `*|bucket` wildcard both consumers already look for is finally produced (dark, reporting-only until armed).

*Cross-item notes:* SHIP ORDER (avoids rebasing the same lines three times): m-41 -> n-39 -> n-41 -> M-21/M-22 -> m-45 C1 -> n-40 -> m-45 C2. Five of seven items live in kalshi_netev_rebuild.py and three touch the same 30 lines: the fill-accumulation loop (:169-201) is m-41's site and is rewritten by M-21's scope set; the families key union and confidence block (:261-281) is n-39's site and gains M-21's incompleteness downgrade; main()'s argparse gains --scope/--mark-open (M-21) and --until/--tz (n-41). m-45 and n-40 both rewrite the same emission loop in kalshi_presence_calibrate.measure (:140-157) and the same `buckets` structure — sequence them, never parallel.

THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT PIECE OF CONTEXT FOR JUDGING THIS SET, and it inverts the usual intuition: NETEV_MIN_MARGIN_PCT is -7.0 in live.env while net_pct_notional is a FRACTION (gas -0.0468), so `poor = net_pct < -7.0` can never fire. Today a 'receipt' grade therefore means QUOTE UNCONDITIONALLY and 'unproven' means take the model fallback, WHICH CAN SKIP. Every fix here that makes the producer more conservative (M-21/M-22 sample shrink, any confidence downgrade) therefore REDUCES quoting rather than tightening a bench — the opposite of what "conservative fix" suggests, and directly against the reward-positive / defect-negative thesis. Nothing in this set should be ARMED until that unit mismatch is ruled on separately; a contained-scope table deployed against a -7.0 knob could quietly bench presence on the six receipt families that currently always quote.

DEPLOY HAZARD, presence side only: kalshi_netev_calibrate.load_table (which the quoter uses for the net-EV table) does NOT check schema, but kalshi_presence_calibrate.load_table DOES (:171-172). Do not bump presence SCHEMA for additive fields; if it is ever bumped, the regenerated table file must land BEFORE the module or PRESENCE_TABLE becomes {} and presence_factor silently returns to 1.0 everywhere on a live gate (PRESENCE_GATE=1). I could not verify whether a presence table file currently exists on the VPS — it is absent from the worktree — so the live effect size of m-45/n-40 is UNVERIFIED.

ARMING IS INVISIBLE TO md5 VERIFICATION for two of these: the presence wildcard (n-40) and the net-EV scope mode (M-21) change live behavior by REGENERATING A TABLE FILE, not by changing a deployed .py. The md5-vs-HEAD-blob check shows green while behavior has changed. Recommend that every armed rebuild's document fields (`scope`, `wildcard_emitted`) be recorded in the deploy checklist, and consider a follow-up (not designed here, since it edits the live quoter) that surfaces the table's `scope` in _netev_table_alarm — _load_netev_table unwraps data['families'] and discards every doc-level field, so the running bot cannot see which scope rule produced the verdicts it is acting on.

D-C / KXAPRPOTUS SPECIFIC: KXAPRPOTUS sits at 36 fills against MIN_RECEIPT_FILLS=40 in the deployed table and is exactly the far-close held shape M-21 describes. Under contained-without-marks it loses its open markets and stays unproven; with marks its net could improve enough to matter. If B8 is meant to produce a verdict on the macro-probe strategy at all, the `marks` half of the M-21 fix is not optional — exclusion alone makes that strategy permanently unjudgeable, which is a worse outcome than the defect it fixes.

### drift
Eight findings across the 2026-07-23 drift-study family (kalshi_drift_{fetch,labels,detect,eval,jump,selftest}.py + signal_book_drift.py) reduce to four mechanism classes, none of which is on the live money path — the quoter imports none of these modules, so no fix here needs a dark flag, and the real gate is publication discipline rather than runtime risk. CLASS 1, fabricated inputs (M-27): one fair-value definition lives in two copies and the audited correction reached only one, so build_timeline still mints mids from 0.20/0.99 shell books and from last-trade prints; the fix is a single canonical kalshi_book_fair helper with per-minute provenance, a None value where the book is unusable, and an exitable-based jump decomposition — capped on SPREAD, not on two-sidedness, because I measured that copying the sibling's b>0/a<1 predicate verbatim would erase 20.5% of TEMP minutes concentrated in the death books. CLASS 2, unchecked alignment (M-28, n-49): list index is treated as minutes in nine detectors and six eval scores, and as candle membership in the tape aggregator; the fix resolves every window by timestamp, abstains when a window is not fully covered, and prints coverage next to every rate. CLASS 3, failures that look like data (m-55, m-57, n-48): a label chain that routes run-over contracts into the control set, a fetch writer that freezes a 5xx as a permanent empty entry, and four blanket except-Exception sites that render a crashing detector as TPR 0.00; each fix makes the failure loud and countable rather than absorbed. CLASS 4, provenance and leakage (m-56, n-50): a nearest-candle lookup that silently substitutes post-fill prices into a pre-fill predictor, and naive-timestamp parsing that would misread a future re-export as machine-local. Two items (M-27, M-28) are flagged concerning because they change published magnitudes that canon already quotes; the rest are no-ops or improvements on the frozen data and are validated only by synthetic tests.

*Cross-item notes:* SEQUENCING (three items collide textually in build_timeline / evaluate / point_samples / matched_window / decompose): recommended landing order is m-55 -> n-50 -> m-57 -> M-27 -> M-28 -> n-49 -> n-48 -> m-56. The first three are independent and no-ops on frozen data, so they can land and be verified immediately. M-27 must precede M-28 because it introduces the None-value abstain plumbing that M-28's ts-resolution reuses; M-27 must precede n-49 because it rewrites decompose. n-48 is placed AFTER M-27/M-28 deliberately: removing the exception net before the None-guards exist would abort the harness on the first short window. The alternative (n-48 first, letting it surface the crashes) is defensible and the operator should pick explicitly rather than discover it mid-run.

REPUBLICATION GATE (applies to M-27, M-28, m-56, n-49 — every fix that moves a number): the .txt/.json files in kalshi_live are frozen 07-23 deliverables that canon cites ('jump not drift', 'prior drift weak', 'life-frac is the free signal'). Do NOT overwrite them. Regenerate under new dated filenames, publish a side-by-side diff table of every headline that moved, and let the operator re-rule the canon lines. Overwriting would silently rewrite history in a corpus that other sessions read as established.

DO NOT ARM A LIVE KNOB FROM THE POST-FIX TABLES. The stated motive for repairing this harness is the queued D-E fills-vs-distance study, and the fixed numbers will be cleaner but not larger: the within-TEMP comparison is 19 + 17 positions at K=5 and both M-28's wall-clock cap and m-56's backward-only lookup shrink it further. A cleaner D2 or prior-drift number at that sample size is still not evidence for a live D2_FEEDBACK-style threshold, and the corrected harness will look more trustworthy than it is precisely because its defects are gone.

WHITELIST INTERACTION: n-50's standing detector is a static import test asserting the live quoter never reaches kalshi_drift_* / signal_book_drift, while M-27 creates kalshi_book_fair.py — a shared helper that could legitimately be imported by live code later. Whichever lands second must name the helper in the whitelist, or the two fixes break each other.

FROZEN-DATA VALIDATION LIMIT, stated plainly: m-55, m-57 and n-49 are verified NON-FIRING on the frozen inputs (0 mislabelled positions, 67/67 tickers usable, all six RUNOVER worst steps on exact 60s spacing). Their tests are synthetic by necessity. They are correct-by-construction fixes to latent holes, not fixes validated against an observed failure, and should be reported that way rather than as 'fixed and proven'.

### tools_other
18 items across the canon-feeding tool layer (kalshi_ledger*.py, w0b/w11/w16/w17, D-A/D-C test coverage). None of them is on the order-placement path — the money risk in this set is EPISTEMIC: four of them (M-3, M-23, M-24, M-25) currently mint or can mint wrong canon that live knobs already lean on (FARCLOSE_PAYING_EXCEPTION=1, D2=1, the $1-floor gate's est feed). Three root-cause clusters absorb ten items: (1) the ledger residual class — M-24 replaces the banned rewards_residual with credit_history receipts and, by doing so, closes m-50 (tautological identity check) and gives m-51 (unpriced lots) and n-47 (--rate window) a real error term to land in; (2) the replay-baseline class — M-25 (shadow read as live) + m-49 (whole-window fill lookahead) + n-44 (top-60 candidate truncation) together mean the existing W11 D2 report must be re-run ONCE after all three land, not after each; (3) the detector-silence class — M-26, n-43, m-47, m-48, m-54 are all instances of "an alarm whose all-clear is byte-identical to an alarm that cannot fire", and every fix adds a printed denominator/provenance line as the standing detector. Only three fixes touch the live quoter and all three are additive telemetry inside existing try/except regions (m-47 plan['knobs'], m-54 family_dropped_n, n-44 caprank depth knob defaulting to the current value) — no trading-behavior flag is needed, but each still needs md5 verification and a green suite. Five items are flagged concerning=true: M-3 and M-23 because correcting them may retract conclusions already absorbed into canon; M-24 because UNEXPLAINED will read amber routinely (accrued-unpaid LIP lives in equity but not yet in credit_history); M-25/m-49 because they invalidate the measurement behind a LIVE knob; m-48 because it moves pool dollars from EARNING into the UNKNOWN headline mid-experiment. Time-critical: M-3 must land and be re-run before the 2026-08-07T15:00Z APRPOTUS est->credit checkpoint, or that checkpoint gets read against the current false BEFORE_CLOSE tags. No item warranted zero work except m-50, which is closed by M-24 rather than separately.

*Cross-item notes:* SUGGESTED LANDING ORDER (one fix per commit throughout).
Ledger file (kalshi_ledger.py): M-24 first (it defines the UNEXPLAINED term and schema 2) -> m-50 rides in M-24's commit as two print/docstring lines -> m-51 (unpriced lots now have somewhere honest to land) -> n-47 (needs schema-2 rows to exist). Landing n-47 or m-51 before M-24 leaves them patching a residual that still swallows their output.
Reconstruct file (kalshi_ledger_reconstruct.py): M-23 (cash model -> canon delegation, includes the switch to L.get_paginated) then n-46 (credential class fix, which also touches kalshi_attribution_ledger.py:45). Reverse order works too, but M-23 cannot be re-run without n-46's env being exported, so verify the env first either way.
Replay file (w11_replay.py): M-25 -> m-49 -> n-44. All three change what the report means; run the W10-snapshot replay ONCE after the third and treat the prior w10_results/w11_replay_report.json as retracted. Do NOT make a D2 knob decision on the current report in between.
w17_coverage_ledger.py takes five items (M-26, m-47, m-48, m-54's reader half, n-43) that all edit main(); each fix should first extract the pure helper it needs (_credit_staleness, _farclose_label, _bucket, _parse_rate) so the commits do not collide on one monolith. m-47 and m-48 both move the UNKNOWN headline — land them in separate deploys or the baseline shift is unattributable.
w16_successor_finder.py takes m-52, m-53, n-45. n-45 (grain labeling) should land with or before m-53 (bigger convicted set), or the first run after m-53 floods the report with single-strike bans labeled as franchise convictions. m-52's "RULINGS NOT APPLIED" section consumes m-53's unioned convicted set, so m-53 before m-52 is cheaper.
QUOTER TOUCHES (m-47, m-54, n-44) are three separate telemetry keys in three separate regions; they can land in any order but each needs the md5-vs-HEAD-blob deploy discipline and a full-suite run. All three write inside try/except regions that swallow exceptions into _SILENT counters — a botched edit fails SILENTLY rather than loudly, so their tests must assert on real plan/caprank rows, not stubs.
m-5 (D-A/D-C follow-up tests) is independent of everything else and can land any time; it is the only item with zero live-behavior surface, and it is a prerequisite for arming D-A/D-C (the arm gate named in the lane state), so it is the cheapest high-value item in the set.
CROSS-CUTTING CLASS worth stating to the operator: eight of these eighteen are the same mechanism — a diagnostic whose failure mode is silence (sentinel that cannot compare, denominator that includes the wrong rows, truncated list read as complete, partial run read as a zero result, residual labeled as income). Every fix in this set therefore adds a printed denominator/provenance/completeness line. If only one policy is adopted from this batch, make it that one: no alarm ships without its denominator.
